# Systems lore

Naming chronology and discovery lore for the inhabited wormhole network,
written during a depth-first crawl of the gate graph starting at the system
nearest Sol. The "present" of the crawl is late 2426. Dates use the standard
calendar (real UTC plus 400 years).

The Union State of Earth (USE) holds Sol and the old Unifleet trunk; the
League of Free Systems (LFS) descends from the outer colonies severed by the
Great Partition of 2257, which restored contact through slipdrive at the
2272 reclamation and signed the Treaty of Concord in 2401. Settlement
populations are not simulated anywhere; every population figure below is
invented as part of the lore, chosen to be plausible for the system's
history, worlds and economy. Each entry's planetary inventory is taken from
the generated orrery itself, not from wishful survey brochures.

Systems already carrying really famous astronomical names keep them.
Systems whose settlements, worlds or character meet the naming
institutions' thresholds of notability receive invented names, with the
naming story recorded here. Everything else remains a catalogue station —
a numbered stop that was never promoted — listed with a brief note.

---
## Proxima Centauri

- **Gate opened:** 2121 (mouth seed dispatched with the first Unifleet sublight push, 2094)
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5853498713190525696 (Proxima Centauri), M, 4.25 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 billion
- **System:** three tidally locked rocky furnace-to-freezer worlds hugging the dim red primary; **Proxima Centauri 4**, a cool ocean world at 0.042 au — dense nitrogen atmosphere, 28% of the surface under shallow seas — is the settled world; an ice world and a two-mooned ice giant complete the register.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Barnard's Star; Trunk gate to GJ 65 B.
- **Registry name:** the Union Naming Board's first ratification, 2119: with one voice the settled solar system's astronomical tradition crossed the gap unchanged. The Board's founding minute records that renaming the first destination would have been "a vanity nobody out here voted for."

Proxima was the proof of the whole enterprise. The first practical paired mouths were fragile things, and the first gate pair hummed between Sol's high orbit and Proxima's for eleven years before a settlement of any size stood under the red sun. What made the system matter was not beauty — the star is a dim ember, the inner worlds are locked roast-stone — but the ocean world: air you can breathe with a mask, water you can drink after boiling, and gravity that doesn't grind your hips. Unifleet's colony planners of the 2120s, shipping cohort after cohort up a single trunk gate, wrote the operational habits that the entire core network still inherits: the exclusion volume, the quarantined seed vault, the settlement charter with public developmental guarantees written in before the first lander.

The city grew where the seas are narrowest, on the long subarctic archipelago of the terminator-adjacent north, and Proximan architecture still shows its pressure-vessel ancestry: rounded, thick-walled, windowed in panes a handspan thick, painted the warm colors the sun refuses to supply. The population is old-stock trunk settler stock — Mandarin-dominant like the rest of the core, with the quiet Anglophone neighborhoods that fleet families have occupied since the ports were cut. Proxima's singular possession is the **First Anchor**, the original gate's high-orbit mooring, decomm issioned in 2298 and preserved as a heritage site: every USE cadet making the trunk passage out of Sol passes it, and the guide script ends on the same line the Board's minute did — nobody out here voted for a vanity.

The Proximan grievance is equally old: flare insurance. The star's temper was known from Earth, and every settlement insurer on the world still rates premiums by the century-count of the observatory record. Every archipelago town drills its children in shelter discipline the way coastal Earth once drilled tsunami signs, and Proxima's delegation to the Council of Nations has sponsored a flare-relief fund in every session since 2201 — always for the worlds farther out, the Proximan delegates say, because Proxima's own shelters were finished two centuries ago.
## Barnard's Star

- **Gate opened:** 2143
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4472832130942575872 (Barnard's Star), M, 5.99 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** nothing you can stand on without a suit — a locked puffball ice giant skimming the star at 520 K, a scorched locked rock, then a cold procession of six ice giants (the outer ones trailing two, five, two, one, two moons) and three frozen iceballs out to 14 au.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Proxima Centauri; Trunk gate to HIP 92403.
- **Registry name:** kept by the Union Naming Board's standing rule on IAU-proper names — the Board minute notes Barnard's was "the first system where the name was older than every settler aboard the seed."

Barnard's was never a place to live; it was a place to fill up. The Union survey of the 2130s rated all ten worlds uninhabitable and then rated the gas giants irreplaceable, and by 2143 the first skimming platforms hung in the high atmosphere of the fourth world, dipping trails through hydrogen and helium with the red star a dull coin behind them. Every trunk push outward in the Proxima corridor took its propellant top-up here, and the fuel depots of Barnard's kept the corridor's schedule honest for a century: the "Barnard Run," Proxima to the skimmer fleet and back, was the first trade route most trunk-era pilots ever learned.

The population has never cracked three million and has never needed to. Skimmer crews work two-week atmospheric rotations and live in the orbital ring of station burbs with names that are all jokes the Union Naming Board eventually stopped pretending to regulate — Fuel Depot 4 has been petitioning to become "Dampierre" since 2288 and the Board keeps deferring it out of affection. The one custom every crew keeps is the jettison toast: the first gram of every fresh skimmed load is vented to the void "for the ship we came on," a line traced back to the fuel-short famine winter of 2177, when the corridor survived on Barnard's margin.
## HIP 92403 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2152; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 9.71 ly — a locked steam ocean world (55% of the surface, 373 K) ringed by frozen outrunners. Condensate-skimming rigs on the night-side ice margin feed the trunk's reaction-mass chain toward Barnard's. The rig crews call the boundary line where the ocean's steam meets the night frost "the weather," and their oldest superstition is never naming a rig after a person still living.
## HIP 80824 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2158; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 14.12 ly — a locked furnace rock, a 425 K ocean world with hot shallow seas, and a small cold rock with no air worth the name. A trunk fuel-and-hab stop where the corridor maintenance crews rotate through the one pressure township at the gate anchor. The township's census office has recorded the same family name — Osei — as resident gatekeepers for eleven generations, a fact trunk bureaucracy finds quietly reassuring.
## Guniibuu

- **Gate opened:** 2166
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4109030160308317312 (Guniibuu), K binary, 19.40 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 billion
- **System:** twin K suns all but indistinguishable (0.74 solar masses apiece, temperatures 4604 and 4607 K — the children's riddle is which is which); **Guniibuu 3**, the settled world, a cold-temperate rocky planet with a working atmosphere at 258 K; hot rocky furnaces inward, a five-mooned gas giant with its own captured locked companion, and the B sun's own string of small mooned rocks and a 108-Earth-mass giant.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 80824, HIP 80018, and GJ 1207; Regional gate to HIP 85523.
- **Registry name:** kept under the standing IAU-proper rule. The name comes from an Australian Aboriginal celestial tradition and crossed nineteen light-years before any settler did; the Union Naming Board's 2165 minute records the settlement petition asking *to keep* it, with the Ngarrindjeri provenance entered into the trunk register in full.

Guniibuu is where the network first forked. The original trunk ran Sol–Proxima–outward like a single cable, and at Guniibuu Unifleet's seed program split it: two new trunk pushes launched from this system within a decade of gate opening, making it the first genuine junction in interstellar civilization. Everything about the place still smells of that decision — the anchor orbital hosts three gates' worth of customs, depot, and registry traffic, and Guniibuuan families have staffed trunk logistics for nine generations the way port cities once staffed harbors.

The settled world is cold and blue-shadowed, with the population packed in the long equatorial river valleys where the air stays liquid and the twin suns together buy back some of the distance from the star. Guniibuu's best thing is its light: with two near-identical suns, every object in the system at certain alignments casts two shadows of equal strength, and Guniibuuan visual culture — the double-shadow theater, the paired-lantern architecture, the two-flare forge festivals — is unmistakably a child of that optics. The living grievance is equally local: twice a generation the suns and the gate exclusion volumes line up so that a double flare washes the anchor approaches, and Guniibuu's pilots' guild has been petitioning to shift the anchor orbit "one degree, just one" since 2310.
## Wye

- **Gate opened:** 2171
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5951824121022278144 (HIP 85523), M, 14.85 ly
- **Population:** 610,000
- **System:** a dim red star with four locked or mooned rocky worlds, none of them air you would trust — furnace rocks inward, cold bare rocks out, the whole register a place to orbit rather than inhabit.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Gaia EDR3 6439125097427143808, Gaia EDR3 5985290231327158144, and HIP 86990; Regional gate to Guniibuu.
- **Registry name:** the Union Naming Board's most-quoted ratification. Three trunk pushes staged from this system inside twenty years, and the fleet crews called the junction "the wye" from the second month of construction. When the depot finally petitioned for a real name in 2203, the Board's published minute read in full: *"Forty-two years and no better suggestion was filed. Ratified: Wye."* The crews consider this the only fully honest document in the Union register.

Nobody lives on the planets; everybody lives in the wye itself. The anchor orbital is a knot of depot rings and staging cradles where three trunk routes and Guniibuu's regional line exchange cargo, crews, and gossip, and the system's whole economy is the exchange — transfer warehousing, crew rotation, hull repair, and the famous Wye cafeteria standard ("eat before Guniibuu" is a fleet compliment). Wye's one cold-blooded fact is traffic discipline: the junction schedules more gate transits per sol than any system this far from Sol, and its dispatch office has been the trunk's training school for controllers since 2198.

The living detail is a plaque, not a monument: the original 2171 wye-marker — a wye-junction rail sign carried from Earth as a superstition by the first depot chief — still hangs over the customs hall, repainted by each controller cohort at their graduation, and departing crews still touch it on the way to the cradles, registering their transit in the oldest ledger the system keeps.
## Gaia EDR3 6439125097427143808 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2175; never promoted.
- A 0.03-solar-mass brown ember, 13.06 ly — its entire retinue of steam oceans and frozen iceballs orbits inside what most systems would call the first week. A volatiles station on the outer ice worlds refuels Wye-bound trunk traffic. The fuel crews' watch discipline runs on the innermost rock's 7.2-hour "day," the shortest shift-cycle in the trunk register, and they defend its brutal rhythm with pride.
## ε Ind

- **Gate opened:** 2168
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6412595290592307840 (ε Indi), K, 11.87 ly
- **Population:** 830 million
- **System:** a quick K dwarf — the star is one of the sky's record proper-motion runners — with a locked furnace rock and a heavy airless hot rock inward, **ε Ind 3**, the settled world, a temperate mooned rocky planet at 281 K with a working atmosphere, a 397 K ocean world in the habitable band's hot wing, and a two-mooned ice world on the rim.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Lacaille 9352 and ε Ind B; Regional gate to Lacaille 8760; Trunk gate back toward Gaia EDR3 6439125097427143808.
- **Registry name:** kept under the IAU-proper standing rule; the Bayer letter had already crossed the gap in every schoolbook, and the Union Naming Board recorded that the settlement's petition again ran the *keep* direction — Guniibuu had set the pattern.

ε Indi was the first trunk world settlers could call pleasant. Proxima's ocean world made settlement possible; ε Ind 3 made it comfortable — genuine seasons under a yellow-orange sun, a single large moon pulling honest tides, grassland latitudes that needed no dome and no mask. The system's farms fed the whole outward corridor for two generations, and "Epsilon grain" is still the trunk cafeterias' word for any honest plain bread. The 415 K heavy rock inward became the foundry: crustal metals with no ecology to damage, worked by robot fleets whose operator clans gave ε Ind its industrial aristocracy of engineers.

The hub role shaped everything else. Four gates made the system the corridor's second junction after Guniibuu and the natural seat of the trunk's outer administration — the Corridor Registry, the seed-stock vault, and the first deep-space hospital worth the name all stand on ε Ind 3's moon or in its orbit. The settled world's population is old trunk stock, and its politics are old trunk politics: municipally dull, quietly proud, and permanently a little aggrieved that the orbital administration answers to Sol before it answers to the farms. The annual Harbour Festival — commemorating the year 2212 grain ships ran the corridor during the Sol famine scare — still opens with the mayor handing the portmaster a loaf, whole, uncut, on the old custom that ε Ind does not sell its neighbors bread in pieces.
## Lacaille 9352

- **Gate opened:** 2172
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6553614253923452800 (Lacaille 9352), M, 10.86 ly
- **Population:** 410 million
- **System:** an orange-red M dwarf holding a small procession: heavy locked rocks inward; a temperate gas giant (252 Earth masses at a room-temperature 277 K) with one moon; **Lacaille 9352 4**, the settled world, a warm ocean planet — 64% of the surface under sea, 306 K, breathable with standard masks, two moons pulling layered tides; an ice world at the rim.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 65 B and HIP 439; Trunk gate to ε Ind; Regional gate to Lacaille 8760.
- **Registry name:** kept under the standing rule; the Lacaille catalogue is one of the old southern-survey traditions and the Union Naming Board has never once been petitioned to disturb a Lacaille name, which its clerks regard as the register's calmest corner.

This is a world of water and weather. Lacaille 9352 4 is warm enough that its habitable belt is the high-latitude archipelagos and the polar shallows, and its two-moon tide — the big moon's sweep stacked on the small one's quick cycles — makes the seas honest: every island harbor is built on piles and every coastal law case is about the waterline. The population lives afloat as much as ashore; hull towns ride the equatorial current, and the children's first license is a boat license. The system's export is protein and kelplands derivatives, and its famous science is the temperate giant next door — the room-temperature gas giant's calm decks are the trunk's best natural laboratory for atmospheric long-haul studies, staffed from the ocean world's university flotillas.

The hub role made the system the western door of the ε Ind corridor, and Lacaille 9352's dockside culture is the trunk's friendliest — the custom of the "third tide supper," where arriving crews are fed at whatever harbor the third tide finds them near, is written into the port tariffs as a landing credit. The standing grievance is the same tide: the pilot guild's suit against the second moon for schedule fraud has been refiled, as a joke and then as tradition, in every session of the harbor court since 2249.
## Meilin

- **Settled:** 2178
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2306965202564744064 (HIP 439), M, 14.17 ly
- **Population:** 190 million
- **System:** heavy locked furnace rock inward; **Meilin** (HIP 439 2), the settled world — a temperate rocky planet at 293 K with a working atmosphere and a single large moon; then a bare cold rock and a mooned ice world.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Lacaille 9352; Trunk gate to Fomalhaut B.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified over the register's objection. The mouth-seed tender's navigator Kang Meilin read the first atmospheric report — *293 K, one atmosphere, breathable* — on an open channel with the sentence "we're home early." When the Registry proposed the standard designation *Survey Unit 14*, the settlement charter arrived with forty thousand signatures reading simply *Meilin*, and the Union Naming Board ratified the crews' name in a minute that thanked the Registry for its thoroughness.

Meilin is the trunk's quiet garden. Broad grass continental plains under a large patient moon, rainfall that comes in decades-long wet-dry rhythms, and no native biochemistry above the microbial — a clean slate the agronomy institutes loved. The settlements are low, wide, and reservoir-centered: every town stands with its back to a dam or a cistern hill, and the planet's whole architecture is a response to the drought cycles the first survey understated. Meilin's best thing is dry-farming: its agronomy corpus is the trunk's standard reference, and Meilin drought-wheat grows from Proxima to the rim.

The world is old settler stock in the trunk mixture — Mandarin-dominant towns, Anglo fleet-line villages, the archipelago of other tongues the Cataclysm recovery made ordinary — and its one unbroken custom is the Lantern Drift: on the settlement anniversary, every reservoir town floats lanterns onto the water it depends on, one lantern per household per year since founding, so that the oldest reservoirs now carry just over two centuries of light on the anniversary night. The municipal argument over whether the Drift is litter or liturgy has run since 2216 and both sides turn out to carry the lanterns anyway.
## Fomalhaut B

- **Gate opened:** 2184
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6604147121141267712 (Fomalhaut B), K, 24.79 ly
- **Population:** 48 million
- **System:** a K sun with a puffed locked ice giant hugging it at 495 K; a heavy hot rock; **Fomalhaut B 3**, the settled world — a 6.3-Earth-mass ocean planet at 310 K, 14% of its surface in shallow warm seas, two moons; then a cold airy rock, a 242-Earth-mass gas giant with a locked companion and four moons, and a long cold tail of ice worlds and giants to 90 au.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 439, GJ 4248, and HIP 109388.
- **Registry name:** kept under the standing rule — the Fomalhaut name is one of the sky's oldest (the "mouth of the fish" of the southern traditions), and the Board has ruled the letter-companion names ride with it.

Fomalhaut B is the heavy world. Six Earth masses make the settled ocean planet a place where a child's first steps come late, a load-bearing frame is a status symbol, and the frontier aphorism — *Fomalhaut breaks your knees so the rest of the sky is free* — is printed on the fitness-institute's crest. What that gravity bought was a deep warm sea under a thick patient atmosphere: the shallow seas run warm and mineral-rich at 310 K, and the planet's economy is subsea — kelp-and-mollusc mariculture on the shelf, chemistry from the brine, and the famous heavy-metal recovery trawls that work the deep basin scarps where the planet's own mass has sorted its ores.

The population is small and dense by trunk standards: forty-eight million, mostly in the forty-one archipelago nations' compact cities, mostly people whose families came for one generation of foundry wages and stayed for the sea. Fomalhaut B's best thing is medicine under load: the gravitational-medicine institutes here wrote the trunk's standards for skeletal maintenance, load-adaptation surgery, and return-to-light rehabilitation — every spacer who ever needed to rebuild a body for a lighter world passes through Fomalhaut protocols. The living custom is the Deep Watch: every harbor town keeps a night-watch of volunteer swimmers who train in the heavy surf, and the Watch's two-century casualty ledger — honored dead and every rescue since 2219 — is carved into the sea wall of the capital's oldest harbor, updated every solstice by hand.
## GJ 4248 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2189; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 25.01 ly — locked furnace rock, a hot airless rock, one cold airy world; nothing a settlement would call a roof. A trunk relay with three gates' worth of signal, fuel, and not much else. The relay station's crew of two thousand keeps the register's oldest continuously broadcasting navigation beacon, and superstitious pilots still won't file a Fomalhaut corridor plan without its callsign on the form.
## HIP 99825 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2193; never promoted.
- K-dwarf, 28.74 ly — a rare well-ordered ladder of airy rocks, every world from the hot 528 K belt outward holding an atmosphere. A comparative-atmospheres research station exploits that ladder, running identical instruments down the whole sequence. The station's long-census finding — that this system's thin middle worlds keep their air ten times longer than model predicts — is quoted in every terraforming seminar as "the HIP 99825 problem," and nobody has solved it.
## HD 196982A *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2197; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 32.36 ly — a locked puffball ice giant at 486 K, then heavy hot rocks and a cold airy super-earth, none of them near the breath line. A survey depot and a helium-3 skim on the outer giant serve the corridor's thin traffic. The depot's one claim to fame is a tea house built in the 2200s inside the skim platform's oldest tank, where the pad crews keep a "century pot" — a fermenting tea blend to which every new crew adds one leaf from wherever they were born.
## GJ 4207 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2201; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 39.45 ly — locked furnace rocks and a small hot ocean world with shallow 356 K seas under a single moon. A condensery on the night coast bottles the steam-slope freshwater that half the corridor's older hulls still run on. The condensery crews hold the register's strangest weather record: rain that falls *up* the steam slope on still nights, verified by instrument and by the two technicians who claim to have stood in it.
## GJ 4198 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2205; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 39.73 ly — locked rocks, a chain of cold airy worlds and mooned ice giants, nothing at the breath line. A three-gate relay depot where the corridor traffic splits. The depot's claim is a grudge: its dispatchers have kept a public tally called the "Sol Surcharge Ledger" since 2231, logging every rate premium imposed from the core, and the ledger's one-line foreword reads *we are not bitter, we are itemized.*
## Gaia EDR3 6686474256863996672 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2210; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 50.26 ly — locked and heavy rocky worlds inward, paired ice giants and cold mooned iceballs out; a dead-end of the corridor. The single gate serves a metals quarry on the 176 K super-earth. The quarry keeps the register's shortest anthem: two notes, whistled down the shaft at shift change, older than anyone's idea of who wrote them.
## HIP 107649 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2213; never promoted.
- A Sol-like G sun, 50.74 ly — hot airy rocks inward, a bare 294 K world one atmosphere short of paradise, and two colossal gas giants trailing captured locked moons at the rim. The corridor's dead end, kept for the terraforming survey that has studied the almost-habitable world for two centuries without once being funded to finish. The survey station's wall motto, unofficial, says: *we can wait; the planet has nothing but time.*
## Gaia EDR3 6517274496976569344 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2215; never promoted.
- A barely-star brown dwarf, 38.90 ly — locked rocks in the ember-light, mooned iceballs and a three-mooned ice giant further out in the dark. A junction depot for the Fomalhaut corridor's two outer trunk fingers. Depot crews named the primary "the Match" for its habit of guttering during flare season, and the local superstition holds the Match never goes fully out — "somebody up there keeps striking it."
## GJ 4350 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2218; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 38.98 ly — small locked rocks, mooned iceballs, a two-mooned ice giant. A one-gate fueling spur of the corridor, worked by an automated skimming plant and a caretaking crew of ninety. The crew's single bylaw, written 2244 and never amended, assigns each member one hour per sol as "listening watch" on the old trunk broadband — not for traffic, for the pleasure of hearing anyone at all.
## GJ 3009 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2221; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 41.77 ly — a hot locked ice giant, then the register's oddity: a 132-Earth-mass gas giant locked with its star, one face held forever at a room-temperature 291 K. A gas-chemistry survey station rides the giant's dawn line, sampling a diurnal cycle eleven years wide. The station's crews leave notes for their successors describing the weather on the face they watched; the archive is called, unofficially and universally, the almanac of somebody else's sky.
## HD 4391D *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2224; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 48.99 ly — locked heavy rocks and twin hot ocean worlds, one of them 82% sea, steaming at 392 K. The deep-ocean chemistry works on the bigger ocean world are the trunk's extreme-pressure crucible, and their alloy patents fund the system's small permanent population. The foundry custom is that no new alloy is named until a sample has survived one full year on the seafloor — "pressure gets the last word."
## HIP 3583 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2227; never promoted.
- A near-Sol G sun, 49.08 ly — hot mooned rocks, cold bare and airy worlds, and two vast gas giants at the rim, the outer one carrying a pair of captured locked moons. A solar-power and mirror-fabric station uses the Sol-like light to calibrate every panel the trunk ever launches; the calibration crews claim they can tell real sunlight from the tests "by its manners." A Concord-era gate now runs from here toward the neutral zone.
## Amity

- **Settled:** 2230 (Union charter colony); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5004776376139340672, M, 71.51 ly
- **Population:** 31 million
- **System:** locked furnace rock, a locked hot ocean world, a bare temperate rock at 258 K with no air worth trusting, a small shallow-sea ocean world with one moon, then ice and a parade of mooned ice giants. Nothing here to live on and everything to live around: the population is orbital.
- **Links:** Concord gate to HIP 3583 (USE); Regional gates to HIP 116645 and Gaia EDR3 4996252755842227072; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2313836325604479616 and Ankaa.
- **Registry name:** the founding cooperative's charter was dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Amity* — the virtue the outer colonies most wanted from the trunk that had delivered them. The dedication survived the Partition, the Slip Wars, and three occupations of paperwork, and after the Treaty of Concord the name was rediscovered by both sides' propagandists at once. The Assembly has declined six ceremonial renamings on the grounds that the virtue is a standing instruction, not a claim it was achieved.

Amity is the door. When the Concord gates opened in the first decade of the settlement, every cargo contract crossing the USE–League boundary in this quarter of the network crossed here first, and the system's orbital ring grew into the Commonwealth's biggest mixed-bloc port: customs halls with two flag poles, bonded warehouses built to two sets of fire code, and the famous Amity trade court, whose docket of USE-v-League contract disputes is cited in every arbitration curriculum in the League. The port's standing joke is that its chief export is signatures.

The settlement itself is old co-op stock — trunk-era families who came out under the Union charter, lived through the severed years, and built the mesh links that stitched the severed quarters back together after 2272. The living custom is the Two Bells: arrivals are greeted at the customs ring with the low bell, departures see off with the high one, and no ship in two centuries has been allowed to skip either — so that nobody enters Amity unannounced or leaves it unsung. The port's permanent grievance is directed at Sol's insurance auditors, who have been double-checking Amity's bonded warehouses since 2404 with, as the harbormaster's annual letter puts it, "a diligence that would flatter a museum."
## Constancy

- **Settled:** 2235 (Union charter colony); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6536984484150014720 (HIP 116645), M, 67.58 ly
- **Population:** 7.9 million
- **System:** locked furnace rocks and bare temperate stones inward; a 284-Earth-mass gas giant with two moon anchorages; a warm 322 K ocean world, 56% of it sea, out where the light goes thin; frozen outrunners beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6550040463175784192 and Amity's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4996252755842227072 and Gaia EDR3 2313836325604479616.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements on the petition of the Long-Haul Guild, whose pilots rode this junction through the severed years. The dedication minute praises *the virtue that keeps faith when no one is watching* — the crews' own phrasing, submitted with the petition and adopted verbatim.

Constancy is the mesh's knot. In the years after 2272, when slip-drive hulls were stitching the severed League quarters back into a web, this system's junction was where four bundles of routes met, and its relay rings grew by accretion into the Commonwealth's densest signal-and-fuel station outside Amity. The settlement economy is transit — everything else is a rounding error: hull work, convoy marshaling, and the signal traffic of half the local League. The warm ocean world gets a research float each decade and a research float's worth of results; the real money orbits the gas giant's moons.

The people are guild stock to the bone — trunk-era settler families turned mesh-era relay clans — and their custom is the Horn: a standing watch on the relay ring that sounds one low note per sol for every convoy arriving safely, so that quiet on the ring is never merely quiet but counted absence. The standing grievance is constitutional comedy: Constancy's Assembly delegates have petitioned since 2409 for a Concord gate of their own, on the floor argument that "Amity should not be the only virtue with a customs revenue."
## Plenty

- **Settled:** 2238 (Union charter colony); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6550040463175784192, M, 66.05 ly
- **Population:** 120 million
- **System:** a locked puffball ice giant hugging the star, then **Plenty** (the second world), a tidally locked rocky planet at 314 K with a true atmosphere — the eternal-dusk terminator belt is the farmland; a hot steam ocean and a chain of cold giants and mooned iceballs complete the register.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6572811211549713792 and Constancy's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 6502618938988110848 and Gaia EDR3 2313836325604479616.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Plenty* on the founding agricultural cooperative's charter. The original petition argued that a world of eternal dusk would feed its settlers "not as a gift but as a promise kept daily"; the Assembly kept the sentence in the dedication minute, and Plenty's schoolchildren still memorize it.

Plenty is the eyebrow world: one face to the ember star forever, one face to the dark, and all human life in the ring of dusk between. The terminator belt at this world's temperature is a warm country — long dusk meadows, terraced down the day side in vine rows and grain, up the night side in greenhouses and cold cellars. The agronomy that made the belt bloom is the Commonwealth's proudest body of applied science: dusk-cycle crop genetics, drip-line water law, and the famed terminator vineyards whose wine crosses the League as "dusk red." What Plenty is genuinely best at is feeding its neighbors: three regional and mesh routes pull its surplus, and its silos have never once run empty — the famine years of the Partition included, a fact the settlement does not let anyone forget.

The people are cooperative stock — charter families, later arrivers, and the strong Anglophone streak the mesh era brought — and their life is belt-shaped: farm towns strung along the dusk roads, the Lamp Line of way-lighting kept lit along every mile by households' rotation as a civic duty. The standing grievance is water: the day-side ice trap and the night-side frost mines both drain toward the belt's aquifers, and Plenty's water court — the oldest and loudest in the Commonwealth — has heard the same boundary case, in some form or another, every year since 2246.
## Fortitude

- **Settled:** 2241 (Union charter colony); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6572811211549713792, brown dwarf, 57.90 ly
- **Population:** 88 million
- **System:** an ember of a primary — brown dwarf light, 0.04 solar masses — holding a locked furnace rock, a locked steam ocean, and **Fortitude** (the third world), a heavy locked ocean planet, 7.35 Earth masses, 285 K, with shallow seas across 15% of its face: the dusk shelf is the country. An ice world rims it out.
- **Links:** six gates — Regional to Gaia EDR3 2403199614187542528, HIP 105312, and Plenty's star; Mesh to Gaia EDR3 6577933973662574080, Gaia EDR3 6576809826101726208, and HIP 109821.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Fortitude*, on the petition of the Deep-Anchor Mariners' Cooperative, whose founding crews spent eleven years proving a heavy dark ocean world could hold a six-way junction. The dedication minute credits *the people who made the hard place central.*

Fortitude is the Commonwealth's keel. The settled world is heavy, dark, and mostly water — a place where the ember sun never rises, the tides are slow, and the light on the dusk shelf is what the settlements make themselves — and its people made the hard place the busiest crossroads in the quarter. Six gates meet at the anchor orbital, which has grown into a full ring city, and the world below sends up fish, chemistry, and mariners: the heavy-g deep-anchor crews are the League's best heavy-load riggers, and the co-op that proved the world trains the Commonwealth's whole heavy-operations curriculum.

The dusk-shelf towns are pressure-built and lamp-lit, strung along the shallows on the same pilings their great-grandparents drove, and the civic ceremony every mariner remembers is the First Pressure: on a crew member's first deep anchor, the mate seals the ritual hatch and the new hand takes the load alone, opening it again to the whole crew's silence and then the whole crew's supper. The standing grievance is the Light Levy — the municipal power tithes that keep the shelf lamps burning, unchanged in rate since the severed years when light was survival and defended like it, and now argued every budget season between the lamp-keepers and everyone under forty.
## Hearth

- **Settled:** 2244 (Union charter colony); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2403199614187542528, brown dwarf, 53.88 ly
- **Population:** 57 million
- **System:** the ember primary barely qualifies as a star — 0.03 solar masses — and its whole retiny orbits in days: a locked heavy furnace rock, a locked steam ocean world 75% sea, and **Hearth** (the third world), a locked rocky planet at 268 K with a working atmosphere, whose dusk belt is the settled country.
- **Links:** five gates — Regional to Tugongli, HIP 105932, Gaia EDR3 2407340615496379008, and Fortitude's star; Mesh to Gaia EDR3 2393563872239260928.
- **Registry name:** the founding cooperative signed its charter as the Hearth Cooperative — the common house of the ember systems, where crews wintering between the dark stars could count on a lamp and a meal — and the Assembly of Settlements dedicated the system to the same word. The dedication minute reads, in full: *the petition needs no argument; the name is one.*

Hearth is the ember cluster's warm room. The cluster of failed stars this region was seeded around gives almost nothing in light and everything in gravity-anchored calm: no flares, no seasons, no weather from the sky worth naming — and the world the settlers took is a cold-temperate dusk rock where the towns live on their own power, their own lamps, and their own manners. Five gates meet here, the densest knot of the ember systems, and the ring town at the anchor has been rebuilt around one unbroken custom: the Common Table, a literal one, where transiting crews eat with the resident crews every evening of the year and have since the severed years, when the Table was for a decade the only place news crossed between whole regions.

What Hearth is genuinely best at is maintenance. The ember cluster's hulls work hard for thin light, and Hearth's yards — founded to refit the mesh convoys, staffed by families who have passed the trade through since — turn around deep-loads faster per dock than anywhere in the quarter. The world's grievance is also its pride, depending on who is speaking: Hearth's children leave, mostly to Fortitude's ring or Amity's courts, and the elders' letters to the Assembly demanding "a reason to stay" are answered, every year, with the same quiet line in the budget: the Table fund, protected.
## Tugongli

- **Settled:** 2247 (Union charter colony); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2719475542667772416 (Tugongli), G, 53.55 ly
- **Population:** 41 million
- **System:** a hot bright G sun, one and a half solar masses — a furnace of a main star; mooned hot rocks inward, a heavy airy world at 363 K, and a 117-Earth-mass gas giant with four moons and a captured locked companion out at the working distance.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Hearth's star and HIP 3206, and — the great one — the Regional gate to Helion system; Mesh gates to GJ 4257 and GJ 4292.
- **Registry name:** kept under the Assembly's Standing Register rule honoring Earth's naming inheritance: the IAU-proper name from the old Chinese tradition crossed with the first settlers and was entered in the Commonwealth register unchanged. The Standing Register's marginal note on the file is often quoted: *the names came with us; so did the people.*

Tugongli is the Commonwealth's front porch. Every hull bound for Helion system itself — seat of the Commonwealth's oldest settlements and its Assembly institutions — stages through here, and has since the Union seed era: the system's population lives almost entirely in the shadow-shielded ring habitats strung like beads in the gas giant's orbital shade, a city-form Tugongli invented out of necessity (a star this hot punishes any other) and exported across the quarter. The yards' specialty is the Helion fitting: convoy loads trimmed, bonded, and scheduled for the last long leg, and the fitting-hall tradition of stamping every certified hull with the shade-bell sigil is two centuries old.

The people are dock-clans and gate-families, trunk-era stock with the mesh-era mix, and their character is exactly what a porch requires: good watchdogs, better gossips, and a fierce local pride in knowing every captain's name before the customs manifest loads. The living custom is the Call Ahead — departing crews for Helion send, ahead of the ship, a recorded greeting to whoever is waiting at the far gate, a service the ring's comms families have run as a public trust since the severed years, when "someone is coming" was the only good news there was. The standing grievance is the sun itself: Tugongli's ring mayors have filed, in every session since 2331, for heat-shade subsidy from the Assembly, arguing with some justice that the Commonwealth's porch should not have to pay for its own umbrella.
## Clarity

- **Settled:** 2250 (Union charter colony, last pre-Partition wave); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2683714304930755712 (GJ 4257), M, 57.64 ly
- **Population:** 73 million
- **System:** locked rocks under the ember star, then **Clarity** (the third world), a locked warm ocean planet — 306 K, two-thirds of its face under shallow sea, breathable at the dusk archipelagos — then a heavy cold airy rock and a mooned ice world.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Tugongli, HIP 105932, Gaia EDR3 2617043665033973376, and HIP 113421; Regional gate to GJ 4204.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Clarity*, on the joint petition of the Shelf Cooperative and the harbor court's founding bench, whose charter line — *clear water, clear ledgers* — became the dedication's text. The double meaning was intended; the petitioners said so.

Clarity is the mesh's favorite shore leave. The settled world is a warm twilight sea — shallow, lit from below by its own bioluminescent shelf, and hospitable in the dusk band in the way that makes visitors plan their return before they have cleared customs. The economy is shellfisheries and shelf chemistry, the famous dusk pearls, and the invisible trade of a five-gate crossroads: brokerage, arbitration, and the hospitality industry that has grown around both. Clarity's harbor court borrowed Tugongli's directness and Amity's procedure and improved on each, and half the quarter's mesh contracts now name it as forum before they name their cargo.

The people are charter-fisher families with a strong mesh-era infusion, and their customs are all about the water being visible: the Reading of the Water, the annual public audit of the cooperative ledgers, is read aloud over the main harbor at dusk, once a year, to whoever gathers — and the whole settlement does. The standing grievance is the upstream toll schedule on the Tugongli mesh bundle, which Clarity's assembly-members have called, in sessions since 2405, "a tax on the Commonwealth's own doorstep," a phrase the toll authorities have never once managed to answer without smiling.
## Respite

- **Settled:** 2279 (slip-delivered, first wave of the mesh era).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2688566793341935616 (GJ 4204), M, 55.06 ly
- **Population:** 22 million
- **System:** locked rocks inward — the second of them, **Respite**, a hot-temperate locked rocky world at 319 K with a true atmosphere, is the settled one, its dusk belt a warm country of terraced orchards; heavy cold rocks and mooned ice worlds out.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 105932 and Clarity's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2617043665033973376 and HIP 107968.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Respite*, on the charter of the Slip Wars veterans' resettlement cooperative — the stated purpose of the founding was *a place that owes nobody a war*. The Assembly entered the dedication with a note that the veterans' charter had waited nineteen years for a world worth the name, and considered the wait part of the dedication.

Respite is a warm world built by tired people. The veterans' cooperative took a hot dusk belt the trunk surveys had skipped past and made it gentle: day-terrace orchards that bear twice a cluster-year, night cellars cut into the cold side where the wine rests at cellar temperature without a machine's help, and four gates' worth of trade that arrives on schedule because everyone who works the junction was, in the founding generation, professionally punctual about arrivals. The system is best at rest itself: its convalescent houses — built for the cooperative's own old crews first — are the quarter's reference for load-rehabilitation, and Clarity's brokers routinely route exhausted hull crews here on medical halfway-orders.

The customs are deliberate and small: every public building keeps a Cool Room, shaded, watered, and open to any traveler who asks, and the oldest rule of the settlement, carved over the first one, is *no questions at the door.* The standing grievance is the Concord gate lottery — Respite has applied in every allocation round since 2401 for a direct link toward USE space, on the argument that its convalescent houses serve both blocs alike, and has been passed over every time in favor of louder systems with better lawyers.
## Vigil

- **Settled:** 2284 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2693940725141960192 (HIP 107968), white dwarf, 73.58 ly
- **Population:** 19 million
- **System:** a dead sun — a white dwarf burning small, hard, and blue-white, never changing — holding tight-orbiting rocks, **Vigil** (the third world), a locked warm ocean planet at 306 K, two-thirds sea, whose dusk archipelagos are the settled country, and a cold tail of heavy gas giants with captured moons.
- **Links:** Mesh gate to Respite's star and to Gaia EDR3 1736707482548494848; Regional gates to HIP 109378 and GJ 4252.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Vigil* on the charter of the Keepers' Cooperative, which proposed the system as the Commonwealth's memorial ground — a world circling a star that had already died, *so that no one would have to explain the light.* The dedication minute adopted the phrase without amendment.

Vigil is where the League buries its honored dead. The founders chose the white-dwarf world for the hardness of its symbol — a sun that ended and still gives light — and the Keepers' Cooperative built the Necropolis Belt, the great orbital ring of vaults and gardens where two centuries of the quarter's dead are carried by families who can reach Vigil by four gates. The world below is warm, dark-watered, and quiet by statute: the dusk archipelagos hold the settlers' towns and the pilgrim hostels, and the sea's bioluminescent shelf — which blooms along the terminator like a lit shoreline made by nobody — is protected as the memorial's living light.

What Vigil is genuinely best at, besides grief, is custody: the vault-clans' security and record-keeping traditions are the quarter's finest, and the same families audit half the Commonwealth's chain-of-title archives as a side trade. The custom every visitor learns is the Reading of Names: at each solstice the Keepers read, aloud and in full, the names added that year, and the whole settlement stands in the shallow water to hear it — arriving trade crews included, and no ship departs Vigil during the Reading. The standing grievance belongs to the hostel-keepers and is lovingly eternal: the pilgrims come at solstice and so does the weather, and the solstice storms of the dusk sea have never once in ninety years agreed to be elsewhere.
## Patience

- **Settled:** 2288 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2619706544757416192 (HIP 109378), K, 69.62 ly
- **Population:** 5.6 million
- **System:** a Sun-like K star with a hot puffed ice giant, then the two-mooned rock the whole settlement exists for — 284 K, temperate, and airless, one atmosphere short of a garden — then a heavy airy rock, a four-mooned ice giant, and cold outrunners.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 2617043665033973376, Vigil's star, and Gaia EDR3 6844219059795207296; Mesh gate to HIP 113421.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Patience* on the charter of the Gardeners' Cooperative, whose founding document is a terraforming plan running three hundred years. The dedication minute records the Assembly's standing ovation *for the nerve of it*, and the cooperative's reply, now famous: *you are welcome to check on us.*

Patience is the Commonwealth's long project. The Gardeners took a world the trunk surveys had dismissed — temperate, water-bearing in its rocks, and airless — and began the slow work of giving it a sky: volatiles skimmed from the hot inner giant, imported atmosphere in tank runs, engineered oxygen-breathing microbial mats seeded across the equatorial basins, all funded by the hub trade that four gates pull through the system. A hundred and thirty-eight years in, the rock has a haze where it had none, and the Cooperative's open ledger — every gram delivered, every projection checked — is the quarter's standard exhibit of long-horizon engineering done in public.

The people are gardener clans, hub dockers, and the peculiar profession the project created: forecasters of a century hence, whose trade is argued over dinner and audited by their grandchildren. The custom is Charter Day: once a year the settlement reads aloud one clause of the Long Charter, in order, and the year's work is sworn to it. The grievance is a nickname — the rest of the quarter calls Patience "the waiting room of Vigil," for the memorial traffic that stages here, and the Gardeners' formal reply, filed with the Assembly and never rescinded, is that *a chair in the waiting room is the only honest definition of a future.*
## Industry

- **Settled:** 2292 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6844219059795207296, brown dwarf, 83.31 ly
- **Population:** 2.1 million
- **System:** another ember primary, its retinue all inside half a light-hour: locked rocks, then two ice giants — one bare, one mooned — that are the whole reason anyone came.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2617043665033973376 and Gaia EDR3 6817856584892201600; Regional gates to Patience's star and Gaia EDR3 2608787122983146240.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Industry* on the charter of the Deep Miners' Guild, whose petition argued the ember cluster "will be given light by whoever does the work." The dedication minute is the Assembly's shortest: *agreed, with respect.*

Industry is the ember cluster's fuel tank. The two ice giants mine easy — cold, calm, shallow-gravity — and the guild's skim fleets have pulled water, hydrogen, and halo-3 out of them for half a century, feeding every lamp, hull, and terraforming project in the quarter, including Patience's long atmosphere deliveries. The system is otherwise unremarkable and the population is small, dense, and well-paid: miner clans on rotation, the junction depot crews who move four gates' worth of freight, and the odd tax auditor from Amity who always seems to leave with a new appreciation for guild accounting.

The customs are miners' customs: the shift bell rung at every skim-rotation change, the tally board in the depot hall chalked by hand with the day's grams, and the unbroken rule that no load leaves the system until its crew has been fed by whoever signed the manifest. The standing grievance is the Ember Levy — the cluster's voluntary volatile-surcharge that funds Hearth's Common Table and Patience's ledgers — which Industry's guild-masters have publicly called "a tax on the lamp oil," and privately, in the hall, with pride, called the price of doing business with family.
## Providence

- **Settled:** 2296 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2608787122983146240, brown dwarf, 92.00 ly
- **Population:** 890,000
- **System:** an ember primary with locked rocks — one of them a bare 293 K world the survey crews list, with a shrug audible in the typeface, as *near-miss class* — and two mooned ice giants out where the skim fleets work.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Industry's star and GJ 4263; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2408845056640573056 and Gaia EDR3 2628531225081851648.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Providence*, on the founding cooperative's argument that a provisioning hub at the ember cluster's far door "makes provision for everyone's providence." The Assembly's minute praised the pun and deplored it in the same sentence.

Providence is the last pantry before the deep quarter. Everything climbing the ember cluster's long routes outward stages here for volatiles, spares, and the provisioning co-op's famous ration packs, and the system's whole small population is in the business of making sure nobody leaves hungry. The depot's custom is the Pantry Book: any crew in genuine need signs it and takes what they need, and any crew that can, crosses off a stranger's line when they pass through — the Book has been kept honestly since 2299, which the Assembly cites in civics class and the depot keepers cite at closing time.
## Diligence

- **Settled:** 2301 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2408845056640573056, brown dwarf, 93.03 ly
- **Population:** 3.2 million
- **System:** an ember primary squeezing three heavy locked super-earths — 6.2, 5.4, and 3.9 Earth masses — into orbits measured in days, with a bare ice giant and a mooned ice world behind them.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2419457405433453696, Providence's star, and Gaia EDR3 2380960097435152000; Regional gates to HIP 116106 and Gaia EDR3 2394193308286591872.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Diligence* on the petition of the Natural Philosophers' Guild, which wanted the system's extreme-gravity laboratory cluster "named for the only virtue science can verify." The minute records one delegate's objection that diligence was being flattered by a system where the instruments work all night.

Diligence is the ember quarter's laboratory. Three crushing locked super-earths in tight orbits give the Guild what no other system in the quarter offers: repeatable extremes — gravity, pressure, tidal shear — close enough to a five-gate junction to ship apparatus in and results out. The institute's low-laboratories hang on the anchor orbital; its hard-laboratories sit on the heavy worlds themselves, run by the famous remote-hand crews whose manipulator work under load is taught by holocast across the League. Materials forged under Diligence's gravities hold half the quarter's structural patents, and Fortitude's deep-anchor rigs are built from them.

The institute's customs are grad-student ancient: Null Tea, the standing argument hour at the zero-g hub, and the Rule of the Broken Instrument, which requires that any apparatus destroyed by an experiment be displayed rather than replaced quietly. The grievance is perennial and comfortable: Assembly funding arrives as multi-year grants with clause nine, "results expected," and the Guild's annual reply, published every year since 2312 in identical wording, is that *the universe does not take instruction.*
## Solace

- **Settled:** 2305 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2419457405433453696, M, 79.66 ly
- **Population:** 26 million
- **System:** an ember-red primary over a tight retinue: heavy locked furnace rock, hot locked stone, then **Solace** (the third world), a cold-temperate locked rocky planet at 252 K with a working atmosphere — the dusk belt is cool country, fir-dark, geothermal — and a mooned ice giant with a chain of airy ice worlds beyond.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to 94 Aqr B and Diligence's star; Regional gates to HIP 1608 and HIP 669.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Solace*, on the petition of a founders' cooperative drawn mostly from the Slip Wars' displaced populations, whose charter named the world *a place that was kind on purpose.* The dedication minute entered the phrase without edit and adjourned early.

Solace is the cold world that made itself warm. The dusk belt is a boreal country — thin forests of native frost-moss engineered into timber, hot-spring towns strung along the geothermal seams, roads kept open by teams whose winter-readiness is the local form of civic virtue. What the system is genuinely best at is climate medicine and cold-operations: its frost-injury corpus, written in the clinics that served the founding generation's march refugees, is the League standard from Proxima's poles to the ember cluster, and Solace search-and-rescue crews work the whole mesh's bad-weather trade.

The people are charter families and everyone their grandparents could talk into coming, and the custom that defines the settlement is the Open Bath: the spring-fed bathhouses of every town are built without locks, by the founders' charter, and kept lit and warm through the deep cold for whoever arrives — resident, traveler, or stranger with no story. The standing grievance is constitutional and cheerful: Solace has petitioned the Assembly, every session since 2370, for a formal re-definition of its climate class from *marginal* to *bracing,* on the grounds that the classification affects insurance rates and the settlement's pride in equal measure.
## Temperance

- **Settled:** 2308 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2411855072801159040 (94 Aquarii B), K, 73.18 ly
- **Population:** 4.1 million
- **System:** a K sun over a retinue of hot thick-aired rocks — 788 K, 570 K, 612 K, each wrapped in dense atmosphere — with one small cold bare mooned rock out at the temperate distance.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 113421, GJ 889 B, HIP 114156, and Solace's star; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 2405722027006062080.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Temperance*, on the Smelters' Guild's petition, with the guild's own joke entered in the minute: *the system is named for what it teaches, not what it has.* Even the Assembly's stenographer is recorded as having smiled.

Temperance is the kiln. Three hot thick-aired worlds make the cheapest high-temperature chemistry floors in the quarter, and the Smelters' Guild built on them: aerogel lattices and crystal grades that need heat no reactor gives as evenly as a whole sky does. The settlements are the cool ones — the cold rock at the rim, the anchor orbital in permanent shade, the floating works above the hot worlds' cloud decks — and the economy is everything half the mesh builds with: Diligence orders its furnace bricks from here, and Fortitude's rigs fly Temperance lattice.

The population is guild families and the dock crews of a five-gate junction, and their customs are heat-discipline customs: cooldown meals taken slow after every shift on the works, shade rotations scheduled like tides, and the Cooling Cup — the ring hall's standing toast, drunk from cups that are always, on principle, allowed to go cold. The grievance is the mild eternal one of kiln towns everywhere: nothing here ever fully cools, and the mayor's office has filed, as its 2390-era contribution to Assembly record-keeping, the quarter's only insurance claim classification for "ambient."
## Craft

- **Settled:** 2312 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2405722027006062080, M, 70.04 ly
- **Population:** 4.9 million
- **System:** a locked hot ice giant, a steaming ocean world (62% sea at 355 K) whose thermal gradients are the system's forge, a cold airy rock, a chain of ice giants, and a mooned gas giant out at the rim.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 2393563872239260928, GJ 889 B, and 94 Aqr B's star (Temperance); Mesh gate to HIP 114156.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Craft* on the petition of the Outer-Works Cooperative, which builds station modules and gate hardware here. The dedication minute adopted the co-op's founding line: *good work is a virtue you can dock at.*

Craft is where the quarter's ports are made. The Outer-Works yards hang above the steam ocean, running their forge lines on the world's thermal gradient — hull sections annealed in the sky-heat, cold-finished in the shadow docks — and the co-op's modules are the standard skeleton of half the mesh's stations from Solace to the deep quarter. The ice giants feed the yards' volatiles; the junction's four gates ship the output; and the settlement is, deliberately, a trades town: co-op families, apprentice halls, and a school-leavers' tradition that sends one working grandchild of every family through the yards.

The custom that matters is the keel-signing: every module leaves Craft with the full names of every worker who touched it stamped inside the frame, and no customer, however grand, has ever bought the stamp out. The grievance is trade politics — the Concord-era tariff schedules let USE yards underbid Craft in neutral ports, and the co-op's assembly file on the subject, updated continuously since 2403, is titled simply and permanently *No.*
## Economy

- **Settled:** 2316 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2384348242516852864 (GJ 889 B), M, 71.96 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a locked hot ice giant, bare and airy cold rocks, then five more ice giants — a system almost entirely made of the volatiles and ices everything else runs on.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Craft's star and HIP 114156; Mesh gates to Temperance's star and υ Aqr B.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Economy* — the old virtue of stewardship — on the petition of the Warehousemen's Cooperative, whose consolidation yards here sort and re-pack four routes' freight. The dedication minute notes the co-op "argues for the virtue with its ledger, which is the only pulpit it trusts."

Economy is the mesh's sorting shed. Almost nothing originates here and almost everything passes through: the co-op's transfer yards re-ship freight between the Craft corridor and the southern mesh lines, and the settlement's pride is throughput per dock, a number they defend against all comers and publish annually whether asked or not. The custom is the co-op's tally feast — the crews who most improve the year's number eat first at the winter tables — and the grievance is that the name invites jokes ("Economy's rates are none"), which the warehousemen absorb with the patience of people who have heard every one since 2316.
## Mercy

- **Settled:** 2320 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6628926642897199744 (υ Aquarii B), M, 75.14 ly
- **Population:** 6.3 million
- **System:** locked hot rocks, then the system's gift — a heavy hot ocean world at 417 K, 72% sea, whose thermophile chemistry is priceless; a heavy two-mooned cold rock and a 77-Earth-mass ice giant behind it.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Economy's star and Gaia EDR3 6617996741403360128; Regional gates to HIP 114156 and Gaia EDR3 6622318573720116864.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Mercy* on the petition of the Healers' Guild, which built the quarter's first deep-thermopharmaceutical works on the hot ocean world. The dedication minute quotes the guild's charter: *of all the virtues, this one must be manufactured at scale.*

Mercy is the medicine chest. The hot ocean's deep-vent ecologies — organisms living at temperatures no sane world allows — yield the protein analogues and radical-scavenger compounds behind half the League's advanced pharmacopeia, and the Healers' Guild runs the extraction fleets and the clinic ring that grew with them. Respite's convalescent houses dispense Mercy compounds; Solace's cold clinics depend on them; the guild's rule, which the settlement enforces like a constitution, is that any compound discovered here reaches the Commonwealth's formulary before it reaches any market.

The custom of the place is the dispensary bell: every clinic ring keeps a bell rung once for every life the year's compounds carried through, and the settlement's new year begins with the bells rung backward through the previous year's count, the guild's way of saying every one of them mattered. The grievance is patent poaching — USE pharmaceutical firms have reverse-engineered three Mercy compounds since the Concord opened trade, and the guild's Assembly file on the subject is updated with each instance, in a hand the archivists describe as *controlled.*
## Certainty

- **Settled:** 2324 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6617996741403360128, K brown dwarf, 76.66 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a failed star on the K/M boundary, its whole retiny in fast orbits — nothing circles slower than a week — including a locked temperate ice giant used as the quarter's clock-dial.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Mercy's star and Gaia EDR3 6622318573720116864; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6783123184369906944 and HIP 106913; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 6812300065441387648.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Certainty* on the petition of the Weighers — the mesh's metrology guild — whose Timing Court here calibrates every gate-clock and slip-standard in the quarter. The dedication minute records the Weighers' argument that *a common clock is the least glamorous of the common goods* and the Assembly's agreement that this was no reason to be shy about naming it.

Certainty keeps the quarter's time. The fast, regular worlds of this system gave the Weighers a natural orrery of ticking orbits, and the Timing Court built on them sells no product at all — just the reference signal every mesh route, arbitration deadline, and gate schedule in the quarter ultimately checks itself against. The population is guild families, signal keepers, and the quiet industry that services the Court: precision fabrication, archive vaults, and the best cafeteria discipline in the League.

The custom is the Second Supper — eaten at the Court's midnight, unhurried, because rushing a meal in the home of the clock is the one local obscenity. The grievance is everyone else: the Weighers' annual circular on *chronological drift in neighboring jurisdictions* has been published without fail since 2326, and its opening sentence, "again," is quoted with affection and dread across the whole Commonwealth.
## Justice

- **Settled:** 2327 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6783123184369906944, brown dwarf, 70.54 ly
- **Population:** 1.7 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a bare ice giant, and a long cold tail of mooned ice worlds — the kind of system the quarter builds institutions in rather than on.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 103389, Gaia EDR3 6777414279481335936, and Certainty's star; Mesh gate to HIP 109821.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Justice* when the Commonwealth's high arbitration circuit chose the system for its permanent seat. The dedication minute is a single, unusually elegant sentence: *where the judges sit, the name should.*

Justice is where the Commonwealth's hard cases come home. The circuit's seat — the Hall of the Bench, a quiet ring structure above the ember star — hears the arbitrations that outgrow Amity's trade docket: water-law appeals from Plenty, toll disputes from the mesh bundles, exit-right cases from every polity that borders the quarter. The population is the invisible profession made visible: benches, clerks, archive families, and the hostel trade that surrounds any court town. The custom is the Open Ledger — every judgment published the same sol it is given, read aloud at the Hall's dusk — and the grievance is caseload: the circuit's request, filed every session since 2380, for a second bench "so that justice may also sleep."
## Radiance

- **Settled:** 2331 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6802426038708609536 (HIP 103389), G, 68.81 ly
- **Population:** 2.2 million
- **System:** a hot bright G sun — one and a fifth solar masses — over wide-spaced rocky worlds and mooned iceballs; the best sunlight in the ember quarter by any measure the Weighers certify.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 105312, HIP 102235, HIP 100925, and Justice's star; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 6806697710102769920.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Radiance*, on the petition of the Lightfarmers' Cooperative, which built the great solar arrays here to sell the one thing the ember cluster's worlds make least of. The dedication minute: *named for the crop.*

Radiance is the quarter's lamp. The Lightfarmers' arrays — hundreds of square kilometres of tuned sail, riding the bright G sun's inner orbits — charge the power hulls that run the cluster's lamps, yards, and transit lines, and the co-op's delivery schedule is the closest thing the ember systems have to a shared heartbeat. The population is small and spread between the farm itself, the anchor depot, and the families who fly the slow power-hull circuits to Hearth, Fortitude, and the deep stations. The custom is the First Light allowance — every household in the cluster's poorest systems gets a guaranteed brightness line in the co-op's tariff, protected since the severed years — and the grievance is bright and simple: USE power brokers, since the Concord opened, have bid twice for the arrays themselves, and the co-op's published answer to the second bid was one word long.
## Memory

- **Settled:** 2335 (slip-delivered).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6858312737278836736 (HIP 102235), M, 62.20 ly
- **Population:** 940,000
- **System:** heavy locked rocks inward, then two heavy cold airy super-earths — 6.6 and 5.8 Earth masses — whose deep cold and steady stone the Archivists' Guild rates ideal for what it keeps.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 105312 and Gaia EDR3 6806697710102769920; Mesh gates to HIP 100925 and Radiance's star.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Memory* on the Archivists' Guild petition for the Commonwealth's Long Archive: every charter, judgment, and Assembly minute since the founding, stored in the cold stone of the heavy worlds. The dedication minute closed with the Guild's motto, entered as text: *what a people cannot lose, they keep.*

Memory is the quarter's attic, and its conscience. The Long Archive cuts its vaults kilometers into the super-earths' stable crust — copies of every settlement's founding documents, the severed-years correspondence, the Slip Wars casualty rolls Vigil's Keepers cross-check each solstice — and the guild families who tend it have one trade and one pride: nothing stored here has ever been lost to damp, heat, war, or forgetting. The custom is the Annual Reading: each year one founding charter of one settlement is read aloud in the vault hall, in full, and the settlement it belongs to sends delegates the way other worlds send holidays. The grievance is quiet and permanent — half the Commonwealth still files its records in formats the Archivists describe, in print, as *a courtesy to future archaeologists.*
## Bathgate

- **Settled:** 2255 (Union charter era, free-port speculators); Freeport charter continuously since.
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6796392812250051712 (HIP 100925), K, 62.97 ly
- **Population:** 14 million
- **System:** a K sun with locked furnace rocks and heavy hot stones inward, then the inventory that pays for everything: two warm ocean worlds — 358 K and 362 K, shallow seas over geothermal springs — with the settling pools, spa archipelagos, and resort licenses that the Freeports auction off in forty-year lots.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6683306151547010688 and HIP 98130; Mesh gates to Memory's star, Radiance's star, and Gaia EDR3 6806697710102769920.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2287, in the Freeports' first great name sale after the mesh reopened the quarter — the record is public and the Freeports are proud of it. The Gatehouse Springs Combine outbid the Amity hospitality houses and a Helion water-cabal at nine million port marks, and named the system for what it sells. The auction minute closes: *sold, to the highest bidder, to no one's embarrassment.*

Bathgate is the quarter's bath house, and it charges admission. The two ocean worlds run warm from below — spring-fed seas that cool into the famous settling pools at the archipelago rims — and the Combine's resort fleets, spa towns, and rehabilitation clinics draw the mesh's moneyed and its merely tired from five gates' worth of routes. The Freeports take their cut three ways: landing tariffs, water rights, and the renewal auctions, which are contested like elections and followed like sport. The year-round population is service families, water engineers, and the pilot clans who run the warm-world shuttles; the felt population doubles at the season's peak, and the whole system's calendar is the season.

The customs are commercial and sincere: the Pools Ledger, posted nightly at every spa town gate, lists every pool's temperature, mineral load, and occupancy in real time, because a Freeport that lies about its water is worth nothing; the grievance is equally commercial — Helion's cooperative tourists file, every year, some complaint or another about "the principle of the thing," and Bathgate's reply, printed at the tariff office and never once revised, is that the water is honest, the price is posted, and the pools are warm.
## Ledger Bay

- **Settled:** 2258 (free-port charter, last pre-Partition wave); continuously chartered since.
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6683306151547010688, M, 55.08 ly
- **Population:** 4.2 million
- **System:** an M sun over a locked steam ocean, a cold airy super-earth, and a long rich tail — a 163-Earth-mass gas giant and a parade of mooned giants and airy iceballs whose cold, quiet moons hold the vaults.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4127182375667696128 and Bathgate's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 6576809826101726208, Gaia EDR3 6773423224070637952, and HIP 98130.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2291 — the Freeports' second great name sale, and the one the Consortium of Free Banks won with a bid that was, by tradition, paid in full on stage. The Consortium named the system for its business: the name auction minute reads *sold, and welcome to the books,* and the Bay has answered to it ever since.

Ledger Bay is where the quarter's money sleeps. The Consortium's vaults are cut into the cold moons of the outer giants — bonded stores for bullion, chain-of-title servers, insurance ledgers, and the famous "cold escrow" for cargoes in dispute — and the system's five gates make it the natural clearing house between the Helion cluster and the southern mesh. Everything here is a service: bonded warehousing, arbitration-ready record-keeping, hull insurance written at the exchange that trades in real time on every convoy the mesh reports, and a small permanent population of tellers, auditors, guards, and the lawyers who commute to Justice.

The customs are banking customs and the Bay keeps them like liturgy: the clearing bell that ends each trading cycle is rung by the youngest employee present, and the annual audit is a public holiday, Audit Day, on which the books are open to any depositor who can read a column. The grievance is moral and mutual — Helion's cooperative assemblies bank here anyway while filing formal protests about usury, and the Bay's standing reply, printed on every bond, is that *virtue deposits at commercial rates.*
## Flagfall

- **Settled:** 2261 (free-port charter); continuously chartered since.
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4127182375667696128, brown dwarf, 53.22 ly
- **Population:** 9.4 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and a steam ocean, then the settled world — a warm ocean planet at 320 K, four-fifths of its face under sea, riding one patient moon — and a cold tail of mooned ice and a four-mooned ice giant.
- **Links:** six gates — Regional to HIP 80268, Ledger Bay's star, HIP 88694, ξ Oph, and (the great one) Vesper system itself; Mesh to HIP 85561.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2294, in the Freeports' third great name sale, to the Registry Combine — the shipwrights, flag-brokers, and salvage courts that run the League's largest open registry. The name is the trade's own word for the day a ship takes a flag. The auction minute: *sold; may she always be flying something.*

Flagfall is where ships become legal. The Registry Combine's Hall of Colors — a sea-city capital riding the warm world's equatorial current — flags and deflags more hulls per cycle than any port in the League, and the Freeports' open register, which lets a hull carry Freeport colors without asking anyone's permission, is administered from here: flags of convenience, salvage titles, prize-court verdicts, and the marine arbiters every mesh insurer recognizes because the alternative is chaos. Six gates meet at the anchor, and the sea below builds what the register bills: the shipyards of the warm archipelagos, hull-breaking yards on the steam world's night coast, and the sea-cities' dry docks that work in open water.

The people are registry clerks, shipwright clans, salvage crews, and flag-brokers' families, and their customs are all maritime-legal: the Colors Board at the Hall entrance flies every flag currently on the register, updated by hand, and no ship may leave Flagfall without finding its own colors on the board. The grievance is the League's oldest maritime joke told straight: Helion assemblies denounce the open register twice a session, and Flagfall's formal reply — *we sell flags, not consciences; those are berthed elsewhere* — has been reprinted so often it now appears, unauthorized, on souvenir towels.
## Coldstore

- **Settled:** 2263 (free-port charter); continuously chartered since.
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6049221163063960320 (HIP 80268), M, 54.00 ly
- **Population:** 1.5 million
- **System:** hot locked rocks inward, then a heavy airy super-earth at 216 K — the warehouse — with cold two-mooned ice and bare rocks beyond.
