- **Links:** Regional gates to Flagfall's star, GJ 3918, and Gaia EDR3 4324728640519965824; Mesh gate to HIP 83541.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2297, to the Provisioners' Exchange, which wanted a name that would appear on manifests "without wasting a clerk's imagination." The auction minute notes the Exchange was the only bidder, and that this was considered proof of the name's quality.

Coldstore keeps the quarter's pantries. The super-earth's cold, calm air is free refrigeration at planetary scale, and the Exchange's silo arcs — grain from Plenty's routes, salt fish from Clarity, meal and mycoprotein from a hundred contracts — sit under it in bonded rows, rotating stock for half the southern mesh. The population is storekeepers, tariff clerks, and the piloting families who fly the short cold hops between silo and gate, and their one proud custom is the Count: the nightly public tally, read over the tariff radio, of every silo's contents, because a Freeport warehouse that cannot say what it holds has nothing to sell but mystery. The grievance is seasonal and profitable: every hardware crisis in the quarter becomes a cold-chain crisis here, and the Exchange's surge pricing — legal, posted, and merciless — is denounceed by everyone who pays it, in the same breath they pay it.
## Silverfin

- **Settled:** 2266 (free-port charter); continuously chartered since.
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4410702581435191296 (GJ 3918), M, 55.17 ly
- **Population:** 5.1 million
- **System:** locked rocks inward, then the fishery — a heavy warm ocean world at 318 K, three-quarters of its face under sea, one moon, heavy gravity — and a cold procession led by a 148-Earth-mass gas giant whose moons house the canneries.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Coldstore's star, Gaia EDR3 4496383055821714816, HIP 75201, and Gaia EDR3 1193520666521113344.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2300, in a two-way fight between the Warm Sea Fisheries Combine and a resort cartel that never understood what it was bidding against; the Combine won and named the system for the catch. The auction minute reads, with the Freeports' usual tact: *sold; the fish had prior claim.*

Silverfin feeds the southern mesh. The heavy warm world's seas run thick with engineered shoals — the deep-runners whose silver flanks gave the system its name — and the Combine's trawler fleets, factory rigs, and moon-based canneries move protein by the convoy-load toward Coldstore's silos and every port that pays. The sea-cities ride the heavy swells with the institutional calm of people whose ancestors chose gravity for a living: hull-built, pilothouse-proud, and constitutionally unable to describe weather as anything but tonnage.

The custom is the Catch Bell: the day's first haul is auctioned at the sea-exchange by bell, live, and the price set is broadcast as the day's tariff floor, so every fisher in the system knows what the morning is worth before breakfast. The grievance is the sea's own accounting: the Combine's fisheries courts fine their own fleets for over-run quotas with a strictness the Helion assemblies publicly admire and privately call "the one honest thing the Freeports have ever done," a compliment the fishers accept, at standard rates.
## Last Call

- **Settled:** 2268 (free-port charter); continuously chartered since.
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4496383055821714816, M, 58.93 ly
- **Population:** 61,000
- **System:** a dim M sun over three small rocks — locked furnace stone, hot airy rock, cold mooned pebble — and nothing else worth the entry fee.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Silverfin's star; Mesh gates to HIP 92283, Gaia EDR3 4542284161585626496, and Gaia EDR3 4523577620507728768.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2303, in the Freeports' cheapest recorded sale: three rocks, four routes, and no bidders — until the station tavern's keeper, one Marguerite Okwuoha, bid the evening's outstanding bar tabs, and the auctioneer, facing a room full of creditors, closed the hammer. The auction minute survives: *sold, with the furniture.* The Freeports consider it the most honest name in the register.

Last Call is a relay that runs on gossip and docking fees. The depot serves three mesh routes' worth of crew rotation, and the system's one real institution is the tavern that named it, which keeps the tradition Marguerite started: the tariff board and the drinks list are the same board, and a crew's docking credit is good at the bar. The settlement's population is depot families and the pilots who never quite left, and their one civic rule is quoted to every newcomer: *nothing in this system is closed except the tab.*
## Compline

- **Settled:** 2249 (founding chapter charter, Time of Troubles emigration wave); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4503794795019024000 (HIP 92283), K, 55.06 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** a K sun over hot airy rocks, a temperate bare rock with two moons, and two colossal giants — 554 and 228 Earth masses — whose captured and mooned satellites hold the hermitages.
- **Links:** Regional gates to GJ 4109, Gaia EDR3 4293873732939569920, and Gaia EDR3 4311982650767794176; Mesh gate to Last Call's star.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter — the Chapter of the Quiet Hours, descendants of the old contemplative orders — who named the system for the night prayer: *Compline, the prayer that closes the day, for worlds at the far edge of the light.* The Commonwealth's Chapter Register entered the dedication without emendation, as it has entered every dedication since.

Compline is the Commonwealth's house of silence. The chapters came out during the Time of Troubles, when the Union's reproductive standards made plain that the old contemplative life would have no room on Earth's terms, and they took a system the speculators had no use for — giants, cold moons, one gardener's rock — and made it a cloister with four gates. The hermitage stations ride the giants' moons in slow procession; the temperate rock's terraced domes grow the settlements' food; and the system's whole population is the support fabric that keeps a great quiet running: farmers, tenders, pilots, and the chapter-brothers and -sisters themselves, in numbers the Chapter Register records to the person, every year, in a ledger that is itself set in type and printed for anyone.

What Compline is genuinely best at is keeping voices. The choir schools here preserve the chant tradition through the Cataclysm's wreckage — the oldest continuous sung liturgy in the settled sky, carried from Earth chapter by chapter — and the Commonwealth's seminaries, its retreat houses, and its famous hospitalidad (the guest houses that feed any pilgrim for three days without a question) all grew around that keeping. The custom that defines the place is the Great Silence: from the last bell of Compline prayer to the first bell of dawn, no one in the system speaks on any public channel, and every ship at the anchor rides the night quiet, engines damped, lights low. The grievance is gentle and perpetual: the Freeports' advertisement packets, which arrive by mesh regardless of hour, and which the brothers answer — when they answer at all — with the register's only running joke, filed annually: *even merchants must sleep, though we notice they do it loudly.*
## San José

- **Settled:** 2252 (founding chapter charter, Time of Troubles wave); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2026037542160935552 (GJ 4109), M, 54.71 ly
- **Population:** 2.6 million
- **System:** locked hot rocks, heavy bare cold super-earths, mooned ice worlds, and a five-mooned ice giant whose shallow gravity wells make easy ore.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 98767, Compline's star, GJ 1241, and Gaia EDR3 1310513342280948480.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *San José* — Saint Joseph the Worker — chosen, the Chapter Register records, *for a system with nothing in it but work.*

San José is the Commonwealth's workshop. The lay brothers' works companies quarry the heavy rocks, skim the ice giant, and run the foundry yards that build the Commonwealth's durable goods: tractors, hab modules, church bells, and the famous San José stoves that half the quarter's kitchens cook on. The settlement's rhythm is liturgical and industrial at once — the foundry shifts are rung by the chapel bells, and the brothers' rule that no shift may begin without a spoken blessing has made the shop-floor blessing a craft of its own, exchanged, adapted, and in a few yards, set to hammer rhythm.

The people are lay families — the Commonwealth's working class, and proud of the title — plus the brothers who work alongside them under the same rule and the same pension plan. The custom of the place is the Finished Piece: every product that leaves the yards is signed by its crew, and San José's makers will rework anything unsigned for free, on the stated grounds that *a thing without a maker is an orphan.* The grievance is Freeport machinery: Coldstore's catalogues sell cheaper knock-offs of the San José stove, and the brothers' annual protest to the Assembly includes, as evidence, a disassembled knock-off arranged as an Advent calendar.
## San Cristóbal

- **Settled:** 2255 (founding chapter charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2029433521248546304 (HIP 98767), K, 52.20 ly
- **Population:** 5.4 million
- **System:** a bright K sun over mooned hot rocks, a temperate bare world at 278 K with two moons — one atmosphere short of a garden, and the chapters' patient project — then airy cold worlds out to the rim.
- **Links:** six gates — Regional to GJ 699.1, San José's star, GJ 4225, Gaia EDR3 1862034696988864896, and the great corridor gate toward Cinder system; Mesh to GJ 1241.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *San Cristóbal* — Saint Christopher, the Christ-bearer, patron of travelers — for the system every pilgrim, settler, and cargo of the northern Commonwealth passes through. The Chapter Register's line is quoted at the anchor chapel door: *here we carry people.*

San Cristóbal is the door of the northern Commonwealth. Six gates meet here, and the chapter built the system's whole life around the fact of transit: the great hospice rings at the anchor — beds, soup, and confession in six languages — the pilgrim hostels on the bare rock's mooned plains, and the customs houses that process the Commonwealth's traffic with the calm of people who have seen every kind of arrival and consider each one, professionally and spiritually, a matter of hospitality. The Threshold Garden project on the bare world is the chapters' long charity: an atmosphere built a century the way a cathedral is, donation by donation, in the open, for the settlement's great-grandchildren.

The people are hospice families, port chaplains, and the workers' districts that keep a gateway fed, and their custom is the Traveler's Blessing: no ship leaves the anchor without a blessing spoken over its hull by whoever is on duty — chaplain, cook, or dockmaster — with the words changing but the rule not. The grievance is tariff theology: the Freeport passenger combines complain annually that San Cristóbal's hospice rates "distort the market for mercy," and the chapter's published reply, gentle as ever, is that the market for mercy was never open.
## San Rafael

- **Settled:** 2246 (founding charter of the Commonwealth's first chapter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1711005951573009792 (GJ 699.1), white dwarf, 53.31 ly
- **Population:** 8.1 million
- **System:** a dead sun — a white dwarf, steady, white, and small — over locked rocks, a hot locked ocean world, cold moons, and a 122-Earth-mass giant with its single patient moon.
- **Links:** Regional gates to San Cristóbal's star, GJ 3768, Gaia EDR3 2281046483684995840, and HIP 86141; Mesh gate to HIP 115147.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapters to *San Rafael* — the archangel, the healer — at the moment they constituted themselves a commonwealth. The Chapter Register's founding line is the polity's oldest sentence: *we took the light that had died and still gave light, and we read our work in it.*

San Rafael is the Commonwealth's heart and its hospital. The founding chapters settled a white-dwarf system because of what it said to them — a sun that had ended and still gave light — and built the see of their church and the great teaching hospitals that give the Commonwealth its name in the rest of the quarter: the Healer's Houses, where anyone is treated at the same posted rates, which are the same low rates, and where half the League's surgeons train. The system's five gates route the sick, the studying, and the pilgrim trade that comes to pray at the founding chapter-house, and the settlement's life runs on rounds and bells.

The people are medical families, seminarians, chapter servants, and the workers' districts that feed a city of hospitals, and their custom is the Healers' Vigil: the night shift of every hospital gathers once a cycle at the dome of the founding house to renew the charter line, and the words are said by whoever has been on the ward longest, not by rank. The grievance is the Commonwealth's quietest and oldest: their own Assembly's habit of praising the hospitals in speeches while funding them in footnotes, answered every year by the sisters' bookkeeping office, which publishes the speeches and the budget on facing pages, with no commentary — none needed.
## San Alberto

- **Settled:** 2258 (chapter charter).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1726678630833373824 (GJ 3768), K brown dwarf, 53.72 ly
- **Population:** 890,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and a mooned ice giant — small, dark, and quiet, which the founding chapter considered an academic virtue.
- **Links:** Regional gate to San Rafael's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2281046483684995840, Gaia EDR3 1139642706975159552, and HIP 38784.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *San Alberto* — Albertus Magnus, patron of scholars — for the Commonwealth's college of natural philosophy, founded here because, the Register records, *the dark sky concentrates the mind.*

San Alberto is the Commonwealth's college town. The chapter's studium — theology, natural philosophy, medicine's pre-clinical years, and the famous observatory school that works the dark between the ember stars — sits above a brown dwarf's dim hearth-light with four routes' worth of students, and the settlement is the town that surrounds any college: tutors' families, hostelries, book-binders, and the slow arguments of cafés. Its custom is the Disputation — the term's closing debate is public, held in the college hall, contested between the two strongest students regardless of rank, and the whole town attends as it attends feasts. Its grievance is perennially graduate: stipends, which the students describe as monastic and the monks describe as generous, and both cite the same founder's will.
## Census

- **Settled:** 2259 (guild charter, pre-Partition outer wave); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1139642706975159552, M, 54.99 ly
- **Population:** 1.2 million
- **System:** a dim M sun over a locked furnace rock, a locked puffed ice giant, and a locked temperate stone at 287 K with no air — the whole retiny inside a fortnight.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1005873614080407296 and HIP 38784; Mesh gates to San Alberto's star and HIP 25110.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Meridian League's Committee on Nomenclature in its first register, 2261, for the system's function: the League's central registry of persons, credentials, and things. The Committee's certificate of name — Meridian practice requires the name itself to be certified like any professional work — is displayed at the registry hall and reads, in full: *Census. Certified accurate.*

Census is the Meridian League's filing cabinet, and the League is proud of it the way other polities are proud of cathedrals. Every member of every Meridian guild is registered here — credentials, continuing education, disciplinary record, the lot — and the registry's neutral-zone office also performs the League's certified function of recognizing foreign credentials: the exams that let a Helion engineer or Freeport navigator work Meridian routes are administered here by a staff whose neutrality is the subject of two centuries of committee minutes and at least one folk song. The settlement is registry clerks, examiners, and the small industry that feeds a town of forms: paper-makers, archivists, and tea.

The custom is the Open Register: once a year the registry's error log — every mistake found, corrected, and annotated — is published and read aloud by the newest clerk, the League's way of proving that even the register of perfection is imperfect and maintained anyway. The grievance is the Freeports' running joke that Census is where fun goes to be alphabetized, which the Committee has never dignified with a reply, though the registry's internal nickname for the joke archive — *Materia Absurdica* — leaked decades ago and has been affectionately used ever since.
## Mansio

- **Settled:** 2262 (guild charter, pre-Partition wave); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1005873614080407296, M, 53.10 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** a dim M sun over locked rocks, a cold airy stone, and a mooned gas giant at the working distance — an unremarkable retiny the League valued precisely for that.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Census's star, Gaia EDR3 912087093295958144, HIP 35136, and — the guild road's great leg — Meridian system itself.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2264, for the system's certified function: the waystation of the guild roads, where the capital's traffic stages, rests, and is inspected. The certificate reads: *Mansio. A stopping place. Certified apt.*

Mansio is where Meridian catches its breath on the way to Meridian. Nearly all traffic bound for the capital's great system stages through here, and the League built the station the profession of stops deserves: guild hostels rated by a published standard, inspection cradles for hull certification, the examinations annex where journeyman candidates sit their route papers before the last leg, and a hostel kitchen whose soup is, per the League's own certified survey, adequate. The population is hostelers, inspectors, and the families of the station guild, and their pride is procedural: no ship has ever missed the capital's gate from Mansio's fault, a record the station master updates, initials, and posts annually.

The custom is the Ledger of Wagers: candidates for the journeyman papers bet one coin each, before the examination annex, on their own pass rate — the pot going to the examined house's hardship fund — the League's dry way of teaching that hope is not a credential. The grievance is the soup, which every annual survey certifies adequate and every resident describes, in the margin, as *terminally.*
## Cardo

- **Settled:** 2265 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-912087093295958144, brown dwarf, 53.62 ly
- **Population:** 3.4 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace super-earth, a locked temperate ice giant, a mooned airy rock, and a long cold tail of ice giants — nothing to live on, everything to trade around.
- **Links:** six gates — Regional to Mansio's star, GJ 3479, Gaia EDR3 755921425282888960, and 10 UMa; Mesh to HIP 35136 and Gaia EDR3 901941452829250560.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2267, for the system's function as the hinge of six roads and the seat of the Guilds' Exchange. The certificate: *Cardo. A hinge. Certified apt.* The Committee's internal minute, later published by the League's own historians, records one member's objection that *apt* was insufficiently dry, and the reply that it was the driest word available for a system this important.

Cardo is the League's market. The Guilds' Exchange — the great trading floor above the ember star, where the products of every Meridian guild and every willing neighbor are sold under certified weights, certified quality, and a dispute procedure that other jurisdictions import whole — sets the reference prices for half the quarter's dry goods and machinery, and the settlement has grown entirely in the Exchange's service: warehousing, assay houses, the bank halls of the guild pension funds, and the schools that train the League's licensed traders, who wear their guild pins the way other polities wear flags. The Exchange's open cry is quiet, punctual, and very fast; visitors expecting the Freeports' theater are always surprised by the silence and always converted by the settlement speed.

The custom is the Tally: every lot sold is chalked, that hour, on the public board by hand, and the board's daily photograph — the League's word for it, unsmiling — is the legal record, published for everyone, so that no price in the quarter is secret. The grievance is the brokers: Freeport intermediaries who buy at Cardo and re-sell at Bathgate margins, and whose operations the Exchange's annual report describes, with the Committee's driest available adjective, as *efficient.*
## Cautio

- **Settled:** 2268 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-907066684548911872 (GJ 3479), M, 57.63 ly
- **Population:** 1.5 million
- **System:** a locked steam ocean, cold mooned airy rocks, and three ice giants — ordinary worlds, extraordinary paperwork.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Cardo's star and Gaia EDR3 901941452829250560; Mesh gates to HIP 35136, 10 UMa, 22 Lyn, and Gaia EDR3 923023248261420672.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2270, for the seat of the League's Surety Guild: *Cautio. A bond. Certified apt.* The Committee noted the system's six roads and pronounced the coincidence convenient.

Cautio is the quarter's escrow. The Surety Guild underwrites the League's contracts from here — performance bonds, convoy sureties, the famous Meridian seal that lets a stranger trust a certified stranger — and the system's population is actuaries, loss adjusters, and the vault crews who hold bonded goods in the ice giants' calm. Every road out of the quarter passes a Cautio seal on something. The custom is the Proof: no bond is filed without a publicly readable loss model attached, and the guild's annual collection of worst predictions — published voluntarily, as discipline — is studied at Mansio's examination annex. The grievance is the Freeports, which sell the same product without the loss models, and whose prices the guild's annual report cites without comment of any kind, which is the comment.
## Vitrum

- **Settled:** 2271 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-976893923544104576 (22 Lyncis), G, 66.54 ly
- **Population:** 760,000
- **System:** a hot bright G sun over mooned furnace rocks and a thick-aired world at 340 K — a star the glassmakers' guild calls, in official documentation, *the annealing fire.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 35136, Gaia EDR3 923023248261420672, and GJ 3444; Mesh gate to Cautio's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2273, for the seat of the Glassmakers' Guild: *Vitrum. Glass. Certified apt.*

Vitrum is where the quarter sees. The guild's works — mirror blanks, lens trains, the famous Vitrum blanks for observatory primaries — are annealed slow under this hot sun's steady light, and every serious telescope from San Alberto's college to the deep stations carries a Vitrum seal somewhere in its light path. The population is guild families, and their customs are the trade's: the First Light ceremony for each finished primary, and the guild rule that no blank leaves without the maker's mark of whoever ground it, errors included, because *a mirror that lies about its maker will lie about anything.* The grievance is sales: Freeport catalogues offer "Vitrum-grade" optics that are not, and the guild's counterfeits file — each fake documented, named, and priced — is kept in the lobby, updated monthly, and read like scripture by visiting opticians.
## Index

- **Settled:** 2275 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-989380492985184768 (GJ 3444), brown dwarf, 78.80 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and three mooned ice giants — a system the League values for its roads out rather than its worlds in.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Vitrum's star, Gaia EDR3 1033808665486561664, HIP 29067, and HD 233453B; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 982196799404971392 and HIP 37061.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2277, for the seat of the Chartmakers' Guild: *Index. A register. Certified apt.* The Committee appended its dry note that a system with six roads out should keep the list.

Index is where the quarter's maps are made and kept. The Chartmakers' Guild surveys, certifies, and publishes the route charts, ephemerides, and hazard registers for every road out of this junction and half the mesh beyond — six routes radiate from the anchor, and each begins, legally, with an Index chart in the pilot room. The population is surveyors, tide-table clerks, and the families of the station, and their custom is the Correction: every chart error found by any pilot, anywhere, is reported to the guild, confirmed, and the reporter's name printed in the margin of the next edition — so Index's charts carry the honor roll of the quarter's most careful navigators, and pilots compete, gently and forever, for margin space. The grievance is the Freeport charts, which are prettier, cheaper, and — per the guild's side-by-side comparison, published without adjectives — wrong in four places.
## Fodina

- **Settled:** 2278 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1033808665486561664, M, 79.67 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** locked furnace rocks, a steam ocean world 84% sea, a heavy mooned airy rock, and three paired-moon ice giants — ore, volatiles, and little else.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Index's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 917859937361774976, HD 233453B, and HIP 37061.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2280, for the seat of the Miners' Guild: *Fodina. A dig. Certified apt.*

Fodina is the League's pit. The Miners' Guild works the heavy mooned rock and the giants' volatiles under a certification regime so thorough that other polities' inspectors come here for training, and the settlement's one famous custom belongs to the crews: the Bound Lamp, a lamp lit at every shift start from the lamp of the system's founding shift, so that every crew works by a light continuous, in the literal belief the League has never endorsed or discouraged, with the mine. The grievance is tonnage quotas set in Cardo's offices by people who, per the guild's annual filing, *have excellent chairs.*
## Caupona

- **Settled:** 2281 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-917859937361774976, M, 80.21 ly
- **Population:** 4.4 million
- **System:** heavy locked furnace rocks inward, then **Caupona** (the third world) — a warm ocean planet at 311 K, two-thirds of its face under sea, riding one patient moon — with a 225-Earth-mass giant and a captured locked companion beyond.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1009198949557703040, Fodina's star, and Gaia EDR3 1013461034585110528; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 923023248261420672.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2283, for the seat of the Innkeepers' Guild and the League's rest-and-training houses: *Caupona. An inn. Certified apt.*

Caupona is where the League takes its leave. The warm sea world's archipelago stations run the guild rest-houses where every Meridian member banks a yearly stay as part of their pension — a policy the Freeports find hilarious and quietly envy — and the settlement's population of innkeepers, pilots, and tide-clerks runs the finest hospitality standard in the quarter, certified annually, to the sheet. The custom is the Open Register: every guest house posts its tide tables, its storm ledger, and its occupancy in real time at the dock, because the Guild's rule is that an innkeeper surprises nobody, not about weather, not about beds. The grievance is Bathgate, whose resorts advertise "warm-world standards at Freeport prices," a phrase the Innkeepers' Guild has formally analyzed in its annual report as *true, in the way a certificate and a coupon are both paper.*
## Nuntius

- **Settled:** 2284 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1009198949557703040, M, 68.83 ly
- **Population:** 710,000
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked rocks and paired ice giants — the League kept it for the quiet, and the quiet keeps it.
- **Links:** Regional gate to 10 UMa; Mesh gates to 15 LMi, Gaia EDR3 923023248261420672, and Caupona's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2286, for the seat of the Signalers' Guild: *Nuntius. A message. Certified apt.*

Nuntius carries the League's words. The Signalers' Guild operates the certified message relay — the quarter's mail, its legal notices, and the famous Verbatim Service, by which any Meridian member's spoken testament may be recorded, sealed, and delivered with guarantees the arbitration circuit treats as bedrock. The settlement's custom is the Delivery Bell: a message delivered in person is announced by hand-bell at the door, a formality older than the guild and kept, the members say, because bad news deserves warning and good news deserves ceremony. The grievance is the Freeports' packet services, which are faster, cheaper, and — the guild's annual report counts — lose things.
## Faber

- **Settled:** 2287 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-821455861647126656 (15 Lyncis Minoris), G, 61.39 ly
- **Population:** 1.7 million
- **System:** a fierce G sun — its innermost rock runs at 891 K — over mooned hot rocks, two hot ocean worlds (398 K and 425 K), and a cold airy rock at the rim.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 755921425282888960, GJ 3600, 10 UMa, and Nuntius's star; Regional gate to HIP 47690.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2289, for the seat of the Boilermakers' Guild: *Faber. A smith. Certified apt.* The Committee's note records that the star does half the guild's work for it.

Faber is the League's forge. The Boilermakers work the hot worlds' thermal gradients — pressure vessels, gate hardware, the famous Faber steam-locomotives of the ice-moon railways — and the system's five routes ship the output to every yard in the quarter, including Craft's, which buys Faber plate and stamps its own name on the modules anyway. The custom is the Cooling: every forge cycle ends with the crew drinking, in full view, one glass of water poured over hot stone, the guild's acknowledgment that the fire is a tool and the smith is not. The grievance is ancient and internal — the smiths want the name of a great smith; the Committee has certified, repeatedly, that *Faber* is a great smith — and the argument has been remanded, by mutual delight, to every session since 2290.
## Sal

- **Settled:** 2290 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-819750862709551360 (HIP 47690), K, 58.69 ly
- **Population:** 580,000
- **System:** a K sun over locked furnace rocks, a hot airy two-mooned stone, a hot brine ocean world (445 K, 36% sea), and cold mooned ice beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 755921425282888960, GJ 3600, and Faber's star; Mesh gate to 10 UMa.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2292, for the seat of the Salters' Guild: *Sal. Salt. Certified apt.* The Committee declined the guild's petition to add an adjective.

Sal keeps the quarter's chemistry honest. The Salters' Guild works the brine ocean's evaporator terraces — salts, industrial brines, the trace chemistry half the quarter's processes depend on — and its assay office sets the reference standards under which Cardo's Exchange trades every reagent in the League. The settlement's custom is the Crystallization: each year's first harvest of the finest grade is auctioned publicly, by law at the guild hall and by tradition at a whisper, and the proceeds fund the guild's schools. The grievance is Bathgate's spa chemists, who buy Sal brines and re-sell them, per the Salters' annual filing, *with adjectives the Committee would not certify.*
## Arva

- **Settled:** 2293 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-847330222106968064 (GJ 3600, binary M), 61.02 ly
- **Population:** 11 million
- **System:** twin red suns — the League's almanac calls them *the Two Farmhands* — each holding locked rocks; the settled world, **Arva** (GJ 3600 B 3), is a cool-temperate airy planet at 255 K around the second sun.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Sal's star, GJ 3560, and Gaia EDR3 824017070904063488; Mesh gate to Faber's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2295, for the seat of the Husbandmen's Guild: *Arva. Fields. Certified apt.*

Arva is the League's breadbasket. The cool-temperate world's long river plains under the second sun grow the grain, root stock, and orchard lines that feed the League's own and half the mesh's dry-goods aisles, farmed under the Husbandmen's certified rotation — a standard so thoroughly observed that Arva's soil ledgers are kept like legal documents, which, under the League, they effectively are. The twin-sun almanac governs everything: the Two Farmhands' joint sky gives Arva its double seasons, and the guild's planting calendar, certified twice a year by law, is the settlement's real constitution. The custom is the Seed Blessing — each cycle's first sowing is done by the youngest certified farmer, in full view of the whole township, on the grounds that *the future should see its own hands.* The grievance is the weather, which the Committee has declined to certify.
## Vinum

- **Settled:** 2296 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1062935140823485184 (GJ 3560), M, 64.73 ly
- **Population:** 3.4 million
- **System:** heavy hot locked rocks inward, then **Vinum** (the third world), a temperate airy planet at 271 K with one large moon — south-slope country — and a long cold tail of airy rocks out.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Arva's star, 16 UMa, and Gaia EDR3 1022050415123922176; Mesh gate to σ02 UMa.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2298, for the seat of the Vintners' Guild: *Vinum. Wine. Certified apt.* The Committee's note observes that the guild petitioned for no ornament, and that this was wise.

Vinum is the League's cellar. The temperate world's long south-facing escarpments — sunlit by the patient red primary, cooled by the moon-driven seasons — carry the terraced vineyards whose certified vintages stock every guild table from Caupona's rest-houses to the capital's halls, and whose blending ledgers, kept with assay-office precision, are the settlement's pride and its principal legal literature. The custom is the First Press: each harvest's first must is pressed at the guild hall in public, tasted by the youngest certified vintner, and sealed with the year's mark — because, the guild rule runs, *the vintage should meet its future honestly.* The grievance is climate's little brother, the frost — Vinum's escarpment weather is certified annually as *challenging*, the Committee's word, and the vintners' own word, published in the margins of the same almanac, is *character-building.*
## Ignis

- **Settled:** 2299 (guild charter); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1040054235130174080 (16 Ursae Majoris), G, 66.01 ly
- **Population:** 1.3 million
- **System:** a hot G sun, a furnace mooned rock, a 520-Earth-mass gas giant burning at 452 K — the system's power plant — a hot heavy ocean world, and a cold mooned pebble.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Vinum's star and σ02 UMa; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 824017070904063488 and Gaia EDR3 1022050415123922176.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2301, for the seat of the Fireworkers' Guild: *Ignis. Fire. Certified apt.*

Ignis is the League's hearth. The Fireworkers skim the great giant's 452 K atmosphere — thermal trawling at a scale the quarter's engineers cite as the standard case study — and sell station heat, process steam, and certified ignition services along four routes, with the big customers being Faber's forges and the deep stations' winter lines. The settlement's custom is the Ember Watch: the power-hall's flame, kept from the first skim in 2301, is never allowed out, and each shift's first duty is to verify it burning, a practice the guild calls *accounting.* The grievance is Radiance — the Helion lightfarmers undersell Ignis on the mesh's southern routes, and the Fireworkers' annual report on the subject is the League's driest document, consisting, in its entirety, of the two co-ops' price tables printed side by side.
## Gemina

- **Settled:** 2304 (slip-delivered, twin-world charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1068982214258617216 (σ² Ursae Majoris), G, 66.91 ly
- **Population:** 96 million
- **System:** a big bright G sun; two temperate airy worlds in the outer warmth — **Gemina Major** (279 K, two moons) and **Gemina Minor** (262 K, one moon) — with a hot airy rock, a four-mooned ice giant, and a strange far-flung family of outcast rocks beyond, including a 1.7-Earth-mass wanderer at 39 au.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 38784, Vinum's star, and Gaia EDR3 1022050415123922176; Regional gate to Ignis's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2306, for the League's twin worlds: *Gemina. Twin. Certified apt.* The Committee's minute records the single witticism in its official history: *certified twice.*

Gemina is the League's second home and its great demographic success: two habitable worlds in one system, settled by charter under two dozen guilds at once, and grown in a century from charter stations to ninety-six million people. Major took the cities — the guild halls, the certified academies, the foundry districts under its double moons — while Minor took the orchards, herds, and the League's agricultural college, and the two worlds' institutions are deliberately twinned: every guild hall on Major has a sister station on Minor, and the ferry trade between them, eleven days by the fast runs, is the League's busiest passenger route. The far wanderer at 39 au — the stray heavy rock with its own atmosphere — is the system's curiosity, claimed by no guild and settled by a handful of licensed hermits whose tax status is, per the Committee's annual note, *under continuous review.*

The custom of Gemina is the Exchange of Keys: every household keeps two house-keys, one for its own door and one symbolic key carried for its sister-house on the other world, exchanged at weddings, graduations, and departures, so that every Geminan's pockets jingle with a promise. The grievance is weather, which being uncetifiable is therefore unarbitrable: Major's storm season disrupts the ferry schedule every year, and the ferry guild's claim of force majeure has been contested, by the same advocate, in the same arbitration hall, for nineteen consecutive years — a case the League's law students attend the way other polities attend theater.
## Rete

- **Settled:** 2307 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1022050415123922176, brown dwarf, 72.07 ly
- **Population:** 7.7 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked ocean worlds — hot steam at 506 K, then two warm twilight seas habitable in the dusk bands (320 K, 62% sea; 287 K, 36% sea) — with airy ice worlds and a mooned ice giant behind them.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Vinum's, Ignis's, and Gemina's stars and to HIP 51248; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 824017070904063488.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2309, for the seat of the Fishers' Guild: *Rete. A net. Certified apt.*

Rete is the League's tide country. Two warm locked ocean worlds give the Fishers' Guild its double fishery — the dusk-band archipelagos netted, dredged, and farmed under certified rotation like any Arva field — and the settlement's sea-towns ride the twilight shorelines in house-fleets that have grown, merged, and intermarried into a floating nation with its own pilot dialect and its own strict manners. Rete's catch, dried, salted with Sal's standard, and packed in Craft's lattice, is the protein ration of half the mesh's working routes, and the guild's catch ledgers — certified by tide, by shoal, by boat — are the largest continuous biological record in the quarter. The custom is the Mending: every net, before every season, is re-knitted in public circles where any fisher may inspect any other's knots, because, the guild rule runs, *the sea grades everyone eventually; the circle grades them first.* The grievance is the drift — Silverfin's Freeport trawlers cross the border shoals in bad years, and the Fishers' annual boundary survey is filed with the arbitration circuit with a precision that has been described, by the circuit itself, as pointed.
## Vectura

- **Settled:** 2310 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-834571523536798848 (HIP 51248, binary G+M), 74.99 ly
- **Population:** 2.0 million
- **System:** a G primary holding a mooned furnace rock, airy hot stones, a 555-Earth-mass warm giant with twin captured locked moons — both basking at 330 K — and an ice giant; the M companion keeps its own rich family, ending in a six-mooned giant with captured ice.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 824017070904063488 and Gaia EDR3 853568610563444224; Mesh gates to Rete's star, HIP 54745, and HIP 54426.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2312, for the seat of the Carters' Guild: *Vectura. Carriage. Certified apt.*

Vectura is where the freight changes hands. The Carters run the junction's five routes with the League's largest certified hauling fleet — transfer cradles, bonded warehousing in the warm giant's calm, and the famous twin-moon warehouses, where the captured rocks' 330 K bask is used to keep hulls, seals, and cargo temperate for free — an economy the guild's annual report attributes, with no wit at all, to *gravity's generosity.* The settlement's custom is the Chain Check: before any convoy departs, every crew signs the couplings ledger of every other crew, each binder accepting the whole chain's load as their own knowledge. The grievance is relay fees — Cautio bonds them, Cardo prices them, and the Carters pay both, an arrangement their filings describe as *correct, certified, and paid under protest.*
## Custos

- **Settled:** 2313 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-761919883981626752 (HIP 54745), G, 74.03 ly
- **Population:** 870,000
- **System:** a plain G sun over bare hot rocks, an airy stone at 446 K, a temperate pebble with no air, and a heavy cold mooned rock — worlds the League describes, in the certified survey, as *present.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 755921425282888960 and HIP 54426; Mesh gates to Vectura's star and Gaia EDR3 763204113562706560.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2315, for the seat of the Way-wardens' Guild: *Custos. A keeper. Certified apt.*

Custos keeps the roads. The Way-wardens' Guild maintains the beacons, anchorages, and rescue stations of four routes from this junction — the League's standing fleet of tugs, medics, and patience — and the settlement's population is their families, their trainees, and the rescue-statistics nerds who run the guild's famous ledger of everyone ever saved. The custom is the Watch Change: every shift rotation ends with the outgoing watch reading the day's incident log aloud to the incoming, name by name, so that *no call is ever just a number.* The grievance is the quiet war against private beacons — Freeport toll-lights that lure tired pilots off the certified anchors — which the wardens' annual report tracks, extinguishes where legal, and describes, where not, as *lighthouse piracy.*
## Libra

- **Settled:** 2316 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-763204113562706560, M, 85.73 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** locked rocks and a hot locked ocean world inward, then the giants — a mooned gas giant and a parade of four-mooned ice giants to the cold rim — a quiet system with good stone and better vacuum.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Custos's star, HIP 54212, and Gaia EDR3 753198111073583488; Regional gate to HIP 54426.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2318, for the seat of the Assayers' Guild: *Libra. A balance. Certified apt.*

Libra weighs the quarter. The Assayers keep the League's reference masses, the certified scales Cardo's Exchange and every port tariff ultimately trace to, and the deep-vault standards against which every scale in the quarter is legally calibrated — including Helion's, by treaty, and the Freeports', by invoice. The settlement's custom is the Null Room: the guild's calibration chamber, open to any visitor who wishes to watch a standard balance nothing, and the line at the door on Open Days is the League's quietest joke about its own soul. The grievance is the Committee's: for ninety years the Assayers have petitioned that *Libra* be certified *apt and precise*, and for ninety years the Committee has replied that it is the Committee's job to know when a word is already complete.
## Vestis

- **Settled:** 2319 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-782266484092919808 (HIP 54212), M, 94.23 ly
- **Population:** 470,000
- **System:** locked hot rocks, a mooned temperate pebble, a bare ice giant, and a cold airy stone — a tailor's system: nothing wasted, nothing flashy.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 833043374172123520, Libra's star, and Gaia EDR3 755711555999758464; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 836099398022603776.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2321, for the seat of the Clothiers' Guild: *Vestis. Clothing. Certified apt.*

Vestis clothes the quarter's workers. The Clothiers' works turn out the certified environments of daily labor — the pressure suit cut to guild standard, the storm kit, the famous Vestis thermals that Fodina's miners and Solace's road teams order by the ton — and the settlement lives inside the industry's rhythm: pattern halls, lofts, and the fitting house where every garment in the catalogue is worn, tested, and signed for by a working member of the trade it is cut for. The custom is the Fitting: no design is certified until a journeyman has worked a full shift in it and reported, in writing, on everything it did wrong. The grievance is fashion — the Freeports' catalogues sell *Vestis-adjacent* styles at thrice the price, and the guild's annual clipping of the worst examples is filed under a title the Committee has asked them, twice, politely, to change.
## Notarius

- **Settled:** 2322 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-833043374172123520, brown dwarf, 83.19 ly
- **Population:** 410,000
- **System:** an ember primary holding three locked rocks in the twilight band — the second of them, **Notarius** proper, a small airy world at 288 K — the whole system inside a week's transit of itself.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 54426 and Vestis's star; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 853568610563444224 and Gaia EDR3 836099398022603776.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2324, for the seat of the Scriveners' Guild: *Notarius. A scribe. Certified apt.*

Notarius prints the League. The Scriveners' Guild holds the public press — the certified editions of every statute, chart, and verdict the League publishes, set in type at the great house on the little dusk world, and distributed by four routes to every guild hall in the quarter — and the settlement's population is the press's own: compositors, proofreaders, and the paper-mill families whose water the ember light keeps warm. The custom is the Reading of the Galley: every certified edition is read aloud, once, in the press hall before the run, errors corrected in public, because the guild's founding rule holds that *a public that cannot hear its laws being made can at least hear them being spelled.* The grievance is space: the archive annexes keep growing, and the Committee has certified the annex budget *apt* for thirty consecutive years, a word the Scriveners now use, in their own annual report, as a lesson in irony for apprentices.
## Dolium

- **Settled:** 2325 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-836099398022603776, M, 90.26 ly
- **Population:** 340,000
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked rocks, a cold airy stone, a mooned ice world, and a four-mooned ice giant — a small system with exactly the stave-wood and metal a cask requires.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Notarius's star, GJ 405, and Vestis's star; Mesh gate to Merak.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2327, for the seat of the Coopers' Guild: *Dolium. A cask. Certified apt.*

Dolium is the League's cask-house. The Coopers make the certified containers — Vinum's casks, Sal's salt bins, Rete's fish crates, the bonded casks of Cautio's escrow vaults — and their settlement is a town of stave-yards, hoop-forges, and the great proving hall where every cask design is pressure-tested before certification, in public, with the guild's cherished ceremony of the slow leak. The custom is the Hoop Dance: a finished cask is rolled three times around the hall by its maker before the stamp, because, the guild says, *a cask should know its maker's stride.* The grievance is the standard crate — the Freeports' shipping boxes undercut certified casks on price, and the Coopers' annual comparison, printed without commentary, is a photograph of what arrives broken.
## Merak

- **Settled:** 2328 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-856096765753549056 (Merak, β Ursae Majoris), F giant, 85.01 ly
- **Population:** 3.1 million
- **System:** a vast, brilliant F giant — fifty-five solar luminosities, white as arc-light — over distant hot rocks, a temperate four-mooned ice giant at 270 K that serves as the system's harbor, a cold airy two-mooned rock, and a 275-Earth-mass giant in the far dark.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 853568610563444224 and Dolium's star; Mesh gates to GJ 405 and Gaia EDR3 1047957520414556032.
- **Registry name:** kept, at the Committee on Nomenclature's own motion, under the League's standing rule for ancient IAU-proper names. The certificate is the Committee's driest: *Merak. Pre-owned. Certified apt.*

Merak is the bright door of the outer quarter. The star's fierce white light makes the system the most visible landmark in this whole region of the sky — to navigators arriving by slip, the giant's glare is the first thing their instruments catch, and the Way-wardens' beacon station here has guided more lost hulls than any other in the League. The population lives in the orbital towns around the temperate ice giant, whose four slow moons give the settlements their calendar, their shielding, and their names, and the local industry is what the light itself pays for: the great mirror-foundries of the Navigators' Guild, annealing in a glare no star in the inner quarter can match.

The navigators keep the Polaris Line: a monument at the old station's axis, aligned — to the arcsecond, by guild law — with the direction of Sol's north star as seen from Earth, because Merak was one of the two pointer stars of the ancient sky, and the guild's members hold that *a star that spent ten thousand years showing one people the way can spend the next ten thousand showing everyone.* The custom is the First Sight: every certified navigator, on their first posting through Merak, stands the Line at local midnight and writes their name in the Line's book, under a sky-white so bright there are no stars to see — the point being, the old masters say, that the pointers must learn to point even when the sky they knew is gone. The grievance is the star's future, spoken of quietly: Merak is a giant, bright because it is leaving, and the guild's long-term hydrographic surveys, filed without adjectives, chart the brightening century by century.
## Funis

- **Settled:** 2329 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1047957520414556032, M, 94.91 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** locked hot rocks, a 98-Earth-mass temperate giant at 255 K, a heavy airy rock, a three-mooned ice giant, and a long cold tail with captured strays.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Merak's, Gaia EDR3 1065113754394291584's, and Gaia EDR3 1066086650386220288's stars; Regional gate to GJ 405.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2331, for the seat of the Ropemakers' Guild: *Funis. A rope. Certified apt.*

Funis holds the quarter together, one filament at a time. The Ropemakers' Guild spins the certified cables — tow lines, anchor hawsers, the famous Funis space-ropes that every yard from Craft to the deep stations specs by breaking strain — in great ropewalks stretched along the temperate giant's orbit, where the guild's walkers lay the long strands the old way, by hand and eye, because the League's fatigue tests, the guild notes, *prefer it.* The custom is the Lay: each walker names the rope they lay, and the names are kept in the walk's ledger, so that a snapped hawser is traced, filed, and remembered as a person would be. The grievance is synthetics — Freeport polymer line, cheaper by the coil, and the guild's annual demonstration of what each kind does under a live load is held, pointedly, in view of the Freeport agents' balconies.
## Via

- **Settled:** 2332 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1065113754394291584, brown dwarf, 90.39 ly
- **Population:** 1.0 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks — 4.6 and 3.1 Earth masses, both furnace-class — a bare locked stone, and one plain ice giant. The certified survey says, in full: *system fit for purpose; purpose incidental.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 853568610563444224, Gaia EDR3 1066086650386220288, and Gaia EDR3 1043287486510907264; Mesh gates to Funis's star, Gaia EDR3 1127267119488142464, and Gaia EDR3 1119768102293659136.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2334, for the seat of the Paviors' Guild: *Via. A road. Certified apt.*

Via is where the League builds its roads — the orbital works, gate approaches, moorages, and ferry cradles that every other Meridian system stands on. The Paviors' central yard, hung at the ember star with six routes' worth of work going out of it, is the League's largest industrial school, and the settlement's population is the guild's working mass and their families, a town of tamps, trunnels, and keel-blocks writ orbital. The custom is the Setting: each new structure's first keel-block is placed by the newest apprentice, formally, in full kit, because *every road begins under somebody's foot.* The grievance is maintenance backlogs, which the guild files as a single annual number and the Assembly files, historically, as next year's problem — a difference of bookkeeping the Paviors have called, in their own publication, *the League's only unbridgeable span.*
## Candela

- **Settled:** 2335 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1127267119488142464, brown dwarf, 88.48 ly
- **Population:** 360,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and three plain ice giants — little to look at, by lamplight or otherwise.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1136496317013163392 and Gaia EDR3 1119768102293659136; Mesh gates to Via's star and Gaia EDR3 1119989933060503680.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2337, for the seat of the Chandlers' Guild: *Candela. A lamp. Certified apt.*

Candela lights the League's rooms. The Chandlers make the certified luminaires — station lamps, navigation candles, the wick-and-photon lanterns that Meridian sentiment keeps in every hall — and the settlement's four routes ship them everywhere the League keeps a threshold. The custom is the Lamplighting: no Chandela lamp leaves the works unlit once, for one minute, in the dark of the proving hall, so that *no light leaves here untested.* The grievance is Radiance, whose bulk power contracts have squeezed the guild's margins for a decade, and whose offer to buy the Chandlers outright was answered by the guild's shortest filing: *the light is not for sale.*
## Panis

- **Settled:** 2338 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1136496317013163392, brown dwarf, 88.34 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary holding a locked puffed ice giant at 454 K, a locked airy rock at 347 K, and a bare locked stone — the certified survey grades the system *compact.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 29761 and Candela's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 553702339077648512, Gaia EDR3 498708306508914048, and Gaia EDR3 552434022352148864.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2340, for the seat of the Bakers' Guild: *Panis. Bread. Certified apt.*

Panis is the League's oven. The Bakers' Guild runs the great proving bakeries where Arva's grain and the quarter's sourdough libraries are turned into the certified loaves, hardtack, and ship biscuit that stock every Meridian larder and half the mesh's, and the settlement's air is famous — visitors claim to identify the system blind by the smell of the works alone, a claim the guild has encouraged, measured, and certified as *apt.* The custom is the First Loaf: each apprentice's certification is a single bread, judged whole, in public, by the working bakers of the entire yard, and the marking is silent. The grievance is yeast licensing — the sourdough libraries are ancient, proprietary to their founding lines, and the guild's internal fights over culture custody are the most passionate procedural disputes in the League, watched with delight by every other polity's lawyers.
## Medicus

- **Settled:** 2341 (slip-delivered, guild charter).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-553702339077648512, M, 82.04 ly
- **Population:** 5.7 million
- **System:** locked furnace rocks inward, then **Medicus** (the third world), a temperate airy planet at 298 K with one large moon — herb country — and a giant-hung outer system: two captured-locked-moon gas giants and a 367-Earth-mass colossus at the rim.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 25110 and Panis's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1152643332462130176 and Gaia EDR3 552434022352148864.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2343, for the seat of the Physicians' Guild: *Medicus. A physician. Certified apt.*

Medicus is the League's sickbay. The temperate world's herb terraces, greenhouses, and the great teaching hospitals of the Physicians' Guild serve every Meridian route's health — the certified dispensaries, the quarantine standards, the epidemic-watch office whose quiet green bulletins every port in the quarter carries — and the guild's exchange with San Rafael's Healer's Houses, two systems of medicine trading cases across the border like rival universities, is the quarter's best-loved institutional friendship. The custom is the Call: every hospital keeps its day's admissions posted in the lobby, by first name only, so that *no patient is a room number and no illness is a stranger.* The grievance is coverage — the routes the Assembly keeps opening faster than the guild can certify doctors, and the Physicians' annual map of underserved stations, colored red, is the most effective budgetary document the League produces, because nobody argues with it twice.
## San Eloy

- **Settled:** 2344 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1152643332462130176, M, 91.78 ly
- **Population:** 880,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks — the heaviest of them a 6.7-Earth-mass stone with a moon, at 226 K — and airy mooned stones beyond: a system the chapters took for the metal in the heavy worlds.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 572487740053132288; Mesh gates to Medicus's star, HIP 107038, and Gaia EDR3 572320030170942848.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *San Eloy* — Saint Eligius, the smith-bishop — for the Commonwealth's western metalworks. The Chapter Register's line: *the hammer is also a prayer when it feeds people.*

San Eloy is the Commonwealth's anvil. The works companies quarry the heavy locked world in slow orbits and run the foundries that supply the chapters' own — bells, hinges, hull plate, and the famous Eloy steel of which half the quarter's tools are made and all the Commonwealth's are — and the settlement is a foundry town with a church at its center, as chapter towns are. The custom is the Blessing of the Tools: each quarter-day, the works pause while every tool in the yards is touched to the anvil-shrine once, a rite the brothers maintain *not for the iron's sake, which is already good, but for everyone else's.* The grievance is competition by night — Freeport hull-breakers undercutting the works with salvage prices, which the brothers answer by leaving the church porch light on at the yards: anyone who brings in a broken tool at any hour gets it mended free and asked nothing, a policy whose cost line the Chapter Register publishes and whose effect the Register does not need to.
## El Faro

- **Settled:** 2345 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-572487740053132288, white dwarf (T=14,927 K), 88.40 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** a ferociously bright white dwarf over locked rocks, a 236-Earth-mass giant, heavy airy super-earths at the cold distance, and a four-mooned ice giant — a system chosen for its glare.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 539145737136323200, San Eloy's star, HIP 107038, and Gaia EDR3 555628309789617536.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *El Faro* — the lighthouse — with the Chapter Register's shortest dedication: *the dead star still gives light; so will we.*

El Faro is the Commonwealth's western beacon. The keepers' chapter tends the great approach works on the white dwarf's hard white glare — the signal stations, the pilgrim waymarks, the rescue hulks cached at the anchor — and every westbound convoy in the Commonwealth's quarter passes this light and its keepers' famous hospitality, which extends the Rule of the Porch: a lamp at every approach, a meal at every dock, and no questions except *where next.* The population is keepers' families and the works that maintain a lighthouse writ large, and their custom is the Watch of the Dead Star: the light itself is monitored by hand at every bell, the ledger of its brightness kept continuously since settlement, in the keepers' plain faith that *what we watch, we keep.* The grievance is traffic's little sins — the Freeport hulls that cut the approach lanes at speed with their running lights off, whose registrations the keepers log, publish, and send politely forward to Flagfall, where, the brothers note, the fines are *filed under the only miracle we are licensed to expect.*
## Viña

- **Settled:** 2346 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-539145737136323200, M, 84.50 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked rocks — one of them a heavy 7.4-Earth-mass stone at 274 K, airless and terraced — a 128-Earth-mass giant with two vineyard moons, and cold airy ice beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to El Faro's star, Gaia EDR3 537194035279352064, and 38 Cas; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 545367014444503296.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Viña* — the vine — for the Commonwealth's sacramental and table vineyards. The Chapter Register: *first the wine, then everything else; it has ever been so.*

Viña is the Commonwealth's vineyard. The chapters grow the altar wine of the whole Commonwealth here — every chalice from Sol's embassies to the deep chapels is filled from these moons — and the table vintage that makes the Commonwealth's guest houses famous, on terraces the brothers have cut into two vineyard moons and the heavy stone's sheltered vales, under a growing science the orders pursue with the patience of people whose product is judged by eternity and whose customers are everyone. The custom is the Blessing of the Must: the first pressing of every year is blessed at every chapel simultaneously, and the must is sent by the cupful to every parish in the Commonwealth, so that, the brothers say, *no table is too far from the vine.* The grievance is frost futures — the cold years that take a whole vintage, which the Freeports' brokers will happily insure at rates the Chapter Register has described, in its one published joke, as *usury wearing a stole.*
## Shin-Saga

- **Settled:** 2243 (Nikkei covenant charter, Time of Troubles emigration wave); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-545367014444503296, M, 73.96 ly
- **Population:** 570,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked airy rocks, a mooned ice giant, and a three-mooned ice giant — the covenant's survey called it *a hearth with good stone around it.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 492053585402171264 and Gaia EDR3 542265876259135232; Mesh gates to HIP 7339 and Viña's star.
- **Registry name:** the Aurora Compact names its systems by founding covenant, and this one's charter families — fisherfolk and boatwrights of the old Kyushu coast — named theirs for the ancestral province: *Shin-Saga*, New Saga. The covenant register keeps the name beside the original town names of every charter family, a two-column page the settlement reads aloud at each founding anniversary.

Shin-Saga is one of the Aurora Compact's covenant towns — the association of Nikkei settlements that left the Union during the Time of Troubles, when the language reforms and the developmental statutes made the old ways of their households legally impossible, and that rebuilt, in the ember quarter, a life they keep by covenant rather than by coercion. The settlement is modest and proud: boat-yards (the Covenant hull tradition is the Compact's most sought craft), the shrine-theater where the old festivals keep their calendar, and the restoration workshops whose ceramics and textile work museums across the League commission by waiting list. The Compact's famous archive culture is here at small scale — the covenant school keeps its recorded language and rites by chain of custody, pre-Partition recordings maintained like title, like the League trusts everything: by lineage.

The custom every visitor learns is the doorway: no one leaves a Shin-Saga station, hull, or house without *itte kimasu* answered by *itte rasshai* — the departure call and the return-call, said at every airlock on the settlement, by rule of nothing but two hundred years of everyone doing it. The grievance is internal and gentle: the covenant school's older teachers, who correct the accent of Compact children raised in mixed households, and the young families who reply — in the assembly, in the language, politely — that a living tongue is not a recording. The argument is the Compact's own, held in its own assembly, in its own words, and both sides would have it no other way.
## Teja

- **Settled:** 2347 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-532057254394731776 (HIP 7339), K, 68.65 ly
- **Population:** 490,000
- **System:** a hot K sun over heavy airy furnace rocks (0.57 and 6.07 Earth masses) and a mooned temperate pebble — clay, fire, and gravity, which is the whole recipe.
- **Links:** Regional gate to GJ 3040; Mesh gates to HIP 4151, Gaia EDR3 492053585402171264, and Shin-Saga's star.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Teja* — the roof tile — for the Commonwealth's tileworks and kilns. The Chapter Register's line: *a roof is a sermon that keeps the rain off the congregation.*

Teja roofs the Commonwealth. The chapters' great kilns work the hot rocks' fire and the heavy stone's clay, and the tiled roofs of every chapter town from here to the deep quarter are laid in Teja's red — a color the settlement controls by covenant, keeping the recipe to a single recorded formula so that *every roof of the Commonwealth matches, as every roof should.* The population is kiln families, glazers, and the hauling crews of four routes, and their custom is the First Fire: each kiln's first firing each year is done by the oldest and youngest worker together, in silence, because the brothers say the work began before them and continues after them. The grievance is the Freeport catalogues' "chapter-style" tiles, whose color the brothers have compared, in a published open letter, to *a homily translated by someone who was not listening.*
## Buen Pastor

- **Settled:** 2348 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-427322415301550208 (HIP 4151), G, 61.66 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a big bright G sun, a heavy hot airy rock, a mooned temperate pebble, and the cold steppe world — airy, two-mooned, at 227 K — where the flocks graze; beyond, an ice giant, a captured locked companion, and a 452-Earth-mass colossus whose far orbit keeps the system's slow clock.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 2281046483684995840 and HIP 114924; Mesh gates to Teja's star, GJ 1015 A, and GJ 1015 B.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Buen Pastor* — the Good Shepherd — for the Commonwealth's flocks and the pasture world that carries them. The Chapter Register: *we asked for grass; the system provided a sky as well; both are used.*

Buen Pastor is the Commonwealth's pasture. The steppe world's long airy plains — cold, wind-scoured, and green where the chapter agronomists made them so — carry the herds whose wool clothes the Commonwealth, whose milk and cheese stock its larders, and whose spring lambing season is, informally but immovably, the quarter's calendar: San José's works schedule, Viña's vineyard labor, and three other systems' hiring fairs all key to the Pasto's lambing bulletin, published by the shepherds' house with ecclesiast calm. The population is shepherd families, wool works, and the route crews of a five-gate junction, and their custom is the Count of the Cold: every flock is counted by hand at each storm's end, every loss recorded by name — for the rams and ewes of the foundation lines have names, and the brothers' flock ledgers read like parochial registers.

The grievance is the dogs — not the settlement's own, which are beloved, but the licensed wolf-deterrence drones whose schedules the shepherds and the Commonwealth's ecology office have argued over, session by session, for forty years, in the politest file in the League's arbitration docket.
## Betania

- **Settled:** 2349 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1995681125599758592 (HIP 114924), G, 67.22 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** a hot G sun holding a furnace-class gas giant (711 K), a captured locked companion, a heavy airy rock, and a small hot ocean world — a system whose chief resource is its roads.
- **Links:** six gates — Regional to Buen Pastor's star; Mesh to Gaia EDR3 2173367633501430272, HIP 115331, HIP 116613, Gaia EDR3 2014924125275395840, and Gaia EDR3 1943493115426301312.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Betania* — Bethany, the house at the crossroads where the road-weary were fed — for the Commonwealth's guest-house hub at the meeting of six roads. The Chapter Register: *we are the inn; the roads chose the site.*

Betania is the Commonwealth's inn. Six routes' traffic meets here, and the chapters built the system's whole life around the door: the great guest-houses of the anchor ring — kitchens, dormitories, chapels, and the famous courtyard markets where traders of every polity set up stalls under the brothers' awnings — plus the wool, tile, and wine brokerages that make Betania the Commonwealth's busiest trade floor after Amity's. The settlement's population is innkeeping families, market clerks, and route crews, and their Rule of the Door is identical to San Cristóbal's hospitality, practiced at triple the volume: no one is turned away unfed, and the guest ledgers, published daily, record arrivals by port of origin — a census of the whole quarter's traffic, kept in bed-counts.

The custom is the Washing: at each shift change, the guest-house masters wash the entry thresholds of the great halls by hand, a humility the chapters practice in public and explain with the founding rule — *the door is the face of the house, and faces are washed.* The grievance is the Freeport hauliers, who run their rest-stop packets through Betania's markets at hours the brothers consider inhuman and the brokers consider profitable, a difference the guest ledgers record without comment, in the hour column.
## Semilla

- **Settled:** 2350 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2173367633501430272, brown dwarf, 59.29 ly
- **Population:** 310,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, mooned ice worlds, and a long cold procession of ice giants with one, three, four, five, and six moons — the cold ladder the chapters wanted.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 106557; Mesh gates to HIP 100017, Betania's star, and GJ 834 A.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Semilla* — the seed — for the Commonwealth's great vault: the living library of every crop, vine, herd-line, and heritage plant the chapters carry. The Chapter Register's line: *we keep what we were given.*

Semilla keeps the Commonwealth's inheritance. The vaults, cut into the cold moons of the outer giants, hold the seed lines of every chapter farm, vineyard, and flock — and, by treaty deposit, the heritage crops of a dozen other polities, including the Union's own relict varieties, entrusted here because the brothers' chain of custody is the most trusted in the quarter. The settlement is vault-keepers, trial-field agronomists, and the ice-haulers who feed the cold, and its custom is the Sowing of the Worst Year: each year's hardest-season seed, the lines that survived the bad harvests, are planted in the vault fields with the most care, because, the brothers teach, *the seed that survived is the one with the story.* The grievance is quiet and belongs to no one: the vaults' oldest deposits — pre-Cataclysm lines from Earth's lost hemispheres — grow in trial fields that no living person has ever eaten from, and the Chapter Register's single note on the matter says only: *kept.*
## Stella Maris

- **Settled:** 2351 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2246581329639103872 (HIP 100017), G, 57.01 ly
- **Population:** 21 million
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over mooned furnace rocks, then the gift: **Stella Maris** (the third world), a cool ocean planet at 277 K — 62% of its face under green temperate sea, one large moon, air you can breathe with a coat on — and a warm sister ocean world behind it, with airy ice worlds to the far rim.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 2281046483684995840, HIP 106557, and HIP 95995; Mesh gates to Semilla's star and θ Cyg.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Stella Maris* — Star of the Sea, the ancient title for the one who watches over sailors — for the first true ocean-garden the chapters were given. The Chapter Register: *the sea asked; we answered.*

Stella Maris is the Commonwealth's beloved world. Its cool green seas — real seasons, real tides under the one patient moon, real weather the settlement argues about with affection — feed the Commonwealth's tables and anchor its imagination: the floating cathedral of Nuestra Señora rides the equatorial current on the same moorings the founding chapter drove, the fleet towns string the dusk-lit archipelagos, and the seminarians of San Alberto and the pilgrims of five routes make the passage because a world this generous asks to be thanked in person.
