The settlement's economy is the sea's: fisheries under chapter rotation, sea-farms, boatyards building the Covenant-style hulls the quarter buys, and the hospitality of a shrine town that has learned to be a port.

The custom is the Blessing of the Fleet: at the season's first sail, every hull in the system — fishing boat, freighter, or ferry — passes the floating cathedral once, bow first, and receives the same words, because the sea grades no ranks. The grievance is drift: Silverfin's far trawlers and the odd Flagfall hull cross the chapter's rotation lines in the bad seasons, and the brothers' boundary filings — precise, patient, annotated with the parable of the loaves and the fishes — are already legendary at the arbitration circuit, which cites them, in seminar, as the gold standard of the genre.
## Pergamino

- **Settled:** 2352 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2238597054155896832 (HIP 95995), K, 59.03 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** a K sun over locked and heavy hot rocks — one 4.4-Earth-mass airy stone with a captured companion — a bare cold super-earth, and a 320-Earth-mass three-mooned giant.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Stella Maris's star, θ Cyg, and Gaia EDR3 1421042126620559104; Mesh gate to 16 Cyg A.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Pergamino* — parchment — for the Commonwealth's press: the breviaries, hymnals, and Chapter Registers of every settlement set in type here. The Register's line: *the word outlasts the voice; the page outlasts the word; keep the page.*

Pergamino prints the Commonwealth's books. The great house — scriptoria, type-foundries, and the paper-mills that turn Buen Pastor's wool-waste and the steppe grasses into the quarter's best archival paper — produces the breviaries the whole League's parishes use, the schoolbooks of the chapters' academies, and the continuous printed record of the Chapter Register itself, of which no electronic copy is authoritative, by rule, because, the brothers say plainly, *paper has never once asked for a subscription.* The custom is the Collation: every print run's first finished book is read aloud, cover to copyright, by the entire house — authors, compositors, and sweepers — before it may ship. The grievance is Freeport reprints of the hymnals, whose typesetting the brothers have analyzed, line by line, in a published errata sheet titled, with the Register's one permitted sigh, *the Third Commandment, typeset.*
## Cirio

- **Settled:** 2353 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1421042126620559104, brown dwarf, 63.34 ly
- **Population:** 240,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a small hot locked ocean world, mooned ice, and a ladder of one-, two-, and three-mooned ice giants — quiet stone, good cold.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Pergamino's star and HIP 83996; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1432442825250178944 and Gaia EDR3 1417872268957468288.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Cirio* — the great candle — for the Commonwealth's waxworks: every altar candle from here to the deep chapels is poured, wick, and smoke of these halls. The Chapter Register: *light is a craft before it is a metaphor.*

Cirio is the Commonwealth's candle-house. The waxworks pour the certified altar candles of half the League's parishes — beeswax from Buen Pastor's steppe apiaries, wick from Funis-grade flax, smoke tested by the brothers' organoleptic standard, which is a form everyone fills out with a straight face and a lit candle. The settlement is small, devotional, and craft-proud, and its custom is the Long Night: once a year the whole system lights only candle-light, every grid and every lamp dark for one vigil, in memory of the severed years, when the chapters' candles were the only light that could not be cut off from them. The grievance is the perennial one of every wax-seller to every treasurer — the candles cost what they cost, and the chapter treasurers' annual gasp is itself an annual custom, expected, cherished, and ignored.
## Órgano

- **Settled:** 2354 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1419999819661235840 (HIP 83996), K, 69.30 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over a locked furnace-class giant (754 K — the works' forge), a locked heavy mooned rock, and airy cold stones out to the rim.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Cirio's star, Gaia EDR3 1432442825250178944, and Gaia EDR3 1417872268957468288; Mesh gate to HIP 83451.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Órgano* — the organ — for the workshops that build the Commonwealth's instruments: the great pipe organs of Compline's hall, Stella Maris's floating cathedral, and half the League's chapels. The Register's line: *the breath of the congregation, given pipes.*

Órgano is the Commonwealth's music works. The workshops cast, voice, and tune by hand — the tin and lead of the pipes from San Eloy's own metals, the wind by the same physics as every other theology — and the settlement's population of pipe-fitters, voicers, and their families measure civic time in commissionings: an Órgano organ leaving the works is a public event, blessed at the dock, its first chord struck in transit rehearsal so the system hears it go. The custom is the Voicing Night: each finished organ plays one full night alone, in the empty works, before shipping — the crews listening in the dark, by tradition, not watching, because the last ear an organ must satisfy, the brothers say, is the room. The grievance is the synthesizer — the Freeports' electronic substitutes, cheap and in tune, whose recordings the workshops' apprentices are forbidden to hear *while under contract, and do.*
## Misión

- **Settled:** 2355 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1437124683199761920 (HIP 83451), K, 81.59 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a K sun over a locked heavy mooned rock, bare mooned pebbles, and a two-mooned heavy stone at 224 K — a waypoint system, not a dwelling one.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1417872268957468288 and HIP 82020; Mesh gates to Órgano's star, Gaia EDR3 1432442825250178944, Gaia EDR3 1637446836968370176, and Athebyne.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Misión* — the sending — for the Commonwealth's dispatch house: the port from which the deep-quarter chapels are founded, supplied, and visited. The Chapter Register: *we are not a station; we are a departure.*

Misión is the Commonwealth's far door. Every new chapter founded in the outer systems stages from here — the founding families, the mission kits (a chapel-in-a-hull of Pergamino books, Cirio candles, an Órgano positive organ), and the letters patent the Register seals with its oldest stamp — and every visitation of every far chapel is provisioned and scheduled from the same halls. The settlement's population is dispatch families, port chaplains, and the famous mission school where lay volunteers of every polity learn the Commonwealth's practical curriculum: first aid, water, bread, and listening, in that order and with equal examinations. The custom is the Sending: each departing mission is walked to its dock by the whole settlement, singing, and the last thing set aboard, after every crate, is a stone from the dispatch hall's doorstep, because *every chapel should stand on the rock it was sent from.* The grievance is distance — the far chapels' supply schedule is calculated, argued, and exceeded every year, and the dispatch house's annual report on the shortfall is titled, with the Register's driest blessing, *Loaves, Fishes, Budget.*
## Santa Lucía

- **Settled:** 2356 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1430517198137007872 (HIP 82020), F, 87.43 ly
- **Population:** 3.4 million
- **System:** a fierce bright F sun (6.8 solar luminosities) over a furnace giant with a captured companion, a heavy hot airy rock, a bare temperate stone — and then the gift: a cool ocean world at 279 K, 63% of its face under sea, one moon, in the wide bright orbit where the light is generous but kind.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Misión's star and Athebyne; Mesh gates to 21 Dra B and HIP 84062.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Santa Lucía* — Saint Lucy, the light — for the Commonwealth's college of the stars: the bright sun, the long nights of the far orbit, and the seeing that only this sky gives. The Register: *we asked for light; we received a library.*

Santa Lucía is the Commonwealth's observatory. The ocean world's sea-college — halls built on the calm water where the atmosphere is steadiest, instruments on the one moon's far side where there is none — is the quarter's finest school of astronomy, and its catalogues of the outer sky, the slip-space surveys, and the ancient light are standard reference from San Alberto's classes to the Lyra Compact's deep stations. The settlement is scholars, sea-farmers, and the great instrument works that grew up around the college: the mirror halls grinding under the F sun's patient fire, in partnership with the Meridian glassmakers, whose guild the brothers address, in joint publications, as *colleagues, at certified length.*

The custom is the Night Office of the Stars: on the world's long clear nights, the college keeps an open tower where anyone — student, fisher, visitor of any polity — may look through the great refractor and say what they saw into the tower's visitor book, which is now two centuries of the quarter's handwriting and wonder. The grievance is the moon: its far side is perfect for instruments and its near side is where the college's families live, and the ferry schedule between the two, the brothers have certified, is *a test of vocation.*
## San Andrés

- **Settled:** 2357 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1420101696285626624 (21 Draconis B, binary G+M), 88.65 ly
- **Population:** 7.6 million
- **System:** the G primary keeps hot rocks and two steam ocean worlds; the small M companion, half a light-hour behind, keeps the gifts: a warm ocean world at 304 K, 83% of its face under sea, and a cool airy two-worlds-out rock at 244 K where the herds graze.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Santa Lucía's star and Gaia EDR3 1368942936370154240; Regional gates to HIP 85268 and HIP 84062.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *San Andrés* — Saint Andrew, the fisherman, the first called — for the warm sea world that feeds a people. The Register: *the sea asked; we answered, again; the Sea seems fond of asking.*

San Andrés is the Commonwealth's western fishery. The warm world's shallow, moonless seas run rich with the chapter aquaculture — fish, shell, and the long kelp lines whose harvest stocks the Commonwealth's far chapels and half the mesh's markets — and the cool rock behind it carries the herds that turn fish-money into wool. The settlement is fleet towns on the warm sea and shepherding villages on the cool rock, twinned by a ferry schedule the brothers run with the calm of people who own both halves of the argument. The system's best thing is preservation: the smoke-houses and salt-cellars of San Andrés wrote the quarter's fish-curing standard, and every route's cargo of Rete's and Stella Maris's catch is graded by it.

The custom is the First Call: each fleet's first catch of the season is shared, boat by boat, with the households that had the hardest winter — a distribution the fleet captains make by judgment, not formula, and defend the same way. The grievance is the warm sea's occasional red tide, which the chapter ecologists have studied for forty years and the Register describes, with the patience of the whole Commonwealth, as *the sea exercising its right to a sabbath, unadvisedly taken all at once.*
## Sinodo

- **Settled:** 2358 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1368942936370154240, M, 87.60 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks — the third of them, **Sinodo** proper, a cool airy dusk world at 255 K — with heavy airy stones and a long cold tail of mooned ice giants out to 9 au.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1417872268957468288, HIP 85268, and HIP 89087; Mesh gates to San Andrés's star, Gaia EDR3 2151176602616711552, and Gaia EDR3 2123432247756223488.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Sinodo* — the synod — for the assembly seat of the ember-quarter chapters: the system where the Commonwealth's western settlements gather to argue, agree, and eat. The Register: *a synod is a village that argues until it is a family.*

Sinodo is the ember west's meeting house. Six routes converge here, and the chapters built the synod halls on the cool dusk world to receive them — the great circular debate hall at the center of the settlement, the guest quarters that have never once been empty, and the market rings that do the quiet work of a hundred settlements' surplus. The synod's sessions, held each season, govern the ember-quarter chapters' shared causes: water law with the Weighers, school standards, the mission schedules, and the long ceremonial argument with Sol that the Register records under *relations, ecumenical and otherwise.* The population is hall families, market keepers, and the lawyers of a polity that argues as liturgy.

The custom is the Round Table: every session opens with all delegates eating together from one shared pot, cooked by whichever chapter's harvest was worst that year — so that the synod begins with the year's hardest field on every tongue. The grievance is the pot itself, which rotates by chapter in an order fixed in 2361 and disputed, annually, with a passion out of all proportion that the Register finds, on the record, *edifying.*
## Descanso

- **Settled:** 2359 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2123090918115438592 (HIP 89087), M, 90.11 ly
- **Population:** 920,000
- **System:** a bright M star over mooned furnace rocks, a bare temperate stone, and two great giants — 278 and 277 Earth masses, carrying ten moons between them — with a cold captured family trailing out to 98 au.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Sinodo's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2153089026998231168, HIP 92549, HIP 91949, and Gaia EDR3 2123432247756223488.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Descanso* — the rest — for the Commonwealth's houses of rest: where the old, the worn, and the convalescent of the chapters come to live slowly. The Register: *the work is long; the workers must be also.*

Descanso is the Commonwealth's porch swing. The rest houses of the mooned giants' calm orbits — orchards under the bright M light, the famous slow kitchens, the infirmaries of the mercy tradition run at walking pace — receive the chapters' old crews, the tired, and anyone whose doctor has prescribed gravity and kindness together, and the settlement's whole economy is the gentle one: gardens, kitchens, record-keeping for those who wish their stories kept, and the children's visits that every house schedules as medicine. The custom is the Long Walk: each resident who is able walks the orchard ring at their own pace, and no one overtakes anyone, by rule — because, the brothers say, *the pace of the slowest is the speed of the house.* The grievance is funding, as ever for mercy's works, and the brothers' annual budget letter is the Commonwealth's most reprinted homily.
## Cuna

- **Settled:** 2360 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2153089026998231168, M, 83.70 ly
- **Population:** 3.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked furnace rocks and hot seas, then the settled world — a cool ocean planet at 279 K, three-quarters of its face under water, one moon — with a 99-Earth-mass two-mooned giant behind it.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 2143386180480200064, HIP 92549, and Gaia EDR3 2151176602616711552; Mesh gates to Descanso's star and Gaia EDR3 2237479125708758784.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Cuna* — the cradle — for the Commonwealth's children's world: the schools, the orphan-houses of the mercy tradition, and the sea they grow beside. The Register's line is the one every Cunan child learns before any other: *here, first, one is young.*

Cuna is where the Commonwealth raises its children. The chapter schools of the sea world — the great campus-harbor towns, the children's houses that receive orphans and foundlings of every polity with the same open ledger, the farm-and-fleet apprenticeships that end in a trade — are the polity's proudest work and its largest: a full third of the Commonwealth's teachers live here, and the settlement's calendar is a school calendar, its holidays real holidays, its seas full of training boats. The world's best thing is the transition: Cuna's graduates step into every chapter trade with the same certification the guild polities give their own, and the Meridian Committee has, twice, examined Cuna's examinations and certified them apt, which the Register reprints with pride in every edition.

The custom is the Fleet Regatta: each year the children's boats race the great harbor course, and the winning boat is retired to the harbor church roof, where the older boats — a century of them — sit gray and patient under the sky, the shape of everyone the world has raised. The grievance is shoes: the children refuse them, the teachers require them, and the Register has recorded the treaty — *shoes aboard, bare ashore* — as binding since 2364, with the footnote that both sides consider it a victory.
## Jardín

- **Settled:** 2361 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2143386180480200064, M, 77.57 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over heavy locked rocks — the second of them, **Jardín** proper, a hot-dusk airy world at 303 K — and a bare cold stone; small, warm, and by the chapters' account, *shaped like an invitation.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to 16 Cyg A, Gaia EDR3 2237479125708758784, and Cuna's star; Mesh gates to HIP 102101 and HIP 98123.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Jardín* — the garden — for the spice and herb terraces of the hot dusk belt, whose harvests season and medicine half the quarter's tables. The Register: *we were given a warm wall; we planted it.*

Jardín is the Commonwealth's kitchen garden. The hot dusk belt's terraced slopes — spice orchards, herb fields, the pepper vines that no one else in the quarter grows with such patience — supply the chapters' pharmacopeia and their tables alike, and the settlement is a world of agronomist-monks, farm families, and the drying halls whose scent arriving crews report before the harbor lights. The custom is the First Taste: each harvest is tasted in public at the drying-hall door, the brothers recording the year's flavor in the Register's most poetic pages — which botanists cite and theologians tolerate. The grievance is the Freeport "garden blends," whose contents the brothers have offered, in an open letter, to identify blind by taste alone, a challenge standing unaccepted for eleven years.
## Bosque

- **Settled:** 2362 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2187263158355763072 (HIP 102101), M, 80.89 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a bright M star over locked furnace rocks, then the settled world — a cool airy planet at 260 K with two moons, given to forest — and a giant-heavy tail: three vast gas giants and mooned ice to the rim.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2168280502430898944, Jardín's star, and HIP 98123; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 2237479125708758784.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Bosque* — the forest — for the timber world: the Commonwealth's groves, sawmills, and the long silviculture the brothers practice like liturgy. The Register: *a tree is a slow sermon.*

Bosque is the Commonwealth's woodlot. The cool world's engineered forests — grove-cathedrals of the great timber pines, the coppices, the paper groves Pergamino contracts by the generation — are cut on a rotation the brothers defend with more certainty than any doctrine, and the sawmill towns under the two moons keep the quarter's finest joinery trade. The custom is the Standing Count: every tree felled is replaced the same year, in the same grove, by hand, and the count of hands is the Register's — because, the brothers say, *the forest keeps books, and its memory is longer than ours.* The grievance is the Freeport timber factors, who bid for standing groves every year and are refused every year, with the same courteous form letter, which the factors now collect.
## Cantera

- **Settled:** 2363 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2168280502430898944, M, 75.08 ly
- **Population:** 360,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, a bare temperate stone, and a chain of ice giants — a system the Register grades, with mason's brevity, *load-bearing.*
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 101382; Mesh gates to 16 Cyg A, Bosque's star, and Gaia EDR3 2082248214375313792.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Cantera* — the quarry — for the marble and granite that build the Commonwealth's halls. The Register: *a cathedral is a mountain that decided to come inside.*

Cantera is the Commonwealth's stone-yard. The quarry orders work the locked heavy rocks and the bare temperate stone in slow, lawful cuts, and the great sawing halls shape the Commonwealth's marble — the white of Sinodo's hall, the green of Stella Maris's cathedral steps, the gray of every chapter-house threshold in the quarter. The settlement's population is mason families, and their custom is the Dedication Cut: each quarry's first block each year is cut for a purpose chosen by lot among the Commonwealth's building funds, so that every quarry keeps a hand in every hall. The grievance is weight — the tariff schedules price stone by mass, and the brothers' annual reminder that *the good stone is heavy, which is why it is good,* has become the freight guilds' standing joke, retold at every rate hearing with affection and no effect.
## Fuente

- **Settled:** 2364 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2067948245320365184 (HIP 101382), K, 71.29 ly
- **Population:** 710,000
- **System:** a K sun over locked heavy furnace rocks, a hot airy stone, a mooned cold rock — a dry system, which is precisely why the chapters came.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Cantera's star and Enduri Senggu; Mesh gates to HIP 98921, Gaia EDR3 1873146567646378880, and Gaia EDR3 2074506110651830784.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Fuente* — the fountain — for the Commonwealth's water works: the purification halls, the ice-haulers, the deep-well brotherhoods that keep every chapter town in the dry quarter drinking. The Register: *whoever gives water, gives.*

Fuente is the Commonwealth's cistern. The brothers' water guilds run the system's whole economy — ice runs from the cold rock's captured comets, purification at the great halls, and the famous Fuente tariff tables that price water across half the quarter by purity, distance, and need, with the need column always reading *same.* The custom is the Fountain Duty: every settlement and station on the five routes keeps a public fountain, run and maintained from Fuente's ledgers, free at the tap to anyone — crews of any polity included — because, the brothers hold, *a thirsty stranger is not a market.* The grievance is arithmetic: the Freeports' economists publish, annually, a calculation of the Fountain Duty's lost revenue, and the brothers publish, annually, the same number under the heading *alms,* which ends the argument by refusing to begin it.
## Colmena

- **Settled:** 2365 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2061876742143320064 (HIP 98921), G, 62.09 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over mooned hot rocks, a cold bare two-mooned stone, and airy ice worlds to a four-mooned giant at the far rim — meadow country by the chapters' reckoning.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1862034696988864896; Mesh gates to θ Cyg, HIP 97223, 17 Cyg B, and Fuente's star.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Colmena* — the hive — for the Commonwealth's apiaries: the wax of Cirio's candles, the honey of every chapter table, and the pollination contracts of half the quarter's farms. The Register: *the bee is the smallest worker we have, and the only one who does not know it.*

Colmena is the Commonwealth's bee-yard. The hives of the cold stone's sheltered vales and the orbital pollen-gardens — engineered meadows turning under the G sun — keep the quarter's largest certified apiary, and the brotherhood of beekeepers runs the trade the way they keep everything: by ledger, by season, and by a patience the bees themselves seem to enforce. The custom is the First Swarm: each year's first swarm is hived by the youngest keeper, at dusk, bareheaded — the brothers insist the tradition is safe, the tradition being precisely that it usually is. The grievance is the imported pollinator, a Freeport-bred competitive drone whose patents the beekeepers refuse, whose invasions they intercept, and whose marketing copy — *pollination at scale* — the Register has annotated, in print, with the single word *ashes.*
## Tránsito

- **Settled:** 2366 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2032017717217569152 (HIP 97223), K, 68.22 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over a locked furnace rock, a hot mooned stone, then **Tránsito** proper, a cool airy world at 246 K with one moon — quiet country, chosen on purpose.
- **Links:** Regional gates to GJ 1241, 17 Cyg B, and Gaia EDR3 2038120006709791360; Mesh gates to Colmena's star and Gaia EDR3 2018474482692940288.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Tránsito* — the passage — for the Commonwealth's houses of the dying: the last hospice, where the sick of every polity come to be carried, not cured. The Register: *the last door deserves a doorkeeper with clean hands.*

Tránsito is the Commonwealth's last house. The hospices of the cool world — and the quiet-town economy that serves them: gardens, kitchens, the famous bell-choirs — receive the dying of half the quarter, of every faith and none, on the one condition the chapters have never amended: that they come as guests, not cases. The settlement's best thing is its plainness: no institution here outranks the person being carried, and the hospice rule that every guest is called by the name they answer to has produced the quarter's most moving archive — the Register's Book of Names, in which the brothers write each guest's name, in their own spelling, once.

The custom is the Passing Bell: when a guest dies, the bell rings once, all traffic holds one minute, and the roads stay silent — a courtesy extended by every polity's pilots without treaty, because everyone's crews have someone there. The grievance is gentle and unavoidable: the Freeports' insurance tables price hospice transfer to Tránsito as a luxury, and the brothers' annual letter on the subject is one sentence long, reprinted each year in the same typeface, in the same position, on the same page: *dying is not a tier.*
## Archivo

- **Settled:** 2367 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2018474482692940288, brown dwarf, 68.25 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary holding a tight ladder of locked rocks — the third of them, **Archivo** proper, a cool airy dusk world at 256 K — and a cold tail of mooned ice.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1821315795663331456 and HIP 93966; Mesh gates to Tránsito's star, HIP 98677, and Gaia EDR3 2038120006709791360.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Archivo* — the archive — for the vaults where the Commonwealth keeps its memory: the Chapter Register's originals, every founding covenant, the severed-years correspondence, and the guest books of every hospice and fountain in the quarter. The Register, keeping its own minute: *if we do not keep it, it was not kept.*

Archivo is the Commonwealth's memory. The vaults of the cool dusk world hold two centuries of paper in cold stone — parochial registers, the Book of Names' parent volumes, the treaties, the famine ledgers, the letters of the severed years that make even Freeport historians go quiet — and the archivist brothers who tend them serve a rule older than any polity in the quarter: nothing kept here is ever destroyed, and nothing kept here is ever removed. Scholars of every polity come; the reading hall is famous for its silence and its chairs. The custom is the Ledger of Arrivals: every document entering the vaults is carried in by hand, by its donor, walked the last corridor in procession — because, the archivists say, *what is given must be seen to be given.* The grievance is duplication — Meridian's Memory holds copies, and the two archives' annual exchange of identical letters affirming their mutual independence and mutual affection is the quarter's most ritualized paperwork, filed, naturally, in both.
## Telar

- **Settled:** 2368 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1807953155988203264 (HIP 98677), K, 62.82 ly
- **Population:** 2.1 million
- **System:** a K sun over locked furnace rocks and a heavy hot stone, then **Telar** proper, a cool airy world at 265 K with two moons — mill country — and a long cold tail of five-mooned ice giants.
- **Links:** Regional gates to GJ 1241 and Gaia EDR3 1821315795663331456; Mesh gates to Archivo's star, Phoenicia, and Gaia EDR3 4314878631298620160.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Telar* — the loom — for the Commonwealth's weaving works. The Register: *clothe the worker; the work follows.*

Telar is the Commonwealth's loom. The great mills of the cool world turn Buen Pastor's wool and Bosque's specialty fibers into the cloth every chapter wears — the gray habit-cloth the quarter knows on sight, the guest-house blankets, the vestment linens that Pergamino's artists then cover with ink — and the settlement is a mill-town system of weaver families, dyers (Jardín supplies), and the repair halls where the quarter's other polities send their irreplaceable textiles to be mended by brothers who have forgotten more about weave than most institutes learn. The custom is the First Thread: every bolt's first meter is woven by the mill's newest apprentice, and no bolt ships without that meter, imperfect or not, in it — because, the Register says, *every cloth remembers being a first day.* The grievance is the loom queue — half the quarter wants the brothers' cloth and the brothers' hours are finite, and the waiting-list ledger, published, is the only luxury Telar permits itself.
## Phoenicia

- **Settled:** 2369 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4224259562941398400 (Phoenicia), K, 64.02 ly
- **Population:** 790,000
- **System:** a K sun over hot airy rocks, a mooned cold pebble, a temperate airy stone, and a cold procession of three- and four-mooned ice giants — a system of little farmland and much deep cold, which is what the works wanted.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4293873732939569920, HIP 101540, and (via mesh) GJ 784.2 B; Mesh gate to Telar's star.
- **Registry name:** the ancient name was carried from Earth's astronomical tradition and kept by the founding chapter on the grounds that *the purple of the vestments should remember the purple of the sea.* The Register's dedication of the dye-works to the old traders' coast is the longest sentence in the book, and every apprentice dye-master is required to memorize it.

Phoenicia is the Commonwealth's dye-house. The great vats of the cold works — working the deep-sea chemistry and the vault-cold of the ice giants' moons — produce the famous chapter purple, the liturgical shades of half the League's vestments, and the fast dyes of Telar's habit-cloth, whose formulas the dye-masters keep as the trade's only secret, taught hand to hand and written nowhere. The settlement is small, stained to the elbows, and intensely proud; its custom is the Vat Blessing — each new vat is started with water carried from Stella Maris's sea, the works' founding symbol, and no exception has ever been recorded. The grievance is counterfeit purple — the Freeport cloth houses' synthetic vestment shades, whose spectrum the dye-masters have published side by side with the true color under the heading *the dye that does not remember the sea.*
## La Trappe

- **Settled:** 2370 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4233129014004310656 (HIP 101540), M, 68.78 ly
- **Population:** 310,000
- **System:** a dim M sun over a locked furnace super-earth, a hot bare pebble, and a locked temperate ice giant — a small retiny, cold at the edges, warm where the vats sit.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Phoenicia's star, Gaia EDR3 1736707482548494848, GJ 784.2 B, and HIP 101345.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *La Trappe* — after the old contemplative houses that prayed and brewed — for the Commonwealth's monastery breweries. The Register: *the cellar is a chapel of patience.*

La Trappe is the Commonwealth's cellar. The chapter breweries — working the temperate giant's steady warmth and the brothers' even steadier hands — brew the table beer of half the Commonwealth's houses and the famous strong ales of the guest-house trade, sold under the covenant that every liter's profits go to the works of mercy and every liter's recipe stays where it was born. The custom is the Tasting of the Council: each new batch is first tasted by the whole chapter, in silence, and the silence is the review — a tradition that settled a hundred brewery disputes and has never once produced a document. The grievance is none: the brothers file no complaints about tariffs, copies, or competition, and their competitors' annual attempts to locate the recipe vault have become, on La Trappe, a sport the novices bet milk on.
## Astillero

- **Settled:** 2371 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6904703228801028864 (HIP 101345), K, 80.68 ly
- **Population:** 870,000
- **System:** a bright K sun over heavy hot airy rocks, a captured locked companion, a steam ocean world, and — the system's own cathedral — an 831-Earth-mass colossus at 18 au whose four moons give the works their room.
- **Links:** Regional gates to La Trappe's star, Gaia EDR3 4221493122971602688, and Gaia EDR3 6906393659206977280; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 6879524790480960896.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Astillero* — the shipyard — for the Commonwealth's hull works. The Register: *a hull is a roof that travels.*

Astillero is the Commonwealth's yard. The great slipways of the colossus's moons lay the chapter fleet — hospital ships, mission hulls, the graceful grain-haulers the quarter knows by their sheer — built to a design rule the brothers defend like doctrine: *slow, strong, and repairable by whoever finds you.* Half the Commonwealth's hulls and more than a few other polities' hospital ships were laid here, and the yard's population of shipwright families keeps the custom of the Keel Stone: a stone from Cantera goes into every keel, because *every hull should remember being a mountain.* The grievance is naval envy — the Freeports' sleek freighters outsail the grain-haulers on every route, and the brothers' annual reply, unchanged since 2378, is that *the race is not to the swift, especially fully loaded.*
## Jabón

- **Settled:** 2372 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4221493122971602688, brown dwarf, 82.23 ly
- **Population:** 190,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a bare locked stone, and a short ladder of mooned ice giants — small, clean, and orderly, which the Register notes is *the whole mission.*
- **Links:** Regional gate to Astillero's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4206618517355209088, HIP 100133, and Gaia EDR3 4230348349098191232.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Jabón* — soap — for the works that keep the Commonwealth clean. The Register's line is the one every pilgrim's hospice keeps by the pump: *cleanliness is half of hospitality and the half you notice first.*

Jabón is the Commonwealth's soap-house. The works render, cut, and cure the plain white bars that stock every hospice, hospital, and chapter kitchen in the quarter — tallow from Buen Pastor, ash from Bosque's clearings, fragrance from Jardín's second-grade herbs — and the settlement is small, spotless, and serenely indispensable, in the way of things nobody writes songs about and everybody stands in line for. The custom is the Casting of the First Bar: each curing batch's first bar goes to whichever route's crews arrive first, free, so that no batch begins that is not first given away. The grievance is the perfumers of Bathgate, whose "chapter-style" soaps the brothers decline to litigate and simply refuse to use, which in the Commonwealth is the stronger ruling.
## Coldharbor

- **Settled:** 2373 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4206618517355209088, brown dwarf, 86.13 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked steam ocean, then the settled world — a cool locked ocean planet at 276 K, a third of its face under cold twilight sea — with ice and a four-mooned ice giant behind it.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4082791277319699072 and HD 184860B; Mesh gates to Jabón's star and Gaia EDR3 4207310419407283456.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2373, to the Deep Bay Combine, whose cold-sea shellfish parks needed a port name that would sell oysters at a distance. The auction minute: *sold; the name is the menu.*

Coldharbor sells the cold. The settled world's twilight sea — cold, clean, mineral — grows the quarter's famous oysters, clams, and cold-water kelp, and the Combine's fleet runs them fresh, chilled, and bonded to every rich table from Bathgate's spas to the League's guild halls, on a tariff schedule that charges by the hour of freshness. The settlement's population of park-keepers, haulers, and tariff clerks lives in harbor towns on the dusk shelf, and its custom is the Tasting Board: the day's catch is graded publicly at the harbor market, every bed's flavor scored in front of the buyers, because a Freeport that lies about an oyster is worth nothing. The grievance is the boundary squabble with Rete's certified fishers, whose dusk-sea grounds overlap the parks' genetic drift lines — a case the fishers file as * trespass* and the Combine as *commerce*, and the arbitration circuit as *dinner.*
## Breakwater

- **Settled:** 2375 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4082791277319699072, M, 85.96 ly
- **Population:** 2.8 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, then the settled world — a warm locked ocean planet at 320 K, three-quarters of its face under shallow twilight sea — with mooned ice and a ladder of giant, colder worlds behind.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Coldharbor's star, Gaia EDR3 6758930664392303232, GJ 781.3, and Gumala; Mesh gate to HIP 95417.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2375, to the Salvage & Steel Combine: *Breakwater* — the yard where old hulls come to die and be reborn as someone else's tonnage. The auction minute: *sold; the sea keeps what we cannot.*

Breakwater is the quarter's breakers' yard. The warm sea world's shore shelves make natural graving grounds, and the Combine's fleets beach, strip, and render the mesh's tired hulls — auctions by the ton, certification by the rivet, and a salvage-law tradition the Freeport courts have elevated almost to liturgy. Half the quarter's second-hand plate, second-hand fittings, and second-hand luck passes through Breakwater's customs, and the settlement's population — breakers, buyers, and the auction-house families — treats every arriving derelict as a cargo of stories to be priced individually. The custom is the Last Bell: each hull's working bell is removed before cutting and hung in the auction hall, and at each auction's close the day's bells are rung once — the yard's only ceremony, which visitors describe as unbearable and which the breakers describe as Tuesday. The grievance is Flagfall's registry, which occasionally certifies as "lost" a hull that later turns up on Breakwater's beaches whole, at which point everyone involved consults lawyers, and the lawyers, as the Combine's minute-books record, *eat well.*
## Twin Ports

- **Settled:** 2376 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6758930664392303232, brown dwarf, 76.23 ly
- **Population:** 4.3 million
- **System:** an ember primary holding a ladder of locked worlds with two habitables: a dusk rock at 291 K with working air, and a twilight ocean world at 283 K with shallow cold seas — the only such pair in the Freeports' register.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 98130 and Kaus Borealis; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6736068072442493312 and Breakwater's star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2376, in the sale the Freeports still call the Bargain of the Century: two habitable worlds, one hammer, one price. The Twin Ports Consortium bid the port-name their own pilots had been using for years — *you cannot trademark a habit*, the consortium's chairman explained — and the auction minute concurred: *sold; the map was already drawn.*

Twin Ports is the Freeports' double bargain. The dusk rock keeps the mines, foundries, and the up-country towns; the twilight sea keeps the harbors, kelp parks, and the fish auctions; and the two worlds' sixty-year-old ferry compact — one tariff board, one court, one very loud hockey league — is the closest thing to statesmanship the Freeports produce voluntarily. The Consortium's ledgers are public and its margins are thin by Freeport standards, because the two worlds' real business is the trade between themselves: ore down, fish up, and the ferry crews' famous neutrality in every dispute their passengers start. The custom is the Port Bell: each ferry departure is rung twice — once for each port — and no captain may ring early. The grievance is the name: tourists keep assuming "Twin Ports" is metaphorical, and the harbor office keeps correcting them, *there are two of them; you are standing on one.*
## Highmarket

- **Settled:** 2377 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6736068072442493312, brown dwarf, 66.75 ly
- **Population:** 5.8 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked airy rocks — the second of them, **Highmarket** proper, a hot-dusk airy world at 306 K — with mooned ice and a three-mooned ice giant behind; a system of nothing but crossroads.
- **Links:** six gates — Mesh to HIP 88694, Kaus Borealis, and Gaia EDR3 4047622985588974848; Regional to GJ 4074, Gaia EDR3 4047622985664694656, and Twin Ports' star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2377, to the Mesh Merchants' Association, which paid a record sum for the name every broker in the quarter was already using for the junction's floating exchange: *Highmarket.* The auction minute: *sold; the Association wishes it to be known that the price of a name is the least of its investments here.*

Highmarket is where the southern mesh does its deals. Six routes' traffic meets above the hot-dusk world, and the Association built what the Freeports consider their civilization's masterpiece: the great market rings — bonded halls, the commodity exchange where Rete's catch, Cardo's dry goods, Silverfin's protein, and every mesh route's marginal cargo clear simultaneously — and a settlement that is, functionally, one long trading floor with bedrooms. The dusk world below supplies the resort trade's overflow, the ember light's cheap process heat, and very little else; the money is all in the sky. The custom is the Closing Walk: the exchange's day ends with the floor-walkers ringing their hand-bells in procession around the great ring, and no deal is honored after the last bell, a rule enforced by tradition because it is never once tested. The grievance is the Association's own success — the exchange's daily volume outgrew its clearinghouse twice, and the Freeports' remedy (build a second one) has been in committee since 2402, a word that at Highmarket means *a kind of long position.*
## Stockyard

- **Settled:** 2378 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4071372539860350464 (GJ 4074), M, 61.44 ly
- **Population:** 1.7 million
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked heavy rocks, then the settled world — a cool airy planet at 262 K with one moon, grass from pole to pole — with mooned ice beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 88694 and Highmarket's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4047622985664694656 and Gaia EDR3 4047622985588974848.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2378, to the Drovers' Association, which wanted a name that needed no advertising. The auction minute: *sold; the steaks are further explanation.*

Stockyard is the southern mesh's pasture. The cool grass world's open range — herds under drover family brands older than the charter — feeds Highmarket's butcher row and half the mesh's tables, and the settlement's culture is every cattle town that ever was: brand law, brand lawyers, the fall round-up as civic festival, and a tariff office that knows every head in the system by ear-tag and by name. The custom is the Count Dinner: at round-up's end, every crew eats at the association hall, and the last table served is the crew with the worst count — both the penalty and the prize, because the worst count cooks next year's opening feast and everyone shows up. The grievance is water law — the range wells are Freeport-licensed to the centiliter, and the drovers' annual petition to buy the aquifer outright has been declined, so far, by owners whose asking price the association describes, in its own filings, as *optimistic, and improving.*
## Grapevine

- **Settled:** 2379 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4047622985664694656, M, 72.80 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked furnace rocks, then the settled world — a cold airy planet at 246 K with one moon, its south slopes running vine rows to the frost line — with a giant-heavy tail beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Stockyard's star and Gaia EDR3 4047622985588974848; Mesh gates to Highmarket's star, Kaus Borealis, and Gaia EDR3 4045967975765745536.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2379, to the Southern Vintners' Consortium, which beat two hotel groups and one eccentric grain baron for the privilege. The auction minute: *sold; may the name age as well as the product.*

Grapevine is the southern mesh's cellar. The cold slopes' hardy vines — grafted stock from Viña's heritage lines, sold at auction price and worth the grumbling — produce the strong reds the Freeports drink, export, and advertise with a shamelessness that makes the Meridian Vintners file annual complaints about *appellation integrity*, a phrase the Consortium prints on its bottles, in small type, under the alcohol content. The settlement is vineyard estates, bottling halls, and the guest trade of five routes' worth of tasting rooms. The custom is the First Crush Festival: the season's opening crush is done in the harbor square, barefoot, by whoever draws the short lot — tourists included, which is the entire marketing strategy, and it works. The grievance is the weather: frost takes a vintage some years, and the Consortium's frost insurance is written by Cautio's sureties at rates the vintners call *confident*, a word the sureties accept as accurate.
## Relay

- **Settled:** 2380 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4045967975765745536, M, 82.61 ly
- **Population:** 1.5 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and two hot steam ocean worlds — nothing to inhabit, plenty to orbit — with mooned ice and a three-mooned ice giant behind.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4047622985588974848, Gaia EDR3 4037085334973293824, and Gaia EDR3 6729483337819038720; Mesh gates to Grapevine's star, HIP 87116, and Gaia EDR3 4041663873163840384.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2380, to the Packet Combine, whose courier service needed a hub name that doubled as a promise. The auction minute: *sold; delivery times on request.*

Relay is the southern mesh's post office. Six routes converge, and the Combine's courier hulls — the fastest small ships money buys, painted in the company's red — sort, swap, and sprint the quarter's mail, legal packets, and market intelligence through the junction at a tempo the settlement's whole population of sorters, dispatchers, and racing pilots keeps like a heartbeat. The Combine's guarantee (*by the next bell, or the fee back, no questions*) is enforced by the pilots' own guild, which considers lateness a personal stain. The custom is the Late Box: every courier hall keeps one box for mail too old to deliver and too treasured to scrap — missives from the severed years, dead ports, and dead lovers — and the Combine's crews still try, once a year, on the Day of the Late, to deliver them anyway. The grievance is Nuntius — the Meridian signalers' certified service undercuts the Combine on the guild routes, and the Combine's annual report on the *market distortions of sincerity* is the Freeports' best-read comedy.
## Longshot

- **Settled:** 2381 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4037085334973293824, K brown dwarf, 86.19 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** a failed star over locked rocks, a hot locked steam ocean, and a cold ladder of airy stones and ice giants — the certified survey calls it *unpromising*, which the Trust quotes in its advertisements.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Relay's star and HIP 87116; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5957041127551353088, Gaia EDR3 6726429375571878912, Gaia EDR3 4041663873163840384, and Gaia EDR3 6735308241178655872.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2381, to the Gaming Trust, whose bid letter argued the name was *not a description of the odds but of the customers.* The auction minute: *sold; the house always wins, and so did we.*

Longshot is the quarter's casino. The Trust's great pleasure halls — gaming floors, race books on six routes' arrival times, and the famous futures exchange where pilots bet on their own salvage rights — hang above the ember star at the crossing of six roads, and the settlement's economy is the oldest one: separating travelers from their money artistically, feeding them excellently, and flying the winners home first. The custom is the Fair Book: every game's odds are posted, every payout published, and the Trust's founding rule — *the house wins on the odds, never on the lie* — is enforced by the Freeport courts with a severity that surprises visitors, because the Trust pays for the enforcement and considers it insurance. The grievance is the Meridian touts who call Longshot *a tax on arithmetic*, which the Trust's proprietors retort is *also a description of Cardo's margins, and ours are posted.*
## Bunkhouse

- **Settled:** 2382 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5957041127551353088, M, 74.59 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a hot locked twilight sea, and mooned ice — a system of bunks, not homes.
- **Links:** Regional gates to λ Ara and Gaia EDR3 6726429375571878912; Mesh gates to HD 154590B and Longshot's star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2382, to the Boarding-House Guild, which bid precisely what the name was worth per night, times the beds, times forever. The auction minute: *sold; sleep tight.*

Bunkhouse is where the southern mesh's working crews sleep. The Guild's lodging rings — cheap, clean, stamped with the Freeports' honest-audit seal — rent by the night to the pilots, riggers, and haulers of four routes, and the settlement's whole apparatus (hiring halls, wage exchanges, the famous cheap kitchens) exists to serve the quarter's itinerant labor, which passes through in the tens of thousands and leaves, mostly, pleasantly surprised. The custom is the Name Board: every bunkhouse wall carries the names of crew who found their ship here, painted over by no one, ever — the Guild's founder having ruled that *a name is a promise that came true.* The grievance is the beds' most famous residents — the couriers of Relay, who sleep four hours and complain about two of them, at volume, in the Guild's only soundproofing budget line.
## Bunkerage

- **Settled:** 2383 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5946685759563224960 (λ Arae), F, 68.35 ly
- **Population:** 860,000
- **System:** a hot bright F sun over mooned furnace rocks and a temperate two-mooned ice giant — fuel, fire, and gravity, the whole business.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 83846 and Bunkhouse's star; Mesh gates to HD 154590B and Gaia EDR3 6726429375571878912.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2383, to the Bunkerage Combine, whose letter of bid ran one line: *every fire needs a woodpile.* The auction minute: *sold; per liter.*

Bunkerage is the southern mesh's woodpile. The Combine's fuel works — reaction mass skimmed from the temperate giant, volatiles cracked from the ice, and the famous quick-fill cradles that turn a convoy's dead time into a tea break — bunkers four routes' traffic at margins so thin the whole settlement can recite them, because the Combine posts them, per liter, per hour, at every dock, and considers transparency a competitive weapon. The population is tank families, dock crews, and the meter-masters whose gauges are certified quarterly by Libra and feared daily by everyone. The custom is the First Liter: each new-hire's first shift ends with them pouring one liter, by hand, into the tank of whichever ship is nearest departure — the Combine's entire culture in one gesture: the fuel moves, and someone takes responsibility for it. The grievance is Ignis — the Meridian fireworkers sell heat cheaper on the shared routes, and the Combine's annual price comparison, published in identical format, is a document both polities' economists read like a duel.
## Turnpike

- **Settled:** 2384 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5965924361179144832 (HIP 83846), K, 67.58 ly
- **Population:** 2.9 million
- **System:** a K sun over locked furnace rocks, a heavy hot airy stone, and a bare temperate pebble — a system whose value is entirely in its roads: six gates, one of them the Concord link to the Union trunk.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Larawag, Bunkerage's star, and HD 154590B; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 6018693257096471424 and Gaia EDR3 6017724140666000896; Concord gate to Gaia EDR3 5935907693782592000.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2384, to the Transit Combine, which owned the port dues and wanted a name that announced them. The auction minute: *sold; thank you for shopping.*

Turnpike is the Freeports' customs house at the Union border. The Concord gate opened in the first decade of the settlement era and made this junction the southern mesh's door to USE space: every hull crossing the demilitarized line here pays the Combine's famously posted tolls, clears its papers in famously neutral bonded halls, and hears, from famously cheerful tariff clerks, the famously final phrase *thank you for shopping.* Two flags fly at the anchor and neither salutes; the Combine's position, published in every port directory in the quarter, is that the border is its best product. The custom is the Ledger of the Two Cities: every toll collected at the Union gate is matched, coin for coin, in the Freeports' open ledger against the equivalent USE tariffs paid by League hulls at Sol's ports — a mirror-accounting the Combine maintains *for the public's arithmetic*, and which both governments cite and neither enjoys. The grievance is reciprocal inspection — the USE customs auditors assigned to Turnpike are polite, thorough, and permanently second-guessed from home, a condition the Combine's annual report describes, with the sincerity of a toll collector, as *the price of a good neighborhood.*
## Larawag

- **Settled:** 2385 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6026152137815012096 (Larawag), M, 64.69 ly
- **Population:** 470,000
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked rocks and bare mooned stones, with a shallow warm ocean world (327 K, 16% sea) and a five-mooned ice giant out in the cold.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Turnpike's star, HIP 83541, and Gaia EDR3 6018693257096471424; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 6017724140666000896.
- **Registry name:** kept, not auctioned. The IAU-proper name — carried from an old Australian Aboriginal sky tradition — falls under the Freeports' heritage carve-out, the one rule in the auction code the brokers never challenge: some merchandise is heritage, and the hammer stays down. The registry note reads: *Larawag; grandfathered by grandfather.*

Larawag is a small honest junction. The Freeports' station families run the usual trade — fuel from the ice giant's skim works, tariffs from four routes' traffic, and the warm ocean world's tiny spa concession, which Bathgate's managers visit to smirk and then, by the second day, to take notes. The settlement's custom is the Sky Night: once a year the station lights go dark for one hour, and the crews of every polity's ships lie back on the hulls and let the old astronomers' sky have the quarter to itself — an observance the port directory attributes to *tradition, tourism, and the remarkable coincidence that the bar closes at the same hour.*
## Stillhouse

- **Settled:** 2386 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6018693257096471424, K brown dwarf, 68.79 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** a failed star over locked rocks, a hot locked steam ocean (83% sea), and a cold ladder of mooned ice giants — good cold, good stone, good everything the trade needs but barley.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Larawag's star and Gaia EDR3 6017724140666000896; Mesh gates to Turnpike's star and HIP 80925.
- **Registry name:* auctioned, 2386, to the Distillers' Guild, whose members had been calling the works *the still house* since before the hammer. The auction minute: *sold; proof on request.*

Stillhouse is the southern mesh's distillery. The Guild's works — whisky from Arva's grain brought down the long routes, the famous ember-gin infused with Jardín's seconds, and the industrial alcohol half the quarter's labs run on — are certified, bonded, and taxed at every stage, with a revenue stamp collection the Guild's clerks guard like scripture. The custom is the Angel's Share Report: each year's evaporation loss is measured, published, and mourned in a one-line obituary the distillers write collectively and take turns signing — this year's reading: *the angels drank well; next year they pay.* The grievance is the Meridian tariffs on spirits, which the Guild's annual letter calls *a tax on joy, itemized*, and which the League's assembly votes on, as tradition demands, very slowly.
## Racecourse

- **Settled:** 2387 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6017724140678769024 (HIP 80925), K, 73.55 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** a K sun over a hot airy rock, a bare mooned temperate stone, and the settled world — a warm shallow-sea planet at 304 K, 28% of its face under sea, two moons — built for spectators.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6017724140666000896 and Gaia EDR3 6019875502661288320; Mesh gates to Stillhouse's star, HD 154590B, and Gaia EDR3 6036120928708232448.
- **Registry name:* auctioned, 2387, to the Racing Trust, which had surveyed the system's orbital geometry and found in it the perfect course. The auction minute: *sold; place your bets.*

Racecourse is where the quarter races its ships. The Trust's great circuit — a graded course threading the settled world's two moons, the gas giants' flybys, and a final slingshot that has ended more careers than the war did — hosts the Mesh Cup, the race every pilot in the quarter has either won, lost, or lied about. The warm sea world below holds the spectator fleets, the betting halls, and the shipbuilders' row where the racing dynasties refine hulls no cargo will ever pay for. The custom is the Losers' Supper: the race's last-place finisher is hosted, at the Trust's table, by the winner's crew, and the toast is always the same — *to the next course* — because the Trust's whole theology is that the race never ends. The grievance is the insurance: Cautio's sureties load racing premiums onto honest hulls because of what the Trust calls *art* and the sureties call *a hobby with a body count*, a dispute both sides cherish and neither will settle.
## Coldquarry

- **Settled:** 2388 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6036120928708232448, brown dwarf, 80.18 ly
- **Population:** 660,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked rocks — the third of them, **Coldquarry** proper, a cold airy dusk world at 248 K — with a mooned icy stone behind it.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Racecourse's star and HIP 77908; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6043237998751939584 and Gaia EDR3 6011124768801217408.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2388, to the Ice Combine, whose bid letter explained the whole business model in four words: *they sell the cold.* The auction minute: *sold; ice is water with patience.*

Coldquarry sells the cold. The Combine's ice fleets cut, lift, and haul the dusk world's glaciers and the ice world's captured comets to every water-poor port on the southern routes — Turnpike's bonded tanks, Longshot's drink halls, the ember cluster's dry towns — and the settlement's cutter crews work the face with a machismo the Combine's insurers price without sentiment. The custom is the First Cut: each season's opening cut is made by the crew with the worst prior season, because, the cutters' rule runs, *the cold forgives nobody, so we forgive each other.* The grievance is Fuente — the Commonwealth's fountain tariffs cap water prices on the mercy routes, and the Combine's annual letter on *the margins of mercy* is the document most cited by both polities' economists and least understood by either.
## Outfitters

- **Settled:** 2389 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6235558525363302912 (HIP 77908), K, 77.41 ly
- **Population:** 3.8 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked rocks, a hot airy stone, and the settled world — a cool airy planet at 263 K — with airy ice and a two-mooned giant carrying a captured companion out in the cold.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Coldquarry's star, Gaia EDR3 4324968638996126976, Gaia EDR3 6247047562878494848, and HIP 75363; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6239491684610275328 and Gaia EDR3 6043237998751939584.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2389, to the Chandlers' Consortium, whose members fit out every hull that passes and bid for a name that was also their shop sign. The auction minute: *sold; you leave heavier.*

Outfitters is the southern mesh's great chandlery. Six routes' hulls put in here to be fitted, stocked, armed, painted, provisioned, and argued over: the great bazaar rings hold a million shops' worth of inventory, and the Consortium's warranty seal — *we outfitted it; ask us first* — is the closest thing to a conscience the Freeport trade has, being entirely voluntary and commercially priceless. The settled world's towns grow the provisioning: the market gardens, the bottling works, the sail-maker dynasties whose canvas-derivatives outlast the hulls. The custom is the Manifest Feast: a newly fitted hull's crew dines, at the fitting-hall's expense, on a table built from the crate-wood of everything they bought, and no departure is sung until the table is cleared. The grievance is the counterfeit trade — the knock-off chandlery of the minor ports, whose sub-standard fuses, mislabeled charts, and optimistic ropes the Consortium's inspectors collect in the annual Hall of Shame, which is lit, tastefully, by the counterfeit fuses.
## Orehouse

- **Settled:** 2390 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4324968638996126976, M, 74.31 ly
- **Population:** 770,000
- **System:** a bright M star over heavy hot airy rocks, mooned ice, and a 322-Earth-mass giant with six moons and two captured locked companions — ore in every direction, and the assay office to prove it.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4352307381441947392 and Gaia EDR3 6247047562878494848; Mesh gates to Outfitters' star and Gaia EDR3 6043237998751939584.
- **Registry name:* auctioned, 2390, to the Metals Trust: *Orehouse*, named for the system's one unambiguous institution. The auction minute: *sold; per gram.*

Orehouse is the southern mesh's assay town. The Trust buys, samples, certifies, and re-sells every mining crew's haul from four routes' worth of belts and moons — the great orehouse itself, a kilometer of bonded bins under one roof — and the settlement's population of buyers, assay chemists, and mining families runs on the assay bell's rhythm: rings when the market opens, rings when the lots clear, rings when someone argues, and it rings a lot. The custom is the Dead Rock Walk: each year the assayers walk the floor of lots that didn't sell — the Fool's Gold Parade, the crews call it — because, the Trust's rule holds, *an honest market shows its failures in the open.* The grievance is the Fool's Gold Parade itself, which the assayers love, the crews hate, and the poets of the quarter write about, which nobody at Orehouse considers a defense.
## Homestead

- **Settled:** 2391 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4352307381441947392, brown dwarf, 68.12 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a small ember primary over locked airy rocks — the second of them, **Homestead** proper, a cool airy dusk world at 284 K — with a bare ice giant behind it.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Orehouse's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4324728640519965824, Gaia EDR3 4140696473069943424, and HIP 84277.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2391, to the Land Office Combine, which had been pre-selling lots for two years under the name and considered the auction a formality. The minute: *sold; title clear.*

Homestead is the Freeports' land office. The Combine sells quarter-acre lots on the dusk world's pleasant belt — real deeds, real surveys, the famous Homestead Warranty that has survived every court in the League — and the settlement grew the way its advertising promised: fast, bright, and argumentative, a frontier town of title-clerks, well-drillers, and smallholders who arrived crew-poor and, within a generation, were arguing about fence lines like every settler in every story. The custom is the Deed Day: each solstice, every new landholder walks their own boundary at dusk, lantern raised, and the whole town's skyline twinkles with other people's promises. The grievance is the water company — the Combine's own subsidiary, whose rates the homesteaders' assembly has contested annually since 2396 in the quarter's longest-running internal Freeport dispute, a case both sides fund with the stubborn joy of people who have discovered politics.
## Towline

- **Settled:** 2392 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4140696473069943424, brown dwarf, 64.18 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked rocks and icy giants — a system whose chart reads like a harbor approach, which the Combine swears was destiny.
- **Links:** Regional gates to ξ Oph, HIP 84277, and Gaia EDR3 4134597202897079936; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4324728640519965824 and Homestead's star.
- **Registry name:* auctioned, 2392, to the Towing Combine: *Towline*, for the fleets of tugs that shepherd the southern mesh's heavy freight through its tricky approaches. The auction minute: *sold; slow and steady.*

Towline pulls the quarter's heavy loads. The Combine's tugs — wallowing, powerful, and crewed by pilots who can dock a moon — take the contracts no fast hull wants: convoy salvage, dead-station moves, the great girder runs to the shipyards, and the rescue tows that make the Combine the first number in every harbor master's book. The custom is the Slow Salute: a tug passing another tug on the routes sounds its horn once, low, regardless of schedule or jurisdiction — the only punctuality the Combine relaxes, and its crews will stop for. The grievance is the racing pilots of Racecourse, who overtake the tow lines for sport, and whose registered hulls the Combine's annual report lists, without comment, beside the phrase *wind resistance, recreational.*
## Fingerling

- **Settled:** 2393 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4134597202897079936, M, 68.08 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a bright M star over a locked heavy rock, then the settled world — a warm twilight ocean planet at 308 K, a quarter of its face under shallow sea — with a steam ocean, two giants, and a captured locked companion behind it.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to ξ Oph, HIP 85561, and HIP 84277; Regional gates to Towline's star and Gaia EDR3 4116840541184279296.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2393, to the Hatcheries Combine, whose entire industry is the sale of very small fish. The auction minute: *sold; sold; sold a thousand more just after.*

Fingerling is the quarter's fish nursery. The Combine's hatcheries — warm-water ponds on the twilight sea's dusk shelf, genealogies kept like bloodlines — supply young stock to every fishery in the quarter: Rete's certified shoals, Coldharbor's oyster parks, San Andrés's chapter pens, and the stocked seas of half a dozen worlds that began with no fish at all. The trade is delicate, profitable, and legally fussy — every shipment crosses borders in bonded water — and the settlement's population of aquaculturists, brokers, and veterinary monks (the Commonwealth licenses its own inspectors, who are, in fact, monks) lives at the tempo of the fry-count. The custom is the Release: each year's first hatch is freed into the settled world's own sea by the crews' children, in the Combine's one uncontested acknowledgment that the fish, like the children, are the point. The grievance is poaching — wild-spawn raiders who seine the dusk shelf's own seas and sell uncertified stock as Combine-bred, a fraud the fry-taggers detect at a glance and the courts fine at a rate the Combine's annual report indexes to inflation, with visible satisfaction.
## Paintworks

- **Settled:** 2394 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4116840541184279296, M, 71.84 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a bare cold pebble, and a ladder of mooned ice giants — storage, silence, and room for the mixing halls.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Fingerling's star and Gaia EDR3 4059215823975992960; Mesh gates to HIP 85561 and Gaia EDR3 4145205501565331712.
- **Registry name:* auctioned, 2394, to the Paintmakers' Trust, the quarter's hull-paint monopoly-in-law. The auction minute: *sold; two coats.*

Paintworks paints the quarter. The Trust's color halls mix, certify, and ship the hull-paints whose color-codes every pilot reads at a glance — Freeport red, chapter gray, Meridian black, the Union's trunk blue — and whose formulations, proof against vacuum, salt, and taste, are the quiet standard under half the sky's livery. The custom is the Patch Wall: every visitor to the mixing halls paints one small square in any color they carry, and the wall, two centuries on, is the quarter's largest and most moving artwork, which the Trust insures for a sum it refuses to disclose and visits, on quiet days, alone. The grievance is fashion — the racing stables of Racecourse demand new colors every season, and the Trust's masters, who consider color a covenant, comply loudly and bill accordingly.
## Quarantine

- **Settled:** 2395 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4145205501565331712, brown dwarf, 67.07 ly
- **Population:** 210,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked rock, two locked ice giants, and mooned cold stone — a system built, by choice and ordinance, of things nobody wants to touch.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4159791176135290752 and ζ Ser; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4101357699404676736 and Paintworks' star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2395, to the Health Board, the Freeports' joint insurance-and-medicine trust, which paid over odds for the name on the theory that *a system named Quarantine is a system nobody has to be told twice to stop at.* The minute: *sold; please wash your hands.*

Quarantine stops what moves. The Board's inspection rings hold, sample, and clear every hull, cargo, and passenger the southern routes can't vouch for — the observation wards are famously comfortable, the fines are famously not, and the settlement's population of doctors, inspectors, and the world's most patient hoteliers works at the intersection of medicine and commerce where the Freeports are, occasionally, exactly what they advertise. The custom is the Clear Bell: a hull's release is rung in, every time, and the crews under observation have made a festival of the waiting — the Quarantine Games, held between ships, whose trophy is a bell-shaped cup nobody has ever won sober. The grievance is the other polities' health offices, which honor the Board's clearances *at their own speed*, a speed the Board's annual report measures, publishes, and describes as *a symptom.*
## Watchworks

- **Settled:** 2396 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4159791176135290752, brown dwarf, 60.96 ly
- **Population:** 140,000
- **System:** an ember primary holding six tiny locked rocks in a ladder — the certified survey calls the system *a watch movement with the face left off.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to ξ Oph, Gaia EDR3 4279261876207080576, Gaia EDR3 4101357699404676736, and Quarantine's star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2396, to the Horologists' Society, which wanted the tiny, precise system and the pun, and bid for both. The auction minute: *sold; on time.*

Watchworks keeps the mesh's clocks honest. The Society's repair halls service the ship's chronometers of four routes — the instruments that decide, in a quarter without relativity's mercies, when a bell means a bell — and the settlement's small population of horologists, apprentices, and the tool-grinders they cannot function without works in the famous quiet the guild maintains by charter and the ember light makes easy. The custom is the Trial of the Thousand Bells: a repaired chronometer must run a full cycle against the master clocks — Certainty's signal, by treaty, is the appeal court — before it is returned, and the thousand bells it strikes are logged by hand. The grievance is the master-clockkeepers of Certainty themselves, whose annual letter on *commercial drift* the Society answers with its own annual letter on *certified condescension*, both published, both read, neither side missing a beat.
## Prizecourt

- **Settled:** 2397 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4279261876207080576, M, 64.96 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** an ember-red primary over a furnace-class locked rock (838 K), hot bare stones, a mooned airy rock, and two giant planets whose calm orbits make good, quiet courtrooms.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Watchworks' star and Gaia EDR3 4258755778197107200; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4293873732939569920 and Gaia EDR3 4311982650767794176.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2397, to the Salvage Bar — the Freeports' association of salvage advocates — which wanted the judgment seat where derelict fortunes are decided to be named honestly. The auction minute: *sold; the finding is final, the fee is due.*

Prizecourt is where derelicts are decided. The Bar's court rings hear the quarter's salvage cases — hulls found, cargoes claimed, finds contested — and the settlement's population of advocates, finders' agents, and auction-house scouts lives on the arguments that arrive with every tow: what was abandoned, what was stolen, and what was merely patient.
