Flagfall's register men commute here, Breakwater's brokers commute here, and the customs of the place are all legal ones: the Show of the Chain (a salvor's towline is displayed in court, link by link, before judgment), and the Loser's Bell, rung once, gently, when a claim is denied. The grievance is the Commonwealth, whose chapels counsel salvors to prayer and whose advocates at Prizecourt are, the Bar's annual report observes, *distressingly good.*
## Truffleport

- **Settled:** 2398 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4258755778197107200, brown dwarf, 75.90 ly
- **Population:** 850,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked airy rocks — the third of them, **Truffleport** proper, a cold airy dusk world at 252 K — with mooned ice and a two-mooned ice giant behind.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Prizecourt's star, Gaia EDR3 4155202364362108544, and HIP 92984; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4101357699404676736 and Gaia EDR3 4281877511292289152.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2398, to the Fungal Produce Combine, whose bid letter consisted of a menu and a number. The auction minute: *sold; the side dishes of the southern mesh are now in professional hands.*

Truffleport farms the dark. The dusk world's cave-ecologies — cold, humid, engineered — grow the quarter's fungal trade: delicacies for the rich tables, bulk protein for the work routes, and the antibiotic cultures half the quarter's pharmacies license. The Combine's spore-vaults are guarded like banks, its harvest is auctioned like art, and its cave-city settlement of mycologists, haulers, and cooks keeps, as its custom, the Lantern Dinner — each harvest's first flush served, in the caves, at one long table lit by one lantern, with the year's newest worker seated at the far end where the dark is deepest, because *the fungus grows where the light stops, and so does the trade.* The grievance is counterfeit spores — the knock-off cultures of the minor ports, whose yield the Combine's assay office publishes annually beside its own under the heading *the proof is in the eating*, and the eating, the report notes, has never once been disputed.
## Azure Bay

- **Settled:** 2399 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4155202364362108544, B (3.1 solar masses, 80 solar luminosities), 77.97 ly
- **Population:** 14 million
- **System:** a fierce blue-white sun over furnace rocks and hot airy stones, and then — far out where its blaze gentles — two temperate ocean worlds: **Azure Bay** proper (274 K, 59% of its face under sea, at 8.2 au) and the far, warm **Long Bay** (311 K, 35% sea) at 34 au, with a mooned gas giant between them.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4101357699404676736, ζ Ser, and Gaia EDR3 4271633361463061376; Regional gate to Truffleport's star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2399, in the sale every broker in the quarter still cites as the standard of value: two habitable ocean worlds under one of the sky's burning blue jewels. The Azure Waters Consortium won it with the largest private bid in Freeport history and named the system for its first world's famous bays — blue by day, blue by starlight, blue by the Consortium's own advertising. The auction minute: *sold; and the Blue Horizon cruise line thanks the Committee.*

Azure Bay is the Freeports' flagship. The near world's brilliant bays — lit by a sun that makes every noon blue-white noon and every sea a sheet of sapphire — carry the Consortium's resort archipelagos, the pleasure fleets of a dozen routes' traffic, and the mariculture concessions that fund the ports; the far warm Long Bay carries the second wave, the settlement grants, and the land-boom the Consortium manages with a mixture of marketing and dread. The system's best thing is light: Azure Bay's blue sun writes itself on everything — the artists' colony at the equator has produced a century of the quarter's marine painting, and the ophthalmology clinics of the ring are the best in the League, because a world this bright teaches its doctors everything there is to know about eyes.

The custom is the Blue Hour: at each dusk bell, every resort, marina, and homestead porch on the settled world turns one lamp to the sea — a thousand answers to one sun — and the Consortium, which did not invent the custom but has failed to prevent it, prints it in every brochure as *heritage*, for once accurately. The grievance is the star itself: the astrophysics is not lost on anyone — a B-class sun is a short bright life, and the Consortium's actuarial office publishes, in the fine print of every land deed, the same footnote every year: *the star is young, and will outlive every deed we sell*, which is true, is meant to be comforting, and somehow is not.
## Waterrights

- **Settled:** 2400 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4271633361463061376, M, 79.96 ly
- **Population:** 2.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over heavy locked super-earths — one of them, at 272 K, airy and habitable at the dusk band — and the deep-water ocean world (305 K, 84% of its face under sea, one moon), with paired three-mooned ice giants behind.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 88622 and Gaia EDR3 4160406589092160896; Mesh gates to ζ Ser, Azure Bay's star, and Gaia EDR3 4281877511292289152.
- **Registry name:* auctioned, 2400, to the Hydro Combine, which owned the water and wanted the name to say so. The auction minute: *sold; per liter, in perpetuity, subject to the Freeports' mercy, which is for sale separately.*

Waterrights is the southern mesh's pump. The Combine brokers the deep ocean world's water — pipeline runs, ice harvests, and the famous water futures traded at Highmarket against the dry quarters' every bad year — and the settlement's culture is entirely downstream of that: tank crews, tariff lawyers, the biggest fountain-maintenance industry in the League, and a municipal debate about the name that has run since 2404, with one faction proposing *something pretty* and the Combine replying, annually, *the name is honest, and so is the meter.* The dusk-rock's heavy super-earth settlements keep the mining lease and the workers' towns; the ocean world keeps the intake cities, riding the warm swell. The custom is the Meter's Birthday — the settlement's founding meter, still running in the tariff hall, is serviced in public each year, toasting, at the appointed hour, *the first liter*, a ceremony the crews attend sober and leave otherwise. The grievance is Fuente's Fountain Duty again — the Commonwealth's free fountains sit, defiantly, on every route Waterrights' tankers serve, and the Combine's annual report on *unfair competition from charity* is quoted, with delight, in every polity's civics classroom.
## Orchard

- **Settled:** 2401 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4471087175915086080 (HIP 88622), G, 78.64 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over mooned hot rocks, then the settled world — a temperate airy planet at 284 K — with two colossal giants (639 and 547 Earth masses) and their captured companions out in the long cold.
- **Links:** Regional gates to ζ Ser and Waterrights' star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4373813863241766784, Cebalrai, and Gaia EDR3 4393807554480456448.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2401, to the Fruitgrowers' Guild, whose members had planted the first orchards two years before the auction, on speculation. The auction minute: *sold; the trees were already voting.*

Orchard is the southern mesh's fruit bowl. The temperate world's river valleys — under a Sun-like light that the Guild's growers describe, poetically and accurately, as honest — carry the orchard industry: fresh fruit down five routes in fast cold-hulls, preserves to every larder, cider to every bar, and the famous Orchard blossoms festival, which the Guild monetizes with such skill that other polities send observers. The population is grower families, picker crews whose labor contracts are the quarter's most-fought-over paperwork, and the dock towns that move it all. The custom is the First Crate: each season's first crate is auctioned at the harbor, hammer-high, proceeds to the pickers' fund — because the Guild's founders were pickers, and their grandchildren's custom is law. The grievance is pickers' wages, argued with genuine heat at every assembly — the Guild's defense being margin, the crews' answer being the first-crate auction's own arithmetic, and the arbitration circuit's annual summary being one word: *again?*
## Ballast

- **Settled:** 2402 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4373813863241766784, brown dwarf, 80.17 ly
- **Population:** 310,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked puffed ice giant, bare locked stones, and a ladder of mooned ice giants — the Trust's survey summary: *heavy in all the right places.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to ζ Ser and Cebalrai; Mesh gates to Orchard's star, Gaia EDR3 4360144043194583296, and Gaia EDR3 4393807554480456448.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2402, to the Ballast Trust, which quarries, grades, and sells the heavy stone every hull must carry and no passenger ever thanks. The auction minute: *sold; you're welcome.*

Ballast keeps the ships honest. The Trust's quarries on the locked heavy rocks cut, grade, and load trim-stone for the southern mesh's freighters — the unglamorous mass that makes every cargo's trim, draft, and center of gravity come out right — and the settlement's population of quarry crews, loaders, and the grading-office families lives in cheerful awareness that they are the quarter's most indispensable afterthought. The custom is the Last Stone: every hull's final ballast stone of every loading is signed by its loader, chalked, and goes into the hold with the name showing — the crews' way of saying *someone is holding you level.* The grievance is the shipping offices, which keep trying to replace stone with water and pumps, a scheme the Trust's annual report evaluates with the phrase *fluid economics, in both senses*, and the stone keeps winning, because the stone, at least, is not on the open market.
## Teahouse

- **Settled:** 2403 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4360144043194583296, M, 77.61 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, mooned cold stones, and paired ice giants — the certified survey: *arable at the margins, roastable in the middle.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 84277, ζ Ser, and HIP 84487; Mesh gate to Ballast's star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2403, to the Beverage Guild, which grows, roasts, blends, and brokers the quarter's tea, coffee, and every argument about them. The auction minute: *sold; milk and sugar are separate negotiations.*

Teahouse keeps the quarter awake. The Guild's plantations on the temperate margin-worlds and its famous blending halls — where every polity's palate is studied, catalogued, and sold back to it — supply the tea of the chapter houses, the coffee of every pilot room, and the taverns' famous *Teahouse arguments*, which are the closest thing the Freeports have to a public intellectual tradition and are conducted, by guild law, standing up. The custom is the First Pot: every new blend is served first to the crews' youngest member, whose honest verdict — published — is the Guild's quality control and its best advertising, because the Guild has never once suppressed a bad verdict, having priced honesty in from the start. The grievance is the Meridian cafeterias' *adequate* tea, a word the Guild's annual report circles, underlines, and files under *provocation.*
## Nursery

- **Settled:** 2404 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4141979774942939264 (HIP 84487), M, 85.38 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a bright M star over locked furnace rocks, then the settled world — a temperate airy planet at 299 K, garden country from pole to pole — with a mooned cold rock out at the frost line.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Teahouse's star and Gaia EDR3 4128848956112011264; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4339994569198556160 and HD 155802A.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2404, to the Planters' Guild, whose members grow the trees, hedges, shade, and ornament of every new street in the quarter. The auction minute: *sold; landscaping is destiny.*

Nursery plants the quarter's yards. The Guild's great growing-fields — orchard stock for Orchard, vine stock for Grapevine, shade for Homestead's new lots, the famous street-tree line that every mayor in the League orders two generations ahead of the houses — turn the temperate world into one vast polite nursery, and the settlement's families of grafters, hybridists, and haulers ship living cargo to every port that has ever planted anything on purpose. The custom is the Sending: every order is walked to its ship by the crew that grew it, and the ship's master signs the growers' book, because, the Guild's rule runs, *a tree is a contract with strangers.* The grievance is the Commonwealth — Semilla's vaults keep the heirloom lines and the Guild's catalogues keep selling the patents, an arrangement the brothers call *custody* and the Guild calls *supply*, and both call, accurately, the slowest negotiation in the quarter.
## Tannery

- **Settled:** 2405 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4128848956112011264, M, 88.87 ly
- **Population:** 240,000
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked rocks, mooned bare stones, and a three-mooned ice giant — a small system that smells, by all accounts, exactly like its name.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Nursery's star and HIP 82621; Mesh gates to HIP 81754 and Gaia EDR3 4128580159879394304.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2405, to the Tanners' Guild, which wanted no confusion about the nature of the settlement. The auction minute: *sold; the breeze carries the news.*

Tannery is the southern mesh' leather yard. The Guild's works turn Stockyard's hides into the quarter's boots, belts, hull-seals, and book-binding — Telar's apprentices learn on Tannery's offcuts, and Pergamino's fine bindings wear its best — and the settlement's population of tanners, lime-hands, and their long-suffering hospitality industry works downwind of the works with the solidarity of people whom the rest of the quarter visits only by necessity. The custom is the Currier's Walk: each finished hide is carried, by its currier, the full length of the works to the grading door, in sight of every crew, because *the leather sees everyone before anyone sees it.* The grievance is the municipal nickname — every other port calls every settlement's worst quarter *the Tannery*, and the actual Tannery's assembly has filed, with the Freeports' registry, a formal request that they *please stop, or visit.*
## Glasshouse

- **Settled:** 2406 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4127420626087054976 (HIP 82621), K, 98.85 ly
- **Population:** 660,000
- **System:** a bright K sun over heavy hot mooned rocks and a shallow hot ocean world (81% sea, steaming gently) — light, water, and heat, which the Trust's survey called *the recipe.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Tannery's star, HIP 81754, Gaia EDR3 4128580159879394304, and Gaia EDR3 4125617908055633792; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 6033155202239036288.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2406, to the Growers' Trust, whose forcing-houses under the bright K light grow the quarter's out-of-season everything. The auction minute: *sold; summer on schedule.*

Glasshouse sells summer in winter. The Trust's great glass ranges — kilometers of engineered light under the fierce K sun — grow the fresh produce the quarter's cold seasons demand: the midwinter tomatoes, the festival flowers, the famous Glasshouse cucumbers that commanders of five routes are known to requisition. The population is grower families, glaziers, and the tariff crews of a five-route junction, and their custom is the First Light: each new range's first picking is done in the dawn by the whole settlement, whoever shows up, and eaten raw, standing, in the aisle. The grievance is heating — the ranges burn power in the dark months, and the Fuel Combine's winter pricing is described, in the Trust's annual report, as *seasonal, in the way bandits are.*
## Anchorage

- **Settled:** 2407 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6033155202239036288, M, 97.98 ly
- **Population:** 330,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a hot ocean world with shallow seas, and a ladder of three-mooned ice giants — good holding ground in every direction.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6024600379004526848 and HIP 84322; Mesh gates to Glasshouse's star and Gaia EDR3 4125617908055633792.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2407, to the Moorages Trust, which rents the quarter a place to stop. The auction minute: *sold; overnight rates available.*

Anchorage is where ships sleep over. The Trust's moorage rings — berth-rental, hull-watch, bonded lay-up for the freighters between charters — hold a floating population of quiet hulls twice the settlement's size, and the town's economy is the economics of waiting: chandleries, cheap kitchens, crew lawyers, and the famous Anchorage pawn desks, where a hard-up spacer's tools are always worth exactly enough for one more berth-night. The custom is the Wake Light: any hull laid up longer than a season is assigned, by lot, to a household that keeps one lamp lit at its berth window — the Trust's only sentimental rule, unpriced and unbilled, and the one its crews defend loudest. The grievance is vacancy — the Trust's winter lay-up rates are the quarter's most litigated number, and the courts have twice required the Trust to publish its vacancy data, which it now does, on a wall, at the scale of a mural.
## Tarpit

- **Settled:** 2408 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6024600379004526848, brown dwarf, 95.01 ly
- **Population:** 180,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked airy rocks, a steaming ocean world, and a cold ladder of mooned ice giants — heavy hydrocarbons in every deep.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Anchorage's star and Gaia EDR3 6037547682484171008; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 6019875502661288320 and Gaia EDR3 6048829840005028736.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2408, to the Pitch Combine, whose tar, resin, and caulking compounds seal every hull seam in the quarter. The auction minute: *sold; no refunds, no leaks.*

Tarpit seals the quarter. The Combine's works crack, cook, and can the pitch that every shipwright from Astillero to the smallest Freeport dock spreads by hand — the trade is ancient, the smell is immortal, and the settlement's small population of retort crews, coopers, and their laundry-fighters lives in the cheerful certainty that no one has ever been happy to see them arrive and everyone is happy they exist. The custom is the Seam Test: each day's first batch is painted, by the master retorter, across a test-seam in the harbor's public floor, where two centuries of daily seams overlap in a black lacquer the quarter's archaeologists have already begun to study. The grievance is the synthetics — the polymer sealants that outsell pitch on the Union's routes, and whose marketing copy the Combine's annual report answers, without translation, with the test-seam's two-hundred-year record.
## Bloomery

- **Settled:** 2409 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6037547682484171008, M, 91.80 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** a bright M star over locked furnace rocks, heavy bare super-earths, a mooned airy stone, and a four-mooned gas giant — ore, cold, and room to work it.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Tarpit's star, Gaia EDR3 6011124768801217408, Gaia EDR3 6048829840005028736, and HIP 78343.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2409, to the Ironmongers' Fellowship, whose bloomery works smelt the quarter's small-batch iron — the tool-grade, the repair-grade, the *someone-needs-it-yesterday* grade. The auction minute: *sold; strike while hot.*

Bloomery makes the iron the quarter repairs with. The Fellowship's works run the old way at industrial scale — small furnaces, hot crews, iron you can trace to its lode — because the quarter's yards learned long ago that certified standard plate is for ships and honest bloom iron is for the tools that fix them. The settlement is furnace families, smith crews, and the honest dust of a working town, and its custom is the Ring of the Anvils: at shift's end, the works' forges answer the closing bell one by one, each crew striking three times before banking its fire — a roll call the whole town can hear in the dark. The grievance is Faber and San Eloy both, whose certified steel outsells bloom iron everywhere but the repair docks, a market the Fellowship's annual report describes as *the one that keeps us honest and them fed*, in that order.
## Sugarhouse

- **Settled:** 2410 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6010950942881973376 (HIP 78343), K, 107.25 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** a K sun over hot airy mooned rocks and a cold airy stone — a refining system, not a growing one: the beets come in by hull, the crystals leave by hull.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Bloomery's star; Mesh gates to HD 145444A-adjacent routes, Gaia EDR3 6035061549263957888, HIP 76200, and HIP 78330.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2410, to the Sugar Trust, whose refinery houses process the southern routes' beet and cane into every crystal the quarter stirs. The auction minute: *sold; sweetness on schedule.*

Sugarhouse refines the quarter's sweetness. The Trust's great refinery halls — boiling rows, centrifuge floors, the famous crystal-grading galleries where the sun through the windows turns the air to amber — run day and night on beets from Arva's leased acres and cane from the southern plantations, and the settlement's families of boilers, graders, and barrel-fillers keep the molasses calendar like a liturgical year. The custom is the First Crystal: each batch's first graded crystals are sent, by standing contract nobody remembers signing, to the children's wards of every hospital on the five routes — a custom the Trust prices as advertising and the crews privately price as something else. The grievance is the tariff dance — the Meridian sweet-tooth and the Freeport sugar lobby conduct an annual border negotiation the circulars call *the Sugar War*, which so far has produced no casualties and excellent prices.
## Mineral Wells

- **Settled:** 2411 (free-port charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5997560330056894336 (HD 145444A), K, 113.42 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over a mooned hot rock, a heavy shallow-sea ocean world at 325 K whose mineral springs are the entire economy, and a mooned airy stone beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Sugarhouse's star and Gaia EDR3 6035061549263957888; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5942616359627164544 and HIP 78330.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2411, to the Springs Company, which bottles, pipes, and rents the hot ocean world's mineral waters. The auction minute: *sold; the waters are inclusive.*

Mineral Wells takes the waters. The Company's spa towns on the warm ocean world's dusk shore — pump rooms, bath halls, and the bottling works whose labeled waters sell across the quarter as *health, in glass* — serve the southern mesh's tired and the doctors who prescribe them, with the famous analysis board listing every spring's mineral content to the milligram. The custom is the Free Glass: the first draught at any public pump is free, forever, by the Company's founding covenant — priced in as goodwill and never once revised, because the sight of strangers drinking free water in a Freeport does more advertising than the catalogues ever have. The grievance is Bathgate, whose bigger resorts poach the Company's best bath-attendants, and whose poaching the Company's annual report logs with the calm of a business that knows its waters cannot be copied — a fact the analysis board proves, to the milligram, in every edition.
## Boomtown

- **Settled:** 2412 (free-port charter, the newest of the wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5942616359627164544, brown dwarf, 109.36 ly
- **Population:** 7.8 million (and rising, loudly)
- **System:** an ember primary over heavy locked super-earths, then two habitables: a hot-dusk airy world at 320 K and a warm ocean world (309 K, 79% sea, one moon) — with six gates' worth of speculation arriving ahead of the settlers.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 84121 and Gaia EDR3 5963261618890909952; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5946241556864971520, Mineral Wells' star, Gaia EDR3 5964658647188097920, and Gaia EDR3 5950764050037597824.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2412, to the Development Combine, which bid a fortune for the name its own critics were already using. The auction minute is the Freeports' proudest: *sold; the name is honest, and so is the boom.*

Boomtown is the quarter's newest argument. Two habitable worlds, six routes, cheap deeds, and a population that has quadrupled in a decade — boom streets on the dusk world, boom harbors on the ocean world's twilight shelf, boom prices on everything, and a Development Combine managing the stampede with the cheerful self-awareness of a polity that named itself honestly. The custom is the Lottery Queue: new arrivals draw homestead lots by lot, in public, in order of arrival, sleep-deprived and standing — the quarter's most democratic institution, run at Boomtown tempo, which is to say fast and loudly. The grievance is infrastructure — the roads, mains, and schools keep exactly one boom behind the population, and the Combine's official position, printed on every deed (*the infrastructure is coming; the boom is here*) is quoted in three languages on the tavern walls, usually with additions.
## Creamery

- **Settled:** 2413 (free-port charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5951458533405131264 (HIP 84121), G, 105.79 ly
- **Population:** 470,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over mooned hot rocks, a small hot ocean world, a bare heavy stone, and a four-mooned giant — pasture is imported; the cream is local.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 84452 and Gaia EDR3 5946241556864971520; Mesh gates to Boomtown's star and Gaia EDR3 5972252905283041664.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2413, to the Dairymen's Guild, whose creameries, cheese caves, and butter-vaults serve the southern routes' tables. The auction minute: *sold; the butter is final.*

Creamery runs the quarter's dairy trade. The Guild's herds — imported feed, local patience — supply the milk, cream, butter, and the famous Creamery cheeses that age in the cold-moon vaults and cross the routes as hard currency by any other name. The settlement's population of milkers, cheesemakers, and cold-vault keepers keeps the dawn shift like a religion, and its custom is the First Pail: the season's first milking is carried, uncovered, through the whole town at dawn, and everyone takes one sip from the dipper that travels with it — the Guild's eucharist, and its best health inspection. The grievance is the margarine lobby, whose product the Guild's annual report analyzes, illustrates, and declines to name, referring to it throughout only as *the yellow situation.*
## Skihaven

- **Settled:** 2414 (free-port charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5953089899407021696 (HIP 84452), M, 95.38 ly
- **Population:** 950,000
- **System:** a bright M star over a locked furnace giant (629 K), hot bare rocks, and the settled world — a cold airy planet at 244 K with real winters and better slopes — with giants and a far captured wanderer beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 88633, HIP 85425, and Creamery's star; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 5972529952179046912.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2414, to the Winter Sports Combine, which surveyed the cold world's mountain ranges and bid before the snow settled. The auction minute: *sold; the lift lines are also negotiable.*

Skihaven sells winter. The cold world's ranges — dry powder under a patient orange sun, slopes that hold from first freeze to late thaw — carry the Combine's resorts, lift systems, and the famous downhill circuit whose annual race stops only for the Freeports' holidays and one memorable avalanche. The population is resort families, ski patrols, and the mountain-town culture of people whose livelihood depends on everyone else's vacations, and their custom is the First Track: the season's first run is made, at dawn, by the entire patrol in single file, the line of their boards writing the year's opening sentence on the mountain, which the resort then opens to everyone. The grievance is climate's little jokes — the Combine's snow-making is the quarter's largest consumer of Bunkerage fuel, and the fuel Combine's winter pricing on Skihaven's contracts is described, in the Winter Sports annual report, as *sloping.*
## Secondhand

- **Settled:** 2415 (free-port charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6707106730011510016 (HIP 88633), M, 95.22 ly
- **Population:** 230,000
- **System:** a bright M star over locked furnace rocks, a locked puffed giant at 385 K, a bare temperate mooned pebble, and a two-mooned hot ocean world — a system of things other people wanted less.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 90223 and Skihaven's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 6705845452725619072 and HIP 87523.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2415, to the Secondhand Consortium, whose great emporium buys, repairs, and re-sells the quarter's everything. The auction minute: *sold; the name was used, and in good condition.*

Secondhand sells the quarter its own history. The Consortium's great bazaar halls — salvage goods, estate lots, Breakwater's fittings, Auctionhall's regrets — run the largest reuse market in the League, and the settlement's population of refitters, appraisers, and haggling families works to the bazaar's single law: *everything is worth what somebody will pay, and somebody will pay anything.* The custom is the Provenance Tag: no lot sells without its story written out — the hull it came from, the house it lived in, the war it survived — because, the Consortium's founders held, *a thing without a story is new, and nothing here is new.* The grievance is the counterfeiting of provenance itself — the invented stories that arrive daily with the goods, and which the appraisers' guild hunts with a rigor the courts call exemplary and the appraisers call *the other half of the job.*
## Joinery

- **Settled:** 2416 (free-port charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6703245382605547520 (HIP 90223), G, 89.18 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** a bright G sun over heavy mooned rocks and cold airy stones — a system of lathes waiting for wood.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6726429375571878912 and Gaia EDR3 6705845452725619072; Mesh gates to HIP 92656 and Secondhand's star.
- **Registry name:* auctioned, 2416, to the Woodworkers' Guild, whose joinery halls build the quarter's furniture, cabinetry, and fittings from Bosque's timber contracts. The auction minute: *sold; dovetailed.*

Joinery builds the quarter's rooms. The Guild's halls — saw rooms, lathe rows, the famous finishing courts where every polish is judged by daylight through the great windows — make the furniture half the mesh sits on, sleeps in, and argues about, and the settlement's population of joiners, carvers, and tool-keepers works to the trade's oldest law: *measure twice, and never once by anyone else.* The custom is the Drawer Test: every apprentice's certification piece is a single drawer, built blind, judged by feel — pulled from the finished pile in the dark by the master's hand, and only the drawers that whisper are certified. The grievance is the flat-pack imports of the Union's mass fabricators, which the Guild's annual report handles by publishing, without comment, the repair statistics of every port's furniture: a document known, across the quarter, as the Recidivist's Register.
## Henrietta

- **Settled:** 2247 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations, Time of Troubles wave); League member since the Outer Compact.
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6637022793692833024 (HIP 92656), M, 83.49 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** a locked furnace-class gas giant (583 K), a heavy locked airy rock, then two habitables: a small warm ocean world (309 K, shallow seas over 13% of its face, two moons) and a cool airy stone at 244 K — the Compact's second-largest research campus and its gateway from the southern mesh.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5812455902560048896, Gaia EDR3 6639310701294761984, and Joinery's star; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 6638876054899764864 and Gaia EDR3 6647518766053564544.
- **Registry name:** the Compact names its systems by petition and peer review, with a founding preference — written into the Committee on Designations' charter — for the scientists their own centuries overlooked. The first such petition, filed 2251 and approved without a dissenting note, named this system for **Henrietta Swan Leavitt**, the astronomer whose period-luminosity relation first measured the scale of the universe while she was classed, salaried, and credited as a computer. The designation's dedication line is the Compact's motto: *the overlooked deserve the sky.*

Henrietta is the Compact's gateway and its second home. The ocean world's shore campuses — the period-luminosity observatories, the metrology institutes that share standards with Meridian's Weighers by affectionate treaty, and the great library rings that copy, freely, everything the Compact publishes — host two and a half million people, roughly a third of them researchers and their families and the rest the settlement that grew to feed, house, and argue with them. The cool stone keeps the long-baseline instruments; the locked furnace giant keeps the extreme-physics benches; the two moons keep the quiet. The Compact's customs are academic ones: the Open Colloquium, where any result may be heckled by anyone; the Rejection Ball, the annual festival at which the year's most gracefully declined papers are read aloud and toasted; and the Leavitt Lecture, delivered each year by the Compact's youngest full member, on the subject of a scientist history forgot.

The grievance is funding, and it is structural: the Compact's endowments are the largest in the League and its grants process is the quarter's most quoted satire, and the two facts are related, and the Compact knows it, and publishes the satire itself, and funds the satire, which is the most Compact thing about it.
## Jocelyn

- **Settled:** 2254 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5812455902560048896, M, 81.02 ly
- **Population:** 560,000
- **System:** a dim M star over locked rocks, hot shallow seas, airy stones, and a three-mooned giant at the working distance — chosen, the petition said, *for the quiet.*
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 5776716483248901760; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 6638876054899764864, Henrietta's star, and Gaia EDR3 5813538887153210496.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2256, for **Jocelyn Bell Burnell**, who found the first pulsar and watched the prize go elsewhere; the system's radio arrays listen for the things the sky repeats, which the dedication calls *her signal, still arriving.*

Jocelyn is the Compact's ear. The great listening arrays — strung across the cold airy stones and the giant's quiet moons — run the quarter's deepest radio sky surveys, the pulsar timing vaults that Certainty's clock-masters check their clocks against, and the long-shot search for anyone else's voice, which the Compact funds with no expectation and no diminishment. The settlement's population of astronomers, engineers, and their famously quiet families works at listening tempo: slow shifts, long patience, and the daily custom of the Quiet Hour, when the arrays' maintenance pauses and the whole station simply hears the sky, together, whatever it is saying. The grievance is the commercial bands — Longshot's race broadcasts and the Freeports' advertising spill across every quiet frequency some quarters, and Jocelyn's annual interference log is filed, as a courtesy copy, with the Freeports' own academies, who use it to teach shame.
## Vera

- **Settled:** 2259 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5776716483248901760, brown dwarf, 75.83 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked airy rocks — the third of them, **Vera** proper, a cold airy dusk world at 250 K — with icy moons and cold stone out to the rim: a system that is mostly dark, which the Committee noted was the point.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 5773317961528696832 and Gaia EDR3 5779113315521020416; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 6368299918479525632 and Jocelyn's star.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2261, for **Vera Rubin**, who proved the galaxies hold more than they show and spent a career being told to wait; the dedication line reads: *named for the woman who measured the dark, in a system that is mostly it.*

Vera is the Compact's dark-matter campus. The cold dusk world's deep-underground labs — shielded, quiet, patient — hunt the quarter's (and every quarter's) invisible mass, and the system's population of physicists, engineers, and the town that sustains them lives above the experiments in the amber half-light, waiting with a profession-wide serenity for instruments to disagree with expectations. Vera's best thing is its null results, published with the same ceremony as its discoveries — the Compact's conviction, institutionalized, that knowing what isn't there is half the map. The custom is the Naming of the Nothing: each generation of researchers adds one entry to the campus's gallery of elegantly refuted hypotheses, read aloud at graduation — the quarter's only monument to being wrong well. The grievance is bandwidth: the deep labs' data needs reach the mesh through Jocelyn's quiet relays, and the annual negotiation between the physicists' throughput and the astronomers' silence is the Compact's own longest-running experiment.
## Grace

- **Settled:** 2264 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6368299918479525632, M, 62.46 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, then the settled world — a cool airy planet at 256 K with one large moon — and six roads out: the Compact's western crossroads.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 107705 and Gaia EDR3 6383229053001389312; Mesh gates to HIP 1292, HIP 98959, Vera's star, and HIP 110719.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2266, for **Grace Hopper**, who taught machines to speak ours; the dedication line: *named for the compiler, at the junction that compiles the Compact.*

Grace is where the Compact's knowledge turns into the mesh's. The western hub hosts the great data-relay rings — the shared archives, the open model libraries the Compact publishes free, and the translation services that render the Compact's findings into every polity's working language — and the settlement's population of librarians, curators, and the engineers who keep the pipes warm lives by the hub's single rule, painted over every router hall: *information wants to be free, and someone still has to carry it.* The custom is the Cite-Out: every departing researcher's work is ceremonially released to the Commons with one spoken sentence of attribution, witnessed by whoever is in the hall — the Compact's liturgy of credit, practiced since the founding and violated never, which in the Compact is the only never. The grievance is the USE — Sol's model registries charge for what Grace gives away, and the Compact's annual comparison of the two catalogues, published side by side without commentary, is the single most cited document in the quarter's whole information economy, which both sides consider a victory.
## Ada

- **Settled:** 2269 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6371169987426204928 (HIP 107705), M, 53.66 ly
- **Population:** 870,000
- **System:** a bright M star over locked furnace rocks, a bare mooned temperate stone, a hot shallow-sea ocean world (339 K, 47% sea, two moons), and cold mooned ice — an unremarkable retiny the Committee designated *for the roads, six of them, meeting.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 436, Gaia EDR3 6577933973662574080, Gaia EDR3 6446274293824799488, HIP 1292, and Grace's star; Mesh gate to HIP 98959.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2271, for **Ada Lovelace**, who wrote the first program for a machine nobody would build for a century, at a junction where the Compact's whole traffic waits for its own machines to catch up. The dedication line: *named at Ada, where everything queues.*

Ada is the Compact's sorting yard. Six routes' traffic — samples, instruments, data-hulls, researchers running late — is marshaled here by the dispatch halls that the Compact has lovingly over-engineered for two centuries, and the settlement's population of schedulers, cargo masters, and the canteen staffs of legend processes the quarter's most punctilious logistics with the humor of people who know exactly what a scheduling algorithm is worth. The custom is the Proof: no hull departs until its route has been run, by hand, through the great wall-sized dispatch board in the public hall — chalk, string, and honest arithmetic — a redundancy the algorithms long ago obsoleted and the Compact long ago declined to abandon, because, the hall's plaque says, *the first program was written by hand.* The grievance is the researchers, whose collective arrival discipline the dispatch office publishes annually, by department, under the title *On Time Distributions, Empirical.*
## Emmy

- **Settled:** 2274 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4706630501049679744 (HIP 436), K, 53.46 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked furnace rocks, a heavy airy two-mooned stone at 341 K, and a mooned temperate ice giant — a working system, the survey says, and the survey means the traffic.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 16069, HIP 523, and Ada's star; Mesh gates to q01 Eri, HIP 1292, and Gaia EDR3 4689789625044431616.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2276, for **Emmy Noether**, who gave physics its symmetries and was denied a chair for being herself; the dedication line, from the petition's closing argument: *the laws of this quarter run on her theorem, and the quarter should say so.*

Emmy is the Compact's western works-hub. The six-route junction carries the institute traffic of half the Compact's campus network — the instrument works that grind under Ada's discipline, the sample fleets, the travelling fellowships — and its population of engineers, dispatchers, and the families of a college town that runs on other people's experiments has built its whole culture around the hand-off: the Relay of the Sealed Crate, the settlement's one great ceremony, in which each season's most delicate instrument shipment is walked, singing, from work-yard to jump-cradle by everyone who touched it. The grievance is the Compact's own physics: Vera's deep labs and Jocelyn's arrays compete for the same freight priorities as every campus east, and the freight office's annual petition to the Committee — *please rank the sciences, or admit there is no such list* — is refiled every year and declined every year, correctly.
## Lise

- **Settled:** 2279 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4673947174316727040 (HIP 16069), K, 53.28 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked furnace rocks, then the Compact's twin campus: two cold-temperate airy worlds (241 K and 248 K, the second with two moons) — with icy moons and a four-mooned giant beyond, and six roads out.
- **Links:** Regional gates to I Car, q01 Eri, HIP 22451, and ε Ret; Mesh gates to Emmy's star and χ Eri.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2281, for **Lise Meitner**, who explained the split of the atom and watched the prize go elsewhere; the twin worlds are named in the petition *for the collaborator*, and the dedication reads: *the discovery had two authors; the sky now has two worlds.*

Lise is the Compact's twin campus. The two cold airy worlds — one given to the physical institutes, one to the life-and-mind institutes — face each other across a common orbital calendar and a shared shuttle schedule, and their two-mooned sky hangs over the quarter's densest concentration of researchers per capita anywhere, a civilization of seminars, greenhouses, computation halls, and the long arguments that are the Compact's true export. The system's best thing is its seminars: the twin-campus tradition of mandatory cross-disciplinary symposia — physicists lectured by farmers, farmers by poets — has produced more genuine discoveries than any grant program in the League, a fact the Committee studies, with appropriate controls. The custom is the Bridge Lecture: each term's opening seminar is delivered twice, once on each world, and the lecturer must change half the examples the second time, because — the campus rule — *the same truth should wear two coats.* The grievance is ferry capacity between the worlds, a number every researcher has an opinion about and no two opinions match, which the ferry office's annual ridership report demonstrates with visible joy.
## Katherine

- **Settled:** 2284 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5205029672996886016 (I Carinae), F, 53.09 ly
- **Population:** 1.2 million
- **System:** a bright F sun over mooned hot rocks, a heavy mooned airy world at 230 K, and a five-mooned ice giant — the road-station on the great leg toward Lyra system itself.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Lise's star, HIP 39342, Gaia EDR3 5229173470875537664, and the Lyra system; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 5249254676369630336.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2286, for **Katherine Johnson**, who calculated the trajectories by hand and whose arithmetic the machines were asked to confirm; the system's own work is navigation, and the dedication is the Compact's most-quoted: *the road to Lyra was checked, twice, by a woman it is named for.*

Katherine is the gate of the capital. Every hull bound for the Lyra system's great campus stages through this junction, and the Compact built for it the thing its honoree would have respected most: the Deep Trajectory Office, the navigation super-center whose hand-checked flight solutions are the quarter's reference standard, taught in every pilot academy from Meridian to the Freeports. The settlement's population of trajectory mathematicians, dispatch crews, and their families keeps the Office's founding custom: the Double Check — every solution the Office publishes is verified by hand, by a second mathematician, in ink, before release, a redundancy no algorithm has ever been allowed to retire. The grievance is the last leg: the Lyra system's own arrival queues are the Compact's most congested, and Katherine's dispatch office publishes, with great delicacy, the annual waiting-time statistics for the privilege of hearing its own mathematicians explain exactly why.
## Annie

- **Settled:** 2289 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5274724279107404544 (HIP 39342, binary K+M), 56.37 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** the K primary holds heavy hot rocks and cold airy moons; the small M companion, riding behind, holds locked furnace stones and a hot shallow ocean world (74% sea) — a binary the survey crew filed under two classifications, *correctly, and on purpose.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Katherine's star and HIP 34890; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5249254676369630336 and Gaia EDR3 5293288295987250816.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2291, for **Annie Jump Cannon**, who classified a quarter of a million stars by eye and built the scheme the sky is still filed in; the dedication: *a binary system, for the woman who sorted them all.*

Annie is the Compact's stellar census house. The campus runs the quarter's great spectral archives — the classification project that has catalogued every star the mesh can see, in the Cannon scheme, with the notebooks preserved in the vault halls and every classifier's name recorded beside their spectra — and the settlement's population of astronomers, archivists, and instrument-keepers maintains the census as a public work: any polity's navigator, farmer, or child may query the archive, free, forever. The custom is the Millionth Star protocol, the settlement's founding tradition: whenever the census reaches a round-numbered star, its classifier reads the star's card aloud at the general assembly, and the hall answers with one knock — the Compact's applause. The grievance is completeness — the archive's motto (*every star, or it isn't done*) is quoted at every budget hearing by the astronomers and at every graduation by the accountants, and both sides consider the motto theirs.
## Sophie

- **Settled:** 2294 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5285775539195330560 (HIP 34890), K, 63.88 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked furnace rocks, a hot mooned airy stone, and the settled world — a temperate airy planet at 279 K with one large moon — with mooned cold stone behind it, and five roads out.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Annie's star, Gaia EDR3 5293288295987250816, and HIP 31711; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5249254676369630336 and HIP 31878.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2296, for **Sophie Germain**, who taught herself mathematics in a room her parents forbade her to study in, and worked for years under a man's name; the dedication: *named for the one who was there all along, whatever name the letters carried.*

Sophie is the Compact's number-works campus. The temperate world's mathematics institutes — proof halls, the computational gardens, the famous Whisper Rooms where results are argued on chalk until they stand — anchor a settlement of mathematicians, cryptographers, and the artisans of their silence, and the system's five routes carry the Compact's proof traffic: verified results, shared proofs, and the secure channels the League's arbitration circuit contracts when its cases require mathematics no one can bribe. The custom is the Chalk Watch: the institutes' great boards are photographed each night at close, and the archive of two centuries of nearly-right answers — the erased, the corrected, the abandoned — is published openly as teaching material, the campus's conviction being that *the mistakes are the curriculum.* The grievance is secrets: the Compact keeps none, the League sometimes requires them, and the annual negotiation over the cryptography halls is the settlement's own proof that some problems are not meant to close.
## Marie

- **Settled:** 2299 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5479778765278589568 (HIP 31711), G, 71.39 ly
- **Population:** 2.1 million
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over furnace-class heavy rocks, then the settled world — a cool ocean planet at 278 K, four-fifths of its face under sea, one moon — with a cold airy rock and a 654-Earth-mass colossus (four moons and a captured companion) beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Sophie's star and HIP 31878; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5293288295987250816 and HIP 30314.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2301, for **Marie Tharp**, who mapped the floor of Earth's ocean and was told the sea floor could not be mapped by a woman; the dedication, for the ocean world: *it can be mapped; she did; here is the sea to prove it.*

Marie is the Compact's ocean-science campus. The cool sea world's floating institutes — the deep-survey fleets, the seafloor cartography halls where the planet's own ocean floor has been charted, trench by ridge, in Tharp's honor — lead the quarter's oceanography, and the world's deep basins, hydrothermal ecologies, and patient moon-tides give the Compact its marine library and its best-loved teaching cases. The settlement is research towns on the dusk archipelagos, survey-crew families, and the famous dockside custom of the Map Room: the planet's seafloor chart is painted, full-scale, on the harbor hall's floor, and each year's new survey is added by hand, in the presence of the whole town, so that *the map is never finished in front of the people it belongs to.* The grievance is depth — the ocean's deepest trench defies the survey fleet's instruments still, a gap the institute describes, with visible happiness, as *work.*
## Ignaz

- **Settled:** 2304 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5481848424118748672 (HIP 30314), G, 77.87 ly
- **Population:** 1.0 million
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over hot mooned rocks, a captured locked companion, then the settled world — a heavy temperate airy planet at 294 K with two moons — and a long cold tail of airy ice to 95 au.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4767716893186840320 and Marie's star; Regional gates to HIP 31878 and HIP 28898.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2306, for **Ignaz Semmelweis**, who was right, and everyone left him to prove it the hard way; the dedication is the Compact's sternest: *named for the man whose data saved them, and cost him everything. Wash your hands.*

Ignaz is the Compact's public-health campus. The heavy temperate world's epidemiology institutes — the outbreak modelers, the field-medicine schools, the famous hand-washing litany above every hall's door — run the quarter's epidemic-watch with Medicus' physicians and the Commonwealth's hospitals, and the settlement's researchers hold the Compact's hardest doctrine, learned from their honoree: *being right is not enough; you must also survive the argument.* The campus teaches persuasion as a science alongside proof, and its graduates staff every polity's health offices. The custom is the Confirmation: each graduating class re-verifies one historically rejected result — chosen by lot from the archive of the overlooked — as the Compact's standing reminder that the archive is not closed. The grievance is the usual one, familiar to their honoree: the quarter ignores its warnings until the warnings come true, and Ignaz's annual report on *precaution's public relations* is the driest document the Compact publishes, which is a very high bar.
## Rachel

- **Settled:** 2309 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4767716893186840320, M, 67.31 ly
- **Population:** 1.5 million
- **System:** an ember-red primary over a locked steam ocean (82% sea), a heavy cold mooned rock, then the settled world — a warm ocean planet at 310 K, three-quarters of its face under sea, two moons — with mooned giants and airy ice beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 5293288295987250816 and Gaia EDR3 5554890115423215616; Mesh gates to GJ 186.1 B, HD 32778B, and Ignaz's star.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2311, for **Rachel Carson**, who wrote the sea's biography and the warning its readers shot the messenger for; the dedication, for the ocean world: *named for the one who listened first, and said so.*

Rachel is the Compact's ecology campus. The warm ocean world's shore institutes — marine ecology, atmospheric chemistry, the famous long-term reef surveys that have run unbroken since settlement — lead the quarter's living-systems science, and the world itself is both subject and instrument: its engineered-but-wild seas are the quarter's best-studied ecosystem, the baseline against which every polity's oceans are compared. The settlement's population of ecologists, marine crews, and the archive families who maintain the world's two-century observation record works to a doctrine their honoree earned: *the evidence is the argument, and patience is evidence too.* The custom is the Quiet Spring count — each year, on the anniversary of the campus's founding, the whole settlement walks the dawn shore and lists, aloud, every living thing they hear, a census the archives keep in the same book, unbroken, two hundred years deep. The grievance is the world's own health — the warm seas' cycles that the long surveys say are shifting, decade by decade, and the campus's annual summary of the drift is filed with the Assembly, with the Freeports, and with the USE, on the theory that everyone is downstream of somewhere.
## Rosalind

- **Settled:** 2314 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5554890115423215616, M, 68.73 ly
- **Population:** 1.7 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, then the settled world — a heavy warm ocean planet at 314 K, four-fifths of its face under deep sea, two moons — with paired gas giants and mooned ice to the rim.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Rachel's star, Gaia EDR3 5541111516746730752, 71 Pup, and Gaia EDR3 2885863400349980288; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 5573532025833121408.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2316, for **Rosalind Franklin**, whose photograph solved the shape of life while the credit found other addresses; the dedication: *named for the one whose picture was the proof.*

Rosalind is the Compact's genetics campus. The heavy warm world's deep-sea institutes — the crystallography halls, the marine-genome libraries, the famous deep-pressure laboratories where the world's own crushing gravity is the instrument — lead the quarter's life-sciences, and the settlement's population of geneticists, deep-sea crews, and the bioethics boards every polity sends observers to works by a rule their honoree's story wrote: *the data belongs to the work, and the credit must follow the data.* The custom is the Negative (the photograph, unasked-for, that decided everything): each year the campus's most important image — dataset, scan, or photograph — is chosen by the graduate students, not the faculty, and displayed in the main hall with its full author list, unedited. The grievance is patents — the USE's bio-registries enclose sequences the Compact published openly years prior, and Rosalind's annual Prior Art Gazette, printed in the Freeports, at Cardo, and at the USE's own registry office simultaneously, is the quarter's most quietly devastating publication.
## Chandra

- **Settled:** 2319 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5541111516746730752, brown dwarf, 69.08 ly
- **Population:** 580,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked rocks and a locked twilight ocean world (288 K, 46% sea) — the settled one — with ice giants and mooned ice behind it.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Rosalind's star and Gaia EDR3 5544880780046222464; Mesh gates to Tureis and HIP 37606.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2321, for **Subrahmanyan Chandrasekhar**, whose limit — the law that bounds the dead stars — was ridiculed at first and is the law now; the dedication, for the study of what stars become: *named for the limit that holds.*

Chandra is the Compact's stellar-evolution campus. The twilight ocean world's shore institutes study the ember cluster's failed stars, its white dwarfs, and the boundaries of stellar death — the densest concentration of degenerate matter specimens in the quarter is right here in the system's own primary — and the settlement's population of astrophysicists, students, and the deep-silence maintenance crews works in the amber dusk on problems their honoree's mathematics framed a lifetime before any of them were born. The custom is the Limit Lecture: every course of study, in every discipline, opens with one hour on the Chandrasekhar story — the young man, the famous senior colleague, the ridicule, the decades, the vindication — not as science history but as ethics, the Compact's single required lesson about *how to be right early.* The grievance is senior faculty, the joke being structural, annual, and aimed entirely inward, which is how the Compact prefers its jokes.
## Tureis

- **Settled:** 2310 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5698015743046182272 (Tureis), G giant (2.2 solar masses, 22 solar luminosities), 63.47 ly
- **Population:** 610,000
- **System:** a vast bright G giant — hot mooned rocks and airy stones to the far distance, including a two-mooned airy world at 367 K where the works sit.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 5602379190880667904 and Chandra's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 2928420272788743424 and Gaia EDR3 5544880780046222464.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the League's standing rule and certified, as the Committee's minute puts it, *apt, and older than the Committee, which is a form of aptness.*

Tureis is the League's lamp standard. The Photometricians' Guild runs its great reference works in the giant's steady, ferocious light — the certified brightness standards against which every lighthouse, navigation lamp, and Candela catalogue in the quarter is calibrated, sold by subscription and defended like doctrine. The settlement's population of photometrists, standards crews, and the shade-engineers who keep the works' interiors habitable lives in a system where sunlight is an industrial resource, and the custom is the Evening Dim: each sol's last calibration is performed, by rule, by candlelight, the guild's memory of what light cost before there were standards. The grievance is Merak — the Navigators' mirror-foundries claim the giant's own reference role for their star, a quarrel between two standards offices that the arbitration circuit has repeatedly declined to standardize.
## Tinctor

- **Settled:** 2314 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5602379190880667904, K brown dwarf, 58.12 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked rocks and mooned ice — and, far out past the cold, a temperate airy two-mooned world at 267 K: the dyers' gardens.
- **Links:** Concord gate to 171 Pup (the Union trunk beyond); Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 2927791352138805504 and Tureis's star; Mesh gates to HIP 34361 and Gaia EDR3 5544880780046222464.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2316, for the seat of the Dyers' Guild: *Tinctor. A dyer. Certified apt.* The Committee's note observes that the temperate world grows every dye-plant in the catalogue and that the system has, therefore, *certified itself.*

Tinctor colors the League. The Dyers' Guild works the temperate world's dye gardens and the great vathouses — the certified color cards that set what *League blue* and *Meridian black* mean, sold under seal and defended in the Freeports' counterfeit courts with a success rate the guild publishes annually, by color. The Concord gate beyond the border carries the Union's fashion trade through the system's five routes, and Tinctor's border-customs hall, where USE buyers learn the guild's only bilingual sentence (*the color is the certificate*), is the League's easternmost daily diplomacy. The custom is the Card Day: each season's new color cards are released at a public reading, where the dyers present each color with its recipe, its light-fastness, and one sentence of its name's history — because, the guild rule runs, *a color without a story is just a wavelength.* The grievance is Phoenicia — the Commonwealth's purple, whose certification the Dyers honor and whose prices they resent, an annual negotiation conducted, with great ceremony, entirely through each other's published color cards.
## 171 Pup *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2318; never promoted.
- G-dwarf, 49.83 ly — hot mooned rocks, a shallow hot ocean world (28% sea), a heavy cold airy stone. The Union's westernmost trunk spur, now the USE end of the Concord crossing to Tinctor and the League beyond. The customs station's crews work two calendars and two tariffs and are examined annually on both; their mess-hall saying, painted above the door, reads *two suns, one paycheck.*
## Fairweather

- **Settled:** 2236 (Union trunk charter, western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5701750715316821248 (GJ 317), M, 49.51 ly
- **Population:** 38 million
- **System:** locked hot rocks inward, a steam ocean world (66% sea), then **Fairweather** proper — a temperate airy planet at 270 K with a working atmosphere, gentle seasons, and no complaints — with mooned cold stone and paired gas giants beyond.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 40693, GJ 326 B, and 171 Pup.
- **Registry name:** the survey report's whole description was two words — *fair weather* — and the settlement charter, filed by the tender crews of the western spur, asked the Union Naming Board to keep them. The Board's minute: *ratified, on the report's own evidence, which the Board notes is the only evidence ever submitted with a settlement petition in verse.*

Fairweather is the trunk's western garden. The temperate world's rolling country — river valleys, grain terraces, orchard slopes under a mild red sun — was settled late in the trunk era by crews who had seen the furnace worlds and knew exactly what a fair one was worth, and its farms, mills, and market towns have fed the western spur for two centuries, through the Partition, the Slip Wars, and the Concord that now runs League trade past its skies. The population is old trunk stock — quiet, municipal, and privately proud that the system's name has never once required an adjective. Fairweather's best thing is its weather service: the world's forecasting office, founded because the settlers' own poetry demanded proof, is the Union's reference standard for atmospheric modeling, and its storm sirens, tested weekly, are the sound of childhood for half the spur. The custom is the Report's Verse: each year's first forecast of spring is read aloud, in the old two-word format, by the office's newest hire, to the town square, whatever the weather is actually doing — Fairweather being fair, the Board notes, in averages. The grievance is the border: Concord traffic parks hulls in the system's outer orbits, and the world's astronomers have petitioned, every session since 2403, for a dark-sky ordinance the trade office keeps declining, politely, at the speed of tariffs.
## HIP 40693 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2239; never promoted.
- K-dwarf, 41.03 ly — hot airy mooned rocks, a shallow ocean world (67% sea, steaming at 357 K), and a long cold tail out to a 430-Earth-mass far giant. A trunk-fuel spur of the western runs. The depot's one distinction is its library: the crews of the western spur leave their finished books here for whoever comes next, and the shelf — two centuries long, in no order anyone can bear to fix — is the best free reading in the Union.
## Lamplight

- **Settled:** 2244 (Union trunk charter, western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3085716990368639744 (GJ 1103 A), M, 30.24 ly
- **Population:** 7.6 million
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked airy rocks — the third of them, **Lamplight** proper, a cool dusk world at 260 K with a working atmosphere — and a mooned ice giant behind it.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HL 4, Gaia EDR3 3100542495921276160, and HIP 40693.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified by the Union Naming Board in 2246: the world's dusk belt never sees a true day, and the first settlement's charter, which required a lit lamp in every window on every street, made the name before the Board did. The Board's minute records that the petition arrived *already written on the sign.*

Lamplight is the western spur's twilight town-world. The cool dusk belt's settlements — lamplit orchard towns, wool towns, and the glassworks that give the lamps their shades — run the spur's domestic trade, and the world's whole culture is its light: the lamp-keepers' guild is the settlement's oldest institution, the lamp-lighting hour is the day's true noon, and children learn the streets not by names but by lamps — *the corner of the Blue Wick, three past the Foundry.* The best thing on Lamplight is the glass: the dusk works, running on the ember light's slow heat, make the Union's best lampware, and Fairweather's municipal offices specify Lamplight shades by ordinance. The custom is the First Lamp: every settlement's founding lamp is never extinguished — the oldest has burned since 2244 — and on each settlement anniversary the whole town dims to that one flame for the hour of the counting of the years, told aloud by the oldest resident. The grievance is the tariff on glass, which the Union's trade office assesses as luxury goods, an opinion the glassworks' annual petition, filed with a sample case and a lit shade, has been correcting without effect since 2261.
## HL 4 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2247; never promoted.
- A barely-star K ember, 21.01 ly — locked heavy rocks, a locked temperate ice giant, a small hot ocean world, and a cold tail. A junction depot of the western trunk's inner spur. The depot crews keep the register's most literal tradition: because the star has no proper name and the system therefore no entry in the almanac's index, the crews hand-letter their own — the current board reads *HL 4, pop. 340, please write in ink.*
## GJ 3379 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2250; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 16.99 ly — locked furnace rocks, bare cold pebbles, and a chain of mooned giants; the inner western spur's quietest junction. The depot's two hundred residents run a cargo-swap so famous among crews that pilots detour for it: leave anything, take anything, the manifest is honor — a system the Union's auditors have audited four times and found, to their own published surprise, perfectly balanced.
## Luyten's Star

- **Settled:** 2253 (Union trunk charter, inner western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3139847906307949696 (Luyten's Star), M, 12.26 ly
- **Population:** 15 million
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked airy rocks — the second of them, the settled world, a heavy dusk planet at 281 K (five Earth masses, working atmosphere, terminator country) — with mooned airy stones and ice beyond.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 1111 and GJ 3379.
- **Registry name:** kept under the standing rule: the star was named for the astronomer who spent a lifetime cataloguing the Sun's nearest neighbors, and the Union Naming Board's minute observes that *the system settled by that catalog now is one, which the Board considers the neatest ratification in the register.*

Luyten's is the surveyor's world. The heavy dusk planet's terminator country — a band of five-Earth-gravity towns and terraces under the ember light — became the Union's great school of stellar cartography: the frontier survey office, founded here in the trunk era because the crews were anyway always mapping something, grew into the Union's western survey institute, and its alumni have charted every route from the Concord border to the deep spur. The population is heavy-world stock, survey-crew families, and the famous patience of people who breathe deeply on principle. The best thing here is the catalogue itself: the institute's open list of nearby systems, continued in Luyten's own tradition, is the reference every polity's navigators use, with the Freeports' editions politely noted, in the preface, for *enthusiasm.* The custom is the New Entry: when the survey confirms a previously unlisted system, the whole settlement walks out at dusk-bell and reads the new name aloud, three times, into the ember light — the world's way of saying the map grew again. The grievance is gravity: at five Earth masses, everything costs more to launch, and the settlement's freight board has filed, annually since 2257, for a trunk-rate concession *for the weight we carry for everyone*, a petition the Union's tariff office has answered, annually, with a form letter the board now collects.
## Kettle

- **Gate opened:** 2256 (last of the inner spur's trunk charters).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-703790044252850688 (GJ 1111), brown dwarf, 11.68 ly
- **Population:** 6.1 million
- **System:** an ember primary holding three locked airy rocks in tight orbits — the second of them, **Kettle** proper, a hot-dusk airy world at 314 K — and nothing else; the whole system inside a week.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 54035, Luyten's Star, GJ 388, and HIP 33226.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified by the Union Naming Board in 2258 over the Registry's proposal of *Inner Spur Junction 4*: the hot-dusk world made the natural rest-stop of the inner western runs, and the crews had called it the Kettle for a decade before anyone asked. The Board's minute: *ratified; the name is the timetable.*

Kettle is the inner spur's roundhouse. Four trunk routes meet at the hot-dusk world, and the settlement built itself around the stop: the great rest-halls of the terminator band — warm enough that the crews' cold-weather gear comes off for the first time in weeks — the canteens famous across the spur, and the dispatch towers that run the inner western schedule to the minute. The population is canteen families, dispatchers, and the engine-works that keep the spur's tenders running, and the world's best thing is its broth: the Kettle Bowl, a station tradition so standardized that its recipe is printed on the Union's inner-spur timetable, edition by edition, unchanged since 2261, because some things the crews will not let the schedule touch. The custom is the Warming: each arriving crew's first hour is off-tariff, by charter, spent in the rest-halls getting warm, on the grounds — the charter's own line — that *cold crews make bad decisions, and the Union needs neither.* The grievance is the Freeports' rest-stop packets at the Concord end of the runs, which undercut the Warming with prices the canteen-clerks describe as *possible only where the broth is water.*
## HIP 54035 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2259; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 8.30 ly from Sol — one of the near ones: a locked furnace super-earth, a hot locked ocean world (54% sea, one moon), bare cold stone, and paired far giants. The inner spur's deep-vault station: the Union's emergency seed, record, and matter archives sit in the far giants' quiet moons, on the principle the station's charter states in full: *what Sol must survive, must not sit in Sol.*
## Shoalwater

- **Settled:** 2262 (Union charter, inner western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3864972938605115520 (GJ 406), brown dwarf, 7.86 ly
- **Population:** 4.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace rock and a hot locked ocean; then the settled world — a heavy locked twilight ocean planet at 290 K, shallow seas across 14% of its face — with paired ice giants behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 54035 and HIP 57548.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2264: the heavy world's shallow twilight seas made shoal-water the first thing every survey noted and the last thing anyone forgot, and the settlement petitioned under the word. The Union Naming Board's minute: *ratified; the hydrographic evidence is the petition.*

Shoalwater is the spur's close-in fish world. The heavy dusk planet's shallow seas — rich, warm, and uncannily calm under the ember light — run the Union's oldest off-world mariculture: the shelf farms, the oyster beds, and the famous Shoalwater pearls that the trade office, to its lasting regret, once tried to classify as industrial output. The population is heavy-world fisher stock and the shore towns' canneries, and the world's best thing is its tides: the ice giants' slow pull makes Shoalwater's seas rise and fall on a six-day rhythm, the settlement's true week, kept in every calendar and kitchen on the spur. The custom is the First Bed: each season's new oyster bed is planted by the crews' youngest, at the spring low, in view of the whole town, because — the fishers' rule — *the sea should always know who's new.* The grievance is gravity again, the canneries' launch costs for the heavy runs, a number the freight board and the tariff office disagree on annually with the deep sincerity of institutions who both know exactly what the other is going to say.
## Fourcross

- **Settled:** 2265 (Union charter, inner western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3796072592206250624 (HIP 57548), M, 11.01 ly
- **Population:** 970,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, cold mooned stones, and a chain of giants — nothing to live on and four roads out, which the charter rated sufficient.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Shoalwater's star, Gaia EDR3 5393446658454453632, GJ 473 A, and GJ 3622.
