- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2267: four trunk routes cross at the junction, and the crews' own sign — FOURCROSS, hand-painted on the first depot's airlock in 2263 — was submitted to the Union Naming Board as the petition. The Board's minute: *ratified, with the sign, which is hereby protected.*

Fourcross is the inner spur's crossroads depot. The junction's transfer halls — cargo-swap, crew rotation, the timetable office whose printed boards are the spur's pulse — serve four routes' traffic with the Union's particular genius for making a stopover feel like a small civilization, and the settlement's population of dispatchers, tally-clerks, and canteen families keeps the crossing's old custom: at each shift change, the four routes' departure boards are read aloud together, as one sentence, in four voices, so that *no crew departs without hearing where the others went.* The grievance is the sign's admirers — preservationists petition annually to move the original FOURCROSS sign to the Sol museum, and the depot's crews petition annually to remind the museum that the sign is load-bearing, which it is, structurally, on purpose.
## Coldspring

- **Settled:** 2268 (Union charter, inner western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5393446658454453632, brown dwarf, 13.19 ly
- **Population:** 3.0 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked rocks — the second of them, **Coldspring** proper, a cold airy dusk world at 242 K — with mooned ice and a three-mooned ice giant behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Fourcross and GJ 3618.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2270: the cold world's geothermal spring belt is the only reason anyone stopped, and the first settlement grew at the springs the crews had been boiling their kettles on for a decade. The Board's minute: *ratified; the springs were the founding document.*

Coldspring is the spur's warm-room world. The cold dusk planet's spring belt — a string of geothermal valleys where the ember light and the deep heat together make the one belt of open water for a hundred light-years in any direction — carries the settlement's spa towns, greenhouses, and the famous spring-fed hatcheries that stock Shoalwater's beds and Lamplight's cold ponds. The population is greenhouse families, spring-keepers, and a health trade the Union's medical office quietly rates as the spur's best. The custom is the Sharing of the Water: each spring's outflow is channeled through the town's public bath-hall before any private use, by charter, in the settlement's oldest sentence — *warmth first for everyone, then for anything* — and the grievance is the frost beyond the belt: the world's empty continents, which the survey office wants to terrace, the conservators want to keep, and the assembly has been voting on, in one form or another, since 2274, with the patience of people whose bath is already warm.
## Warmspring

- **Settled:** 2271 (Union charter, inner western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5254061535106490112 (GJ 3618), brown dwarf, 15.76 ly
- **Population:** 4.1 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks — the second of them, **Warmspring** proper, a warm airy dusk world at 308 K, hot-spring country — a bare locked stone, a small steaming ocean world (one moon), and a two-mooned ice giant.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Coldspring's star and HIP 61874.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2273 with a straight face the Board has never since reproduced: the same survey run that charted Coldspring charted this one, its warm spring belt steaming in the dusk, and the crews' paired names went to the Union Naming Board together. The Board's minute: *both ratified; the Register now contains the Springs, which it should have had from the start; the Registry is thanked for its proposal, which was a number.*

Warmspring is the spur's hothouse. The warm dusk world's spring belt — wider, hotter, and steamier than its cold sister's — carries the greenhouse industry that feeds half the inner spur's fresh markets, the greenhouse-school that agronomists from Meilin and Fairweather visit on rotation, and the famous terraced orchids that turn up, improbably, in every Union citation ceremony's lapels. The population is greenhouse families and spring-keepers, and the world's best thing is light-war: the warm belt's engineered canopies, tuned over two centuries, grow more food per lamp-hour than any installation in the Union, a statistic the trade office recites and the settlers recite louder. The custom is the Sister Call: at each solstice, Warmspring's spring-keepers signal Coldspring's across the eleven light-years — one pulse for the year's first planting, answered — because, the keepers' rule runs, *the Springs keep time together or not at all.* The grievance is the naming, forever: the almanac's cross-references keep mistyping the pair, the museum keeps mixing their charters, and the settlements' joint annual letter requesting *clerical diligence* is, by now, the inner spur's most beloved piece of bureaucracy, framed at both ends.
## Southgate

- **Settled:** 2274 (Union charter, western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6133474952391837824 (HIP 61874), M, 26.27 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** an ember-red primary over locked rocks, a hot airy super-earth, cold airy stones, and a six-mooned giant — the junction where the inner spur's roads hand their cargo to the southern runs.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Warmspring's star, HIP 60559, Añañuca, and Gaia EDR3 6109610086872510592.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2276: everything on the western spur heading south crosses here, and the crews called the crossing what it was. The Board's minute: *ratified; the sign is at the customs hall, and it faces south.*

Southgate is the spur's hand-off yard. The junction's transfer depots — bonded halls, crew rotation, the tariff office whose southern rate-cards are the spur's most consulted literature — pass the inner runs' cargo to the southern routes at a tempo the settlement's population of tally-clerks and canteen families describes as *weather with paperwork.* The custom is the Counting Bell: each sol's last transfer is rung in by hand, one clear note per manifest closed, and the crews keep count by ear — the day's number is the town's news. The grievance is the southern rates themselves, which the trade office sets for the whole spur from the comfort of Sol, and which the tariff office's own clerks have, in their private capacity and with their union's blessing, petitioned against annually since 2281, a conflict of interest the Union Naming Board, of all bodies, has cited as *the healthiest thing in the Register.*
## HIP 60559 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2277; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 28.95 ly — locked heavy furnace rocks and bare cold pebbles. A fuel spur on the southern hand-off, running an automated skimming plant and a small crew station. The plant's operators keep a two-century wager book on the annual flare count, stakes settled in canteen credit, and the current record — nine flares in one sol, set 2333 — is honored with a plaque and, on its centennial, a re-enactment involving the whole station and a very convincing alarm.
## Sunhill

- **Settled:** 2280 (Union charter, southern run).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3487062064765702272 (HIP 58345), K, 33.18 ly
- **Population:** 8.7 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked furnace rocks, hot airy stones, then the settled world — a warm airy planet at 316 K with one large moon, its hill country the only farm belt for a hundred light-years — with paired gas giants and mooned ice beyond.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 60559 and GJ 3668.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2282: the warm hills were the first thing every southbound crew saw at the end of the furnace run, and the settlement petitioned under the word they'd all used at the rail. The Board's minute: *ratified; the name has been in oral circulation among the crews since before the Board was constituted, which makes this ratification, the Board notes, a formality and a pleasure.*

Sunhill is the southern run's garden. The warm hill country's terraced orchards and vine rows — apricots, olives, and the famous Sunhill amber that the trunk's canteens ration with visible pain — feed and sweeten the southern routes, and the world's hill towns, with their porch culture and their evening promenades in the last of the K sun's amber light, are the run's one beloved stop between the furnaces and the deep spur. The population is orchard families, vintner clans, and the hill councils whose irrigation law — the most carefully adjudicated water rights in the western spur — is taught in the Union's administrative academy as the standard case of *how to share a hill.* The custom is the First Apricot: each season's first ripe fruit is picked by the town's oldest and youngest together, from the town's founding tree, and eaten on the spot, in the square, before anyone sells, ships, or mentions a single apricot otherwise. The grievance is the frost that comes some years to the high rows, the crop insurance that follows, and the adjusters from Sol whose comprehension of a hill's microclimates the vintners describe, in the co-op's annual letter, as *seasonal, in every sense.*
## GJ 3668 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2283; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 37.53 ly — locked furnace rocks and a long chain of mooned ice giants. A three-gate relay of the southern run, crewed by one hundred and eleven people and their families. The relay's claim is the register's oldest continuing joke: its beacon code, chosen by the founding crew and grandfathered by the Union Navigation Office ever since, spells out in trunk Morse an ancient, unprintable complaint about the furnace run, which every southern pilot reads, transcribes, and passes on with a straight face.
## GJ 1140 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2286; never promoted.
- A white dwarf's hard white light, 40.02 ly — locked rocks, paired locked ice giants, and a long cold tail. A three-gate relay and the southern run's official "check-your-shadows" station: crews use the dead star's pinpoint light to sight hull alignment, a calibration tradition the Union Navigation Office has never once written down and never once let lapse.
## HIP 56157 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2289; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 43.95 ly — a locked furnace rock, a locked puffed giant, and a temperate 111-Earth-mass giant: the dead end of the southern run's oldest finger. The skim station on the temperate giant's calm works the run's last fuel point; its four crew families' one rule, posted at the mess, is the spur's last word on solitude: *whoever arrives is family; whoever stays is staff.*
## GJ 398 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2292; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 49.82 ly — locked heavy rocks, mooned ice, and paired giants with captured companions. The southern spur's Concord-era gateway: a relay station and tariff point on the new crossing toward GJ 3553. The station crews' private name for the system, used in every log but never once on a form, is *the Sigh* — for the sound the old pumps make when the customs cycle ends, which the crews maintain is the only honest summary of the tariff code ever recorded.
## Portorium

- **Settled:** 2318 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3845829101135351296 (GJ 3553), M, 57.50 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a bare temperate stone, and a mooned ice giant — a system the certified survey grades *functional*, holding six gates, two of them Concord crossings to the Union trunk.
- **Links:** Concord gates to GJ 398 and τ01 Hya (USE); Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 3080857458209146368, HIP 44295, Gaia EDR3 3080039799513337216, and Gaia EDR3 3844632759469347584.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2320, for the seat of the Customs Guild, the League's certified border office: *Portorium. A toll. Certified apt.* The Committee's note observes that the system holds two Concord gates and that the name therefore *earns twice.*

Portorium is the League's toll-booth. The Customs Guild's border halls — bonded warehouses built to two codes, arbitration rooms wired to two registries, and the famous bilingual tariff boards, certified and stamped on both sides — process the USE-Meridian trade of the whole western quarter, and the settlement's population of assessors, brokers, and the translators every border eventually employs runs the least glamorous and most indispensable institution in the League. The custom is the Counting: at each dusk the day's duties are tallied in public, both currencies, and posted to the hall's door — because, the Guild's charter holds, *a toll kept in the dark becomes a tax, and a toll kept in the light stays a toll.* The grievance is structural and cheerful: the Union's customs office mirrors every procedure here in its own halls beyond the gates, and the two offices' annual exchange of audit teams, robes, and politeness is the quarter's best-attended diplomatic theater, reviewed by both sides' newspapers under the shared headline *correct.*
## Puteus

- **Settled:** 2321 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3080857458209146368, M, 56.40 ly
- **Population:** 490,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked steam ocean (69% sea), a mooned airy rock, and mooned cold stone — a system of deep water and deep quiet.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 2927791352138805504, Portorium's star, and Gaia EDR3 3136232952593850496; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 3080039799513337216.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2323, for the seat of the Well-Diggers' Guild, the League's water-infrastructure specialists: *Puteus. A well. Certified apt.*

Puteus is where the League drinks. The Well-Diggers' Guild sinks the certified deep wells, cisterns, and water systems of half the League's worlds — the gem-drills that find the ice, the shaft-works that raise it, and the assay that proves it drinkable — and the settlement's population of diggers, assayers, and their famously thirsty families works to the guild's one rule, painted at every shaft head: *you do not certify what you have not drunk.* The custom is the First Draw: each new well's first water is drawn by hand, drunk by the whole crew, and its assay is read aloud with the same solemnity the League gives a verdict, because in the ember quarter, it is one. The grievance is Waterrights, whose tanker rates undercut the guild's wells on the dry worlds, an economics the diggers' annual report describes as *buying rain and selling thirst*, a phrase that has entered three polities' parliamentary record and one, so far, into poetry.
## Fistula

- **Settled:** 2324 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3136232952593850496, brown dwarf, 57.79 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** an ember primary over three locked rocks in tight orbits — the whole system a fortnight wide, which the certified survey calls *cozy* and the Guild calls *adequate.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Puteus's star, GJ 3410, and Gaia EDR3 3155195714240292736; Mesh gate to Alzirr.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2326, for the seat of the Plumbers' Guild, the League's pipe- and pressure-works specialists: *Fistula. A pipe. Certified apt.* The Committee's appended note — *the medical faculties of four polities have written to complain; the Committee declines to diagnose* — is the most-quoted line in the Register.

Fistula keeps the League's pressure honest. The Plumbers' Guild builds and certifies every pipe, valve, and pressure line the League's worlds run on — the deep infrastructure nobody sees and everybody uses — and the settlement's population of pipe-fitters, welders, and the assay crews who test every joint at twice its rating works to the trade's single oldest maxim, cast in bronze at the hall door: *everything is under pressure; behave accordingly.* The custom is the Tapping: a new line is opened with a ceremony of one closed valve, one opened, and total silence until the gauge settles — the Guild's theology in one minute, and the quarter's most punctual liturgy. The grievance is callouts: the Guild's emergency crews are famous, expensive, and always worth it, and the annual invoice book, published as required by League law, is read at Cardo's Exchange for its drama.
## Ephemeris

- **Settled:** 2327 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3126453655659835136 (GJ 3410), white dwarf (T=6,383 K), 59.01 ly
- **Population:** 2.9 million
- **System:** a dead sun's hard white light over a tight ladder of locked worlds — two of them habitable: a dusk airy rock at 314 K and a locked ocean world at 298 K, 85% of its face under sea — the twin worlds the Guild calls *the instruments.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Fistula's star, Alzirr, and Gaia EDR3 3117320802840630784; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 3325157736332326784, HIP 29860, and Gaia EDR3 3155195714240292736.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2329, for the seat of the Almanac Guild, the League's astronomers of record: *Ephemeris. A table of times. Certified apt.*

Ephemeris keeps the League's calendar. The twin locked worlds around a white dwarf give the Almanac Guild the steadiest reference in the quarter — a dead star's unchanging light, two habitable clocks — and the Guild's great observatory, strung between the worlds, publishes the certified ephemerides every polity's calendar, harvest table, and arbitration deadline ultimately cites, including Certainty's, by affectionate treaty. The population is astronomers, calendar-printers, and the farmers of the dusk rock who grow the tables' paper — a settlement that lives by the quarter's time and lives on it, since every almanac sold carries the Guild's stamp and the Guild's breakfast. The custom is the Correction: each year's almanac closes with the errata of the last, read aloud at the print-hall by the youngest astronomer, because, the Guild's charter runs, *time is certified, not claimed, and the correction is the certificate.* The grievance is leap-time — the League's polities keep five local reckonings and the Guild's annual comparison table of the drift between them is the most quietly devastating document published anywhere.
## Urushi

- **Settled:** 2328 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3325157736332326784, M, 60.15 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace giant, a hot airy rock, paired mooned heavy stones, and a three-mooned ice giant — the covenant's survey: *stone for the workshops, quiet for the lacquer.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 25716, HIP 29525, and HIP 29860; Mesh gates to Ephemeris's star and Gaia EDR3 3348325893730781952.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the lacquer-workers' families who left the Union with the Compact's first wave and signed their charter under the old craft's name: *Urushi*, the tree, the sap, the patience. The covenant register's line: *a hundred coats, each one thin, each one honest.*

Urushi is the Compact's lacquer town. The covenant's workshops — the kobo-halls where the ember quarter's instruments, hulls, and heirlooms receive the lacquer that outlasts their owners — supply the League's durable-goods trade with a finish no Freeport copyist has matched, and the settlement's families keep the craft's law: the sap is tapped, never forced; the coats dry in their own weather; and the maker's name is written where only the next maker will find it. The customs are the craft's: the First Coat (a new apprentice's first piece is lacquered entirely by the master's hands while the apprentice watches, so that *the first thing you make here is watching*), and the Showing, the annual exhibition where every workshop's failures are displayed beside its masterworks, because — the covenant's oldest teaching — *the coat that ran teaches what the coat that held only proves.* The grievance is synthetic lacquer — the Union's instant-cure substitutes, whose warranties the workshops have outlived so often that the covenant keeps a wall of expired guarantee certificates as its only advertisement.
## Washi

- **Settled:** 2330 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3390818582171734656 (HIP 25716), M, 56.04 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** a bright ember primary over a locked furnace rock, a hot bare stone, and a mooned cold pebble — the covenant's survey: *water, fiber, patience, in that order.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 3410478399832994688 and Urushi's star; Mesh gates to HIP 21482 and Gaia EDR3 3348325893730781952.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant of paper-makers, under the old word for the craft's soul: *Washi*, the paper that is made by hand and lasts by choice. The covenant register: *what is written here will outlive what was written about us.*

Washi is the Compact's paper town. The covenant's mills — the fiber fields on the cold moon, the clear-water works, the drying lofts where the quarter's finest archival paper is couched sheet by sheet — supply the Compact's schools, the League's notarial registries, and the collectors of a dozen ports with the paper that centuries of record demand, and the families who make it keep the craft's full liturgy: the fibers' names, the waters' seasons, and the rule that no sheet ships until its maker has held it to the light. The custom is the Tearing Test: each day's first sheet is torn, by hand, in front of the whole mill, and the way it tears is the day's grade — the covenant's daily honesty, published in the mill's ledger, in ink that dries on its own paper. The grievance is humidity — the trade routes' cargo holds, which the mills' annual letter on archival conditions grades, ship by ship, to the consternation of every haulier in the quarter.
## Soba

- **Settled:** 2332 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3410478399832994688, brown dwarf, 55.32 ly
- **Population:** 460,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a hot locked shallow sea, mooned airy stones, and paired giants with three and four moons — a mill system: stone, water, and room to work.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 3195979005694112768, HIP 21482, and Aldebaran; (regional approach to Washi's routes).
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the miller and noodle-making families who asked, in their charter's single petition line, that the settlement carry *the food everyone makes room for.* The covenant register entered *Soba* without comment, the register's highest compliment.

Soba is the Compact's flour-and-noodle town. The covenant's mills grind Arva's grain and the Compact's own buckwheat — grown on the mooned airy stone's terraces — into the flour that crosses the mesh in every pantry crate, and the settlement's noodle houses, which serve every shift, every polity, and every grief, are the quarter's consensus on what a meal is. The population is miller families, farmers, and the hostel-keepers whose tables the covenant's rule keeps open: *no crew eats alone here unless they ask to.* The custom is the First Bowl: each sol's first noodles are served by the mill's own miller, to whoever is first at the counter, on the covenant's belief that *the day should begin with somebody fed by somebody's hand.* The grievance is flour futures — the Cardo exchange prices the covenant's grain, the covenant disagrees with the pricing, and the annual letter to the Exchange is composed, by tradition, over dinner, which explains both its tone and its length.
## Ama

- **Settled:** 2334 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3195979005694112768, brown dwarf, 54.37 ly
- **Population:** 880,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace giant, a locked hot ocean, and paired locked ice giants — then the settled world, a cool ocean planet at 278 K, a third of its face under cold clear sea, with a five-mooned giant riding behind it.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 23147, HIP 18859, GJ 9163 A, and Soba's star; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 3178132420149741824.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the diving families who signed under the old word for the women who harvested the sea on one breath: *Ama.* The covenant register's line: *the sea keeps its promises; so did they; so do we.*

Ama is the Compact's sea-harvest town. The cool ocean world's clear cold seas — dived the old way, on breath and knowledge, under the ember light — yield the harvest the quarter's kitchens and clinics both crave: shellfish, kelp, and the famous Ama pearls, seeded, gathered, and graded by the diving families whose breath-training is the settlement's curriculum and whose catch-ledgers are its law. The population is diving families, kelp farmers, and the boat-wrights whose hulls the whole Compact buys, and the customs are the sea's: the First Dive of each season is made by the youngest certified diver, on one breath, with the whole settlement at the surface; and no harvest day ends without the counting of the catch by every crew, together, on the shore — because, the covenant teaches, *the sea shares with those who count together.* The grievance is the trawlers — Silverfin's far boats cross the cold seas in the deep seasons, and the covenant's boundary markers, renewed each year by hand, are cited in the League's drift courts with a weight that surprises everyone who has never watched an Ama crew come home empty.
## Sakura

- **Settled:** 2336 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-256487136159035136 (HIP 23147), M, 54.19 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a bright ember primary over locked furnace rocks, bare mooned stones, and an airy cold rock — the system the covenant holds for one reason: the Regional gate to Aurora system itself.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Ama's star, Gaia EDR3 271438845031711104, HIP 26505, and the Aurora system.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant under the emigrants' own word — *Sakura*, the blossom that falls at its perfect moment — for the garden of remembrance every family of the Compact plants a tree in when someone dies. The covenant register: *we could not keep the old country; we keep its springs.*

Sakura is the Compact's doorway. All roads to the Aurora system's great seat stage through this junction, and the settlement's life is built for arrival and for grief: the staging yards and guest-halls of the gateway trade, and the memorial groves — terraced down the cold rock's sheltered vales, a tree for every death in the Compact's two centuries, tended by every family that can reach it — that make the system the Compact's quiet capital of memory. The population is gateway crews, gardeners, and the archives-keepers who maintain the groves' registry, in which every tree's name is read aloud each spring at the blossom festival, however long it takes, because, the covenant's rule runs, *the reading is the remembering, and we have all day, and longer.* The custom is the Planting: every visitor to the Aurora system, of any polity, is offered one tree in the groves — planted under their own name, to await whatever they choose to be remembered by. The grievance is frost: some springs the blossom comes late or not at all, and the gardeners' letters to the Compact's weather office about the *irregularities of remembrance* are the only official correspondence in the League's records to be filed under both science and liturgy.
## Taiko

- **Settled:** 2338 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-271438845031711104, M, 64.39 ly
- **Population:** 570,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks and a ladder of mooned ice giants — a junction system whose chief acoustic property, the covenant insists, is that it carries.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Sakura's star and HIP 18324; Mesh gates to HIP 26505, HIP 23140, Gaia EDR3 204584689771692288, and HD 24409C.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the drummers, dancers, and festival-makers who signed under the drum's own word: *Taiko.* The covenant register: *the year has a pulse; somebody must keep it.*

Taiko keeps the Compact's time. The covenant's great festival halls — drum workshops, costume vaults, and the dance-yards where the Compact's whole calendar is rehearsed — stage the quarter's loudest, brightest, and most punctual celebrations, and six routes' worth of traffic adjusts its schedule around the festival almanac the covenant publishes, which other polities' navigation offices attach to their boards because crews plan leave around it. The population is drum-making families, dancers, and the hauliers of celebration, and the custom is the Sunrise: every festival opens at dawn with one drum, struck once, by the covenant's newest member, before anyone sings — the settlement's founding rule being that *the year should know it has begun, and so should the drummer.* The grievance is noise, filed annually, affectionately, by the observatory-schools of three neighboring polities, whose measurements the covenant publishes beside its own under the shared heading *the sky's opinion, invited.*
## Hoshimi

- **Settled:** 2340 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-474648763011926272 (HIP 18324), K, 68.38 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** a K sun over hot airy heavy rocks, a captured locked moon at 229 K, a temperate three-mooned giant (241 K — the residential orbit), and a 478-Earth-mass far colossus with captured companions.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Taiko's star, HD 24409C, and Gaia EDR3 492053585402171264; Mesh gate to HIP 14286.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the star-watchers, who signed under the old word for the pleasure of looking up: *Hoshimi.* The covenant register: *the ancestors navigated by stars; we keep the appointment.*

Hoshimi watches the sky. The covenant's observatories — riding the temperate giant's calm moons, working the quiet between the routes — keep the Compact's own sky-culture: the star festivals timed to real events, the navigation-star curriculum the Compact's schools teach, and the famous Hoshimi almanac, which cross-references Annie's great census with the covenant's own annotations of which stars the old songs are about. The population is watcher families, instrument-keepers, and the farmers of the giant's moons, and the custom is the Naming Night: each year's newly certified watchers name one star from the unnamed catalogue, choosing by lot from the entries no polity has claimed — the covenant's belief being that *the sky should have more names than committees, and some of them should be given at a party.* The grievance is light — the junction's own traffic washes the faint stars from the outer observatories, and the covenant's annual dimming schedule for the approach lamps, negotiated with the pilots' guilds, is the most carefully observed quiet in the quarter.
## Tsunagu

- **Settled:** 2342 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-466294295706341760 (HIP 14286), K, 76.82 ly
- **Population:** 1.0 million
- **System:** a Sun-like K star over mooned furnace rocks and an airy hot stone — a system of crossroads, the covenant's charter says, *and the crossroads are enough.*
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Hoshimi's star, HD 24409C, and Gaia EDR3 462116220235041920; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 492053585402171264, HIP 15673, and Gaia EDR3 467356110409096832.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the factor families who connect the Compact's crafts to the mesh's markets — under the verb of their trade: *Tsunagu*, to join, to tie together. The covenant register: *the knot is the smallest covenant; we just work larger.*

Tsunagu ties the Compact to the world. The factor houses of the junction — Urushi's lacquer, Washi's paper, Ama's pearls, Soba's flour, all brokered here under one roof of famously fair dealing — move the Compact's craft-trade to six routes, and the covenant's pride is its escrow custom: every deal over a year's earnings is witnessed by three families, none of them the buyer's or the seller's, because, the register holds, *trust is a knot that needs three hands.* The population is factor families, warehousers, and the tea-house keepers whose negotiations-tables are the quarter's most productive furniture. The custom is the Bow: at each deal's closing, buyer and seller exchange one bow of exactly the depth of the deal's difficulty — the quarter's most eloquent accounting, read fluently by every factor in the League. The grievance is the Freeports' brokers, who call Tsunagu's three-hands custom *inefficient*, and whose insolvency rate the covenant publishes annually, with the same three hands, one of which is, everyone notes, for waving.
## Tanada

- **Settled:** 2344 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-443016706832479488 (HIP 15673), K, 77.97 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked furnace rocks, hot airy stones, then the settled world — a cool airy planet at 255 K with two moons, its valley terraces the covenant's whole argument — with paired giants and captured companions beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Tsunagu's star and Gaia EDR3 439801494314097664; Mesh gates to HIP 17491 and Gaia EDR3 432824886875420672.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the rice-growing families, who signed under the word for the terraced paddies their ancestors cut into mountains: *Tanada.* The covenant register: *the ancestors terraced mountains to feed their children; the mountains remember; so do we.*

Tanada grows the Compact's rice. The cool world's terraced valleys — engineered step-farms running water downhill in the ancestors' mathematics, under the two moons' patient seasons — carry the paddies that feed the Compact's tables and half the mesh's pantries besides, and the settlement's farming families keep the covenant's whole liturgy: the water-law read aloud at each planting, the harvest festival whose drum is borrowed from Taiko every year and returned blessed, and the rule that no terrace is ever left unplanted *while any family remains to plant it.* The custom is the First Water: each spring's opening of the terraces' upper gate is done by the year's youngest certified farmer, one hand on the sluice, the whole valley watching the water find its way down — because, the register holds, *the water is the elder; we only open the door.* The grievance is seed-right — the Union's agronomy office keeps proposing "optimized" strains for the terraces, and the covenant's annual letter declining them is the same every year: *the terrace feeds what the terrace knows.*
## Manyo

- **Settled:** 2346 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-244808226728958848 (HIP 17491), K, 80.89 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** a K sun over locked furnace rocks, airy stones, and a 418-Earth-mass colossus with captured companions — and then, far out at 48 au, the gift: a warm ocean world (291 K, 45% sea, two moons) riding the long light.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Tanada's star, Gaia EDR3 239732949773406976, HIP 17666B, and Gaia EDR3 439801494314097664; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 237363467857253504.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the poets, who signed under the word for the oldest anthology: *Manyo*, ten thousand leaves. The covenant register's dedication is itself the register's most-quoted poem: *we asked for a far shore; the sky answered with water; write it down.*

Manyo is the Compact's far shore. The warm ocean world at the system's edge — long seasons, slow light, the great colossus hanging in the winter sky like a lantern — is the poets' covenant's home: the anthology-halls where the Compact's whole written art is collected, the retreat cottages where any member of any polity may come to write, and the shore villages whose fishing feeds the muse as well as the table. The population is poets, fishers, archivists, and the hostels of a settlement that considers hospitality a literary form; the custom is the Reading: each dusk at the anthology-hall, one poem is read aloud — drawn by lot from two centuries of the collection, resident and visitor and ancestor equally likely — and the far sea's answer is whatever the weather does next. The grievance is the mail — Manyo's submissions arrive by the mesh's slowest bundle, and the anthology's annual apology for the delay, composed as a poem, has become the most-requested piece in the collection, which the poets describe, with a shrug that travels, as *the genre's revenge.*
## Hashi

- **Settled:** 2348 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-239732949773406976, M, 78.47 ly
- **Population:** 760,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a hot locked shallow sea, a heavy bare stone, and a chain of mooned giants — a system whose six roads out are the whole story.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HD 19071, HIP 17666B, and Gaia EDR3 432824886875420672; Mesh gates to GJ 3190, Gaia EDR3 237363467857253504, and Manyo's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the ferry crews and bridge-keepers — under the word for the oldest crossing: *Hashi*, the bridge. The covenant register: *the water does not care how wide it is; somebody still has to cross.*

Hashi carries the Compact's ferries. The junction's ferry yards — the short-haul hulls, the passenger packets, the great car-floats that move whole settlements' freight between the Compact's scattered worlds — run the quarter's densest small-ship trade, and the covenant's crews keep the ferryman's law: every crossing is public, every schedule is posted, and no vessel ever leaves with a seat empty while someone waits — a rule the register explains has nothing to do with profit and everything to do with *what waiting in the cold does to a person's opinion of their neighbors.* The custom is the Last Crossing: each sol's final ferry is run, by tradition, at whatever hour the last traveler actually arrives, and the ferry-house's lamp stays lit to greet them — the settlement's founding image, painted in the hall, of one small hull and one lit window. The grievance is the weather of six roads' worth of schedules, which the covenant's annual report logs, blames, and forgives, in the same paragraph, like a family.
## Kampo

- **Settled:** 2350 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-240259375324767744 (HD 19071), K, 72.34 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a dim K sun over hot airy mooned rocks, then the settled world — a cool airy planet at 272 K with two moons, its slopes the covenant's whole pharmacy — and a 327-Earth-mass giant beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to 50 Per, GJ 3190, and Hashi's star; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 432824886875420672.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the herbalist physicians, who signed under the old word for their tradition: *Kampo*, the way of the south's medicine, carried whole across the sky. The covenant register: *the ancestors packed their pharmacy; it weighed nothing; it heals everything.*

Kampo is the Compact's medicine world. The cool world's terraced herb gardens — the formulary fields where the covenant's physicians grow, dry, and compound the traditional pharmacopeia alongside the League's licensed practice — supply the Compact's clinics and a growing share of the quarter's, and the settlement's schools of herbal medicine teach a two-register curriculum: the old formulary by lineage, the modern assay by treaty, with the covenant's quiet pride that the two always agree when tested, and its quieter grief when the old one was right first. The population is physician families, gardeners, and the tea-hall keepers whose decoctions are the settlement's social glue. The custom is the Tasting: each batch of the formulary's principal compound is tasted by the school's senior physician, in public, before it ships — the tradition's own quality control, and its oath, in one bitter sip. The grievance is regulation — the League's pharmaceutical registries price the covenant's compounds as *traditional*, a category the physicians note has never once been applied to anything that didn't work.
## Shio

- **Settled:** 2352 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-225668203191521280 (50 Persei), G, 69.12 ly
- **Population:** 570,000
- **System:** a hot bright G sun over mooned furnace rocks and cold airy stones, a five-mooned giant, a six-mooned ice colossus — and then, far out at 58 au, the settled world: a warm ocean planet (306 K, 24% sea, two patient moons) riding the long year.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 204584689771692288 and Gaia EDR3 218519487823384192; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 160221315437314560 and Kampo's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the current-readers, the far-fisher families who signed under the word for the tide: *Shio.* The covenant register: *the ancestors read the water; the water has not changed its language.*

Shio is the Compact's far fishery. The warm ocean world at the system's edge — long seasons, long light, the great colossus's slow weather in the sky — carries the covenant's deep-current fleets, whose skippers read water the way Hoshimi reads stars, and whose harvest, dried and salted in the shore towns' centuries' rhythm, stocks the Compact's pantries and half the mesh's besides. The population is far-fisher families, shore-keepers, and the tide-masters who teach the reading; the custom is the Drawing of the Chart: each season's currents are drawn, by hand, on the harbor-hall's floor in chalk — the elders' knowledge and the young crews' soundings together, one chart, argued over until the sea agrees — because, the covenant holds, *the current belongs to whoever can name it, and naming is a family act.* The grievance is the long year: the outer seasons run slow, the catches run with them, and the covenant's markets' impatience, expressed through Cardo's futures prices, is answered annually by the tide-masters' one-line market commentary: *the sea does not trade on margin.*
## Ai

- **Settled:** 2354 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-160221315437314560, M, 66.81 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a hot ocean world (83% sea), and the settled world — a cool airy planet at 266 K with one large moon — with mooned ice and a four-mooned giant beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 21482, GJ 3320, and Gaia EDR3 163152549013270400; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 204584689771692288 and Shio's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the indigo dyers, under the word for the dye and for the color and for the love: *Ai.* The covenant register's dedication, the most quietly deliberate in the Compact's book: *the ancestors dyed in indigo because it outlasted them; so will we; the word has three meanings; we meant all of them.*

Ai is the Compact's indigo town. The cool world's dye-terraces — the fields of the fermenting indigo plant, the vat-houses whose smells the settlement's children are born knowing — produce the deep blues the Compact's covenant-cloth is cut from: the work clothes of half the League, the Freeport sailors' jackets by contract, and the ceremony cloaks the Compact's assemblies wear, every garment carrying the vat-marks of the family that dyed it. The population is dyer families, weavers in trade with Telar, and the vat-keepers whose fermentation craft is the covenant's guarded patience. The custom is the Vat's Birthday: each vat is begun under its own name, and its first cloth is dyed by the family's youngest, hung at the harbor for the whole town to see — the covenant's belief being that *the color should meet the sky it will spend its life under.* The grievance is synthetics — the Union's instant blues, whose fading the dyers document in a public gallery of two-century-old indigo beside two-year-old substitutes, a display that requires no caption and accepts no argument.
## Kagami

- **Settled:** 2356 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3419611630606267392 (GJ 3320), M, 73.71 ly
- **Population:** 2.1 million
- **System:** a bright ember primary over a locked furnace super-earth and a hot locked ocean, then the twin settled worlds — a cold airy mooned rock at 250 K and its sister at 260 K — with mooned ice and giants beyond.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Aldebaran, Gaia EDR3 3400111547084348928, and Gaia EDR3 3410976925275317760; Regional gate to Ai's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the twin worlds' settlers, who signed under the word for the mirror: *Kagami.* The covenant register: *two worlds, one charter, and the humility of knowing which one you woke on.*

Kagami is the Compact's twin settlement. The two cold-temperate worlds — near enough in the sky that each hangs in the other's dawn — were chartered by one covenant deliberately split: the north world took the herds and the water, the south took the orchards and the ice, and each settlement is governed as the other's mirror, with each law passed on one world reviewed by the other before it takes effect, a constitutional arrangement the League's jurists cite as the quarter's most elegant experiment in *checking oneself before anyone else has to.* The population is herding and orchard families, ferry crews, and the twin assemblies whose debates are conducted, by custom, in alternating worlds, on the theory that *an argument should travel or it isn't one.* The custom is the Reflection: at each solstice, the two settlements exchange one item of ordinary life — a tool, a meal, a song, chosen by lot — and each world must use it as its own until the next exchange, because, the register holds, *the mirror only works if both sides are honest about what they see.* The grievance is the ferry, whose weather-delayed crossings have delayed two laws, four weddings, and one whole budget season, and whose captain's standing title — the Mirror's Keeper — is understood by all parties to be ninety percent apology.
## Bunraku

- **Settled:** 2358 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3400111547084348928, M, 74.28 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked heavy furnace rock and bare mooned stones — a small dark system, which the puppeteers chose the way they choose everything: for what you can't see from the front row.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 3348325893730781952 and Gaia EDR3 3347284175182472832; Mesh gates to Kagami's star and Gaia EDR3 3375742869007697152.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the puppet masters, stagehands, and chanters of the old theater — under their tradition's own name: *Bunraku.* The register: *the puppets need three hands; so does the sky; we counted.*

Bunraku is the Compact's stage. The covenant's great theater — a hull-launched hall that plays every port on four routes, and the home stages on the dark stones — keeps the old drama alive in the ember quarter: the chanted narrative, the instruments, the puppets whose makers' names are cut where only repairers will look, and the annual marathon performance of the whole cycle, from dawn chant to dawn chant, which the quarter's critics attend like pilgrims and the covenant's children like a birthright. The custom is the Lighting: every performance begins with one stagehand, in full view, lighting one lamp — the theater's entire founding statement about who does the work and where. The grievance is the Freeports' "immersive holographic theater" — a medium the covenant's masters have reviewed annually, in print, with the same verdict: *the hands are the story; remove them, and you have removed the news.*
## Tetsu

- **Settled:** 2360 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3347284175182472832, white dwarf (T=10,125 K), 80.55 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** a dead sun's blue-white light over locked rocks, a bare temperate stone, and a mooned ice giant — six roads out, and a forge-light that never wavers.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 3369132536382082432 and Gaia EDR3 3348740650136554368; Mesh gates to Bunraku's star, Gaia EDR3 3375742869007697152, HIP 28066, and Gaia EDR3 3340814889285222400.
- **Registry name:* named by its founding covenant — the smiths, under the word for iron: *Tetsu.* The covenant register, in full: *the ancestors folded steel until it was true; we have simply continued.*

Tetsu is the Compact's forge. The smiths' covenant works the white dwarf's constant, shadowless light and the quiet system's cold quench-stones to make the quarter's fine metalwork: the laminated hull-tools, the kitchen blades every polity's cooks defend like heirlooms, and the ceremonial steel the Compact's assemblies and half the League's honors offices specify by covenant mark. The population is smith families, apprentices, and the quench-yard keepers whose water-rights are the settlement's constitution. The custom is the Folding: every blade's layers are counted at the forge, and the count is stamped where the handle will cover it — because, the smiths' rule runs, *the proof is for the maker, not the market.* The grievance is mass production — the Union's fabricators sell "laminated-pattern" implements at a tenth the price, and the covenant's annual demonstration, in which a fabricator blade and a Tetsu blade are both put to the same work until one of them isn't, is the quarter's most satisfying recurring event, listed in more than one port's almanac as entertainment.
## Nawa

- **Settled:** 2362 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3369132536382082432, M, 81.46 ly
- **Population:** 190,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace super-earth, a locked warm ice giant, and a bare locked stone — a system of fiber, twist, and tension, the surveyors wrote, *and that is a complete inventory.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 3348740650136554368, Gaia EDR3 3375742869007697152, and Gaia EDR3 3369132776897491712; Mesh gate to Tetsu's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the rope-makers — under the word for the rope itself: *Nawa.* The register: *a rope is a promise, twisted.*

Nawa ties the Compact together. The covenant's rope-walks — the long halls where the fiber from Tanada's terraces and Ama's kelp-fields is twisted into hawsers, rigging, and the famous Nawa mooring-lines that Flagfall's register grades as their own class — supply half the quarter's ships with what holds, and the settlement's families keep the walk's rule: every rope is walked its whole length by the maker, once, before it ships, because — the register's oldest line — *the rope must know the hands before the load does.* The custom is the First Twist: each new apprentice's first rope is hung, fully loaded, in the hall's doorway, and everyone who enters touches it on the way past — the settlement's daily reminder, felt in the palm, that somebody was new once. The grievance is synthetic line, which the covenant declines to test against Nawa rope on the stated grounds that *we have already buried what we needed to bury.*
## Chochin

- **Settled:** 2364 (covenant charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3369132776897491712, brown dwarf, 83.45 ly
- **Population:** 660,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked warm ice giant, a heavy locked stone, and the settled world — a cool airy dusk planet at 265 K, where the light is the whole industry.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 3348740650136554368, Gaia EDR3 3375742869007697152, and HIP 31660; Regional gate to Nawa's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the lantern-makers, under the word for the paper lamp: *Chochin.* The register: *the ember sky made us; we simply made ourselves brighter.*

Chochin lights the Compact. The lantern works — paper from Washi, frames from the bamboo groves of the dusk valleys, and the light that half the quarter's ports and every Compact festival hangs — supply the mesh's warm light, and the settlement's dusk-world families keep the craft's seasons: the frame-cutting of the cold months, the papering of the long dusk, and the Great Floating, the settlement's founding festival, when the whole year's lanterns are lit at once down the valley river and the world below sees, for one night, what the ember sky permits everywhere else. The custom is the First Lighting: each apprentice's first lantern is lit at the harbor and sent, unaccompanied, down the river road, and the settlement watches until it turns the bend — the register's line being *a light should be useful to somebody you cannot see.* The grievance is electric glare — the Freeports' flood-lights, which the covenant's annual letter measures, mourns, and outlasts, one bend of the river at a time.
## Rota

- **Settled:** 2366 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3352152743956693376 (HIP 31660), K, 86.84 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a Sun-like K star over a heavy furnace rock, hot mooned stones, and the settled world — a temperate airy planet at 277 K with one moon — with cold airy rocks beyond.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 34567 and e Gem B; Mesh gates to Chochin's star and Gaia EDR3 3344649676604345472.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2368, for the seat of the Wheelwrights' Guild: *Rota. A wheel. Certified apt.* The Committee's note: *the guild makes everything that turns; the name turns; we are satisfied.*

Rota makes the quarter turn. The Wheelwrights' Guild — wheels, gears, turbines, and every certified rotational assembly from Caupona's kitchen mixers to the great gate-anchor bearings — works the temperate world's metal and the League's roads, and the settlement's population of turners, millers, and the gear-cutters whose apprenticeship is the quarter's longest keeps the guild's rule at every bench: *what turns must be true, and what is true must be proved, twice, in opposite directions.* The custom is the Proof Spin: every finished assembly is run, by hand, by its own maker, at the bench, in silence, for exactly one minute, before it ships — a minute the settlement's halls honor collectively, wherever they are, at the bench-bell's ring. The grievance is tolerances — the Union's fabricators ship parts *within spec* that the guild's benches prove to be within spec only sometimes, and the annual list of returned shipments, published with the guild's compliments and the fabricators' serial numbers, is the League's most effective consumer publication and its least favorite import.
## Tector

- **Settled:** 2370 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-882872210352301568 (HIP 34567), K, 82.88 ly
- **Population:** 580,000
- **System:** a K sun over mooned hot rocks, two shallow ocean worlds, cold airy stone, and — far out at 45 au — a heavy airy wanderer: a system of surfaces waiting for roofs.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 34222, HIP 36853, and Gaia EDR3 3379956708664289280; Mesh gates to Rota's star and Gaia EDR3 891106452933162112.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2372, for the seat of the Plasterers' Guild: *Tector. A roof. Certified apt.*

Tector roofs the League's settlements. The Plasterers — the sealing, dome-skin, and pressure-roof specialists whose work makes every habitable hall in the quarter airtight by certificate — run their works and their training scaffold-halls from this junction, and their crews, who travel with the Guild's famous mobile scaffolds, are the quarter's most-booked contractors and its most patient, because, as every settlement learns, *the roof goes over everything, so everything waits for the roof.* The custom is the First Seal: every new structure's roof is finished by the apprentice who began it, wherever in the quarter the job took them, and the Guild pays the fare — because, the charter's line runs, *the one who starts a roof should be there when the weather comes.* The grievance is weather: the Guild's annual league-wide leakage survey, published with every settlement's name and score, has ended friendships, improved standards, and been nominated, twice, for the Committee's driest publication, which it lost, both times, to Fistula.
## Sera

- **Settled:** 2374 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-884039857341503616 (HIP 34222), M, 74.46 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** a bright M star over locked heavy rocks, a hot ocean world, mooned cold stone, and a long giant-heavy tail to the cold rim.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 868209703104242048 and HIP 34414; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 3435919896308345856 and Tector's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2376, for the seat of the Locksmiths' Guild: *Sera. A lock. Certified apt.* The Committee's note: *the name is now locked; the certificate is the key.*

Sera keeps the League's things shut. The Locksmiths' Guild makes and certifies every lock, vault-seal, and chain-of-custody chamber the League's registries, banks, and archives trust — Ledger Bay's vault doors are Sera work, by contract and by pride — and the settlement's population of lockmakers, safe-builders, and the lock-smiths' school, where picking is taught so that making can be taught better, lives in the quiet certainty of people who know exactly how much everyone else depends on being unable to get in. The custom is the Locking: at each sol's end, the guild's own vault is locked by the day's newest apprentice, in front of the whole hall, and unlocked the next dawn by the same hands — the trade's daily catechism that *every lock has two duties, and only one is keeping people out.* The grievance is the Freeports' bump-keys, which the guild's annual demonstration defeats with a new design each year, a public arms race the audience brings picnics to.
## Cuprum

- **Settled:** 2377 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3435919896308345856, M, 62.31 ly
- **Population:** 490,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a heavy mooned airy stone, and a two-mooned ice giant — a small system that assays, in the Guild's own phrase, *orange all the way down.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Wasat and Gaia EDR3 868209703104242048; Mesh gates to HIP 34414 and Sera's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2379, for the seat of the Coppersmiths' Guild: *Cuprum. Copper. Certified apt.*

Cuprum runs the quarter's pipes and pans. The Coppersmiths' works — the stills of Stillhouse, the cookware of every galley, the pressure-lines Fistula specifies where steel is too proud — turn the heavy stone's ores into the League's copper trade, and the settlement's population of smiths, assayists, and the apprentices whose first pot, by guild law, is sold only to their own family, keeps the metal's old reputation bright: copper forgives, copper lasts, and copper tells you when it is failing — *the honest metal*, the charter calls it, in the quarter's most-quoted metallurgy. The custom is the Annealing Chorus: the works' great annealing runs are sung, literally, by the crews who time the metal's color by chord — the trade's oldest instrument, still certified. The grievance is aluminum, the Union's light upstart, whose case the Guild's annual pamphlet rests entirely on the words *ask the sea*, every hull in the quarter being, at the end, a kind of question the sea answers.
## Wasat

- **Settled:** 2380 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-866177431955478144 (Wasat), F (1.8 solar masses), 60.68 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** a fierce F sun — hot airy super-earths basking far out, airy mooned cold stone, and paired four- and three-mooned giants at the rim.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Alzirr, Gaia EDR3 868209703104242048, and Cuprum's star; Mesh gate to Gaia EDR3 3155195714240292736.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the League's standing rule — *Wasat*, the middle, the old Arabic word the star carried from Earth's sky — certified for the junction the whole quarter already called the middle. The Committee's minute: *apt, ancient, and everyone has been calling it that anyway; ratified with relief.*

Wasat is the middle the name promises. Four routes' traffic swaps crews, cargo, and gossip at the junction the quarter's own idiom treats as its center-of-gravity — *shipping through the middle* means Wasat, and every polity's crews say so, in four languages, meaning the same thing — and the settlement's population of tally-clerks, brokers, and the barber-surgeons' hall at the crossing's corner serves the whole trade's hair, health, and confessions. The custom is the Middle Bell: at each shift's change, the junction rings one bell for every route currently open, and the crews count them by ear — the quarter's most-trusted schedule, more than any almanac. The grievance is the name's own success: every meeting of any consequence gets scheduled *at the middle*, and the Wasat assembly's annual letter requesting that someone, somewhere, hold a meeting somewhere else, is co-signed by every innkeeper in the system and published, unread, by everyone.
## Obstetrix

- **Settled:** 2383 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-868209703104242048, M, 63.26 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a heavy steam ocean world, mooned airy stones, and a four-mooned giant — a quiet system kept quiet on purpose.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Wasat; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 901941452829250560, Cuprum's star, and Sera's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2385, for the seat of the Midwives' Guild, the League's birthing service: *Obstetrix. A midwife. Certified apt.*

Obstetrix delivers the League. The Midwives' Guild — the birthing houses, the family medical corps, the teaching schools whose graduates staff every League world's maternity — runs its whole service from this quiet junction, and the settlement's population of midwives, nurses, and the families the guild has delivered for three generations keeps the system's lights low by charter, because — the guild's founding line — *people arrive here; arrivals deserve calm.* The custom is the First Cry: every birth in the birthing houses is logged, and at each sol's end the day's names — first names only, chosen by the families — are read aloud at the guild hall door, to whoever gathers, because, the register holds, *the community was present at every one of these, whatever the traffic says.* The grievance is coverage — the guild's endless petition for birthing stations on every far route, against the Assembly's budget arithmetic, is the League's longest-running moral audit, and the guild's published map of uncovered routes, colored by waiting time, is the only political document in the quarter that no politician has ever once argued with.
## Corbis

- **Settled:** 2386 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-884951489919636352 (HIP 34414), K, 74.97 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over mooned hot rocks, a shallow ocean world, a heavy airy mooned stone, and a 271-Earth-mass far giant — a system the survey grades *a larder in want of baskets.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 893653883934749824 and HD 263175B; Mesh gates to Cuprum's and Sera's stars.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2388, for the seat of the Basketmakers' Guild: *Corbis. A basket. Certified apt.*

Corbis carries the quarter, by hand. The Basketmakers' works — the certified hampers, fish-baskets, grain corbs, and the bonded carrying-cases the League's courts use for evidence — turn Bosque's willow and the ember world's cane into the containers everything else gets moved in, and the settlement's population of weavers, apprentices, and the wicker-keepers whose damp-halls maintain the material's moods keeps the trade's old economy: everything a basket holds is somebody's, and the basket is the guild's. The custom is the Loading: no design is certified until the youngest apprentice has carried it, full, the whole length of the works, alone — because, the guild's charter runs, *the basket must forgive whoever has to carry it.* The grievance is the crate — Coldstore's standardized boxes, which stack where baskets curve, and whose efficiency the Guild's annual report concedes, mourns, and answers with the report's own annual binding, which is, of course, woven.
## Carbo

- **Settled:** 2389 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-893653883934749824, brown dwarf, 83.50 ly
- **Population:** 180,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, airy mooned stones, and paired giants — a system of fuel, patience, and the long slow burn.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Corbis's star; Mesh gates to HD 263175B, Gaia EDR3 891106452933162112, and Gaia EDR3 890297349813509248.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2391, for the seat of the Charcoal-Burners' Guild: *Carbo. Charcoal. Certified apt.*

Carbo keeps the quarter's fires honest. The Burners' guild works the slow kilns that turn Bosque's coppice-wood into the cooking charcoal half the League's kitchens prefer and every smith's forge demands — the trade that invented patience, the guild's own almanac says, and still practices it daily. The settlement's kiln families keep the custom of the Lighting: each kiln's burn is begun by the crew's eldest, with the youngest carrying the brand — the whole trade, one flame, handed both ways — and the grievance is the fuel-cell lobby, whose annual attempt to reclassify charcoal as *heritage cuisine* for tariff purposes is defeated, annually, by the smiths, who appear at the hearing with forges.
## Pecten

- **Settled:** 2392 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-890297349813509248, M, 93.57 ly
- **Population:** 140,000
- **System:** a bright ember primary over a locked furnace giant, mooned cold stones, and a four-mooned ice giant — small, quiet, and entirely worked by hand.
- **Links:** Mesh gate to Carbo's star; Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 891106452933162112, HIP 36285, and Gaia EDR3 939169129838317440.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2394, for the seat of the Comb-makers' Guild: *Pecten. A comb. Certified apt.*

Pecten smooths the quarter. The Comb-makers — horn, bone, and the famous Tetsu-tempered fine combs that half the League's barbers and weavers specify — work the smallest certified trade in the League from this small quiet junction, and the guild's pride is proportionate to the inverse of its product: no comb leaves the works that has not been drawn, by the maker, through the maker's own hair — the trade's only test, and its whole theology. The custom is the First Tooth: each apprentice's first comb keeps one deliberately unpolished tooth, forever, as the guild's reminder that *the beginner is in the tool, and should stay.* The grievance is nothing, which the guild's annual report states as a complete document, a blank page that has won the Committee's driest publication award three separate years and is framed at the works' door, polished like everything else.
## Atramentum

- **Settled:** 2395 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-900556927171103232 (HIP 36285), K, 99.48 ly
- **Population:** 190,000
- **System:** a Sun-like K star over mooned hot airy rocks, a temperate two-mooned ice giant, and a far cold tail of airy ice — a system of small vats and long words.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 37789; Mesh gates to HIP 39612, Pecten's star, and Gaia EDR3 900690105517649280.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2397, for the seat of the Ink-makers' Guild: *Atramentum. Ink. Certified apt.*

Atramentum writes the League's permanent record. The Ink-makers — the guild whose certified archival inks flow in every notary's pen, the Chapter Register's oldest pages, and the League's treaty originals — compound their blacks and blacks alone, on the theory the guild charter states once and observes forever: *what must outlast the writer should be darker than any opinion about it.* The settlement's vat families keep the trade's one custom, the Testing: each batch is used to write one sentence — the same sentence, every batch, two centuries — on Washi paper, and the sample is hung in the hall's window, in full light, to age; the hall's wall is now two hundred years of the same sentence fading at different speeds, the guild's entire argument, made visibly. The grievance is the quick-dry substitutes of the Union's offices, which the Register's archivists — the ink's only customers whose opinion outlasts everyone's — grade, sample by sample, in the margin, in ink that has never once been theirs.
## Moneta

- **Settled:** 2398 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-905437831085898752 (HIP 39612), M, 97.30 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** a bright M star over mooned hot airy rocks, airy cold stones, and a three-mooned giant — a small system with one guarded building and the quarter's whole attention on it.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 41820; Mesh gates to Atramentum's star, Gaia EDR3 681064719453783040, and Gaia EDR3 874683391345874816.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2400, for the seat of the Moneyers' Guild, the League's mint: *Moneta. A mint. Certified apt.*

Moneta makes the League's money. The Moneyers strike the League-mark — the coin that travels across every border in the quarter because every treasury in it has audited the mint, and keeps auditing, twice a year, on the theory that *trust is a coin too; strike it daily* — from works whose security is certified by Sera, whose metal by Cuprum, and whose margins by the Assembly, grudgingly. The settlement's population of die-cutters, assayists, and guards is small and incorruptible in the specific way of people who are audited for a living. The custom is the First Strike: each year's first coin is struck by the Assembly's youngest member, on the mint's floor, in public, and spent immediately, at the mint's canteen, on soup — the League's annual demonstration that the money is ordinary on purpose. The grievance is the Freeports' paper, which circulates fine, is redeemed sometimes, and is described, in the Moneyers' annual report, with the guild's entire emotional range: a table.
## Ancora

- **Settled:** 2402 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-903616971110812544 (HIP 41820), K, 95.70 ly
- **Population:** 370,000
- **System:** a Sun-like K star over bare hot rocks, an airy ocean world, and two colossal giants — 428 and 799 Earth masses — whose deep gravity wells test everything the Guild makes.
- **Links:** Regional gate to 5 LMi; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 718905266820857216, Moneta's star, and HIP 44458.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2404, for the seat of the Anchor-smiths' Guild: *Ancora. An anchor. Certified apt.*

Ancora holds the quarter down. The Anchor-smiths forge and certify the station-anchors, mooring-mounts, and deep-well holdfasts every port in the League rides on — tested, by guild law, in this system's own monstrous gravity wells, because — the charter's line — *an anchor untested by the worst sky is a decoration.* The settlement's population of forge crews, test-riggers, and the inspectors who stamp every holdfast is small, muscular, and constitutionally serious. The custom is the Drop: each certified anchor's first test drop is performed with the whole crew silent and watching, hands on the cable, because, the smiths' rule runs, *the crew that feels the sea take the anchor never forgets what the metal is for.* The grievance is the cheap imports that flood the Freeports' chandlers, whose failures the Guild collects, stamps *exhibit*, and returns, annually, in a crate, to the auction halls that sold them, freight collect.
## Praeco

- **Settled:** 2405 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-716109930305855360 (5 Lyncis Minoris), G, 89.31 ly
- **Population:** 880,000
- **System:** a hot bright G sun over mooned furnace rocks, airy cold stones, and the great leg: the Regional gate to Meridian system itself.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 718905266820857216, Ancora's star, and the Meridian system; Mesh gate to HIP 44458.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2407, for the seat of the Heralds' Guild, the League's certified announcers, at the gate of the capital: *Praeco. A herald. Certified apt.* The Committee's note: *the capital's door should be named for the voice.*

Praeco speaks for the League, on purpose. The Heralds' Guild — the certified announcers whose voices, by guild law, must be believed — proclaim the League's proclamations at every port, moderate the Assembly's debates, and run the capital's gateway, where every arrival is greeted by name and every departure is read into the record; the settlement's population of heralds, their trainers, and the families who have spoken the League's words for three generations keeps the trade's oldest rule above the hall door: *the voice carries; the truth carries further.* The custom is the First Reading: each newly certified herald's first proclamation is their own certificate, read aloud at the gate, to the arriving traffic of the whole capital route — the guild's welcome to its newest voice, and its warning, simultaneously, that the voice is now public property. The grievance is the echo — the Freeports' advertising channels, which imitate the heraldic cadence for commerce, and whose impersonations the Guild's annual report reproduces, voice-annotated, for the courts and for the philologists, who describe the document as *unusually fun.*
## Dolabra

- **Settled:** 2408 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-718905266820857216, M, 91.90 ly
- **Population:** 180,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a hot airy super-earth, bare mooned stones, and mooned ice — a system the survey graded, with the sawyers in mind, *cleavable.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to Praeco's star, Gaia EDR3 1013461034585110528, and HIP 44458; Mesh gate to Ancora's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2410, for the seat of the Sawyers' Guild: *Dolabra. An axe. Certified apt.*

Dolabra clears the quarter's timber. The Sawyers — the felling, milling, and shaping crews who work Bosque's rotations under the forestry chapters' strictest law — base their guild here, and the settlement's population of saw crews, tool-keepers, and the axe-grinders whose marks every handle in the League carries is small, loud, and precisely as cheerful as a profession of axes permits. The custom is the Fall Count: every tree the crews fell is named, aloud, before the cut — the species, the age, the grove — because, the Guild's charter holds, *the wood keeps its own register; we read it out.* The grievance is synthetic lumber, the Union's pressed board, whose longevity the Sawyers' annual demonstration measures by water, fire, and the patience of a two-hundred-year chair.
## Ripa

- **Settled:** 2411 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-718976395775952256 (HIP 44458), G, 98.33 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over mooned hot rocks, a shallow airy ocean world, and two colossal giants (427 and 527 Earth masses) whose moon-harbors give the junction its whole landscape.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 914420428766930816; Mesh gates to Praeco's star, Gaia EDR3 1013461034585110528, Ancora's star, Gaia EDR3 818602457173176192, and Dolabra's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2413, for the seat of the Wharf-keepers' Guild: *Ripa. A shore. Certified apt.*

Ripa keeps the League's shores. The Wharf-keepers — the harbor masters, berth-brokers, and dock-certifiers whose seals open every port in the League — run their great training harbors in the giants' moon-roads, where six routes' traffic learns to berth, and the settlement's population of wharfingers, tally-clerks, and the line-handlers whose throws are taught by rote and legend is the quarter's discipline made visible: a League berth clears on time, or the wharfinger explains why, in writing, twice. The custom is the Mooring: each new berth-certified wharfinger ties their first hitch on the hall's ceremonial bollard, and the hitch stays, added to two centuries of knots by every hand before them — the guild's whole history, one on top of another, holding nothing and meaning everything.
