The grievance is congestion: the six-route traffic outgrew the moon-harbors a decade ago, and the Wharf-keepers' annual berth-waiting table, published by the hour, is the single most quoted number in the League's budget debates, which the Assembly spends and the harbors prove.
## Caelator

- **Settled:** 2414 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-818602457173176192, white dwarf, 109.37 ly
- **Population:** 190,000
- **System:** a dead sun's white point-light over locked rocks and a small locked steam ocean — a system of shadows sharp enough to cut, which the engravers describe as *studio lighting.*
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 48423; Mesh gates to 37 Lyn B, Ripa's star, Gaia EDR3 912718071240545152, and Gaia EDR3 813090571023685248.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2416, for the seat of the Engravers' Guild: *Caelator. An engraver. Certified apt.*

Caelator cuts the League's lines. The Engravers cut Moneta's dies, the seals of Sera's certification, the plates of Pergamino's fine editions, and every certifying stamp in the quarter — the trade whose work is read at arm's length by millions and seen whole by nobody, a condition the guild's charter describes as *adequate.* The settlement's small population of engravers, apprentices, and the microscope-hall keepers works under the white dwarf's knife-sharp light, and keeps the one custom: the Master's Plate — every retiring engraver's final plate is cut blind, from memory, and the guild's collection of two centuries of them, each one a master's whole hand recalled in the dark, is the quarter's most quietly overwhelming museum. The grievance is machine etching, which the guild's annual exhibition answers by hanging, side by side, one master's plate and one machine's plate, unlabeled — an answer that has never once needed the labels.
## Lucerna

- **Settled:** 2417 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1019361632454363776 (37 Lyncis B), M, 95.03 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** a bright M star over locked furnace rocks, a heavy hot ocean world (65% sea), a 384-Earth-mass giant, and a long cold tail — fuel, glass, and a junction's worth of need.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1013461034585110528, Gaia EDR3 1021518908626955648, and HIP 48423; Mesh gate to Caelator's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2419, for the seat of the Lamp-makers' Guild: *Lucerna. A lamp. Certified apt.*

Lucerna lights the League's rooms and roads. The Lamp-makers make the certified luminaires of every station hall, road beacon, and study desk in Meridian space — the warm work-light standard that Vitrum's glass and Faber's brass jointly built — and the settlement's population of lampwrights, wick-hands, and the standardization office that guarantees every certified lamp burns the same warm white, from Mansio's hostels to the capital's halls, is small, bright, and definite. The custom is the First Flame: each new lamp design is lit at the guild hall and left burning for a full season before certification, and the hall's record of the burn — kept in the lamp's own light, by hand — is the certificate. The grievance is the Union's cold-white standard, which the Guild's annual comparison describes as *accurate, and sad*, a ruling the quarter's poets have endorsed without appeal.
## Molitor

- **Settled:** 2420 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1021518908626955648, brown dwarf, 104.67 ly
- **Population:** 180,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked furnace-class rocks — the whole system inside a week, and every rock turning, which the millers approved of professionally.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 48423 and 18 UMa; Mesh gates to Lucerna's star and Gaia EDR3 1021178472339004288.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2422, for the seat of the Millers' Guild: *Molitor. A miller. Certified apt.*

Molitor grinds the League's daily bread. The Millers run the great stone mills that turn Arva's and Tanada's harvests into the League's flour — the certified grind-grades, the bakery contracts, the sacks Corbis weaves and Nuntius routes — and the settlement's population of millers, millwrights, and the stone-dressers whose crossed-hammer marks every wheel carries is the quarter's oldest-smelling town and its proudest. The custom is the Morning Grind: each sol's first grind is done with the hall's doors open and the mill's song audible to the street, because, the Guild's charter holds, *the League should hear its breakfast being made.* The grievance is the stone — the quarry rates of Cantera and the tariff's classification of millstones as *ornamental rock*, a phrase the Millers' annual letter has been correcting, in increasingly patient type, since before most of the current Committee was born.
## Mergus

- **Settled:** 2423 (guild charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1022858010710856576 (18 Ursae Majoris), A (1.9 solar masses, 12 solar luminosities), 116.88 ly
- **Population:** 2.1 million
- **System:** a fierce white A sun over mooned airy furnace rocks, then the settled world — a heavy ocean planet at 273 K, two-thirds of its face under sea, one moon, and a captured locked companion — with cold heavy stones beyond, where the light goes long and blue.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1021178472339004288 and Gaia EDR3 1029222426776662656; Mesh gates to Molitor's star and Gaia EDR3 1040611378288748032.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2425, for the seat of the Diving Guild: *Mergus. A diver. Certified apt.* The Committee's note observes that the heavy ocean world *practically certifies the name back.*

Mergus works the League's deep water. The Diving Guild — salvage divers, deep-construction crews, and the hull-inspection corps whose reports Flagfall's registry treats as scripture — trains and certifies every working diver in the League from the heavy ocean world's shore schools, where the planet's own crushing gravity makes graduates who find the rest of the quarter's seas restful. The settlement is dive families, the training halls, and the salvage yards where the deep-towed prizes come apart under supervision; the custom is the Weighing: at graduation, each new diver is handed a single tool-belt, chosen by lot from two centuries of retired belts, and told the name of the diver who last wore it — the Guild's way of saying *you do not go down alone.* The grievance is the bends, the trade's oldest enemy, whose incident tables the Guild publishes with a completeness no other polity's safety office matches — a document that has quietly rewritten three polities' labor law and is the only publication in the League the Freeports have asked to reprint, unedited, at their own expense.
## Explorator

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the newest in the League's register).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1040611378288748032, M, 121.16 ly
- **Population:** 820,000 and arriving
- **System:** a bright ember primary over locked furnace rocks, then the settled world — a heavy hot-dusk airy planet at 309 K with one moon — the League's newest certification and its newest argument.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1042071701528617728 and Gaia EDR3 1083965014396208640; Mesh gates to Mergus's star, τ UMa, and Gaia EDR3 1029222426776662656.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, in the year 2426 itself, for the seat of the Survey Corps, the League's far- frontier prospectors: *Explorator. A surveyor. Certified apt.* The Committee's minute is its newest and its proudest: *certified before the paint was dry; the paint was the petition.*

Explorator is the League's edge. The Survey Corps' newest base — the fifth such, and the first founded under the Concord's open-border accords — stages the far mapping of the League's outer quarter: the route surveys Index certifies, the hazard buoys Custos places, and the first-descent logs the League's archives already bind in real time. The heavy dusk world's base town is barracks, assay halls, and the famous Last Canteen, which serves the departure toast the whole quarter knows: *to the ones who find out.* The custom is the Marking: each survey crew, leaving the base for the far dark, chalks its course on the base's great door — and each crew, returning, closes its own line — so that the door, two centuries from now, will be the map of everything the League once didn't know. The grievance is the future tense — the base's budget is approved a year at a time, for a work measured in generations, and the Corps' annual letter to the Assembly observes that *the dark does not negotiate in fiscal years*, a sentence the Assembly has begun, at last, to quote back.
## Venator

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1042071701528617728, G brown dwarf, 114.10 ly
- **Population:** 190,000
- **System:** a failed star over a tight ladder of locked rocks — two furnace-class, a bare 261 K stone, an airy locked pebble — with six roads out into the frontier dark.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1039003544395162752 and τ UMa; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1067390671177351040, Gaia EDR3 1035127392245593984, Gaia EDR3 1031984129019591936, and Explorator's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Game-keepers' Guild, the League's wildlife service for the new worlds: *Venator. A hunter. Certified apt.* The Committee's note: *the name names the old work so the new work can differ from it.*

Venator keeps the new worlds living. The Game-keepers — ecologists with rifles and rifles with ethics — manage the introduced and protected stocks of every fresh settlement, write the hunting seasons the frontier actually obeys, and run the League's one rule about new worlds, quoted at every frontier assembly: *nothing goes extinct on our shift.* The settlement's population of keepers, trackers, and the assay-biologists who census what the frontier's seas and slopes are growing into is small, camouflaged, and profoundly respected. The custom is the Counting: each new world's first full ecological census is read aloud, species by species, at the base's hall, before any introduction is approved — the League's liturgy of *know what you have before you change it.* The grievance is the frontier's impatience itself, which the Guild's annual report measures, with visible restraint, in introduced species, escaped stocks, and the specific number of committees it has told, how many times, exactly, no.
## Magister

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1067390671177351040, M, 104.01 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and mooned cold stones, with airy ice and a two-mooned giant behind — the frontier's five-road crossroads and its newest homework.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1043287486510907264 and Gaia EDR3 1039003544395162752; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1066086650386220288, Gaia EDR3 1119989933060503680, and Venator's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Schoolmasters' Guild, the League's traveling schools of the frontier: *Magister. A teacher. Certified apt.*

Magister teaches the frontier's children. The Schoolmasters' circuit-riders — certified teachers who rotate through every far settlement the Assembly can fund and several it cannot — base their frontier school here, and the settlement's population of masters, hall-keepers, and the archive of homework (a real vault, two centuries deep, which the children are shown and always, always laugh at) keeps the League's one absolutely unbroken service: wherever Meridian citizens settle, a school follows within a season. The custom is the Bell: each circuit-teacher, departing for a far route, takes one bell from the base's hall, rings it once at the gate, and returns it when they come back — so the hall's bell-rack, some bells present, some always out, is the school system's whole map, heard in metal. The grievance is arithmetic, the eternal one: the Guild's budget request and the Assembly's budget reply are published together each year, in parallel columns, and the space between the columns is, by the Guild's own annual annotation, *where the children are.*
## Herbarius

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1035127392245593984, M, 106.78 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked furnace rocks, then the settled world — a cold airy planet at 240 K with one moon — and, far out, a second airy wanderer at 249 K: two candidates for the Guild's slow green work.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1043287486510907264, Gaia EDR3 1031984129019591936, and Gaia EDR3 1094596746378690688; Mesh gate to Venator's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Botanists' Guild, the League's plant-introduction service for the new worlds: *Herbarius. A herbalist. Certified apt.*

Herbarius greens the frontier. The Botanists — seed-masters, soil-builders, and the patient introducers of grass, grain, and shade to worlds that never had them — work from the cold world's trial gardens with Venator's census and Semilla's vaults, and the settlement's population of botanists, gardeners, and the seed-testers whose germination ledgers are the frontier's most carefully read documents runs the League's slowest and most beloved campaign: the greening, acre by certified acre, of every world the League settles. The custom is the First Acre: each new world's first certified planting is done with seed carried, by hand, from this system's own gardens — because, the Guild's charter holds, *the green should know where it came from.* The grievance is time — introductions take generations, the frontier wants gardens, and the Guild's annual report on the difference between a landscape and a lawn has become, quietly, the frontier's best agricultural education.
## Limes

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1094596746378690688, M, 112.29 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a bare temperate pebble, and a long chain of mooned giants — six roads out, five of them into the unmapped dark.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 40918; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1111624642439469824, HIP 34024, Herbarius's star, Gaia EDR3 1083965014396208640, and Gaia EDR3 1095240922752846976.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the March-wardens' Guild, the League's frontier service: *Limes. A border. Certified apt.*

Limes holds the edge. The March-wardens run the League's far beacons, rescue caches, and boundary rings — the legal frontier where settlement stops and the survey begins — and the settlement's population of wardens, signalmen, and the beacon-hall families lives at the edge of everything mapped, six roads out, five into the dark, which the wardens' own saying grades *correct, and improving.* The custom is the Last Light: at each sol's end, the frontier beacons flash their all-clear in sequence, outward from this hub, and the wardens log the pattern's return from the farthest ring — the League's nightly proof that its edge is still attached. The grievance is loneliness, filed annually as a staffing request, and answered, this year, for the first time, with a second canteen — a budget line the wardens' annual report describes, without the Committee's permission, as *apt.*
## Campana

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1111624642439469824, M, 107.06 ly
- **Population:** 950,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, then the settled world — a heavy temperate airy planet at 270 K with one moon — and a 148-Earth-mass giant with captured companions riding behind it.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1106845580792191872 and HIP 34024; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1105499091364329088, Limes's star, and HIP 27246.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Bell-founders' Guild: *Campana. A bell. Certified apt.*

Campana rings the League in. The Bell-founders cast the first bell of every new settlement — the frontier's founding instrument, hung before the hall, before the walls, before the budget — and the settlement's population of founders, tuners, and the foundry-wardens whose strike-tests are certified by ear and record works the old bronze mathematics on the heavy world's patient stone. The custom is the First Ring: every cast bell is rung once, at the foundry, by the settlement's delegate before shipping — because, the Guild's charter holds, *a bell should meet its people's hands before it meets their hours.* The grievance is silence — the frontier's audio-only emergency channels, which the Guild's annual report acknowledges, endorses, and cannot help describing as *functional, like a doorless house.*
## Gubernator

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1106845580792191872, M, 102.82 ly
- **Population:** 880,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, then the settled world — a cool locked ocean planet at 299 K, 61% of its face under twilight sea — with mooned ice and paired giants behind.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 498708306508914048 and Gaia EDR3 1105499091364329088; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1100090726807510016 and Campana's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Harbor-Pilots' Guild: *Gubernator. A pilot. Certified apt.*

Gubernator steers the frontier's harbors. The Pilots — the boarding-boat crews who take every unfamiliar hull through the new worlds' unmarked shallows — train and certify from the twilight sea's harbor schools, where the locked ocean's dawn-line makes the whole planet one long lesson in local weather, and the settlement's population of pilots, tide-readers, and the families who keep the pilot-cutters is the frontier's guarantee that no ship meets a new port alone. The custom is the Handover: each pilot, bringing a hull in, talks the whole approach aloud, to the bridge and to the log, so that every harbor the Guild enters is entered with its own narration — the frontier's charts being, as yet, half made of voices. The grievance is the charts themselves, which the Pilots' annual request for survey funding describes as *racing the traffic, and losing politely.*
## Figulus

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1100090726807510016, M, 97.91 ly
- **Population:** 570,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked heavy airy rocks — the third of them, **Figulus** proper, a hot-dusk airy world at 302 K with one moon — the frontier's clay and fire.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 29548; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1008217631133579392, Gubernator's star, Gaia EDR3 1105499091364329088, and HIP 31552.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Potters' Guild: *Figulus. A potter. Certified apt.*

Figulus makes the frontier's vessels — the cups, crocks, and bonded jars every new settlement eats and stores from, before the glass, before the metal, before the catalogs. The Potters' frontier school turns the dusk world's clay beds into the League's homeware, and the settlement's population of throwers, kiln-crews, and the glaze-masters whose recipes are guild property and family gossip keeps the trade's oldest liturgy: the First Firing, when each new kiln's first load is opened at a gathering, and the settlement's oldest member takes the first vessel from it, whatever it is, and drinks from it, whatever it holds. The grievance is breakage in transit, which the Guild's annual freight report analyzes by route, by carrier, and, in a footnote that has ended three haulage contracts, by name.
## Calx

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1008217631133579392, M, 88.21 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, a captured companion, and mooned airy stones — the frontier's mortar-mix, with five roads out.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 29067 and HIP 29548; Mesh gates to HIP 29761, Gaia EDR3 287767760932246144, and Figulus's star.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Lime-burners' Guild: *Calx. Lime. Certified apt.*

Calx binds the new settlements. The Lime-burners quarry, burn, and slake the mortar-bases of every frontier hall — the trade the Guild's own almanac calls *the least glorious chemistry in the League and the reason it is standing* — and the settlement's kiln families keep the craft's one rule above every gate: *slaked lime is patient; so are we; wait for the set.* The custom is the First Course: each new settlement's first mortared wall is raised by a Lime-burners' crew, on Guild contract, gratis — the trade's founding tithe, and its best advertisement, since every settlement remembers who held the first wall up. The grievance is the fast-set imports, whose three-year-old walls the Guild's annual inspection tour photographs beside their own three-hundred-year-old ones, a comparison requiring, as the Guild notes, no text.
## Mamori

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-287767760932246144, brown dwarf, 93.06 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** an ember primary holding three locked airy rocks in the cold dusk band — the third of them, **Mamori** proper, habitable at 246 K — the Compact's newest garden and newest watch.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Calx's star, HIP 29548, and Gaia EDR3 1105499091364329088; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 478737262637242240.
- **Registry name:* named by its founding covenant — the watchmen's families, who signed under the word for guarding: *Mamori.* The covenant register: *the ancestors kept watch over a village; the village has grown; the watch grew with it.*

Mamori keeps the Compact's edge. The watchmen's covenant — the beacon-tenders, rescue crews, and the courtesy patrols whose lanterns mark the Compact's far roads — holds the dusk world's new towns and the four routes' worth of quiet, and the settlement's population of watchers, signal-families, and the farmers who feed them runs the Compact's answer to the frontier's oldest question: who answers the far bell. The custom is the Watch of the Names: the beacon-hall keeps a board of every crew currently out beyond the far rings, and each sol's last bell, the board is read aloud, name by name, to an empty hall — because, the register holds, *someone should say the names every night, and the night is exactly who we say them to.* The grievance is the far routes' weather, which the watch's annual report logs, mourns, and forgives, in the covenant's characteristic order.
## Ringo

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-478737262637242240, M, 91.33 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, then the settled world — a cold airy planet at 243 K with one moon — with mooned airy stones, a two-mooned giant, and a four-mooned colossus at the rim: orchard country at the Compact's new edge.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 19861; Mesh gates to Mamori's star, Gaia EDR3 475364991756045824, and Gaia EDR3 471423551805832064.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the orchard families, who signed under the word for the apple: *Ringo.* The register: *the ancestors crossed the water with a seed; the water is wider now; the seed is the same.*

Ringo plants the Compact's newest orchards. The covenant's first-fruit world — the cold temperate valleys, the grafted stock from Orchard's lines and Semilla's vaults, the first cider presses already installed ahead of the first harvest — is the frontier's most hopeful smell, and the settlement's population of grafters, pressers, and the hoop-oak coopers Corbis contracts is small, seasonal, and certain, in the way orchard people always are, that the best years are ahead. The custom is the Wassail: at midwinter, the young trees are sung to, each by name — a frontier practice the covenant brought whole, on the grounds that *new land is hard on the young, trees included.* The grievance is patience: the first true cider is four years out, the investors want a taste now, and the covenant's answer — *water the trees* — has become the Compact's newest proverb.
## Yadoya

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-469486109244322176 (HIP 19861), K, 91.21 ly
- **Population:** 470,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over mooned hot rocks, a bare temperate pebble, airy cold stones, and paired far giants — a junction of five roads with room to sleep.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 462116220235041920 and Gaia EDR3 475364991756045824; Mesh gates to 9 Aur C, Ringo's star, and Gaia EDR3 277006015997383168.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the innkeeping families — under the old word for the inn: *Yadoya.* The register: *the road is long; that is the whole business.*

Yadoya rests the frontier. The covenant's great inns — the futon-halls, the bath-houses, the kitchens whose menus are posted by distance-traveled — stand at the junction of five roads, and the settlement's population of innkeeper families, bath-keepers, and the cooks who feed every polity's far crews lives by the innkeeper's one rule, carved at every door: *enter as a stranger, leave as a neighbor; the bath is the treaty.* The custom is the Shoe Count: the entry-hall's shelves, two centuries from now, are meant to hold every far-crew's footwear in the world's most diverse arrangement — a custom that is, at present, four years old and already photographed. The grievance is the frontier's arithmetic: the inns are full before they are finished, the covenant is building constantly, and the register's annual note on expansion is the single word *good*, which the Compact's budget office has asked them to elaborate, twice, and been answered, twice, with the same word, correctly.
## Sumiyaki

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-265724476942021248 (9 Aurigae C), K, 87.65 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked and airy hot rocks, cold airy stones, and five roads' worth of quiet — a system the survey graded *fuel, and patient.*
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 23140 and HIP 28634; Mesh gates to Yadoya's star, Gaia EDR3 969488128616580224, and Gaia EDR3 277006015997383168.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the charcoal-burners — under their craft's own word: *Sumiyaki.* The register: *the ancestors burned slow and clean; so do we; it is the same sky.*

Sumiyaki keeps the frontier's hearths. The charcoal covenant — the kiln families whose slow burns supply the frontier kitchens, the smiths' forges, and the tea-houses that consider any other heat a vulgarity — works the airy stones' coppice, and the settlement's population of burners, carters, and the kiln-wardens whose night watches are the trade's whole poetry keeps the one custom the old craft kept: the kiln is never left, and the watcher sings — because, the register holds, *a watched fire behaves, and a sung fire behaves better.* The grievance is the fuel-cell again, the modern one, which the covenant's annual report defeats the same way every year: a single frontier kitchen, one meal, two fires, and the visiting crews' verdicts, which the Freeports' brokers have stopped attending because the verdicts, the covenant notes with perfect calm, have never once been close.
## Bokujo

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-969752767321089792 (HIP 28634), G, 90.01 ly
- **Population:** 470,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over hot heavy rocks, an airy hot stone, a warm ocean world (73% sea), and the settled world — a cold airy heavy planet at 247 K with one moon — with captured-locked companions and a 495-Earth-mass colossus at the rim: pasture at the far edge.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 969488128616580224; Mesh gates to Sumiyaki's star, Gaia EDR3 970866499583941120, and Gaia EDR3 991165550112776448.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the ranching families, who signed under the plain word for the ranch: *Bokujo.* The register: *the ancestors moved with their herds; the herds move with us; the rest is scenery.*

Bokujo feeds the frontier's tables. The ranchers' covenant runs the heavy cold world's open range — the engineered herds, the winter barns, the twice-yearly drives whose routes the settlement's maps mark like highways — and the population of ranch families, drovers, and the farrier-smiths who shoe what needs shoeing lives by the range's one law, which the register states entire: *the herd eats first; the land eats with it; we eat after; nobody starves.* The custom is the First Milk: each spring's first milking is carried, unbroken, to the settlement's oldest member and the youngest child together — the covenant's whole economy in one carried pail. The grievance is the cold, which the ranchers' annual report describes as *character-building, in the way avalanches are curriculum*, and the Assembly's frost-relief budget answers, annually, a little better.
## Stannum

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-970866499583941120, brown dwarf, 92.05 ly
- **Population:** 190,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, airy mooned stones, and a five-mooned giant — the frontier's tableware, in ore form, waiting.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 982196799404971392 and HIP 30023; Mesh gates to Bokujo's star and Gaia EDR3 969488128616580224.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Pewterers' Guild: *Stannum. Tin. Certified apt.*

Stannum plates the frontier's tables. The Pewterers — the humble-metal guild whose plates, mugs, and the famous Stannum measure-cups standardize the League's kitchens — work the small system's ores into the goods every new settlement unpacks first, and the settlement's population of casters, polishers, and the assayists who keep the alloy honest by law and by supper is small, bright-shelved, and quietly indispensable. The custom is the Casting of the First Cup: each new settlement's founding order includes, by unwritten law, one Stannum cup cast with that settlement's name in the run — the Guild's way of being at every frontier table without a ticket. The grievance is plastic, the eternal modern one, whose annual defeat the Guild achieves the pewterers' way: by lasting.
## Argentarius

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-968931157257437312 (HIP 30023), G, 106.76 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** a hot bright G sun over furnace-class mooned rocks, airy hot stones, an airy ocean world, and the settled world — a cold airy mooned planet at 262 K — with a far airy stone at the system's edge.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 956717266700229888, Gaia EDR3 991165550112776448, and GJ 3391; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 969488128616580224, Stannum's star, and Gaia EDR3 961439802283033728.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Silversmiths' Guild: *Argentarius. A silversmith. Certified apt.*

Argentarius brightens the frontier's occasions. The Silversmiths — the fine-metal guild whose cups, medals, and the League's ceremonial everything are struck in certified silver from the frontier's new ores — hold their newest seat here, and the settlement's population of smiths, chasers, and the assayists who grade every hallmark is small, exacting, and quietly rich in the way of trades whose products are all keepsakes. The custom is the Hammering-In: each apprentice's first hallmark is struck at the guild hall's annual gathering, on a piece of their own work, in front of the whole craft — the guild's entire meaning, one stamp: *this was made by a person with a name.* The grievance is the ore rush — the frontier's silver strikes attract the Freeports' mineral men in numbers the Guild's annual land-report describes as *certified, stamped, and welcome to try*, three words that have settled more claims than the courts.
## Navis

- **Settled:** 2426 (guild charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Meridian League (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-956717266700229888, M, 106.84 ly
- **Population:** 580,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, airy cold stones, and paired giants — six roads out, and the frontier's newest ways to travel them.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Argentarius's star; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 939169129838317440, Gaia EDR3 961439802283033728, Gaia EDR3 954898227790196480, Gaia EDR3 3440604056360466048, and GJ 3391.
- **Registry name:** certified by the Committee on Nomenclature, 2426, for the seat of the Shipwrights' Guild, the frontier yard: *Navis. A ship. Certified apt.*

Navis builds the League's far boats. The Shipwrights' newest yard lays down the small hulls the frontier runs on — the packet ferries, the rescue cutters, the family traders — under the guild's law that every hull's plans are published open, because, the charter holds, *the frontier must be repairable by whoever is standing there when it breaks.* The settlement's population of shipwrights, apprentices, and the loft-hall families keeps the yard's custom: the Naming — each finished hull is named, aloud, by the first crew who will ride her, and the name is cut into the keel by the youngest apprentice, because *the ship should hear her name from the people who will keep it.* The grievance is tonnage envy — the big yards of the core worlds call the frontier boats toys, an assessment the Shipwrights' annual report answers with the frontier's rescue statistics, which the big yards have stopped reading, on medical advice.
## Taru

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-954898227790196480, brown dwarf, 115.39 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and a long ladder of ice giants — the far frontier's barrel-wood, cold-storage, and five roads.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 3450993685330287488 and Gaia EDR3 3440604056360466048; Mesh gates to Navis's star, Gaia EDR3 939169129838317440, and GJ 3391.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the coopers — under the word for the barrel: *Taru.* The register: *what the frontier makes, the barrel keeps; the barrel is therefore the frontier's calendar.*

Taru keeps the frontier's harvests. The coopers' covenant makes the barrels — Ringo's first cider, Soba's flour, Bokujo's salt meat, the whole frontier's winter — in stave-yards whose work the Meridian Dolium masters inspect annually and, for three straight years, have gone home quoting, which the covenant's register records with visible pleasure and no comment. The population is coopers, stave-cutters, and the hoop-smiths whose iron rings are stamped with the covenant's plum-blossom mark. The custom is the First Fill: each new barrel's first contents are chosen by the whole yard, by lot, from anything anyone donates — the frontier's strangest breakfast was, by this rule, one oyster, three liters of tea, and a poem, and the barrel in question is a shrine. The grievance is stave-wood, always, which the frontier's fast-building forests are growing as fast as they can, and the covenant's thanks, likewise.
## Koyomi

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3450993685330287488, brown dwarf, 115.22 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked airy rocks — the third of them, **Koyomi** proper, a cool airy dusk world at 255 K — a hot shallow sea, mooned stones, and a five-mooned giant at the rim: the frontier's clock and its crossroads.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 3444098098156617472; Mesh gates to HIP 25580, Taru's star, HIP 27527, Gaia EDR3 3440604056360466048, and Gaia EDR3 182605933368364544.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the calendar-keepers, who signed under the word for the almanac: *Koyomi.* The register: *the frontier's farms need festivals, its fleets need dates, and its children need birthdays; we keep all three.*

Koyomi keeps the frontier's time. The calendar covenant — the almanac-makers whose frontier yearbook tracks six routes' harvests, tides, and festivals by hand and by Erlaubnis (Ephemeris' certified tables, by treaty) — holds the dusk world's observatory-villages, and the settlement's population of time-keepers, printers, and the festival-judges whose rulings on calendar disputes are legally binding and personally beloved serves the frontier's whole sense of *when.* The custom is the Turn of the Year: the covenant's printed almanac is read aloud, cover to cover, in the great hall, at each new year — a full sol's reading, attended in shifts by the whole settlement, with soup — because, the register holds, *a year everyone has heard is a year everyone can keep.* The grievance is drift: the far worlds' local reckonings, which the covenant's annual comparison corrects, forgives, and prints, politely, in the back, where the errata live and the Assembly's budget committee, the covenant notes, does not.
## Hama

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-182461725546788480 (HIP 25580), G, 111.71 ly
- **Population:** 690,000
- **System:** a bright G sun over heavy hot rocks, then the settled pair: a cold airy mooned world at 267 K, and — far out at 37 au, in the long light — a warm ocean world (284 K, 79% sea, two moons): the town and the far shore.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 191093849599008128, Gaia EDR3 3444098098156617472, and Gaia EDR3 182605933368364544; Mesh gate to Koyomi's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the shore families who signed under the word for the beach where the land meets the sea: *Hama.* The register: *the town is the boat; the far sea is the field; the name is the meeting.*

Hama lives on both shores. The cold rock's sheltered towns keep the settlement, the harbor, and the covenant hall; the far warm ocean world keeps the harvest — kelp, shellfish, and the long-season fisheries that the shore crews work in rotating seasons across the whole 37-au gap, a voyage of patience that the covenant's children's counting-song teaches the way other cultures teach the alphabet. The population is shore families, far-sea fishers, and the ferry crews whose two-world schedule the almanac keeps by treaty. The custom is the Waiting: at each far-shore fleet's departure, the whole town turns out, and the same at every return — and nobody has ever once asked the covenant to explain the ceremony, because it explains itself, to everyone, the first time. The grievance is the freight rates on the long haul, which the covenant's annual letter to the Guilds describes as *the distance charging for itself twice*, a phrase the tariff committees have now, officially, framed.
## Kaya

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-191093849599008128, M, 107.31 ly
- **Population:* 190,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, airy mooned stones, and a long chain of giants — frontier thatch, grown by the covenant, cut by the same.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HD 33077A, Gaia EDR3 961439802283033728, and Gaia EDR3 207670606592768256; Regional approach to Hama's star.
- **Registry name:* named by its founding covenant — the thatchers, under the word for the grass that roofs the ancestors' houses: *Kaya.* The register: *every settlement's first roof is a promise; ours are the grass ones, cut and kept and sung over.*

Kaya roofs the frontier's new towns. The thatchers' covenant grows the reed-fields on the airy stones' quiet vales, cuts and bundles the season's grass, and lays the roofs that the frontier, at the covenant's gentle insistence, starts with — the first roof being thatch, by Compact custom now, so that every settlement's first year is spent under something that grew. The population is thatcher families, reed-cutters, and the roof-keepers whose ladder-inspections are the frontier's favorite sound. The custom is the Raising: a new roof is laid by the whole town in one sol, and the ridge-cap bundle is thrown, by the youngest cutter, to be caught by the oldest thatcher, or by the ground, which is also traditional and much funnier. The grievance is wind, the thatcher's ancient debating partner, whose annual arguments the covenant's repair ledger transcribes, storm by storm, with respect.
## Sato

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-201487468594945408 (HD 33077A), K, 101.16 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a K sun over locked furnace rocks, then the settled pair: a cool ocean world (292 K, 48% sea, one moon) and a far airy stone at 247 K — the sea-farm and the home-field, the covenant's two hands.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 22576, HIP 22596, and Gaia EDR3 207670606592768256; Mesh gates to Kaya's star and HIP 21847.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the village families, who signed under the plain word for home: *Sato.* The register, in full: *we left home to build one; the word travels well.*

Sato is the frontier's village. The ocean world's sheltered bays and river mouths carry the covenant's paddies and fish-ponds, the far stone keeps the orchards and the dry-field beans, and between them the settlement lives the Compact's oldest pattern at its newest edge: village, fields, sea, and the almanac's patient year. The population is farm families, pond-keepers, and the ferry crews of a five-route junction that the whole frontier's traffic passes and, by the innkeeper's rule of Soba and Yadoya alike, eats at. The custom is the Mura-awase: each season, the village's households set one table together in the square, every family's dish on every family's cloth — the covenant's founding statement that *the village is one table, whoever brought what.* The grievance is quiet and ordinary, the frontier's first genuine one: the fence-line dispute between the pond families and the dry-field families, now in its third year, argued with the deliberation of people who intend to be neighbors for a thousand years and are in no hurry about the fence.
## Kuri

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-174156255225869440 (HIP 22576), K, 97.97 ly
- **Population:** 790,000
- **System:** a fierce K sun over airy furnace rocks, then the twin settled worlds — cold-temperate airy stones at 258 K and 259 K — with far giants carrying whole captured villages of moons.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 179371719912574208 and HIP 21847; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 180503800659357312 and Sato's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the chestnut-growing families, under the word for the tree that feeds the winter: *Kuri.* The register: *the ancestors planted chestnuts for their grandchildren's grandchildren; we are both the grandchildren and the ancestors now.*

Kuri feeds the frontier's winters. The twin worlds' sheltered valleys — the Covenant's newest chestnut orchards, grafted from stock the Compact has carried since Earth — will, in thirty years, be the frontier's flour; in the meantime the settlement plants, grafts, and waits, in the tree-farming patience the covenant's founding rule states in full: *the orchard outlives the planter; act accordingly.* The population is orchard families, grafters, and the fence-and-barn crews who build for trees first. The custom is the Planting Circle: each new world's first orchard is planted by every household together, tree by tree passed hand to hand down a line of the whole settlement, so the orchard is — physically, provably — everybody's. The grievance is the waiting, which the covenant's annual report declines to file, on the grounds that *the trees are the report, and they are doing fine.*
## Ishiku

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-180503800659357312, M, 91.41 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, a bare temperate stone, then the settled world — a cold airy mooned planet at 263 K — and a two-mooned giant: the frontier's stone-yard.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 21832; Mesh gates to HD 30973B, Kuri's star, and Gaia EDR3 154282891790317952.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the stonemasons, under their trade's own word: *Ishiku.* The register: *the frontier builds; the stone remembers what the timber forgets; build in stone.*

Ishiku builds the frontier's permanence. The masons' covenant cuts the settled world's ledges into the corner-stones, lintels, and foundations of every settlement on the far routes — each frontier town's first stone hall is Ishiku work by the covenant's founding tithe — and the settlement's population of masons, apprentices, and the quarry-families whose mark is on every block is small, patient, and unmistakably the trade's. The custom is the Setting of the Corner: each new building's first stone is set by the oldest mason and the settlement's newest arrival together, whoever the newest arrival happens to be, on the founding belief that *a building should start with an invitation.* The grievance is concrete, the poured kind, whose speed the masons' annual report concedes, whose two-century look the report declines to guess at, and whose first-century cracks, already visible in the frontier's earliest rush jobs, the report photographs — patiently, in stone-cutter's daylight.
## Akagane

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-204358907633155840 (HIP 21832), G, 89.01 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over mooned furnace rocks, an airy hot ocean world, and a heavy cold airy stone — with a long chain of four-mooned giants, the frontier's plumbing in the rough.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 19930 and Gaia EDR3 204244012964192640; Mesh gates to Ishiku's star, Gaia EDR3 179371719912574208, and HIP 20800.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the coppersmiths, under the old word for the red metal: *Akagane.* The register: *water should travel in something honest; here is ours.*

Akagane pipes the frontier. The coppersmiths' covenant works the heavy stone's ores into the frontier's water-lines, cook-pans, and the still-heads Stillhouse's frontier branch contracts by year — the honest metal, as the Meridian guild's own charter says, and the Compact's smiths agree, in their own language, with their own hands. The settlement's population of smiths, casters, and the apprentices whose first pot, by covenant law, is sold only to the smith's own family, is small, ruddy, and essential. The custom is the First Solder: each new water-line is joined by the whole crew, one joint each, and the last joint — always the hardest, always the same — is the youngest apprentice's, with the master's hand ready and, by tradition, unused. The grievance is the ore rush's freelancers, whose tailings the covenant's annual watershed report measures with a care the freelancers have called *hostile*, which the register translates, accurately, as *correct*.
## Fune

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-177425034575534464 (HIP 19930), K, 79.74 ly
- **Population:** 290,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over bare hot rocks, a shallow airy ocean world, a cold pebble, and a three-mooned giant — timber, water, and four roads, which is the whole bill of materials.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 218519487823384192; Mesh gates to HIP 17666B, HIP 17458, and Akagane's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the boatwrights, under the plain word for a boat: *Fune.* The register: *the ancestors left by boat; it worked; the principle scales.*

Fune builds the Compact's small hulls. The boatwrights' covenant lays the packets, cutters, and pond-ferry hulls the frontier runs on — Shin-Saga's tradition, applied at the new edge — and the settlement's population of boatwrights, apprentices, and the loft-families whose patterns are drawn on a floor two centuries of feet have made holy is small, sawdusted, and quiet in the way of shops where the work is the conversation. The custom is the Launching: each finished hull is walked to the water by the whole yard, and every hand on the line is a hand that built her, because, the covenant's rule runs, *a boat should know every one of her makers at least once, together.* The grievance is the Freeports' kit-hulls, flat-packed and cheaper by half, whose first-year failures the boatwrights' annual report catalogs, unpainted and unfixed, in the yard's own back boneyard, which the tourists photograph and the buyers, eventually, visit.
## Waraji

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-218366758786141440 (HIP 17458), M, 83.80 ly
- **Population:** 180,000
- **System:** a bright M star over locked furnace rocks, a bare temperate mooned stone, and a procession of three-mooned, four-mooned, five-mooned giants — straw, rope, and room.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 222804628232689792, HIP 15323, and Gaia EDR3 220989197096055040; Mesh gate to Fune's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the straw-crafters, under the word for the sandals that walked the ancestors' whole roads: *Waraji.* The register: *the ancestors went far on straw; so will we; it is a matter of weaving, not distance.*

Waraji weaves the frontier's feet. The straw-crafters' covenant — the rope-sandals, matting, rain-cloaks, and the thatch-ties Kaya's roofs depend on — works the ember world's grain stubble and reed-fields into the frontier's humble gear, and the settlement's population of plaiters, braiders, and the elders who teach the knot-names is small, deft, and dressed, in the covenant's phrase, *in the whole year's harvest twice over.* The custom is the Walking: each year's first sandals are worn, for their first hundred steps, by the whole settlement at once — everyone in new straw, walking the village loop together, because, the register holds, *the road should be opened in step, or it was never really opened.* The grievance is boots, the sealed kind, whose winter case the covenant's annual report concedes completely and whose summer case the report answers by pointing, silently, at everyone's feet in the square, in straw, in summer, in the square's own warm light, which is the argument ending.
## Mame

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-222804628232689792, brown dwarf, 82.51 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy super-earths — the second of them, **Mame** proper, a hot-dusk airy world at 307 K — a small hot locked sea, and mooned giants behind: the frontier's bean-belt and its crossroads.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 237363467857253504; Mesh gates to HIP 17666B, Waraji's star, and Gaia EDR3 220989197096055040.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the bean-growing families, under the plain word for the crop that feeds every winter: *Mame.* The register: *the bean asks for little; the bean gives everything; act accordingly.*

Mame feeds the frontier's long winters. The covenant's dusk-belt farms — beans, pulses, the soy that the Compact's kitchens turn into the frontier's protein three ways before breakfast — hold the hot-dusk world's terrace country, and the settlement's population of farm families, millers, and the fermenting-hall keepers whose miso and the rest age in the giants' cold moons is small, warm-smelling, and the reason the far routes eat. The custom is the First Pot: each harvest's first beans are cooked, whole, in the square, in one pot, by the year's newest household — and the settlement eats standing, from the same ladle-line, because, the register holds, *the bean is the compact the Compact is named for.* The grievance is the freight rates on the long routes to the far ports, which the covenant's annual letter to the tariff committees prices — precisely, politely, in beans-per-crate — an accounting so simple that it has survived every attempt to complicate it.
## Kinoko

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-122704230645584768 (HIP 15323), G, 84.99 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over hot mooned rocks, a temperate three-mooned giant, and a heavy airy cold stone — five roads out, and the dark, damp, quiet places the covenant came for.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 13642, HD 23040, Gaia EDR3 69927191475070080, and Gaia EDR3 220989197096055040; Regional approach to Waraji's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the cave-farming families — under the plain word for the mushroom: *Kinoko.* The register: *the forest keeps its pantry in the dark; we learned the address.*

Kinoko farms the dark. The cave-growing covenant's fungi-terraces — the log-lines, the damp vaults, the frontier's soup-trade — supply the Compact's far tables with the food that asks for no light, and the settlement's population of growers, log-cutters, and the spore-keepers whose library is the covenant's guarded warmth is small, earth-smelling, and entirely content with the arrangement. The custom is the First Log: each season's first inoculated log is carried, by hand, around the whole settlement before it enters the caves — the year's harvest shown to the town, in the light, once, before it goes into the dark to work. The grievance is damp, which the covenant's annual moisture report grades, cave by cave, with a precision the Meridian agronomy office has called *reassuring to a degree we have decided not to examine.*
## Sumi

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-116037204451525376 (HIP 13642), K, 73.41 ly
- **Population:** 180,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over hot heavy rocks and airy stones, with a 764-Earth-mass colossus at 4.5 au — the soot, the pine, and the slow press the covenant asked for, by accident of geology.
- **Links:** Regional gates to 51 Ari, HIP 13258, and (via GJ 3190's mesh) Kinoko's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the ink-stick makers, under the word for the ink itself: *Sumi.* The register: *the ancestors wrote with soot and patience; the recipe survives both.*

Sumi inks the Compact's frontier. The ink-stick covenant — the pine-soot presses, the binders, the slow curing in the colossus's moon-cold — makes the sticks the Compact's schools, calligraphers, and seal-cutters grind fresh, the tradition holding that ink made in advance is ink half-dead, an opinion the Meridian Atramentum guilds have formally studied and, to their own published surprise, declined to dispute. The settlement's population of soot-masters, binders, and the apprentice-graders is small, black-thumbed, and precise. The custom is the First Grinding: each school-year's opening class grinds the year's first ink together, one stick, one stone, in silence — the covenant's founding scene, re-enacted by every generation, which the register calls *the only ceremony that is also homework.* The grievance is the liquid inks of the Union's offices, which the covenant's annual demonstration defeats by the simple method of asking the audience which bottle they would trust with their grandmother's name.
## Ocha

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-115311458058061440 (51 Arietis), K, 69.13 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** a K sun over hot mooned rocks, a hot airy stone, and airy cold stones with five roads out — the covenant asked for mist, slope, and patience, and the survey found all three.
- **Links:** Regional gate to GJ 94; Mesh gates to Sumi's star, GJ 3190, HIP 13258, and HD 23040.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the tea-growing families, under the word for the leaf and the pot and the pause: *Ocha.* The register: *the ancestors negotiated everything over tea; we intend to keep the method.*

Ocha steeps the frontier's diplomacy. The tea covenant's mist-terraces — the shaded rows, the rolling halls, the famous first-flush the Compact sends to every polity's assembly as its annual courtesy — hold the frontier's high country, and the settlement's population of growers, rollers, and the tea-masters whose brewings arbitrate more frontier disputes than the circuit courts is small, fragrant, and quietly powerful in the way of people everyone must eventually sit down with. The custom is the First Cup: any dispute brought to the covenant's hall is heard over tea the disputants must pour each other, because — the register's line — *you cannot pour for your enemy without learning the exact size of his hands.* The grievance is weather: the mist the covenant's craft requires is the frontier's scarcest rain, and the covenant's annual prayer for fog — filed, in its way, with the Assembly's weather office — is the Compact's most beautiful bureaucratic document.
## Kaki

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-327930412432710784 (GJ 94), M, 56.89 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a mooned airy cold stone, and a chain of giants — the frontier's orchard-belt and its four roads.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 21482 and Gaia EDR3 342743754636574208; Mesh gates to Ocha's star and Gaia EDR3 345680648977744896.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the persimmon-growing families, under the word for the fruit that ripens by frost: *Kaki.* The register: *the ancestors loved a fruit that needs the cold; we are, in every sense, their people.*

Kaki sweetens the frontier's autumn. The persimmon covenant's orchards — the frost-ripening varieties Ringo's nurseries grafted, the drying-racks, the festival of the hanging fruit that lights the settlement's eaves orange through the whole cold season — hold the airy stone's sheltered vales, and the population of orchard families, dryers, and the children whose autumn chore-harvest is the Compact's favorite smell is small, bright, and busy in the way of villages whose calendar is a fruit. The custom is the First Frost-Check: the year's first frost is announced, officially, by whoever spots it, at whatever hour, by bell — and the whole settlement turns out to see, because, the register holds, *the frost is the orchard's co-signer.* The grievance is birds, the orchard's ancient rivals, whose treaties the covenant negotiates annually with nets, decoys, and one very specific scarecrow whose name changes with the fashion.
## Tatami

- **Settled:** 2426 (covenant charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-342743754636574208, M, 66.12 ly
- **Population:** 190,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked heavy rocks — the frontier's reed-beds in the making — with four roads out.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 345680648977744896; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 311392417520107136, Gaia EDR3 303438275527023744, and Kaki's star.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the rush-mat weavers — under the word every room in the Compact is measured by: *Tatami.* The register: *a house is a number of mats; a guest is a number of mats; a life is a great many mats, in the end.*

Tatami floors the Compact's frontier. The weavers' covenant grows the rush, cuts and dries it by season, and binds the mats whose count is the Compact's whole architecture of welcome — every new settlement's first rooms are floored by covenant tithe, because a frontier house that cannot be sat in, the register holds, *is a cargo hold with delusions.* The population is rush-growers, binders, and the apprentices whose first mat is, by law, given away, sight unseen, to whoever needs one most that week. The custom is the Count: at each settlement's founding, the covenant's master weaver recites the settlement's first floor-plan aloud, room by room, in mats, and the number is entered in the register — the Compact's way of saying that even the newest town is already a known size of home. The grievance is imported synthetic flooring, which the covenant's annual demonstration handles with a barefoot audience, in winter, on both, in the order the audience chooses — and the audience always chooses the same order, twice.
## Resolve

- **Settled:** 2426 (Assembly charter, the frontier wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-311392417520107136, M, 66.43 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked heavy rock, a hot locked ocean world (82% sea), bare cold stones, and a ladder of mooned giants — the Commonwealth's newest crossroads, far from home.
- **Links:** Regional gates to GJ 1024 and Gaia EDR3 303438275527023744; Mesh gates to HIP 3206, Tatami's star, and Gaia EDR3 365124382538652032.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Resolve* — the frontier virtue, petitioned for by a founding cooperative of Commonwealth families who had already survived two failed colonies and declined to fail a third. The dedication minute: *the name is not an aspiration; it is a record.*

Resolve is the Commonwealth's far door. The newest of the Assembly's charters, founded the same year as the crawl's present, holds five roads' worth of frontier traffic and the co-op families who asked for the charter — millers, well-diggers, and the stubborn craft-stock the ember quarter runs on — and the settlement's whole character is in its name: not hope, which the Assembly dedicates elsewhere, but the plainer thing that gets up in the cold. The customs are new and being made: the co-op's founding supper, eaten standing, is repeated at each sol's end — *so that nobody forgets what a new town eats like* — and the grievance, already traditional in its first year, is the Assembly's frontier levies, which Resolve's first annual letter describes, in the Commonwealth's own style, as *a tax on the virtue itself.*
## Fidelity

- **Settled:** 2402 (Assembly charter, Concord era).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2787758616366123136 (GJ 1024), M, 59.47 ly
- **Population:** 2.4 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked furnace rocks, then the settled world — a cool ocean planet at 281 K, 44% of its face under shallow sea, one moon — with a long cold tail of mooned giants.
- **Links:** Concord gate to GJ 3060 (USE); Regional gates to HIP 6069 and Gaia EDR3 91196316201965184; Mesh gates to Resolve's star and Gaia EDR3 94712996769289856.
- **Registry name:* dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Fidelity* — the virtue the Concord demands of its borders — on the petition of the settlement families who live on both sides of the treaty's edge. The dedication minute: *we did not choose the border; we chose to be true at it.*

Fidelity is the Commonwealth's northern Concord door. The cool ocean world's shore towns and orbital customs houses handle the mixed traffic of the demilitarized zone's newest crossing — Union trunk freight on the far side, Commonwealth co-op trade on this one — and the settlement's two-flag harbor life, built deliberately on the Amity model at smaller scale, is the Assembly's quiet demonstration that the border can be worked by people who mean it. The population is fisher families, customs co-ops, and the arbitrators of the small-water cases a shared sea inevitably breeds; the custom is the Toast of the Two Waters: at each sol's close, the harbor pours one cup from the settled world's sea and one from the Union-side river world's imported ice, and drinks them mixed — the treaty, in a glass, kept nightly. The grievance is the demilitarized patrol schedule, which both sides' officers must share, and whose annual negotiation, the harbormaster notes, is the only place in the quarter where two navies have ever agreed on a calendar, a fact both navies find quietly precious and neither will confirm.
## Threefields

- **Settled:** 2403 (Union Concord-era charter, northern spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2805045756653326080 (GJ 3060), M, 48.88 ly
- **Population:** 12 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace rock and a locked puffed giant, then the gift the survey called *an embarrassment*: three habitable worlds — a cold airy stone at 242 K, a warm ocean world (320 K, 78% sea), and a temperate airy rock at 269 K — the Union register's only such triple.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 3028 and Gaia EDR3 309977380415042432; Concord gate to GJ 1024 (Fidelity, Commonwealth).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2405: three livable worlds around one dim star, and the crews' own survey shorthand — *the three fields* — went to the Union Naming Board signed by every hull on the spur. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board considered a classical name and was advised by the petition, in writing, to count.*

Threefields is the Union's small miracle. Three worlds, one sun: the cold stone keeps the herds and the ice-hay, the ocean world keeps the fisheries and the warm ports, the temperate rock keeps the grain and the towns — and the triple's whole economy runs on the schedule of the short ferry triangle between them, the Union's busiest domestic route outside Sol's own system. The population spreads its twelve million thin and proud across the three, and the system's best thing is redundancy: the triple has never once had a bad year on all three worlds at once, a resilience the Union's civil-administration colleges teach as the standard case for settlement design — *the field rotation, writ planetary.* The custom is the Round: at each sol's turn, each world sends one delegation, one cargo, and one song to the next world in the triangle, and the songs accumulate, so that the harbor halls' repertoires are two centuries of all three worlds' music, kept in three keys. The grievance is representation — the Council of Nations seats the triple as one constituency, and the Threefields assembly has petitioned, annually, for three seats on the argument that *we already have the worlds; the arithmetic is not hard*, a position the Council's rules committee has received, filed, and sung about — the song arrived last year, from the ocean world, in three keys.
## GJ 3028 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2406; never promoted.
- A failed star's ember light, 39.98 ly — locked rocks, an airy cold super-earth, mooned ice. A fuel spur of the northern Concord run. The station's crew of eighty runs the system's single famous institution: the Register's shortest census return, filed annually and reading, in full, *still eighty, still fine, thank you.*
## Northfield

- **Settled:** 2409 (Union Concord-era charter, northern spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-392350008432819328, M, 37.49 ly
- **Population:** 4.8 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, then the settled world — a cold airy planet at 252 K with two moons, its long river valleys the spur's newest farm belt — with three roads out toward the Concord border.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 3069, GJ 3028, and Gaia EDR3 1925025168366253952.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2411: the northern spur's own word for its newest fields, submitted by the freight crews who had been hauling the settlement's first harvests and, in the petition's own line, *already calling it that on the manifests.*

Northfield is the spur's new farm country. The cold river valleys — grain, root crops, the famous Northfield flax that the Concord tariff schedules have already learned to spell — were settled by the Union's Concord-era land lottery, the first opened after the treaty, and the population of lottery families, mill crews, and the flax-hall towns is young, loud, and in the middle of inventing its own customs out of the Union's old ones: the Harvest Bell, rung from both moons' relay stations at each year's first grain-ship departure, has already been adopted by two other settlements on the spur. The grievance is the border's own shadow: the land lottery's neighbors on the Commonwealth side got their land by co-op charter, the two systems of ownership face each other across the demilitarized sky, and the annual conference of the two settlements' farm associations — held, by rotation, in Northfield's flax hall and Fidelity's harbor house — argues politely about everything except the one thing both sides farm: the fact that the soil, as the conference's joint minute records, *does not care, and is right.*
## Hitching Post

- **Settled:** 2412 (Union Concord-era charter, northern spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-411311395692644224 (GJ 3069), brown dwarf, 36.23 ly
- **Population:** 690,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a hot locked airy stone, and paired mooned giants — the northern spur's tie-up yard, four roads deep.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to ι Per, GJ 1053, HIP 7635, and Northfield's star.
- **Registry name:** the crews' joke, ratified 2414 with the Board's own relish: the junction where the northern runs tie up, change horses, and argue — the crews' own sign, painted on the depot's first piling, read *Hitching Post, feed & fuel*, and the Union Naming Board ratified the sign entire, including the ampersand. The Board's minute: *the Registry objects to the ampersand; the Board overrules the Registry; the sign is better prose.*

Hitching Post is the northern spur's yard. The junction's tie-up rings, transfer cradles, and the feed-and-fuel halls the sign promised (and delivered) serve four routes' traffic with the specific competence of a place whose whole population can back a trailer in the dark, and the settlement's dispatchers, tally-clerks, and canteen families keep the custom the crews brought with the name: the Check — every hull tied up at the Post gets its lines checked, by hand, by whichever crew is nearest, regardless of registry, cargo, or flag, because, the yard's oldest chalk-line says, *a tied ship is everybody's neighbor.* The grievance is the name's endless utility to comedians, which the depot's masters have answered, for a decade, with the same printed notice: *yes; we know; feed and fuel.*
## ι Per

- **Settled:** 2415 (Union Concord-era charter, northern spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-436648129327098496 (ι Persei), G, 34.50 ly
- **Population:** 21 million
- **System:** a bright G sun over hot mooned rocks, then the pair the survey called *the spur's apology for everything inward*: a temperate airy two-mooned world at 295 K, and a cool ocean planet at 283 K, 63% of its face under green sea.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 3147 and Hitching Post's star.
- **Registry name:* the Bayer designation, kept under the Union Naming Board's standing rule for the old astronomical tradition — the register's marginal note observes that ι Persei's light was catalogued from Earth before the Cataclysm, *which entitles it, the Board holds, to keep the address it had.*

ι Per is the northern spur's garden pair. The airy world's hill country — orchards, towns, the survey college that the land lottery's second wave built — and the ocean world's green sea — fisheries, ferry ports, the floating campus the two settlements share — make the system the Concord era's showpiece: two livable worlds, one bright sun, settled in a single decade by a Union that needed, visibly, to prove it still could. The population is lottery families, fisher stock, and the college towns' mixed generations; the two worlds' best thing is each other, and the ferry line between them — the Union's second-busiest after Threefields' triangle — runs on the shared clock both worlds' mayors set by treaty, a clock that has never once been reset and is, by now, a civic monument. The custom is the Pairing: each year's new ferry crews, from both worlds, train as mixed crews — one half from the hills, one half from the sea — because, the charter's line holds, *the system's two worlds are one argument, and the crew should hear both halves.* The grievance is precedence — which world is the capital, a question the charter famously declined to answer by placing the system's administration on the ferry itself, an office that moves sixty times a day and has therefore, the mayors agree, never once been captured.
## GJ 3147 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2418; never promoted.
- A failed star's embers over locked furnace rocks, a mooned icy stone, and one giant. A three-gate fuel spur of the garden runs. The depot's fuel crews have adopted, as their only tradition, the public reading of ι Per's ferry schedule every sol at the same hour — on the stated grounds that *it's nice to hear a place doing well.*
## Achird (gaia-edr3-425040000962559616)

- **Settled:** 2421 (Union Concord-era charter, near trunk).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-425040000962559616 (Achird, η Cassiopeiae A), K, 19.32 ly
- **Population:** 520,000
- **System:** a Sun-like K star over bare hot rocks, a heavy airy stone, a bare temperate mooned pebble — and, far out, a 776-Earth-mass colossus with captured companions whose slow weather the science corps came for.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Achird (η Cassiopeiae B); Trunk gate to GJ 3147.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule — one of the near-north's old light-houses, named before the Cataclysm, carried after it. The Board's minute: *the name was already resident; the Board confirmed the lease.*

Achird is the near-north's quiet giant-watcher. The Union's deep-survey corps holds the system for its colossus — the great far world's four-mooned slow-brewing storms, mapped now for four years and provisionally rated the quarter's finest long-term weather study — and the settlement of survey crews, fuel-tenders, and the archive families who keep the storm library serves the two trunk runs that pass through. The custom is the Storm Naming: each colossus-moon storm that survives a full season is named, by the survey's youngest member, from the register's open list — a list the crews have, for four years, drawn only from the names of the Cataclysm's lost cities, which the science corps' charter calls *the only weather worth remembering.* The grievance is funding, as ever; the storm library's budget line is renewed annually at exactly the same figure, a constancy the corps describes as *either fiscal prudence or a very slow storm of its own.*
## Achird B

- **Settled:** 2424 (Union Concord-era charter, near trunk).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-425040000962497792 (Achird, η Cassiopeiae B), M, 19.21 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** the K-giant's small companion: locked furnace rocks inward, a bare temperate pebble, then the settled world — a cold airy planet at 255 K with one moon — and a mooned giant behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to HIP 1475; Trunk gate to Achird (η Cassiopeiae A).
- **Registry name:** registered as *Achird B*, the companion's letter added under the Union Naming Board's binary rule: the pair shares the ancient name, the register distinguishes the address. The Board's minute: *the sky named them together; the Register agrees, and files them in order.*

Achird B is the near-north's newest garden. The cold temperate world's river country — the Concord era's third land lottery, and the last of the near-north's — carries orchard towns and the ferry traffic of a binary's twin settlements, close enough in the sky that Achird A's storm-watchers and Achird B's farmers share one harbor calendar and, increasingly, one set of grandchildren. The population is lottery families, orchardists, and the storm-library's outreach station, which teaches the farmers to read the colossus-watching corps' forecasts for their own weather's distant signatures. The custom is the Twin Bell: at each sol's dusk, the settlement rings its harbor bell and waits — and Achird A's station answers, across the quarter-light-hour, the binary's own call-and-response, started as a navigational check in 2424 and kept, by both sides, long after the navigation stopped needing it. The grievance is the tariff's binary rule, which files the pair as one trade zone and prices the ferry accordingly — an arithmetic the ferry crews describe as *correct, and lonely.*
## Two Coals

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, near trunk).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-385334230892516480 (HIP 1475, binary M+M), 11.62 ly
- **Population:** 2.9 million
