- **System:** twin dim red suns, each with its own retinue — the companion's seventh world is the settled one, a temperate airy planet at 271 K with one moon, under a sky where two faint fires take turns.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 905 and Achird B.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426, the year itself: the near-north's pilots had called the twin suns *the two coals* since the first trunk runs, and the settlement petitioned under the crews' word with the letter's whole argument in one line: *the coals kept us warm; here is the hearth.*

Two Coals is the Union's newest near-garden. The temperate world's valley towns — the Concord era's fourth and final land lottery, drawn mostly by young families from the crowded core, which the settlement's ballot-ledger records with the note *the Union still builds* — hold orchards, the first generation of municipal everything, and a sky of two small suns whose doubled dusk the poets of the young towns are, visibly, already working on. The population is new-settler stock, trunk crews, and the ferry yards; the custom is the Second Dusk: the twin suns' offset evenings give the world two nightfalls a sol, and the towns keep the second one — the later, redder, quieter one — as the civic hour, with all halls lit and the streets walked, because, the charter's youngest signatory wrote, *the day is long; we keep its second door open.* The grievance is nothing yet, and the census office's first annual report says so in one line, which the Register's archivists have already framed: *ask again in ten years; we are busy.*
## Slowlight

- **Settled:** 2418 (Union Concord-era charter, near trunk).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1926461164913660160 (GJ 905), brown dwarf, 10.31 ly
- **Population:** 3.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked airy worlds — two of them habitable at the cold dusk band (262 K and 246 K, the second with a moon) — with icy outrunners and a two-mooned giant behind: the near-north's twin-shade garden.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 65 B, 61 Cyg A, and Two Coals' star; Regional gate to HIP 91768.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2420: the ember light's long red afternoons — the near-north's pilots timed their approach runs by the system's famous slow dawns — put the word in the crews' logs before the settlement existed. The Board's minute: *ratified; the light was already named; the Register concurs.*

Slowlight is the near-north's dusk junction. The twin cold worlds' dusk-belt settlements — orchard towns, the twin ferry line, and the approach-control hall that times four routes' traffic by the star's own slow rhythm — run the Union's newest close-in hub, and the settlement's young population of lottery families, controllers, and the astronomers who came for the light and stayed for the neighbors has built one custom already: the Long Afternoon, the hour before each dusk when the whole town walks the terrace-line and greets everyone they meet, because — the first charter's line, already famous on the spur — *the light is slow; so are we; that is the point of the place.* The grievance is the second dusk's timing, which the two worlds share only approximately, and whose four-minute offset the twin towns' mayors have litigated, negotiated, and finally scheduled a festival around.
## 61 Cyg A

- **Settled:** 2420 (Union Concord-era charter, near trunk).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1872046609345556480 (61 Cygni A, binary K+M), 11.40 ly
- **Population:** 4.1 million
- **System:** the famous binary: a K star with a small M companion; furnace-class locked rocks inward, and — around the companion — the settled world, a cool airy planet at 265 K, one of the near-north's temperate gardens.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 91768 and Gaia EDR3 2019802555283940608; Trunk gate to Slowlight's star.
- **Registry name:** kept under the standing rule for the sky's old landmarks: 61 Cygni was the first star whose distance from Sol was ever measured — the parallax that began the map. The Union Naming Board's minute is the Register's most-quoted: *this is where the map started; the settlement now lives there; the Board considers the circle closed and the honor discharged.*

61 Cyg A is the surveyor's shrine. The cool world's observatory towns — the great parallax memorial, the Union's metrology museum, and the survey academy that cadets visit like a pilgrimage — hold the binary that opened humanity's measurement of the sky, and the settlement's population of astronomers, academy families, and the orchardists who feed them lives in the specific gravity of the fact: everyone here is, professionally or temperamentally, aware of standing at the origin of the map. The system's best thing is the academy's annual re-measurement: each year, with the original 1838 mathematics, the students measure the parallax again, by eye, and the academy publishes both numbers, which is the Union's most quietly devastating lesson in what continuity of method means. The custom is the First Question: every visiting student must ask, at the memorial, one question about the parallax that the answer cannot settle — a tradition that has produced two centuries of the quarter's best astronomy and at least one poet. The grievance is light pollution, in the observatory sense: the trunk traffic's approach lights wash the memorial's faint target star some nights, and the pilots' guild's annual dimming protocol is negotiated with the reverence of people who also, mostly, once asked a first question here.
## Gaia EDR3 2019802555283940608 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2422; never promoted.
- A barely-star, 22.28 ly — locked rocks, a locked temperate ice giant, and cold mooned ice. A relay spur of the near-north runs. The station's crew keeps the register's only parallax-themed toast, drunk each year on the anniversary of the 1838 measurement, to *the first number and all the ones since* — a custom borrowed, with permission and some pride, from 61 Cyg A's academy.
## GJ 1256 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2423; never promoted.
- A dim ember, 31.09 ly — locked heavy rocks, a cold airy super-earth, and a ladder of mooned giants. A relay spur of the near-north's outer runs. The relay's watch-crew of forty maintains the system's one monument: the fuse-board from the founding year, still lit, its original label reading, in the founder's hand, *do not touch, this means you*, honored now by three generations of crews who have never once touched it.
## Kindling

- **Settled:** 2424 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1749030362195988096, brown dwarf, 31.72 ly
- **Population:** 1.0 million
- **System:** a barely-star holding a tight ladder of locked rocks and ices — the second of them, **Kindling** proper, a cold airy dusk world at 263 K — and paired giants behind: the smallest livable ember on the register.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 1236 and (via GJ 1256) the near-north runs.
- **Registry name:** the crews' joke, ratified with delight: the near-north's pilots called the tiny star with the one warm world *the kindling* on the approach charts, and the settlement — a young town on a cold dusk world around a barely-there sun — petitioned under the word with the argument, in full: *we checked; it's accurate.*

Kindling is the Union's smallest garden. The cold dusk world's single settlement — one long terrace town down the terminator's warm seam, glasshouses, the famous fire-hall whose hearth has burned since the founding — holds a million people who chose the smallest light on the register, and the town's whole culture is warmth kept communally: the fire-hall's blaze is public by charter, the glasshouses share heat by law of neighbors, and the settlement's one great festival is First Flame, the anniversary night when every household lights its own hearth from the fire-hall's coal, carried in procession, small and enormous at once. The grievance is the tariff's fuel schedule, which prices the system's warmth as a luxury import, an opinion the settlement's annual petition answers by enclosing, each year, one small lump of the fire-hall's coal, *for the Committee's consideration, and, we hope, its comfort.*
## Waterside

- **Settled:** 2425 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4295619138933719808 (GJ 1236), M, 34.63 ly
- **Population:** 5.8 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, then the settled pair: a cool ocean world (301 K, 58% sea, one moon) and a cold airy two-mooned rock at 255 K — the water and the field, sharing one small sun.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 92871 and Alshain; Trunk gate to Kindling's spur.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the ocean world's harbors were the spur's first landfall for the Concord-era settlement runs, and the crews' approach-call — *coming up on the waterside* — was the petition's entire text.

Waterside is the spur's young pair. The ocean world's port towns — fisher families, the ferry yards, the pearl concessions already leased to Coldharbor's combine, which the settlement's charter accepted with visible arithmetic — and the airy rock's new farms make the Union's latest two-world charter, and the young population's whole calendar is the ferry between them, whose schedule the two mayors set together each sol over the same working breakfast, a custom the settlement calls *the floating cabinet.* The custom that matters more is the Landfall Bell: each arriving settlement ship is met at the harbor by whoever is ashore, no matter the hour, because — the charter's line — *we were all landed by somebody; the bell remembers.* The grievance is the pearl lease, whose terms the farms' delegates call *generous to the wrong world*, an opening position the port's delegates have described, in the same session, as *a good sign: everyone is negotiating like neighbors.*
## HIP 92871 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2425; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 36.28 ly — locked furnace rocks and a mooned cold pebble. A fuel spur on the young runs toward Alshain. The depot's crew of sixty has adopted Waterside's Landfall Bell custom, rung for every hull that tops the approach — a borrowing the Union's Register notes, with approval, *travels faster than the tariff tables.*
## Stillwater

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest of the near-north).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4512525016089353472, M, 45.91 ly
- **Population:** 2.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over locked furnace rocks and hot shallow seas, then the settled world — a cool ocean planet at 274 K, two-thirds of its face under calm sea, two patient moons — with airy ice and a five-mooned giant beyond.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 92871 and HD 176051A.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified in the year 2426 itself: the ocean world's famous calm — the two moons' tides cancel on a nine-day rhythm, leaving the equatorial sea literally still — was on every approach chart before the settlement drew lots. The Board's minute: *ratified; the sea signed first.*

Stillwater is the Union's calmest garden. The cool ocean world's glass-flat doldrums belt — the settlement's harbor towns, the ferry fleets, the sailing academy the near-north's crews already train at — makes the system the spur's newest pride, and the young population of fisher families, academy staff, and the lottery's last wave lives by the nine-day still tide, whose return each cycle the whole culture times itself to: markets on the still days, work in the chop, festivals on the turn. The custom is the Mirror: on each still day's noon, the harbor towns set one lamp burning on the sea's surface itself, floated, and the towns' lights and their reflections are photographed together — the settlement's own image of itself, on the flattest water in the register. The grievance is the mirror's tourists, whose wake the academy's boats are teaching, patiently, to be slower than the sea.
## Shallow Bay

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the spur's far end).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2043885295914531072 (HD 176051A), G, 48.58 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over hot mooned rocks and an airy stone, then the settled world — a cool ocean planet at 278 K, a fifth of its face under shallow sea, two moons — with paired far giants and a long cold tail.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Stillwater's star (the spur's only road).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the far spur's terminus world, its bays shallow, warm, and famous for their light — the crews' fishing-camp charts said *the shallow bays*, and the settlement kept the plural. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register notes that the name is descriptive and the description is kind.*

Shallow Bay is the far spur's last harbor. The cool world's bay country — a scattering of fishing towns, the tidal farms that the two moons' laddered tides make the most productive in the near-north, and the light-house coast the crews' fishing camps built before the charter existed — holds the spur's end-of-road population, and the settlement's whole character is that of a place everyone arrives at on purpose: the lottery's farthest lots, chosen by families who asked for the edge and got it. The custom is the Lighthouse Watch: the coast's old camp-lights, kept burning since before the settlement, are maintained by the whole town in rotation, and no light may be modernized without a public vote — a conservatism the charter explains in one line: *the lights brought us here; we owe them the fire.* The grievance is the road itself: the single gate's tariff schedule prices the spur's end as a detour, an arithmetic the settlement's annual letter answers with the bay's catch totals, which the tariff office has begun, quietly, to circulate.
## Alshain

- **Settled:** 2422 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4296708789290712064 (Alshain, β Aquilae), K giant (1.5 solar masses, 5.5 solar luminosities), 44.36 ly
- **Population:** 890,000
- **System:** a vast K giant — the old sky's falcon-star — over a furnace-class mooned rock, and a colossal 818-Earth-mass giant at 1.4 au, warm at 343 K, four moons riding it: the system's whole life is in the colossus's calm.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 1236 and 31 Aql.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: one of the Falcon's three stars, named from Earth's sky before the Cataclysm, carried with the register since. The Board's minute: *the falcon flies with the Register; the Register is honored.*

Alshain is the warm-giant world. The colossus's four moons — bathed in the old giant's amber light, warmed by the great planet's own heat — hold the settlement's orbital towns, the gas-works that skim the warm giant's calm, and the science corps' largest atmospheric-institute in the near-north, which studies the warm giant's 343 K decks as the register's finest natural case of a benign sky. The population is moon-town families, skim crews, and the institute's staff; the customs are the falcon's: at each sol's first light, the moon-towns' children fly kites in the amber wind — the Falcon's star, kept in the sky by hands — a tradition the science corps dated, provisionally, to the first crew's first day. The grievance is brightness: the K giant's slow swelling, charted by the institute for a century on Earth before the settlement, is charted here now, and the moon-towns' children's kites fly in a light the Register's marginal note calls *ancient, ample, and — the note is careful — on schedule.*
## 31 Aql *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2423; never promoted.
- K-dwarf, 48.67 ly — furnace-class locked rocks, a 540-Earth-mass hot giant, airy cold stones, and a long captured tail. A dead-end spur past Alshain, held for the assay office's giant-studies station. The station's crew of thirty keeps the register's hottest recorded choir: they sing, in the hot giant's radio emissions, the interference pattern the science corps transcribed into four-part harmony, and the arrangement is performed annually, in the station's mess, to an audience of everyone.
## Jiu

- **Settled:** 2424 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1891598193816300544 (Jiu), G (1.4 solar masses), 38.84 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** a hot bright G sun over heavy hot airy rocks, a cold airy stone, and a 546-Earth-mass giant with four moons — a system with no ground worth farming and one famous name.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 1256, HIP 103441, and Helvetios.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: *Jiu* — wine, from the old Chinese tradition — a name the Union Naming Board ratified with a minute that has entered the Register's small anthology: *the system is named for wine and grows none; the Board declines to apologize for the sky.*

Jiu is the near-north's wine-run hub, named, impossibly, for the thing it cannot grow. The settlement is orbital — the giant's moon-towns, the assay office, the freight rings that move the Concord-era's wine trade (Sunhill's orchards, ι Per's vine terraces, the whole spur's harvest) through three gates — and the population's own joke, adopted formally as the settlement's crest, is a vine growing upside down, rooted in orbit: *from the sky, everything.* The customs are the crews': the Tasting, each freight cycle's first wine-lot opened and drunk at the tariff hall, by whoever is on duty, in the customs of every polity the cargo will pass — the trade's one universal liturgy. The grievance is the name's endless confusion with the vintage charts it ships, an ambiguity the harbor office has monetized, with the Board's horrified blessing, on every label in the quarter.
## HIP 103441 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2424; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 47.07 ly — locked rocks and cold mooned stone out to a 91-Earth-mass giant. A fuel relay on the wine-run's quiet leg. The relay's crew of forty-two maintains the run's only sober custom: a grapevine, growing in the mess-hall window, planted from Jiu's crest-seed, producing exactly enough each year for one small jar of jam, which is divided, by lot, into forty-two parts.
## Helvetios

- **Settled:** 2425 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2835207319109249920 (Helvetios, 51 Pegasi), K, 50.65 ly
- **Population:** 490,000
- **System:** a Sun-like K star over hot airy mooned rocks, a cold airy two-mooned stone at 215 K, and a warm 213-Earth-mass giant with four moons — the register's quiet monument.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Jiu (the spur's only road).
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: Helvetios, the old word for the Swiss, the star whose first found companion — the first world ever detected around another sun, found by the old astronomers' patient math before the Cataclysm — opened the map the whole register now lives in. The Board's minute: *ratified with the Register's oldest honor; this star is why the Register exists.*

Helvetios is the exoplanet's shrine. The Union's deep-history science corps holds the dead-end spur for the monument itself: the found-world memorial — a simple ring station, the first discovery's mathematics cast into its wall, and the names of the two old astronomers who found the first world, cut where every visitor's hand can reach — anchors a settlement of curators, the science corps' archive annex, and the wine-run's quietest port. The population is small, deliberate, and uniformly aware of standing in the prologue of everything: the custom is the Founding Year, the anniversary the whole settlement marks by re-deriving, publicly, on paper, with the original math, the presence of the first world — an hour of arithmetic the visitors' book records as *the register's only ceremony that ends in applause for a number.*
## HIP 91768 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2416; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 11.42 ly — locked furnace rocks, a temperate bare mooned stone, and airy far rocks. The near-north's quiet junction, held for its assay office and the famous parallax-anniversary relay: the station's instruments are those 61 Cyg A's academy trusts for its annual northern check-sightings, a duty the crew lists, in the port directory, under *honoraria.*
## HIP 91772 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2416; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 11.49 ly — locked rocks, a bare mooned stone, and a long chain of giants. A fuel spur of the near-north's inner runs. The fuel crew's one distinction is the register's most dedicated garden: a hydroponic allotment whose tomato surplus is famous on three systems, and whose seeds, the crews insist, came from 61 Cyg A's academy canteen, which the academy neither confirms nor denies, honorably.
## HIP 86162 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2417; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 14.84 ly — locked rocks, a small hot ocean world with one moon, icy mooned stone. A three-gate relay of the near-north's inner runs. The relay crew's long-running claim to fame is the navigational almanac's footnotes, which cite the station's sighting-logs more often than any other on the register — a distinction the crew attributes to *the sky being extra interesting here*, and the academy, dryly, to the crew writing it all down.
## Alsafi

- **Settled:** 2417 (Union Concord-era charter, near trunk).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2261614264931057664 (Alsafi, σ Draconis), K, 18.80 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** a K sun over a locked furnace mooned rock, hot bare stones, and a 178-Earth-mass giant with a captured companion — a system whose name, from the old Arabic sky, meant the cooking-tripod.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 86162 and Gaia EDR3 2161121135533953536; Regional gate to GJ 1227.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: *Alsafi*, the tripod, the pot-holder of the old Draconis sky. The Board's minute is the Register's most quoted after 61 Cyg A's: *the star has always been the cook; the settlement now is too.*

Alsafi is the near-north's kitchen. The spur's great canteen complex — the Union's cooking academy annex, the supply halls, and the famous Tripod, the great common-kitchen the whole settlement eats from — anchors the junction's three routes, and the crews of half the near-north time their runs by its menu, which is posted, in the true spirit of the register, on the almanac's inner cover. The population is canteen families, academy staff, and the provisioners who keep the Tripod's fires (three, by charter, never fewer — the tripod honored literally) burning. The custom is the Shared Pot: at each sol's turn, one dish is cooked in the Tripod by a different route's crews, on the theory — the charter's line — *that whoever eats together files no complaints; cook, therefore.* The grievance is the Union's ration schedules, which price flavor as a luxury, an opinion the Tripod's cooks answer by feeding the tariff office's auditors so well, when they visit, that their reports have apologized, twice, in print.
## Saltcellar

- **Settled:** 2418 (Union Concord-era charter, Alsafi run).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2161121135533953536, M, 25.42 ly
- **Population:** 870,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked steam ocean, then the settled world — a cool locked twilight ocean planet at 283 K, shallow seas over 28% of its face — with mooned icy stone behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Alsafi and GJ 1227.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2419 with the Tripod's cooks in open support: the cool-sea world's salt, salt fish, and shallot-cress supply Alsafi's great kitchen, and the crews called the settlement the cellar. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register notes the pantry is organized.*

Saltcellar is Alsafi's pantry. The cool twilight seas' fisheries and salt-flats — the shallow shelf's shellfish, the evaporator terraces, the cress-beds that the Tripod's chefs specify by tide — feed the near-north's most famous kitchen and half the spur's tables besides, and the settlement's population of fisher families, salt-makers, and the delivery skiffs' crews lives on the kitchen's schedule and the kitchen's gratitude, which is paid, by Alsafi's charter, in first-helpings: each sol's Tripod pot starts with Saltcellar's catch, and the crews deliver it personally. The custom is the First Ladle: the delivery crew that brings the sol's first catch also eats the sol's first bowl, at the Tripod's high table, by right — the register's tastiest law. The grievance is the tariff's classification of the cress as *decorative produce*, a ruling the settlement's annual appeal encloses, each year, one bunch of the cress in question, which the tariff office reports, ruefully, is delicious.
## GJ 1227 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2419; never promoted.
- An ember primary over a locked puffed giant, a bare temperate pebble, and one cold stone. A short spur off the kitchen runs, held by the assay office for the giant's convenient 491 K. The station's crew of thirty keeps the run's funniest single tradition: a small bell, rung once per sol, at the moment the giant's flare watch reports *nothing doing*, which the crew calls *the good news service.*
## HIP 83945 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2419; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 24.78 ly — locked rocks, a hot ocean world two-thirds sea, and an airy mooned stone. A fuel spur on the near-north's western runs. The depot crew's one institution is the world's smallest lending library, two crates of books that travel with the fuel tenders and return, mysteriously, always one volume heavier.
## Two Harbors

- **Settled:** 2420 (Union Concord-era charter, western runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4594497769766809216 (86 Herculis, binary K+M), 27.20 ly
- **Population:** 5.1 million
- **System:** the bright K primary holds hot rocks and the settled pair — two warm ocean worlds (309 K, 46% sea, two moons; and 314 K, 17% sea) — with the small M companion's own family of giants riding behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to HIP 83945 and HIP 83043.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2421: the two warm worlds' harbors, taken together, were the charter's whole prospectus, and the crews' approach-call named them before the survey finished. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register observes that the system is, top to bottom, a docking diagram.*

Two Harbors is the Concord era's twin-port. The two warm ocean worlds' harbors — the inner world's two-mooned tidal city and the outer world's long shallow-sound ports — were chartered as one settlement with two doors, and the young population of fisher families, port crews, and the ferry trade that runs between them at six-hour intervals has built the Union's newest twin-city culture: one harbor's catch is the other's market, one world's storm season is the other's calm, and the two mayors' shared office sits, by treaty, on the ferry itself, in perpetuity. The custom is the Twin Toast: at each sol's close, both harbors' lighthouse keepers signal the day's safe arrivals to each other across the system's quiet, and each harbor's bars drink to the other's ledger, a courtesy that has survived two storms, one strike, and the budget office's suggestion that the office, at least, might move ashore.
## HIP 83043 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2420; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 33.89 ly — locked rocks, a shallow hot ocean world, and a long ladder of giant, mooned ice. A three-gate fuel relay of the western runs. The relay crews' long game is the register's most patient: an ongoing attempt, now in its sixth year, to breed a tomato that ripens in the light of a dim red sun, which the crew concedes is *not why we're here*, and the almanac's garden column concedes is *why we keep reading.*
## Lantern Bay

- **Settled:** 2421 (Union Concord-era charter, western spur's end).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1348028919540189056 (HIP 84790), M, 39.90 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked puffed giant, then the settled world — a cool locked twilight ocean planet at 298 K, shallow seas over a quarter of its face, one moon — with the spur's road ending at its gate.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to HIP 83043 (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2422: the far sea world's harbor lights — the first settlement's lamp-line across the bay, lit the night the charter crews arrived before the domes were raised — named the port from its own first evening. The Board's minute: *ratified; the lights were the petition.*

Lantern Bay is the western spur's end. The cool twilight sea's bay towns — fisher families, lamp-keepers, the tidal farms — hold the Union's farthest Concord-era settlement, and the population's whole culture is the lamp-line: the bay's original string of lights, kept burning by charter, added to by each generation, so that the harbor's nightly reflection now carries two centuries of arrivals in it. The best thing here is the shellfish: the bay's cold-shelf harvest, graded by Saltcellar's standards and served at Alsafi's Tripod on feast sols, is the spur's luxury, and the settlement's fishers are quietly rich and loudly humble about it. The custom is the Last Lamp: the lamp-line's farthest light, the one that faces the dark of the spur's end, is tended by the settlement's newest adult each year, by rotation, on the stated grounds that *the newest hand should hold the farthest light, and learn what far means.* The grievance is the tariff's edge-of-map surcharge, which the annual letter prices in lamp-oil, a unit the tariff office has twice had to ask about, which the settlement considers the point.
## GJ 1215 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2421; never promoted.
- A failed star, 40.35 ly — a locked furnace rock, a locked temperate giant (286 K), and mooned ice to the rim. A two-gate fuel spur of the western runs. The station's claim is the assay office's oldest wager, still standing: a sealed bet on the locked giant's core temperature, to be opened when the science corps' instruments settle the question — the bet's two sides have been arguing about the stakes' definition for a decade, which the Register notes is *also a kind of science.*
## GJ 3997 A *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2421; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 44.64 ly — heavy locked rocks, a mooned airy stone, and a giant-heavy tail. A fuel spur past the spur's own spur. The depot's small crew holds the western runs' unofficial record: the most consecutive clean flare-watches in the register, a streak of eleven years that the crews attribute, in the log's only editorial remark, to *good behavior.*
## Fathom

- **Settled:** 2422 (Union Concord-era charter, far western spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4568060562432051584 (HIP 83762), M, 45.96 ly
- **Population:** 660,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, then the settled world — a cool ocean planet at 278 K, four-fifths of its face under deep sea — with a three-mooned giant behind it.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 3997 A and 72 Her.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2423: the ocean world's soundings defeated the survey's first two lines — the third, at last, found the floor, and the crews called the world by the depth it took to find it. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register notes the name is also a unit, and the system is both.*

Fathom is the far spur's deep sea. The cool world's ocean — deep, quiet, and five kilometers over its own floor in the great basins — holds the settlement's floating towns, the deep-liner fleets, and the Union's newest oceanography annex, which came for the basins and stayed for the peace. The population is liner families, float-town crews, and the scientists whose depth charts now fill the annex's halls; the custom is the Sounding: each sol's first liner casts the old lead-line, by hand, once — not for the data, the annex's charter specifies, but for the habit, because *the floor is real, the hand should know it.* The grievance is the fish markets' deep-speculation — the futures desks pricing the basins' undiscovered stocks, an optimism the annex's annual letter grades as *hydrologically premature* — and the crews, who have watched the basins keep their secrets for a decade, side with the letter.
## 72 Her *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2422; never promoted.
- A Sun-like G star, 47.57 ly — hot airy rocks, a bare temperate two-mooned stone, and a captured companion. The far western spur's terminus: an assay station and the science corps' watching post for the spur's end-of-map surveys. The post's crew of twenty-one keeps the register's most isolated garden allotment, whose produce is traded, by mutual arrangement, with Fathom's float-towns for fish, an economy the almanac's margin calls *the spur's whole answer to the tariff.*
## Deltoton

- **Settled:** 2417 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-326173044957518080 (Deltoton, δ Trianguli), K, 35.59 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** a Sun-like K star over hot mooned rocks, a bare temperate world at 281 K — the register's nearest miss — and airy cold stones with a long icy tail.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 3147, Titawin, and Gaia EDR3 121254932585133568.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: Deltoton, the old sky's triangle. The Board's minute observes the system's own near-miss world completes a familiar shape — *the world, the survey, and the sky: three points that do not quite close* — and ratifies the name with what the Register calls *visible enjoyment.*

Deltoton is the near-north's almost-world. The bare temperate stone — 281 K, water-bearing, airless, the third such in the Union's holdings — anchors the science corps' near-north terraforming annex, the sibling study of HIP 107649's two-century patience, and the settlement of scientists, survey crews, and the annex's long-game staff lives beside a world one atmosphere short of a garden, on purpose: someone has to watch the door for when the sky arrives. The custom is the Two-Column Table: the annex publishes, each year, the bare world's readouts beside a hypothetical inhabited column, side by side — a ledger of the distance between here and there that has narrowed, at current rates, by exactly the width of one instrument's error bar since the charter. The grievance is the timeline, which the annex's annual letter states, without complaint and without mercy: *we are on schedule; the schedule is long; both facts are the project.*
## Titawin

- **Settled:** 2418 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-348020482735930112 (Titawin, binary G+M), 43.95 ly
- **Population:** 2.9 million
- **System:** the bright G primary holds hot mooned rocks and the settled pair — a cool ocean world (299 K, a third of its face under shallow sea) and, far out, a cold airy two-mooned rock at 251 K — with the small companion's own locked family riding behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Deltoton, Gaia EDR3 373838214051410816, and HIP 7918.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule — a name from the old Earth's naming decade, carried with the register. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register notes the name's origin in the old world's twentieth century of discovery, which the Register keeps, as it keeps everything, with respect.*

Titawin is the near-north's paired garden. The ocean world's shore towns — the fishing fleets, the salt trade, the first-generation harbor culture the Concord charters all grow — and the far cold rock's highland farms, terraced under the long orbit's patient seasons, make a two-world settlement whose young population has already produced the spur's best small poet (the far rock's winters) and best small comedy (the ocean world's tourists, every year, attempting to swim in the rock's ponds). The custom is the Carrying: each year's harvest exchange between the two worlds is moved, hull by hull, by the settlement's teenagers — a chore by charter, a status by tradition, and the subject of the poet's second-best poem. The grievance is the orbit itself: the far rock's long year means its farms miss some trade cycles entirely, an arithmetic the harbor's futures desks find fascinating and the farmers find, in the word their annual letter chose, *agricultural.*
## Wintershade

- **Settled:** 2423 (Union Concord-era charter, Titawin's spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-373838214051410816, M, 47.69 ly
- **Population:** 390,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked airy rocks — the third of them, **Wintershade** proper, a cold airy dusk world at 257 K — and a two-mooned giant behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Titawin (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2424: the cold dusk world's long blue-gray afternoons, the quietest light on the spur, gave the settlement its word before the lots were drawn. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register observes that the name is a weather report, and approves of weather reports.*

Wintershade is the spur's quietest garden. The cold dusk belt's greenhouse towns — glasshouse commons, the wool markets, the settlement's famous blue-gray weaving, whose shade the town's dyers pull from the light itself — hold the spur's smallest livable world and the population of growers, weavers, and the coasters who run the one road to Titawin's harbors. The best thing here is the cloth: Wintershade gray, the weavers' trademark, is the near-north's garment standard the way Fairweather's bread is its pantry standard, and the crews' coats are cut from it by tradition nobody had to start. The custom is the Shade Market: on the sol's longest dusk, the whole town trades cloth outdoors, by lamplight, at prices the buyers set by auction and the sellers set by pride — a market so fair the almanac cites it in its ethics section, which exists, the almanac's preface notes, because of this market.
## HIP 7918 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2418; never promoted.
- A Sun-like G star, 47.95 ly — mooned rocks, a temperate giant, and a 703-Earth-mass far colossus. A dead-end spur past Titawin, held by the survey corps for the far giant's slow clock. The post's crew of eighteen keeps the register's outermost garden: a hydroponic bay whose peas, the science corps' annual report confirms with a straight face, *grow toward Sol, which is not visible from this system; the Register declines to interpret.*
## Firefly

- **Settled:** 2418 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-121254932585133568, M, 44.55 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked airy rocks — the third of them, **Firefly** proper, a cold airy dusk world at 267 K with one moon — and a bare cold stone at the rim.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Deltoton, HIP 19337, and GJ 3181.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2419: the dusk world's settlement lights — small, numerous, and famously strung — were the first thing every approach run saw, and the crews' approach-calls said *fireflies* until the name settled itself. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board has visited the lights, and concurs.*

Firefly is the near-north's lamplit crossroads. The cold dusk belt's terrace towns — glasshouse commons, the junction's three gates' worth of canteen and tariff traffic, and the string-light culture the settlement is named for — hold the runs' youngest hub, and the population's custom is the Lighting: at each sol's dusk-bell, every household hangs one lamp on the town-line for the travelers' last hour of approach, a charter duty performed with what the almanac's margin calls *visible enthusiasm* — the whole world's edge, every evening, briefly an ornament. The best thing here is the glasshouse craft: the dusk belt's growers wrote the near-north's low-light farming standard, which the Concord-era settlements from Kindling to Boomtown now cite. The grievance is the tariff's classification of lamp-oil as a luxury good, a ruling the settlement's annual appeal answers by sending the tariff office, each year, one lamp, unlit, with the note: *you will know when.*
## Windrow

- **Settled:** 2419 (Union Concord-era charter, Firefly's spur).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-170639948323693952 (HIP 19337), M, 45.41 ly
- **Population:** 580,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a mooned temperate pebble, then the settled world — a cold airy planet at 256 K with one moon, its hedged fields the spur's newest farm belt.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Firefly (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2420: the cold world's first harvests were windrowed — laid in rows to dry in the ember light — and the crews who hauled the first grain called the place by its rows. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register observes that farmers name by function and crews by sight, and the two agreed here.*

Windrow is the spur's grain tail. The cold world's hedged country — the windbreaks the charter crews planted before their own houses, the grain-drying rows that named the place, and the famous first-harvest bell the settlement still rings each year — holds the far runs' newest farm population, and the settlement's young families, mill crews, and the hedge-keepers whose windbreak lines are already two generations deep live by the almanac's drying tables, which Windrow's farmers now write. The custom is the First Windrow: each harvest's opening row is cut, raked, and turned by the whole town, in one afternoon, because — the charter's line — *the first row teaches the year.* The grievance is the drying weather, which the farmers' annual letter to the almanac's climate desk grades, row by row, in a format the climate desk has described as *the most useful complaint in the Register.*
## GJ 3181 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2419; never promoted.
- A failed star, 45.55 ly — locked rocks, a hot shallow sea, and paired giants. The far end of Firefly's second spur, held by the assay office's volatiles works. The works' crew of thirty-one keeps one tradition: the sol's-end swim in the temperate giant's upper clouds, in a sealed skiff, which the Register's safety office has inspected, licensed, and — the inspection report's final line concedes — *considered joining.*
## Lastlight

- **Settled:** 2420 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-547381594623756416 (GJ 1053), M, 39.46 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a ladder of locked rocks — the second of them, **Lastlight** proper, a cool airy dusk world at 297 K — and a mooned ice giant behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Hitching Post's star and Errai.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2421: the dusk world's terminator light — the long red last-of-day that its belt settlers live in — was the crews' word on every approach chart. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register notes the day here never ends so much as it leaves.*

Lastlight is the runs' dusk country. The cool belt's terrace towns — the glasshouse commons the charter crews raised, the red-grain mills, and the observatory annex that came for the terminator's stable seeing — hold the spur's calmest settlement, a place where the whole culture runs on the ember light's long, even leaving. The population is grower families, mill crews, and the annex's astronomers, whose seeing conditions the near-north's academy cites as the spur's best; the custom is the Watching: at each dusk's deepest red, the whole town pauses for one bell's length — no tools, no talk, the long light only — a custom the charter's single explanatory line calls *the settlement's whole religion, in one minute.* The grievance is grain prices, which the futures desks of Jiu and Two Harbors set from a distance the farmers describe, in the annual letter, as *further than the light.*
## Errai

- **Settled:** 2420 (Union Concord-era charter, near-north runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2281778105594488192 (Errai, γ Cephei), K giant (1.75 solar masses), 44.98 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a vast K giant — the old sky's shepherd — over a mooned hot rock, then the settled world: a cool ocean planet at 296 K, half of its face under green sea, two moons — and, far out, a 569-Earth-mass giant holding captured companions.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Lastlight (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: *Errai*, the shepherd, from the old Arabic sky — and the star the old astronomers' math said would stand, some millennia hence, at Sol's own pole, the north star of a future Earth. The Board's minute is the Register's gentlest: *the shepherd will guide a world it no longer sees; the settlement keeps the watch.*

Errai is the shepherd's watch. The cool ocean world's shore towns — the fishing fleets, the great meridian observatory the science corps built on the two-mooned tides, and the small settlement that tends both — hold the near-north's most quietly moving monument: the pole-star calculation, the old math's projection of Errai's slow walk to the north of Sol's sky, cast into the observatory's meridian floor in metal, marked with the year the Register computes. The population is observatory families, fisher stock, and the curators of the monument; the custom is the Shepherd's Bell, rung at each year's turn, one clear note for the star's patient journey — a distance the settlement knows it will not see the end of, and keeps anyway, because — the observatory's charter line — *the sky's long promises are the only kind worth keeping.*
## HIP 7635 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2420; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 42.98 ly — locked heavy rocks and a mooned airy super-earth. A two-gate relay of the near-north's outer runs. The relay crew of forty-four maintains the register's most photographed mailbox: a standard-issue Union mail locker, hand-painted, whose key is kept by tradition in the pocket of whoever is youngest, and whose combinations, the crews report, have been changed one thousand and one times, always by the previous youngest.
## Glasswater

- **Settled:** 2421 (Union Concord-era charter, the runs' far end).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-524551232110220800, M, 46.67 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a tight ladder of locked rocks and hot seas, then the settled world — a cool locked twilight ocean planet at 286 K, half of its face under clear shallow sea — with icy outrunners and a four-mooned giant behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to HIP 7635 (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2422: the twilight sea's famous clarity — you can see the floor through forty meters of it — was the survey's whole margin note, and the crews' word. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Register confirms the water, and the name, are both transparent.*

Glasswater is the far runs' clear sea. The locked ocean world's twilight shelf — the shallow bays, the sea-glass beaches the settlement is named for, the diving-crew schools the near-north's salvagers now certify through — holds the spur's farthest livable world and its smallest population, a young settlement whose whole economy is the shelf: the glass-fisheries, the diving trade, and the sea-glass craftworks whose lamps and beads have already reached the markets of Jiu. The custom is the Clear Day: each sol when the shelf's clarity reaches the season's best — measured, by tradition, by how far down the settlement's youngest diver can count the sea-grass rows — the town closes its ledgers early and swims. The grievance is the one road: the freight schedule prices the far spur's glassworks as *exotic*, a word the annual letter returns each year, corrected, with samples.
## Gaia EDR3 1925025168366253952 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2420; never promoted.
- A barely-star, 47.18 ly — locked rocks and a cold ladder of mooned giants. A three-gate relay whose Concord crossing opens toward the League's northern border. The station crews keep the register's only bilingual tariff board — Union and Commonwealth both, by choice — which the customs office calls *efficiency* and the crews call *manners*, correctly.
## GJ 1268 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2420; never promoted.
- An ember star, 51.14 ly — locked rocks, a hot locked sea, and one bare stone. A junction of the Concord-era crossings toward the League's north. The relay's small crew holds the Union side of a famous courtesy: with the Commonwealth relay across the border, the two stations exchange weather reports daily, the only bilateral forecast in the demilitarized zone, which both navies cite, off the record, as the best in the quarter.
## San Gabriel

- **Settled:** 2381 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1973094614149871872 (HIP 107376), M, 74.75 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a bright ember primary over locked furnace rocks, a bare temperate stone, mooned icy worlds, and a six-mooned far giant — the Commonwealth's northern door, five gates wide.
- **Links:** Concord gate to GJ 1268 (USE); Regional gate to GJ 834 A; Mesh gates to GJ 4260, Gaia EDR3 1952552782000889216, and Gaia EDR3 1974270159574386176.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *San Gabriel* — the messenger, the herald, the archangel of the announcement — for the border see where the Commonwealth's north meets the Union's traffic. The Chapter Register: *at the door, put the herald.*

San Gabriel is the Commonwealth's northern door. The border see's guest-houses, bonded halls, and the great chapel-of-the-gate where arriving crews of any polity are blessed (or, by request, simply welcomed) hold the northern Commonwealth's mixed traffic, and the chapter's hospitalidad extends the porch rule this far: no one crosses the door unfed. The population is chapter families, gate-keepers, and the brokers of the northern trade, and the customs are the door's: the Tolling of the Gate, each bell at each arrival and departure, rung by the youngest porter, whose ledger of bells is the border's most honest census. The grievance is the Concord's tariff schedule, which the chapter's annual letter grades, with ecclesiastical patience, as *a document in need of grace.*
## San Tomás

- **Settled:** 2383 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1956721064938340224 (GJ 4260), M, 75.27 ly
- **Population:** 780,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a bare temperate pebble, a hot shallow sea, and a chain of mooned giants — the northern Commonwealth's crossroads and its busiest argument.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 111685, HIP 109527, and Gaia EDR3 1952552782000889216; Mesh gates to San Gabriel's star and Gaia EDR3 1974270159574386176.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *San Tomás* — the apostle who asked to see — for the Commonwealth's northern survey and verification office: the chapters' own corps of measurers, checkers, and professional doubters. The Chapter Register: *blessed are those who check; they shall be cited.*

San Tomás keeps the Commonwealth honest. The verification office — the corps whose surveyors re-measure every boundary, toll, and harvest claim the northern chapters file — sits at this five-gate crossroads, and the settlement's population of surveyors, notaries, and the archive families who keep the checking itself checked works under the office's one rule, cut above the hall door: *trust, then verify, then trust again, in that order.* The custom is the Doubting: each new surveyor's first commission is to verify, personally, one finding of their own teacher — an institutional humility that has corrected two borders, one census, and, once, the Register itself, which the Register thanks them for, annually, in print. The grievance is the findings themselves — the toll schedules the office has now thrice declined to certify, an impasse the Chapter Register's annual letter describes as *ongoing, and good for everyone's soul, though not, currently, for the schedule.*
## Belén

- **Settled:** 2385 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1908305169922494208 (HIP 111685), M, 69.60 ly
- **Population:** 2.9 million
- **System:** a bright ember primary over a mooned hot rock, then the settled world — a temperate airy planet at 274 K with two moons, the northern Commonwealth's farm belt — and a heavy hot ocean world behind it.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 112800 and San Tomás's star; Regional gates to GJ 834 A, HIP 109527, and HD 218868B.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Belén* — Bethlehem, the house of bread — for the northern Commonwealth's grain world. The Chapter Register: *the name is a menu; the world delivered.*

Belén is the north's house of bread. The temperate world's broad grain country — the chapter farms, the mills that Arva's standard rates equal to its own, and the famous harvest bells that ring the whole northern Commonwealth's calendar — feeds five gates' worth of tables, and the settlement's farm families, millers, and the bakers' confraternity whose ovens the Register protects by statute live by the harvest's rhythm and the porch rule's extension: no crew leaves Belén unfed, and no granary opens until the poor tithe is measured out first. The custom is the First Bread: each harvest's first loaf is baked by the youngest baker, blessed at the chapel door, and carried, whole, to the system's oldest resident — the two poles of the settlement meeting at the loaf, which the Register calls *the whole polity in one sentence.* The grievance is the tariff, as ever, and the bakers' confraternity's annual letter on the grain levy is the Commonwealth's most-quoted homily after the water office's.
## Verity

- **Settled:** 2389 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1891459758430361088 (HIP 112800), M, 63.00 ly
- **Population:** 870,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a bare temperate pebble, and a hot shallow ocean world (66% sea) — the northern Commonwealth's crossroads of four gates.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 117059, GJ 4292, and HIP 115331; Mesh gate to Belén's star.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Verity* — on the petition of the northern arbitrators' circle, who wanted their junction named for what the work requires. The dedication minute: *the virtue is the trade's license; we shall keep both current.*

Verity is the north's clearing-house of small truths. The arbitrators' circle — the circuit-adjacent bench where the northern Commonwealth's boundary, water, and trade cases are heard — holds the junction, and the settlement's population of arbiters, clerks, and the witnesses' hostels lives at the pace of the docket and the standard of the name: every award published, every record open, every losing party heard twice. The best thing here is the archive: two centuries of published awards, indexed and open, which the League's law schools cite as the northern standard and the Commonwealth's own Assembly quotes back at itself, on the record, when it forgets. The custom is the Reading of the Ledger: at each year's turn, the whole year's awards are read aloud, title by title, in the square — a full day's reading, attended in shifts — because, the circle's rule holds, *justice that cannot be read aloud is not justice.* The grievance is caseload, the eternal one, answered this year with the circle's newest and most quoted line: *the docket is full because the Commonwealth is alive; long may we complain.*
## Reflection

- **Settled:** 2392 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2871730307948650368 (HIP 117059), white dwarf (T=10,700 K), 60.67 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** a dead sun's hard light over locked rocks, then the twin settled worlds: a locked twilight ocean planet (294 K, 34% sea) and, further out, a cool ocean world (289 K, 56% sea, one moon) — two seas under one steady star.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 3206 and Verity's star; Mesh gates to HIP 115331 and HIP 116416.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Reflection* — on the petition of the twin worlds' founding cooperatives, who observed that each world hangs in the other's sky at dusk, over the water, like its own reflection. The dedication minute: *the Assembly has inspected the view; the name is not a metaphor; the view is.*

Reflection is the Commonwealth's twin-sea world. The two locked ocean worlds' dusk-shore settlements — the inner sea's lamp-line harbors, the outer moon-tide towns — share one star, one name, and one charter, and the culture of the pair is the mirror itself: each world's market keeps the other's prices, each world's fleet answers the other's storms, and the twin assemblies sit, by treaty, facing each other across the system's quiet on a shared relay channel, a formality the Register's margin calls *the Commonwealth's most photogenic government.* The custom is the Counting of Lights: at each sol's dusk, each world counts the other's harbor lamps through the survey scopes — a tradition that began as navigation and became census, festival, and, once, the timely discovery of a fire. The grievance is symmetry itself, which the inner world's assembly, with feeling, describes as *the outer world getting our weather two days later and our complaints forever.*
## Confluence

- **Settled:** 2395 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2871343486014101888 (HIP 116416), K, 78.82 ly
- **Population:** 1.4 million
- **System:** a K sun over locked furnace rocks, an airy hot stone, a hot ocean world, then the settled world — a cool airy mooned planet at 264 K — and a giant-heavy tail with captured companions: the north's six-gate meeting.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1928108370769224576, Gaia EDR3 2870356816063753728, HIP 117946, and HIP 117492; Mesh gates to Reflection's star and HIP 114886.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Confluence* — the meeting of waters — for the junction where the northern Commonwealth's whole trade comes together. The dedication minute: *six gates, one name; the Assembly notes the rivers have been doing this for longer and will accept the instruction.*

Confluence is the north's gathering. The six-gate junction's great exchange halls — the northern Commonwealth's freight-matching, its annual assembly circuit's rotating sessions, and the famous hall of delegations where every polity's northern office keeps a desk and, more to the point, a lunch table — hold the quarter's most diplomatic traffic, and the settlement's population of brokers, hall-stewards, and the co-op families who feed everyone has built its whole culture at the meeting of streams: the custom is the Sharing of the First Pot, each cycle's opening feast cooked jointly by the crews of all six gates' first arrivals, a dish that has never once been planned and never once been bad, which the Register calls *the strongest evidence in the Commonwealth* and the arbitrage circle, gently, *the most persuasive.*
## Continuance

- **Settled:** 2397 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2870356816063753728, M, 81.17 ly
- **Population:** 600,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks, a locked twilight sea (76% ocean), mooned airy stone, and a two-mooned giant — a junction of five of the north's quieter roads.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 114886; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1888914766968562048, Confluence's star, HIP 117946, and HIP 117492.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Continuance* — on the petition of the road crews' cooperative, whose members keep the northern routes maintained and whose petition argued that *someone should be named for the part that goes on.* The minute: *the Assembly concurs, and adds its thanks.*

Continuance keeps the north's roads. The road crews' co-op — the maintenance halls, the beacon-works, the crews who go out in every weather the quarter's north can devise — base the maintenance circuit at this junction, and the settlement's population of road families, dispatchers, and the depot-keepers who feed them lives by the co-op's one great tradition: the Naming of the Roads, each maintained route's informal crew-name entered, at year's end, in the road-book alongside the official designation — a two-name register the crews maintain themselves, which the Assembly's official records cite, in the preface, as *more accurate.*
## Communion

- **Settled:** 2399 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1888914766968562048, M, 78.11 ly
- **Population:** 700,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace giant, heavy locked rocks, a warm shallow ocean world (79% sea, one moon), and a long chain of giants with whole villages of moons — the north's quiet crossroads.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to HIP 109527, Continuance's star, and HIP 114886; Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 1928108370769224576.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Communion* — the shared table — for the junction whose founding cooperative built the northern Commonwealth's common-hall circuit, the traveling feast that visits every member settlement in rotation. The minute: *the Assembly confirms the virtue is a potluck.*

Communion carries the north's common table. The common-hall circuit — the great traveling supper whose route the whole northern Commonwealth's calendar keeps, and whose pot, famously, never carries the same dish twice — stages from this four-gate junction, and the settlement's population of hall-stewards, hauliers, and the cooks whose reputations are made on the circuit lives in the specific warmth of a polity that decided, at founding, that its principal institution would be dinner. The custom is the Circuit's Portion: each hall that hosts the common supper keeps one ladle of every prior stop's stew frozen and carried forward — the pot's own ancestry, tasted at each new stop and known by name — a chain, the Register notes, now two hundred suppers long and visibly thicker than it began.
## Penumbra

- **Settled:** 2401 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1909581805999126784 (HIP 114886), K, 81.23 ly
- **Population:** 800,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over heavy hot airy rocks, mooned bare stones, and airy cold outlands — the junction of the north's quietest five roads, and the shade the name came for.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1928108370769224576, Communion's star, HIP 113829, and Continuance's star; Regional approach to Confluence's routes.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Penumbra* — the half-shadow — on the petition of the optics cooperative, whose instrument works need a light that is neither one thing nor the other. The minute: *the Assembly certifies the shade as scheduled.*

Penumbra shades the north's instruments. The optics cooperative — the interferometry arrays that read the half-light between the stars, and the calibration works the Lyra Compact contracts northward — holds the junction, and the settlement's population of opticians, shade-keepers, and the archive families whose whole profession is *the almost* lives in the deliberate twilight the charter's shade ordinances maintain. The custom is the Dimming: at each instrument-run's start, the settlement's own lights are stepped, by hand, through the seven shades of the town's scale — a discipline the Register's margin describes as *the only liturgy where turning lights off is the procession.*
## Kindred

- **Settled:** 2404 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2832641058969396864 (HIP 113829), G, 79.24 ly
- **Population:** 900,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over bare hot rocks, airy mooned stones, and a four-mooned giant — the junction where the northern Commonwealth's four roads pass through one another's yards.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 110951 and HIP 112496; Mesh gates to Penumbra's star and HIP 113884.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Kindred* — on the petition of the junction's founding cooperative, an alliance of four settlements' grandchildren who settled the crossroads together and asked for the word that described them. The minute: *the Assembly notes the family resemblance in the charter signatures and ratifies the obvious.*

Kindred is the north's cousin town. The four-gate junction was settled by the third generation of four neighboring settlements — families whose charter-signing page the Register reproduces in facsimile because it looks, the margin notes, *like a birthday card* — and the settlement's population of those families, their spouses, and the new arrivals they keep adopting runs the northern Commonwealth's friendliest freight exchange: cargo swapped between kin-houses at rates the tariff office audits annually and, annually, declines to classify as commerce, because mostly it isn't. The custom is the Sunday, untranslatable and unmissed: the one day each week the whole settlement eats at whichever house is oldest on the street, the guest list decided by weather and birth order.
## Boniface

- **Settled:** 2407 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2738145555048846080 (HIP 110951), M, 72.63 ly
- **Population:** 850,000
- **System:** a bright ember primary over locked rocks, an airy mooned stone, and a chain of giant, mooned worlds — the north's guest-hall junction.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to GJ 4292 and Kindred's star; Regional gates to GJ 857.1 A and Gaia EDR3 1768695093298872832.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Boniface* — the good fate — on the petition of the hospitality cooperative whose guest-halls serve every northern route. The minute: *the Assembly wishes the settlement the name's own luck, and notes the petition was submitted with pastries.*

Boniface hosts the north. The hospitality co-op's great guest-halls — the northern Commonwealth's asylum tradition made architectural, the kitchens that have never once closed, and the famous welcome-book whose signatures include three future mayors, one apostate, and a USE customs auditor who stayed a week — hold the four-gate junction, and the settlement's population of hall families, cooks, and the door-keepers whose trade's whole skill is noticing stands in the specific warmth of a place whose industry is arrival. The custom is the Book's Page: at each year's end, the welcome-book's newest page is read aloud at the halls' assembly, every signature, every arrival reason, in the writers' own spellings — the settlement's annual reminder that the book is the town.
## Halcyon

- **Settled:** 2410 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1875076004382831616 (GJ 857.1 A), K, 68.06 ly
- **Population:** 470,000
- **System:** a dim K sun over locked heavy rocks, hot airy stones, a bare mooned pebble, and a long chain of giants — the north's calmest crossroads, by measurement.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to GJ 4225, GJ 4292, and Gaia EDR3 1768695093298872832; Regional gate to Boniface's star.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Halcyon* — the calm days — on the petition of the weather office, whose instruments find this system's orbital weather the quietest in the northern Commonwealth. The minute: *the Assembly ratifies the name on the evidence, and requests the calm be contagious.*

Halcyon is the north's quiet. The weather office's great reference station — the calm-anchored instruments against which every northern storm is measured — holds the four-gate junction, and the settlement's population of meteorologists, instrument-keepers, and the depot families who supply four roads' worth of traffic lives in the deliberate calm the office both studies and defends: the quiet ordinances, the slow-traffic rules, and the charter's celebrated line, *the weather here is perfect, which is a public resource.* The custom is the Still Count: at each sol's noon, the station logs the day's wind — and on the deep-calm days the register marks with a small drawn bird, the office's own symbol, which the crews have adopted as the junction's flag.
## Whetstone

- **Settled:** 2412 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Helion Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1768695093298872832, M, 75.44 ly
- **Population:** 400,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks, a bare locked stone, and a chain of giant, mooned ice — the northern Commonwealth's sharpening ground.
- **Links:** Mesh gates to GJ 4218, Halcyon's star, and HIP 106231; Regional gate to Boniface's star.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Whetstone* — on the petition of the toolmakers' cooperative, whose works here supply the northern crews with edges, and whose argument to the Assembly was one line: *the roads are dull enough.*

Whetstone keeps the north sharp. The toolmakers' works — the grinding halls, the temper-ovens, the edge-testing station whose published standards every northern crew's kit is certified against — hold the four-gate junction, and the settlement's population of grinders, smiths, and the shop-families whose knives are half the north's kitchens keeps the trade's oldest rule above every bench: *an edge is a promise; keep it.* The custom is the Edge of the Year: at midwinter, the settlement's whole stock is displayed, and each crew takes home one tool sharpened by the hand of the master they trade with least — the cooperative's annual insistence that everyone's edge is everyone's business, a sentence the Register has called *the Commonwealth's whole foreign policy, in workshop form.*
## Luminaria

- **Settled:** 2387 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1792668153341911680 (GJ 4218), M, 69.93 ly
- **Population:** 960,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace giant, hot bare stones, then the settled world — a cold airy dusk planet at 260 K — and mooned ice beyond: the northern Commonwealth's lantern.
- **Links:** Regional gates to GJ 4225 and 78 Cyg A; Mesh gates to Whetstone's star and HIP 106231.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Luminaria* — the festival of lanterns — for the dusk world whose settlement charter required one lit lamp in every window before the first wall was raised. The Chapter Register: *the brothers built in light; the rest followed.*

Luminaria is the north's festival world. The cold dusk belt's lamp-lit towns — Cirio's candle trade's northern home, the lantern-processions that end each work-sol, and the famous paper-hall where the settlement's children learn the folding — hold the Commonwealth's northern joy, and the chapter's custom is the Procession of Returns: at each year's end, every traveler who has left and come back walks the lamp-line carrying the light they carried out, re-lit — a procession whose length the Register records as *the settlement's true census, and its best one.*
## Fragua

- **Settled:** 2389 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1801210568415251072 (78 Cygni A), G (1.5 solar masses, 5 solar luminosities), 72.25 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** a fierce G sun over a mooned airy hot rock, a temperate four-mooned giant, and airy cold stones — a system of fire by daylight and works by choice.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Luminaria's star and HIP 106231; Mesh gates to HIP 109527 and GJ 4232.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Fragua* — the forge — for the northern works whose forges run on this sun's free heat. The Chapter Register: *the fire was provided; the brothers did the rest.*

Fragua is the north's forge. The works companies' great annealing fields — run, by charter, on the G sun's direct glare, the Commonwealth's cheapest industrial heat — supply the northern chapters' tools, bells, and the foundry-order steel, and the settlement's population of forge brothers, field crews, and the shade-engineers whose trade is keeping people alive next to the work lives by the works' clock and the sun's own. The custom is the Cooling: at each field's day-end, the crews walk the cooling lines barefoot on the cold side of the shade-wall — a physiotherapy the infirmary prescribed and the brothers kept, because, the Register's line, *the feet that work the fire should also feel the cold, so the judgment stays honest.*
## Fuensanta

- **Settled:** 2391 (chapter charter, slip-delivered wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1799991798431932800 (GJ 4232), M, 80.91 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace super-earth, then the settled world — a cool airy dusk planet at 283 K, its spring belt the whole country — and mooned icy stone behind, with five roads out.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 106231 and 15 Peg; Mesh gates to Fragua's star, HIP 107070, and Gaia EDR3 1892963615458402304.
- **Registry name:** dedicated by the founding chapter to *Fuensanta* — the holy spring — for the dusk world's artesian belt, the settlement's whole reason and its patron's office. The Chapter Register: *the water found the brothers; the brothers stayed; both were correct.*

Fuensanta is the north's water. The dusk belt's spring country — the artesian wells the chapter capped and consecrated at founding, the bath-houses, the famous pilgrimage of the well-walkers who carry one dipper of every spring to every other spring each year — holds the northern Commonwealth's gentlest world, and the settlement's population of well-keepers, bath families, and the tea-house keepers whose trade's whole calendar is the walkers' arrival lives by the water's schedule, which the Register notes is *the only schedule in the Commonwealth that has never once been argued with, or needed to be.*

Pastora is the north's range. The cold world's high pastures — the flocks, the shearing festivals, the cheese-vaults in the giant's cold moons that the chapter's blue-ribbon wheels age in — hold the northern Commonwealth's protein and its wool, and the settlement's population of shepherd families, vault-keepers, and the six roads' worth of freight crews who carry the range's wealth out lives by the flock-year's rhythm, the Register's oldest northern calendar. The custom is the First Shearing: each spring's opening day is worked, by tradition, by the whole settlement — every household at the pens before dawn — because, the chapter's line, *the wool belongs to everyone who ever wore it.*

Pomar is the north's orchard. The cold world's river valleys — the apple rows, the pressing halls, the famous northern cider the chapter blesses at each pressing — hold the Commonwealth's fruit and its autumn, and the settlement's population of orchard families, pressers, and the four roads' freight crews who carry the cider out lives by the blossom calendar, the Register's northern spring. The custom is the Blessing of the Blossoms: at each year's first full bloom, the whole settlement walks the rows at dusk, one row per household, and the chapter blesses the orchard house by house — the Register's note: *the blessing is agricultural; the walk is not.*

San Pedro is the north's lake country. The cool world's great cold lakes — the net-fleets, the ice-fisheries, the famous smokeries whose output the northern Commonwealth's tables and half the League's besides — hold the chapter's northern fishery, and the settlement's population of fisher families, smokers, and the ferry crews of four roads lives by the lake's calendar and the Register's shortest northern statute: *the catch is shared; argue elsewhere.* The custom is the First Cast: each season's opening net is thrown by the fleet's oldest and newest together, one hand on the net and one on the line, because — the chapter's line — *the water should know both ends of the tradition at once.*

El Retablo makes the north's beauty. The workshop halls — the painting courts, the gilding lofts, the famous carving-line whose altarpieces the whole League's churches commission by waiting list — hold the northern Commonwealth's sacred art, and the settlement's population of painters, carvers, gilders, and the apprentices whose first work is always, by tradition, a small door (because — the masters' line — *every art begins by learning what opens*) lives by the commission calendar and the workshop's rule: *no piece ships until the youngest apprentice has spent one hour looking at it, silent.*

San Buenaventura outfits the north's far works. The supply houses — the mission kit-halls, the freight rings, the celebrated packing-yard whose crates the Register protects by trademark — hold the Commonwealth's northern frontier, and the settlement's population of packers, freighters, and the mission families who pass through toward the far chapels lives by the far roads' schedule and the yard's one rule, cut above the gates: *nothing goes out that would not serve a stranger at midnight.* The custom is the Crate: each year's first mission crate is packed by the whole settlement, item by item, in public — a liturgy of inventory the Register calls *the Commonwealth's whole theology, in wood.*
## Wayfare

Wayfare keeps the far roads moving. The hauliers' transit halls — the long-haul rest stations, the cargo transfer yards, and the famous dispatch board whose honest timetables the whole quarter's northern trade quotes — hold the Commonwealth's far-flung crossroads, and the settlement's population of haulier families, dispatchers, and the canteen crews whose soup the Register protects by custom lives at the tempo of four roads' traffic, always arriving, always leaving. The custom is the Road Roll: at each sol's end the dispatch board's departures are read aloud, and the crews still ashore answer each with one word — *clear* — a roll of the living road.
