Candor prices the far north. The new exchange — six gates' worth of the northern Commonwealth's harvest, freight, and futures, cleared in an open hall whose books the Assembly audits by surprise — holds the junction, and the settlement's population of brokers, clerks, and the co-op families whose lunch counters are the exchange's true floor lives by the hall's one rule, painted over the board: *the price is the truth; post it or leave.* The custom is the Open Bell: the exchange's day opens with one public lot, sold at auction by the hall's youngest clerk, whose proceeds endow the settlement's schools — the market's daily reminder, in the Register's words, *that the first price here was paid for children.*

Steadfast holds the far lights. Reprieve rests the far roads. The rest cooperative's stations — the sleep-halls, the meal courts, and the famous long-bath whose queue the crews call *the honest tariff* — hold five gates' worth of the north's tired trade, and the settlement's population of hall families, bath-keepers, and the dispatch crews who guard sleep like a cargo lives by the cooperative's one rule, carved at every hall door: *arrive broken; leave mended; the road did it; we undo it.*

Quietude listens. The acoustic observatory — the great silent instruments that read the deep worlds' slow sounds, and the calibration vaults the Lyra Compact's northern expeditions share — holds the dusk world's sheltered valleys, and the settlement's population of listeners, vault-keepers, and the farmers whose machinery is, by ordinance, the quietest in the quarter lives at the observatory's tempo: slow bells, soft speech, and the famous Hour of Nothing, the sol's one silent stretch when even the observatory rests. The custom is the Hearing: each new listener's first instrument is their own ear, one full sol of the world's sound, logged by hand — the Register's margin: *the old way; still the best; still the only way to start.*

The Standing Register's line: *named for a library that outlived its city; the settlement means to do the same, in order.*

Ebla keeps the north's memory. The warm sea world's great archive — the Commonwealth's northern co-op network's records, the Assembly's circuit minutes, and the famous clay annex, where the settlement's most important contracts are still pressed into fired tablets in the old city's own tradition — holds half the north's institutional history, and the settlement's population of archivists, potters, and the fisher families who feed them lives by the archive's one law, cut in the reading-room door: *what is written here is written twice, on what burns and on what doesn't.* The custom is the Firing: each year's most important records are pressed, fired, and shelved in one public ceremony — the Register's margin: *the year, in clay, kept where the sea and the fire can argue about it.*

Concordance harmonizes the north. The musical societies' halls — the great choir-schools, the song-archive, and the famous assembly-sings where the whole settlement learns each year's new verses for the Commonwealth's shared occasions — hold the temperate world's river towns, and the settlement's population of singers, teachers, and the farmers whose fields the choirs have, by tradition, sung in and out of season lives by the hall's rule, cut above every door: *learn your part; listen for the others.* The custom is the Great Sing: at the north's midwinter, the settlement's whole population performs the Commonwealth's oldest round, in the round, each voice a part, the Register's margin noting, as it has for a century, *the north, in tune.*

Tranquility is the north's calm sea. The cool ocean world's shore farms — the oyster beds the Freeports' combine now contracts by the season, the float-gardens, the retreat halls where the northern crews come to remember silence — hold the twin suns' quiet world, and the settlement's sea-farmers, gardeners, and hall-keepers live by the shore's one rule, cut in the float-gardens' gate: *the sea is calm; keep it that way; start with yourself.* The custom is the Float: each settlement's guest-day, the whole shore lights one lamp per household on the calm water — the Register's margin: *the calm, counted.*

- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 5143825976706932480 and Gaia EDR3 5149417440010659072; Mesh gates to Threshold's star, HIP 6097, 37 Cet, and Gaia EDR3 5139880551029408768.
- **Registry name:* dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Ledger's Rest* — on the petition of the accountants' cooperative, whose year-end halls here host the northern Commonwealth's annual closing of books. The minute: *the Assembly ratifies the name, and balances.*

Ledger's Rest balances the north. The accountants' halls — the closing-courts, the audit annex, and the famous year-end supper where every settlement's books are declared and every cook is an auditor — hold the six-gate junction, and the settlement's accountants, clerks, and the supper-families whose year-end tables the Register calls *the Commonwealth's truest arithmetic* live by the halls' rule, cut in the closing-court's floor: *add it twice; then eat.*
## Lampblack

- **Settled:** 2425 (Assembly charter, slip-delivered wave).

- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Lampblack* — on the petition of the printers' cooperative, whose ink-works here supply the north's presses with the old soot-and-oil black. The minute: *the Assembly ratifies the name, in writing, in it.*

Lampblack prints the north. The ink-works — the soot-halls, the binderies, and the famous quick-press whose northern almanac supplements the whole quarter's crews carry — hold the dusk world's small towns, and the settlement's printers, binders, and the paper-merchants who trade in Washi's sheets lives by the works' rule, cut over the press: *the word leaves here; make it true.*
## Grayfall

- **Settled:** 2426 (Assembly charter, the newest wave).

- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5131300889999108608, M, 62.89 ly

- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Grayfall* — the founding cooperative's own word for the ember light's long dusk, when the settlement's first lamps were lit. The minute: *ratified in the year 2426 itself; the Register notes the ink is still drying.*

Grayfall is the newest north. The settlement — one town, four roads, a charter assembly younger than most of its members' children — holds the Commonwealth's latest dedication, and its whole population of founders, newborns, and the arriving young lives in the specific light the name records: gray, long, and kind. The custom is already one: the Lighting-Up, each dusk's first lamp lit by the youngest resident present, whatever age that is — the Register's newest note: *the virtue begins here; so does everything else.*
## Understory

- **Registry name:** dedicated by the Assembly of Settlements to *Understory* — on the petition of the surveyors' newest cooperative, whose far-north mapping office reads the quarter's second layer. The minute: *the Assembly ratifies, and reads underneath.*

Understory reads beneath. The survey office's new far-north annex — the second-pass mapping, the sub-surface sounding halls, and the apprentice school whose whole curriculum is *what the first survey missed* — holds the six-gate junction, and the settlement's surveyors, sounders, and the archive families live by the office's one rule, cut in the sounding-hall's floor: *the first map is a draft; the world is the correction.*

Mullein keeps the newest roads. The road-crew cooperative — the beacon-lit verges, the lay-bys, and the famous verges themselves, seeded with the settlement's namesake until every new road in the far north blooms yellow at dusk — holds the four-gate junction, and the young population of crews, seeders, and the lamp-keepers whose trade the flower shares its name with lives by the verges' rule, cut at the first lay-by: *light the road; plant it too.*
## Payne

- **Settled:** 2326 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-5080543413173527424 (HD 23588B), B (3.1 solar masses, 80 solar luminosities), 67.48 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** a fierce blue-white star — one of the quarter's brightest suns — over distant mooned rocks, a 34-Earth-mass temperate giant at 403 K, and a far 766-Earth-mass colossus with captured companions: a system of light and distance.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4871414343064541824 and Gaia EDR3 4855697167942783872; Mesh gates to HD 21175B and Mullein's star.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2328, for **Cecilia Payne-Gaposchkin**, who worked out what the stars are made of and was told, by the authorities of her day, that she must be wrong; the dedication, for the brightest star in the Compact's holdings: *she said the stars were hydrogen; the sky agreed; we are still catching up.*

Payne studies the bright stars. The Compact's stellar-composition campus — the spectroscopy halls that read what every sun in the quarter is made of, working from the model the honoree built and the century after her proved — holds the fierce B star's shadowed orbits, and the settlement's population of spectroscopists, students, and the instrument-keepers who maintain the shields that make a bright star livable at all works in the blue-white light the Register's margin calls *her proof, still shining.* The custom is the Reading: each new student's first spectrum is read aloud, element by element, at the hall — hydrogen first, always, and the whole hall answers *yes* — the Compact's whole history in one call and response. The grievance is the shielding budget, the physics of bright light being, as the annual report states, *expensive, and worth it, like her.*
## Bartók

- **Settled:** 2331 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).

- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4852870701504645504 (HD 21175B), M, 57.75 ly
- **Population:** 400,000
- **System:** a bright ember primary over locked heavy rocks and airy mooned stones, with paired far giants — the Compact's listening room.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HIP 22451; Mesh gates to χ Eri, γ Dor, and Payne's star.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2333, for **Béla Bartók**, who went into the villages with a phonograph and brought back the music nobody notated; the dedication, for the Compact's ethnomusicology archive: *the songs were there; somebody had to carry them.*

Bartók carries the songs. The Compact's ethnomusicology campus — the great sound archive that records and keeps the quarter's folk music, every polity's, on principle — holds the ember world's quiet halls, and the settlement's population of archivists, field recordists, and the families whose own songs are collection number one lives by the archive's rule, cut in the listening-room door: *the song belongs to the people; the copy belongs to everyone.* The custom is the Round: each sol's end, one recording from the collection — chosen blind — plays in the square, and whoever recognizes it calls out its home.
## Maury

Maury classes the variables. The Compact's variable-star campus — the timing vaults that chart every pulsing star in the quarter, from this system's own prototype sun outward — holds the cool ocean world's shore institutes, and the settlement's population of observers, timers, and the archive families whose vaults of stellar heartbeats are the Register's steadiest long data live by the campus's rule, cut in the timing-hall: *the star repeats; so do we; count.* The custom is the Beat: each new variable confirmed from Maury is announced by the hall's clock-bell, struck once for each class — a chime the settlement's children learn to read the sky by.

Janaki grows the science of growing. The Compact's field-botany station — the cold dusk world's trial gardens, the seed-exchange halls that supply Herbarius and the Commonwealth's vaults alike, and the famous chromosome labs that read what a plant is at the level of its own instructions — holds the junction, and the settlement's botanists, gardeners, and the seed-clerks whose packages cross six roads lives by the station's rule, cut in the garden gate: *plant it; note it; share both.* The custom is the Exchange: each year's new seed-lines are released, free, at a public sowing — the Register's margin: *the future, handed out in envelopes.*
## Hypatia

Hypatia teaches the teachers. The Compact's teaching academy — the school whose graduates staff every campus in the quarter, and whose founding curriculum is the honoree's own: mathematics, sky, and the duty to explain both to anyone who asks — holds the hot-dusk world's shade-halls, and the settlement's master teachers, students, and the archive families who keep the school's library live by the academy's rule, cut in the lecture hall's stone: *no question turned away; no teacher above the question.* The custom is the Assembly of Questions: each term closes with a public hour when the students ask anything of the masters, and the answer, whatever it is, is entered into the school's open record — the Register's margin: *the hour the Register most attends.*
## Al-Kashi

Al-Kashi computes. The Compact's great computation halls — the open-model works, the archive of every algorithm the quarter runs, and the famous hand-verification court — hold the junction, and the settlement's computers, verifiers, and the school of mental arithmetic that keeps, as sport and standard, the old master's own methods lives by the halls' rule, cut in the verification court's floor: *the machine is fast; the court is sure.* The custom is the Digit: each year the settlement extends, by hand, in public, one more place of the old master's number — a wall of digits the Register calls *the longest patience in the quarter, at one stroke a year.*

Inge listens to worlds. The seismology station — the ocean world's deep listening arrays, the quake-vaults that read every world's interior in the quarter from its own slow shivers — holds the cool sea's shore institutes, and the settlement's seismologists, array-keepers, and the fisher families whose boats double as instrument platforms lives by the station's rule, cut in the array-hall: *the world speaks once; the echo is also testimony.* The custom is the Marker: each confirmed interior map is marked, publicly, by a single bell-tone of the array's own — the Register's margin: *the world's interior, rung.*

Chandra's Rest continues the study. The outer campus — the white-dwarf and ember-star annex that extends the Chandra campus's work into the Compact's far quarter — holds the junction, and the settlement's astrophysicists, students, and the support families live by the annex's inherited rule, cut at the dormitory door: *be right early; be patient after; both are the science.* The custom is the Inheritance: each graduating class walks, at dusk, the campus's whole perimeter and names the roads as they pass — the dead stars, the limits, the honorees — the Register's margin: *the syllabus, walked.*
## Sofia

Sofia extends the work. The mathematics annex — the halls where the Compact's summer schools gather, and whose founding lecture is always on the walls themselves, on the principle that *the boundary condition comes first* — holds the cold world's sheltered valleys, and the settlement's mathematicians, students, and the valley families who host them live by the annex's rule, cut in the wall it was named from: *the problem is the door; walk through it anyway.*
## Arnaldo

- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2373, for **Arnaldo Gunzi** — the network engineer whose published protocols the whole mesh runs on, and who never once charged a license; the dedication, for the Compact's far relay station: *the packets pass; he charged nothing; neither do we.*

Arnaldo relays. The Compact's far relay — the message halls, the open protocols, and the famous zero-fee clause the honoree's estate renews annually by treaty — holds the junction, and the settlement's relay crews, archive keepers, and the small support population live by the relay's rule, cut in the message hall: *what passes through here belongs to its senders; the passage belongs to everyone.*

Srinivasa reads the letters. The open-submission hall — the drop-box, the open-mathematics court where any citizen of any polity may file a result for review, and the famous famous rapid-response desk that answers everything within a season — holds the far junction, and the settlement's referees, clerks, and the archive families live by the hall's rule, cut above the drop-box itself: *no letter unread; no letter unpaid; the mathematics belongs to whoever does it.*

- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2383, for **Zhang Heng**, who built the first instrument for feeling the ground's distant trouble and the armillary that read the sky; the dedication, for the far seismology station: *the bronze jar dropped its ball a thousand miles from the quake; the principle still works.*

Zhang Heng feels the far ground. The seismology station's outer works — the great ground-arrays, the warning relays that carry the quarter's deep news, and the calibration halls — hold the junction, and the settlement's observers, relay crews, and the archive families live by the station's oldest instruction, cast in the array's first stone: *the world will tell you; build something that listens.*

Wang Zhenyi explains. The outreach campus — the Compact's far teaching annex, whose whole mission is that the quarter's farthest children meet the sky as a subject and not a rumor — holds the six-gate junction, and the settlement's teachers, translators, and the school-fleet crews who carry the campus to every far port live by the annex's rule, cut in the teaching-hall's two doors: *one for listening; one for asking; both are always open.*

Saha heats the physics. The high-temperature works — the plasma halls that study what the giant's 424 K and the fierce sun's pressure do to matter, and the calibration lab that extends the honoree's equation to the whole quarter's hot stars — hold the five-gate junction, and the settlement's physicists, engineers, and the shield crews who make a bright system livable live by the works' rule, cut in the hall's wall: *heat is information; take the reading before it takes the instrument.*

Al-Jazari builds the gentle machines. The robotics works — the automata halls, the machine-ethics annex, and the famous test-kitchen where every new machine is graded on table manners — hold the junction, and the settlement's engineers, apprentices, and the calibration crews live by the works' rule, cut in the first automaton's brass: *the machine serves; who does it serve; answer both.* The custom is the Showing: each new machine is presented, in public, performing its duty and then stopping, on command, immediately — the Register's margin: *the politeness test, passed.*

Bharati writes the quarter's science out loud. The public-writing annex — the translation halls that render the Compact's findings into every polity's plain speech, and the famous press whose briefings the quarter's news services carry free by treaty — holds the cool ocean world's shore towns, and the settlement's translators, editors, and the fishing families whose voices are the annex's test audience live by the annex's rule, cut in the press-hall: *if the person who caught your lunch cannot read it, it is not published.*
## Takamine

Takamine works the quarter's chemistry of living. The biochemistry annex — the compound-isolation halls, and the famous cherry gardens the honoree's own gift-tree descendants now shade — hold the junction, and the settlement's chemists, gardeners, and the archive families live by the annex's rule, cut under the garden's oldest tree: *find the active thing; then plant something kind.*
## Somerville

Somerville crosses the lines. The interdisciplinary annex — the halls where the Compact's fields are required to talk to each other, structurally, by seating arrangement — holds the six-gate junction, and the settlement's mixed researchers, students, and the support families who feed them all live by the annex's rule, cut in the great hall's round table: *the person next to you is from another discipline; that is the method.*
## Ibn Sina

- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2418, for **Ibn Sina**, the physician of the Canon whose clinical method — observation, then reasoning, then the record — the Compact's medical archives still run on; the dedication, for the medical-history annex: *he wrote the case down; the case outlived the empire; write the case down.*

Ibn Sina keeps the cases. The medical-history annex — the great archive of the quarter's clinical records, open by treaty to every polity's physicians, and the teaching hall where the honoree's own method is the first lesson — holds the junction, and the settlement's archivists, physicians, and the support families live by the annex's rule, cut in the archive's stone: *the patient is a person; the record is the medicine's memory.*
## Moseley

- **Settled:** 2421 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations).

- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4801001549701424768, white dwarf (T=10,125 K), 82.31 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** a dead sun's hard light over locked rocks and an airy mooned stone — the third world, **Moseley** proper, a cold airy temperate planet at 260 K — with mooned ice behind, five roads out.
- **Links:** Regional gate to HD 41742B; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 5573532025833121408, Ibn Sina's star, HIP 30104, and HIP 28790.
- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2423, for **Henry Moseley**, who ordered the elements by their own X-ray law at twenty-six and was killed at twenty-seven by a war that then learned, too late, what the world loses when it loses its young scientists; the dedication, for the periodic-tables annex: *he numbered the elements so they could not lie about themselves; keep the numbering honest.*

Moseley orders the elements. The spectroscopy annex — the X-reading halls that verify every element claim in the quarter, and the calibration vaults certified against the white dwarf's own light — hold the cold world's sheltered valleys, and the settlement's spectroscopists, vault-keepers, and the school whose students learn the honoree's law as the first honest numbering live by the annex's rule, cut in the vault door: *the element answers by number; so should everyone.* The custom is the Call: each new element confirmed from the annex is rung in on the hall's bell — the quarter's rarest bell, by a wide margin.
## Burbidge

- **Settled:** 2426 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations, the newest).

- **Registry name:** designated by the Committee on Designations, 2426 itself, for **Margaret Burbidge**, whose name was moved to second place on the paper that explained how the stars cook the elements — and who spent the career after making sure the door stayed open for the ones behind her; the dedication, for the stellar-nucleosynthesis works: *the giants make everything; the credit should go where it does.*

Burbidge studies how stars cook. The nucleosynthesis works — the great giant-watching arrays that read the warm giants' slow enrichment of themselves, and the teaching annex that explains, to every polity's students, where every atom in their bodies was made — hold the six-gate junction, and the settlement's observers, theorists, and the archive families live by the works' rule, cut in the array-hall: *the star made the iron; the iron made the ship; the ship carries the gratitude; pass it on.*
## Wazn

- **Settled:** 2426 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations, the newest).
- **Sovereignty:** Lyra Research Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-2887731882922767744 (Wazn, β Columbae), K giant (2.6 solar masses, 41 solar luminosities), 88.38 ly
- **Population:** 420,000
- **System:** a vast aging K giant — hot airy mooned rocks far out, and a warm 144-Earth-mass giant with a captured companion — the newest jewel of the Compact's holdings, five gates wide.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4827639486628478592 and Gaia EDR3 2890783233848206208; Mesh gates to Burbidge's star, Gaia EDR3 2885863400349980288, and Gaia EDR3 2882438455989090176.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the Compact's standing rule: *Wazn*, the weight, the old Arabic sky's scale-star of the Dove. The Committee's minute: *the star named weight is now leaving the equilibrium it was named for; the Compact notes the aptness, and the responsibility.*

Wazn is the Compact's newest patient. The aging giant — forty-one suns of light, a star visibly leaving its balance — is the newest and greatest natural laboratory the Compact has held: the works that study Wazn's slow swelling watch, in one star, the whole future of every giant in the quarter, Merak's and Tureis's and all the rest. The settlement of observers, modelers, and the newest families of the Compact's newest charter lives in the giant's amber light and by the works' rule, cut in the array-hall: *the star is changing; write it down; that is the whole job.* The custom is the Weighing: at each sol's noon the array posts the star's readings beside its ancient name's meaning — a running ledger the Register calls *the sky's scale, held honest, and the hand not shaking.*
## Banneker

- **Settled:** 2426 (charter to the Outer Research Foundations, the newest).

Banneker computes both tables. The almanac works — the Compact's public computation annex, whose open almanacs serve every polity's farmers and navigators, and whose founding collection includes the honoree's own — hold the cold world's sheltered halls, and the settlement's computers, printers, and the farm families who test every table live by the works' rule, cut in the press: *the eclipse does not care who computes it; the table is for whoever reads it.*

The calibration annex — the reference vaults, the instrument proving grounds, and the school that teaches measurement as a moral discipline — holds the junction, and the settlement's metrologists, apprentices, and the small support population live by the annex's rule, cut in the proving ground's first stone: *a number you will not stand behind in public is not a measurement; it is a hope.*

Caroline hunts the comets. The comet-watch works — the sweep arrays, the far-visitor catalogue, and the school of patience whose whole curriculum is looking where nothing was found yesterday — hold the junction, and the settlement's observers, students, and the sweep crews live by the works' rule, cut in the sweep-hall: *the sky owes you nothing; look anyway; that is how the comets were found the first time.* The custom is the Sweep: each new observer's first night is spent, by tradition, at the exact eyepiece station the honoree's method made standard, alone, and the first thing they may log, if nothing appears, is *clear* — the Register's margin: *which is also a result; she said so first.*

Wu designs the decisive experiments. The experiment-design annex — the halls where the Compact's hardest questions are turned into apparatus, and the famous review court that asks every design the honoree's own question, *what would change your mind* — holds the junction, and the settlement's designers, machinists, and the students live by the annex's rule, cut in the court's door: *state what would refute you before you run it; if nothing would, it is not an experiment.*

Sancar studies the repairs. Tsiolkovsky derives the flight. Agnesi teaches the whole life. Miriam drills the deep past. Bruno counts the other suns. The plurality annex — the halls that estimate, from the great surveys, how many of the quarter's uncounted stars hold worlds, and the archive of every confirmed plurality — hold the F sun's shielded orbits, and the settlement's statisticians, observers, and the museum-annex that keeps the honoree's sentence in every language the quarter speaks live by the annex's rule, cut in the hall: *say the number; show the error bars; never the certainty.*

Flossie reads the fast-changing. The molecular-genetics annex — the sequencing halls, the pathogen-watch that the quarter's health offices subscribe to by treaty, and the teaching annex — hold the cool world's valleys, and the settlement's geneticists, clinicians, and the support families live by the annex's rule, cut in the sequencing hall: *the thing will change; read it again; that is the work, and the mercy.*

Apgar greets the newborn. The neonatal-works annex — the maternity-training halls the Compact shares with every polity's medical offices, and the instrument works that make the honoree's score universal — hold the junction, and the settlement's clinicians, teachers, and the instrument-makers live by the annex's rule, cut in the maternity hall: *the first minute belongs to the child; everything else can wait.*

Fizeau measures light. The interferometry annex — the long-baseline halls that stretch the Compact's newest instruments across the junction's quiet, and the calibration school that teaches the honoree's own patience with a wheel and a hill — hold the junction, and the settlement's opticians, students, and the support families live by the annex's rule, cut in the calibration shed: *if the hill is far enough, you are the instrument.*

Struve is the Compact's triple. The three habitable worlds — the airy world's observatory towns, the ocean world's shore campuses, the far wanderer's hermit-stations — hold the Compact's newest and most astonishing holding, chartered as one settlement with three temperate belts and a shared administration that rides, like ι Per's, on the ferry. The population of researchers, boat crews, and the very few, very smug hermits lives by the charter's one-line constitution, cut in all three harbors: *one sky; three worlds; share them.* The custom is the Trine: at each sol's turn, the three worlds signal their news to each other in sequence — airy world first, ocean second, wanderer last, in order of sunrise — a relay the Register's margin calls *the Compact's newest, oldest habit.*

Ørsted measures the fields. - **Sovereignty:** Aurora Compact (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3278207077994156416, M, 108.87 ly
- **Population:** 250,000
- **System:** a bright ember primary over locked rocks, a small hot sea, and paired giants — the Compact's newest unhurried country, four gates wide.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 16442680997316096 and Gaia EDR3 36629890579758592; Mesh gates to Shirakaba's star and HIP 17936.
- **Registry name:** named by its founding covenant — the slow-works, under a word they built for themselves from the ancestors' *take it easy*: *Yukkurikan.* The register: *the covenant does everything at the speed of doing it once, properly.*

Yukkurikan does things once. The slow-works — the joinery halls where no deadline has ever been signed, and the famous guarantee that nothing leaves until it would satisfy its maker's grandchildren — hold the junction, and the settlement's joiners, coopers, and the patient families live by the covenant's rule, cut in the workshop door: *slowly, please; the wood is older than you; act like a guest.*

- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2426, to a novelty-hospital combine whose bid named the dark little system for a word out of old fiction nobody could quite place but everyone liked. The auction minute: *sold; the Registrar notes the name is spelled correctly, probably.*

Mirkwell sells the dark. The novelty combine's retreat — cave-halls in the mooned stone, the famous blackout resort where the whole point is what you can't see, and the darkness-certification office, the only one in the quarter — hold the junction, and the settlement's hoteliers, cavers, and the newest families live by the combine's one posted law, hung, of course, unlit: *the dark is the product; mind the steps.*
## Warmwave

- **Settled:** 2426 (Auction charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1192473897093821568, brown dwarf, 71.79 ly
- **Population:** 300,000
- **System:** a failed star over a ladder of locked rocks — the second of them, **Warmwave** proper, a locked twilight ocean planet at 303 K, three-quarters of its face under warm sea — with six roads out.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1193465794018333824, GJ 1199, and Unukalhai; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1193520666521113344, Mirkwell's star, and 49 Ser B.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2426, to the wave-garden combine: the locked world's famous swell — the tide that never breaks, running round the twilight shore forever — named the resort before the hammer fell. The auction minute: *sold; the surf was already booked.*

Warmwave sells the endless swell. The wave-gardens — the shore resorts where the quarter's surfers ride a tide that has been running since the world was, the wave-farms that tap the swell's steadiness, and the warm shore towns — hold the junction, and the settlement's wave-farmers, hoteliers, and the newest families live by the combine's posted law, painted on the first board ever ridden here: *the wave is endless; the price is by the hour; both true.*
## Kettleblack *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted — the auction never found a bidder.
- A dim ember, 65.52 ly — locked heavy super-earths and a two-mooned giant: the newest wave's first system offered at auction with no takers, kept instead as a public depot by the Freeports' own registry. The depot's crews keep the first unsold plaque, hung at the dock: *some things wait; that is also a price.*
## Unukalhai

- **Settled:** 2426 (Auction charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4429785739602747392 (Unukalhai, α Serpentis), K giant (2.6 solar masses, 45 solar luminosities), 74.24 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** a vast orange giant — the Serpent's neck of the old sky — over hot airy mooned rocks, a bare mooned temperate pebble, and a four-mooned giant: the newest wave's old name, kept.
- **Links:** Regional gate to 5 Ser B; Mesh gates to HIP 75201, Kettleblack's star, Warmwave's star, and GJ 1199.
- **Registry name:** kept, not auctioned — the ancient IAU-proper name, the serpent's neck, falls under the Freeports' heritage carve-out, and the auctioneer opened the sale by withdrawing the lot to applause. The registry note: *Unukalhai; grandfathered; the applause is entered into the record.*

Unukalhai is the orange giant's waystation. The settlement is small and mostly orbital — depot rings in the giant's amber light, assay halls, and the famous Serpent's Rest, the heritage lounge where the newest wave's crews stop to see a sun the color of the ancestors' autumn — and the population of depot families, assayers, and keepers lives by the registry's one rule for the kept things: *the name was here first; the star was here first; mind the order.*
## Shellbank

- **Settled:** 2426 (Auction charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4421455530273467904 (5 Serpentis B), M, 82.01 ly
- **Population:** 350,000
- **System:** a bright ember primary over a locked furnace mooned rock, a bare temperate pebble, and a warm airy ocean world (334 K, half sea, two moons) — the newest wave's shell country.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 76233, GJ 1188, and HIP 75686; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 1161026421427860992 and Unukalhai's star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2426, to the lime combine — the newest wave's ancient trade, lime from seashell — whose bid named the bank where the shells are gathered. The auction minute: *sold; the chemistry is older than money, but the money is welcome.*

Shellbank burns the shells. The lime works — the shell-banks of the warm ocean world, the kilns, and the famous mortar-supply contracts that the newest settlements' builders sign at the mortar-hall — hold the junction, and the settlement's kiln-crews, gatherers, and the newest families live by the combine's posted law, cut in the first kiln's brick: *the sea made the shell; the fire makes the wall; the house is the whole ledger.*
## Double Entry

- **Settled:** 2426 (Auction charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4400452830957830656 (HIP 76233), G, 83.07 ly
- **Population:** 680,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star over hot airy mooned rocks, then the settled pair: a cool ocean world (286 K, shallow seas over a seventh of its face) and a cold airy two-mooned rock at 256 K — with two colossal far giants carrying whole captured villages of moons.
- **Links:** Regional gate to Gaia EDR3 4401037668063146752; Mesh gates to Shellbank's star, Gaia EDR3 6319402059411076224, and Gaia EDR3 6322574734573102848.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2426, to the accountancy combine, whose bid letter argued the system's two habitable worlds were *the only honest bookkeeping metaphor in the sky: everything twice, once on each side.* The auction minute: *sold; the ledger balances; the auditors are delighted; nobody else understands it, which is also on brand.*

Double Entry keeps everything twice. The twin worlds — the ocean world's harbor towns and the airy rock's highland farms — are run by the combine as one settlement on two pages: every contract signed in the harbor is countersigned on the rock, every load weighed on one world is re-weighed on the other, and the famous Reconciliation, the year-end when the whole settlement walks through its books in public and forgives the remainder to charity. The population is bookkeepers, farmers, fishers, and the newest families; the custom is the Balanced Meal — every feast ends when both worlds' tables are equally empty, a discipline the combine calls *the only arithmetic anyone enforces at dinner.*
## Sixways *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted — the auction reserve wasn't met.
- An ember star, 73.89 ly — locked heavy super-earths and a mooned icy world: a six-gate junction offered at auction and bought by nobody, kept instead as the newest wave's public crossroads. The depot crews' name for it, *Sixways*, was entered by the registry clerk the next morning, who noted: *nobody owned it; everybody uses it; the name follows the facts.*
## Tollgate

- **Settled:** 2426 (Auction charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3642410791343099008 (HD 125455B), M, 66.78 ly
- **Population:** 850,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked rocks and a ladder of icy stone — a system whose whole value is its position: six gates wide, two of them Concord crossings to the Union trunk.
- **Links:** Concord gates to HIP 69485 and Gaia EDR3 3621610157353795584 (USE); Regional gates to HIP 75201, GJ 3900, and Gaia EDR3 3639512375613095424; Mesh gate to Sixways' star.
- **Registry name:** auctioned, 2426, to the crossing combine: *Tollgate*, the name of the whole business model, purchased at what the auction minute calls *a price that included a certain self-awareness.* Sold; the tolls are posted; the gates are counted twice daily.*

Tollgate collects the crossings. The newest wave's border hub — bonded halls built to two codes, the bilingual tariff boards, and the famous double-gate dance, where Union and Freeport customs officers process the same hull in parallel and compare notes at dinner — holds the junction, and the settlement's assessors, brokers, and the newest families live by the combine's one standing rule, posted at both Concord gates in both languages: *the border is a door; doors are for opening; the toll pays for the hinges.*
## Gateward *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 59.75 ly — locked furnace rocks, heavy mooned stones, airy ice. The Union's customs spur at the Tollgate crossing, newest of the Concord doors. The customs crews' name for their own post, entered by petition the first week: *the border's a door; doors are boring; someone has to hold it; coffee's on.*
## GJ 3809 A *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 59.45 ly — locked rocks, airy mooned stones, and a four-mooned giant. A two-gate relay spur of the newest Concord runs. The relay crew of sixty keeps the newest wave's first tradition: a doormat, real, woven, at the airlock, changed monthly, and every arriving customs officer wipes their feet, both blocs, by treaty.
## Milestone

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3732539683617410816 (59 Virginis), G (1.2 solar masses), 57.38 ly
- **Population:** 880,000
- **System:** a bright G sun over heavy hot airy rocks, a warm ocean world, then the settled world — a cold airy mooned planet at 249 K — with five roads out, every one of them new.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Tepiamenit, Gaia EDR3 3699797155055781760, 70 Vir, GJ 3809 A, and Gaia EDR3 3931880591977203584.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426, the newest entry in the Register: the junction where the Union's newest runs measure their distance from — the crews' own mark, the first stone. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board notes the Register's newest page is now the newest page for a reason.*

Milestone is where the newest runs begin. The cold world's junction towns — the survey monument, the dispatch halls, and the famous zero-stone, from which every new route's distance is measured — hold the Union's newest hub, and the settlement of dispatchers, farmers, and the newest families lives by the charter's one-line rule, cut in the zero-stone itself: *everything new is measured from somewhere; be the somewhere.*
## Tepiamenit

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1244571953471006720 (Tepiamenit), G (1.3 solar masses), 50.92 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** a bright G sun over heavy mooned hot rocks and a temperate four-mooned giant — and then the settlement, far out at 34 au in the long year: a warm ocean world (311 K, 59% sea, one moon) riding the deepest habitable orbit in the Union's register.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 1171, Genghe, and Milestone's star.
- **Registry name:** the ancient name, kept under the standing rule — one of Earth's inheritance, carried with the register across the gap. The Board's minute: *the name crossed with the ancestors' maps; the world beneath it is the Union's farthest garden; both are kept.*

Tepiamenit is the Union's farthest garden. The warm ocean world's shore towns — the deep-orbit observatories that came for the long year's stable dark, the far-farms, and the famous Slow Year, the settlement's calendar, which counts one season where the inner worlds count four — hold the Union's strangest and calmest charter, and the population of observers, farmers, and the newest families lives by the charter's own line, cut in the harbor stone: *the year is long here; so is the welcome; both arrive eventually.*
## GJ 1171 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A barely-star, 48.80 ly — locked rocks, a locked temperate giant, and a mooned giant behind. A two-gate relay of the newest runs toward the far garden. The relay crew of forty keeps the runs' oldest joke, painted on the tank: *further from Sol than Tepiamenit's far shore, and still nobody's grandmother lives here.*
## GJ 3779 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 44.85 ly — locked furnace rocks, a mooned cold stone, and a long chain of giants to a six-mooned far giant. A two-gate relay of the newest runs. The crew's single tradition is the Loneliest Dinner, held whenever the relay goes a full cycle with no arrivals at all: the crew of thirty-one eats together, at one table, with an empty chair set at the head — *for whoever's coming.*
## HIP 65012 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 44.12 ly — a locked steam ocean, a mooned airy super-earth, and a two-mooned giant. A two-gate relay of the newest runs. The station's one institution is the Register's newest window-box: a hydroponic sill garden whose cuttings are given, by standing custom, to every crew that passes — the newest runs' greenery, passed from hull to hull, and the Register's margin: *the garden travels; the crews carry it; nobody remembers who planted the first.*
## Gaia EDR3 1554433777791815808 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A barely-star, 39.75 ly — a locked furnace rock, a locked temperate giant, bare cold stone, and a ladder of mooned giants. A two-gate relay of the newest runs. The crew of twenty-eight holds the runs' newest record: the first relay to be entirely crewed by natives — every one of the twenty-eight born somewhere other than Earth, the Register's margin: *the quarter raises its own; the quarter notes the fact with something it declines to name.*
## GJ 537 A *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- Twin ember suns, 36.40 ly — each with its own family of locked furnace rocks and mooned icy stone. A three-gate relay of the newest runs. The crew of fifty keeps the runs' only double timekeeping: two clocks, one per sun, ticking three minutes apart, which the crews call *the argument*, and which nobody has ever proposed to fix.
## Warden *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- K-dwarf, 32.83 ly — locked furnace rocks, a small hot ocean world, airy mooned stones, and a three-mooned giant. A four-gate junction relay of the newest runs, held for the survey corps' approach-watch. The crews' petition to the Board — *call us Warden; we watch the gate* — was ratified the same cycle, the Register noting: *a station that asks for a name usually knows what it does.*
## Chara

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1534011998572555776 (Chara, β Canum Venaticorum), G, 27.64 ly
- **Population:** 580,000
- **System:** a Sun-like G star — the old sky's Joy, one of the Hunting Dogs' pair, and one of the first stars the ancestors' radio ears ever pointed at — over hot mooned rocks, airy stones, and airy cold outlands: a five-gate junction named for a hope.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Gar, HIP 66906, Noquisi, and Warden's star; Regional gate to β Com.
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: *Chara* — joy — the star the ancestors' first listeners watched, expecting a word that never came. The Board's minute is the Register's quietest: *the name is a hope; the hope is still open; ratify both.*

Chara keeps the listening. The junction's relay halls — the survey's arrays, the newest runs' dispatch, and the famous Quiet Wing, the continuation of the ancestors' old listening program, still pointed at the sky's nearest suns — hold a settlement that runs on the old star's old meaning: joy, unhurried. The population of listeners, dispatchers, and the newest families lives by the Quiet Wing's single instruction, cut in the array-hall door: *the sky has not answered; the sky has not been told we stopped; do not tell it.*
## Gar

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3735000631158990976 (Gar), M, 26.35 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace giant, a locked hot sea, then the settled world — a cool ocean planet at 294 K, three-fifths of its face under calm water, one moon — with icy outrunners and a two-mooned giant behind.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 473 B, Chara's star, and HIP 62687.
- **Registry name:** the ancient name, kept under the standing rule — one of Earth's inheritance. The Board's minute: *the register keeps the ancestors' words; the ocean world beneath this one keeps the ancestors' water; the keeping is the same act.*

Gar is the newest runs' ocean. The cool sea world's shore towns — the fishing fleets, the first-generation harbor culture, and the famous Listening Boats, the fishing craft that double as Chara's relay platforms, carrying the Quiet Wing's hydrophones out on the calm — hold the Union's newest garden charter, and the population of fisher families, listeners, and the newest arrivals lives by the charter's single rule, cut in the harbor stone: *the sea was not empty; the sky is not either; fish and listen.*
## Stillsea

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3902874650601954816 (GJ 473 B), brown dwarf, 14.60 ly
- **Population:** 940,000
- **System:** a failed star over locked rocks and paired hot seas — the second of them, **Stillsea** proper, a locked ocean planet at 298 K, three-quarters of its face under windless water — one of the near-deep's quiet gifts.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to GJ 473 A and Gar's star.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the locked world's seas never know weather — no rotation, no wind, one permanent glassy calm — and the crews' approach-calls all said the same two words. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board notes the water is, in fact, still, and commends the crews' restraint in not saying so twice.*

Stillsea is the calm world. The locked ocean planet's shore towns — the tideless harbors, the glasswater fisheries that feed the near-deep, and the famous mirror-nights, when the ember light doubles on the windless sea and the whole settlement goes quiet to watch — hold the Union's newest ocean charter, and the population of fisher families, mirror-watchers, and the newest arrivals lives by the charter's own line, cut in the harbor stone: *the sea is still; the settlement need not be; but it usually is.*
## Hedgerow

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3689602277083844480 (HIP 62687), M, 34.75 ly
- **Population:** 1.9 million
- **System:** a bright ember primary over locked furnace rocks, then the settled world — a cold airy mooned planet at 241 K — with airy mooned stones beyond: the newest runs' first hedge country.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Gar's star, Zavijava, and Porrima.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the settlement's charter crews planted the windbreak hedges before their own houses — the oldest rule of cold-country farming — and the crews' manifests had said *the hedgerow planet* since the first survey. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board notes the priority of the hedges with approval and some envy.*

Hedgerow is the newest runs' farm belt. The cold world's hedged country — the windbreak lines, the grain fields, and the famous Hedge Charter, which requires every settler's first act to be planting, not building — holds the newest runs' first real farms, and the population of farm families, hedge-keepers, and the newest arrivals lives by the charter's own line, cut in the first hedge: *the hedge protects the field; the field protects the town; the town protects the hedge; sign here.*
## Zavijava

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3796442680948579328 (Zavijava, β Virginis), G (1.4 solar masses), 35.88 ly
- **Population:** 1.1 million
- **System:** a bright G sun over mooned furnace rocks and hot airy stones, then the settled world — a warm ocean planet at 317 K, two-fifths of its face under shallow sea, two moons — the newest runs' warm water end.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Hedgerow's star (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: *Zavijava*, the old Arabic sky's word for the corner where the barking dog sat — the watch-star of the ancestors' harvest watch. The Board's minute: *the ancestors watched the harvest by this star; the settlement now farms beneath it; the register closes the loop with pleasure.*

Zavijava is the warm end of the newest runs. The warm ocean world's shore towns — the salt-pan farms, the two-moon tide mills, and the famous harvest watches, kept each year in the old star's own honor, the whole settlement counting the grain ships out by lamp — hold the Union's newest water charter, and the population of salt farmers, millers, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the tide mill's beam: *the moon gives the mill its turning; the star gives the year its counting; the town gives both its thanks.*
## Porrima

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3683687763520080384 (Porrima, γ Virginis), G (1.5 solar masses), 39.20 ly
- **Population:** 260,000
- **System:** a fierce G sun over mooned hot rocks, airy two-mooned stones, and a 323-Earth-mass six-mooned giant — the old sky's goddess of childbirth and prophecy, kept for what she watches over.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Hedgerow's star (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the ancient IAU-proper name, kept under the standing rule: *Porrima* — the ancestors' goddess of the childbed and the forecast — and the settlement keeps the register's own favorite joke: the newest runs' maternity annex sits here, beneath the childbed's star, and the Board's minute records that *the sky, on this occasion, has made a scheduling decision, which the Board ratifies.*

Porrima is the newest runs' childbed. The settlement is small, orbital, and precious: the maternity annex that the newest runs' far crews trust more than any nearer station, staffed by crews who chose the goddess's star on purpose, and the famous delivery bell, cast by the annex's first class, rung once for every child born under Porrima's light. The population of doctors, nurses, and the support families lives by the annex's single rule, cut in the bell's yoke: *every child is a forecast; deliver the forecast well.*
## Quietfield

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1470462322431834880 (HIP 66906), M, 29.98 ly
- **Population:** 700,000
- **System:** an ember primary over locked heavy rocks and bare stones, then the settled world — a cold airy mooned planet at 258 K — the newest runs' quiet farm belt.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Chara's star, β Com, and HIP 72944.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the settlement sits beside Chara's listening junction, and its charter asks for quiet — no heavy industry, no loud traffic — so the arrays can hear. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board asks the crews to whisper this one.*

Quietfield farms in the quiet. The cold world's farm towns — the low-noise fields, the hand-belled livestock (a real charter rule), and the famous hush markets, held in silence with chalk boards — hold the junction beside the Quiet Wing, and the population of farmers, hosts, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the market gate: *the sky is listening; let it hear us living.*
## Mufflestone *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- M-dwarf, 32.73 ly — locked heavy rocks, mooned airy stones, and a chain of giants: a three-gate relay of the newest runs, sister to Quietfield's hush country, with the same noise ordinances. The crew's petition asked the Board for *a name that sounds like what it does*; the Board, in the Register's shortest minute of the year, answered: *Mufflestone. Yes.*
## Muning

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).

- **System:** a bright G sun over hot airy mooned rocks, then the settled world — a heavy airy planet at 319 K, the hot edge of habitable, its dusk band the whole country — with two roads out.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Mufflestone's star and GJ 585.
- **Registry name:** the ancient name, kept under the standing rule — one of Earth's inheritance, carried across the gap. The Board's minute: *the register keeps the word; the world keeps its own dusk; the keeping is the same act.*

Muning is the newest runs' hot garden. The dusk belt's terrace towns — the shade-grown orchards, the night-side cellars, and the famous Dusk Market, which opens at the day's end and closes when the day begins again — hold the Union's warmest charter, and the population of growers, sellers, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the market's post: *the day is too hot for business; the dusk is for both.*
## GJ 585 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A barely-star, 36.59 ly — locked rocks, a mooned cold stone, and paired giants. The newest runs' quietest dead-end, held for the survey corps' calibration: the system's near-empty sky is the array's blank page, and the crews keep it that way — the Register's newest form of scenery, *nothing, certified.*
## Gaia EDR3 1172618435080418176 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A failed star, 41.52 ly — a tight ladder of locked rocks and airy stone, paired mooned giants. A three-gate relay of the newest runs. The crew of thirty keeps the runs' newest superstition: the relay's third rock, at 245 K with air, is *almost* habitable, and the crews toast it every cycle — *to the almost; the best of the lot; keep trying* — a toast the Register has, by now, stopped pretending to discourage.
## Gaia EDR3 1227705135863076864 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A barely-star, 43.15 ly — locked rocks and paired mooned giants, the whole system inside a month. - **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the ember light's long fall across the dusk belt was the first thing every approach crew described the same way. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board has read four surveys and one poem; all say the word.*

Emberfall is the dusk junction. The cold belt's terrace towns — the low-light farms, the ember-warm orchards that ripen by residual heat, and the famous Falls-Watch, the settlement's nightly hour when the whole town watches the light go — hold the junction, and the population of growers, keepers, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the terrace wall: *the light falls; the town stands; that is the arrangement.*
## Slackwater

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6329012134635132544 (GJ 545), M, 45.09 ly
- **Population:** 480,000
- **System:** an ember primary over a locked furnace giant, then the settled world — a heavy locked ocean planet at 298 K, three-quarters of its face under windless sea, one moon — the newest runs' still-water leaf.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Emberfall's star (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the locked world's sea never rises and never falls — no tides to speak of, no weather to speak of — and the crews called the harbor what it was. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board checked the tide tables; there are no tide tables.*

Slackwater is the still leaf. The locked ocean world's shore towns — the tideless harbors, the deep-shelf fisheries, and the famous Year List, the settlement's tradition of recording the year's entire weather on one line, which has fit on one line for two years running — hold the Union's calmest water charter, and the population of fishers, list-keepers, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the harbor stone: *nothing happens here; be ready; that is the skill.*
## 18 Sco

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4345775217221821312 (18 Scorpii), G — the solar twin: 1.02 solar masses, a light the old astronomers could not distinguish from Sol's own — 46.11 ly
- **Population:** 420,000
- **System:** the sky's most famous near-twin of the Sun — over mooned hot airy rocks, a bare temperate rock at 305 K that is one atmosphere short of being an Earth, and a long chain of giants to the rim: a system that looks, from far away, almost exactly like home.
- **Links:** Trunk gates to Emberfall's star and Sharjah.
- **Registry name:** kept under the standing rule for the famous designations: *18 Scorpii* — the solar twin, the star the old astronomers pointed at and said *there, almost.* The Board's minute is the Register's most-quoted since 61 Cyg A's: *the Sun's twin has no Earth; the Sun does; we are the difference; act like it.*

18 Sco is the almost-home. The settlement is small, orbital, and unusually quiet: the science corps' twin-study station — the arrays that measure the difference between this sun and ours, one part in a thousand at a time — the visitors' gallery, where crews from every polity come to look at the light that most resembles the one their ancestors left, and the famous Bare Rock Watch, the long observation of the one-atmosphere-short world, maintained in case it ever changes, which everyone knows it will not. The population of scientists, keepers, and pilgrims lives by the station's single instruction, cut in the gallery rail: *almost is not home; home is not forgotten; both facts pay the light bill.*

Sharjah is the runs' reading room. Longdeep is the deep-water Concord port. The ocean world's float-cities — the deep-diving crews, the Concord customs ring, and the famous First Soundings, the settlement's yearly attempt to find one floor, any floor, celebrated cheerfully each failure — hold the Union's newest deep charter on the League's border, and the population of divers, customs crews, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the first float's keel: *the sea keeps its secrets; respect them; then, gently, audit one.*

- **Settled:** 2405 (Free State charter, the demilitarized zone's first civil settlement).

The first referendum, held aboard every ship in the demilitarized zone, chose *Middleground* with a majority the Free State's minute called *suspiciously total*: *the crews of two blocs agree on one thing; this is it; record it as precedent and miracle.*

Middleground is the Free State's first floor. The neutral zone's civil hub — the joint arbitration annex, the demilitarized zone's only bonded warehouses that both blocs' inspectors certify together, and the famous Double Hall, where every meeting room is built with two doors and no head of the table — holds the Free State's founding settlement, and the population of arbiters, warehouse families, and the delegates of every polity who are resident here on purpose lives by the Free State's single law, cut in the Double Hall's threshold stone: *enter from either door; sit anywhere; the floor belongs to everyone.*

## Evening Watch *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A barely-star, 115.05 ly — a ladder of locked heavy rocks, one at 320 K with air, and airy ice beyond. A four-gate junction of the Free State's outer web, named for its shift: the zone's far evening watch, the last relay before the unaligned dark. The crews' single-line charter, kept by referendum: *we watch the evening; the evening watches back; keep the light small and the kettle on.*
## Quarterdeck *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- An ember star, 106.15 ly — locked heavy rocks, a mooned giant, and airy icy stone. A four-gate junction of the Free State's outer web, held for the joint patrol's outer academy: where the zone's watch officers, both blocs' navies together, are taught. The academy's single rule, cut in the quarterdeck's rail: *the deck belongs to whoever keeps it; keep it.*
## Cor Caroli

- **Settled:** 2426 (Free State charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Concord Free State (independent)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1517698716348324992 (Cor Caroli, α Canum Venaticorum), A (2.7 solar masses, 48 solar luminosities), 99.83 ly
- **Population:** 460,000
- **System:** a fierce white A sun — the old sky's Heart of Charles, named by an astronomer for a dead king — over a mooned airy rock, then the settled world: a cold airy two-mooned planet at 285 K, far out at 6 au in the long bright light.
- **Links:** Regional gates to GJ 3760 B and α CVn B; Regional approach to Quarterdeck's routes.
- **Registry name:** kept under the Free State's heritage clause — the ancient name, one of the oldest dedications in the old sky, predates every bloc. The Free State's minute: *a name that has already outlived one kingdom will comfortably outlast two; keep it.*

Cor Caroli is the bright edge of the zone. The far cold world's small towns — the farms under the doubled starlight of the Hunting Dogs' famous heart, the outer academy's annex, and the famous Bright Watch, the settlement's nightly hour under a sun nearly fifty times Sol's glare — hold the Free State's brightest charter, and the population of farmers, watch-crews, and bloc-crossed families lives by the Free State's single line, cut in the watch-stone: *the light is old; the heart is named; neither belongs to anyone; sit with both.*
## Deadfall *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- An ember star, 90.39 ly — a ladder of locked heavy rocks and a mooned stone. A four-gate junction of the Free State's outer web, named for the crews' oldest safety note: the system's tight orbits give arriving traffic no room for error. The junction's single posted rule, kept by referendum: *the trap is the schedule; arrive slow; the dead fall last.*
## Keelhaul *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A dim K sun, 85.95 ly — a locked furnace giant, hot airy rocks, and a 639-Earth-mass colossus at the rim. A four-gate junction of the Free State's outer web, named for the crews' honest description of the system's weather: the colossus's wake across the anchor approaches drags everything sideways once per pass. The junction's one rule, kept by referendum: *when the big one comes through, nothing else does; that is not a rule; that is physics; we just wrote it down.*
## Wayfarer *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- An ember star, 86.15 ly — a ladder of locked rocks, one at 251 K with air, and a giant-rich tail. A four-gate junction of the Free State's outer web, named for its traffic: the far couriers of both blocs, who meet here by custom, exchange mail, and part without incident. The junction's single-line charter, kept by referendum: *the letters go on; the navies don't; that's the treaty working.*
## Allnight *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted — and the last of the Free State's outer web.
- A failed star, 87.10 ly — a locked steam sea and airy ice, all its roads already walked. A three-gate closing junction of the zone's far quarter, named for the crews' last watch-word: the relay that sees the demilitarized zone's darkest stretch through to dawn. The crews' single-line charter, the Free State's newest minute: *the night is long; we are longer; good night.*
## α CVn B

- **Settled:** 2426 (Free State charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Concord Free State (independent)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1517698613271954304 (α Canum Venaticorum B, the famous Heart's companion), F (1.5 solar masses), 106.55 ly
- **Population:** 570,000
- **System:** the Heart's other sun — a fierce F star over hot airy rocks, then the settled pair: a cold airy rock at 262 K and, far out at 8.7 au in the long light, a warm ocean world (284 K, five-sixths of its face under sea, two moons) — the Free State's brightest pair of gardens.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 1522768049067813504, Gaia EDR3 1518572759373468160, and Gaia EDR3 1520198009358021888; Regional approach to Cor Caroli.
- **Registry name:** kept under the Free State's heritage clause: the famous companion of the old sky's Charles's Heart, designation and all. The Free State's minute: *the Heart has two suns; the register keeps both; the zone keeps the light.*

α CVn B is the Heart's second garden. The cold airy world's farm-towns and the far warm ocean world's sea-towns — twin settlements riding one star's long light, the Free State's farthest habitable pair — hold the zone's newest charter, and the population of farmers, fishers, and bloc-crossed families lives by the Free State's single line, cut in both harbors: *two worlds; one star; no flags; dinner at dusk.*
## Deepfield *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A failed star, 112.15 ly — a ladder of locked rocks, one at 292 K with air, and icy giants. A three-gate relay of the Free State's far web, named for its instrument: the zone's deep-sky array, pointed past everything mapped at the void between the suns. The crews' single-line charter, kept by referendum: *we watch what is not ours; that is the zone's whole job; pour again.*
## Windward *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A failed star, 116.77 ly — locked rocks and a chain of icy giants. A three-gate relay of the Free State's far web, named for its position: the first junction the zone's ships reach coming down the long wind from the Great Bear's tail. The crews' single-line charter, kept by referendum: *the wind brings everyone here eventually; be the harbor, not the weather.*
## Frostbite *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A dead sun's white light, 107.58 ly — a tight ladder of locked heavy rocks and icy stone. A four-gate junction of the Free State's far web, named for the crews' honest review of its location: the coldest watch in the register, all routes outward. The crews' single-line charter, kept by referendum: *dress for it; the zone does not supply mittens; share.*
## Snowdrift *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- An ember star, 108.93 ly — locked rocks, a mooned cold stone, and a five-mooned giant. A four-gate junction of the Free State's far web, named for the crews' description of its traffic: the far quarter's cargo piles up here in storm season like the old winter word, and melts the same way. The crews' single-line charter, kept by referendum: *what piles up, digs out; bring a shovel; the zone thanks you.*
## Longwatch *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A dim K sun, 116.03 ly — a locked furnace rock, hot airy stones, a temperate mooned world at 293 K, and a giant-rich tail. A five-gate junction of the Free State's far web, named for its shift: the zone's longest watch, five routes of patience in one system. The crews' single-line charter, kept by referendum: *the watch is long; the kettle is shared; keep both going.*
## Warm Rest *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A dim K sun, 111.82 ly — a mooned hot rock, a temperate giant at 268 K, and a mooned airy world. A three-gate relay of the Free State's far web, named for the crews' only request: after Longwatch, this. The referendum kept it without amendment. The crews' single-line charter: *sleep first; file later; the zone forgives the paperwork.*
## Halfway Warm *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A failed star, 108.29 ly — a ladder of locked rocks and airy icy stone. A three-gate relay of the Free State's far web, named for the crews' thermometer joke: warmer than Frostbite, cooler than anywhere, exactly halfway. The referendum kept the joke, as it keeps most of them. The crews' single-line charter: *warm is a direction; walk that way.*
## Firstlight House *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- An ember star, 104.21 ly — locked rocks, a small hot sea, and airy icy stone. A four-gate junction of the Free State's far web, named for the crews' own harbor custom: the first crew to arrive each sol lights the station's lamp, and the last to depart puts it out. The referendum's single-line minute: *the lamp belongs to whoever's here; that's everybody; keep it burning.*
## Hollow *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- A failed G-star, 103.61 ly — a tight ladder of locked rocks and paired giants. A four-gate junction of the Free State's far web, named for its honest inventory: nothing here, all roads out. The crews of twenty-six keep the runs' one disputed record: the station's galley soup, which both blocs' visiting patrols have rated, in writing, in their own logs — and whose recipe the station will release, the crews say, *at the next armistice.*

The crews of thirty-three keep the runs' newest shared custom, adopted from the Free State's book: the lamp at the window, lit for whoever's coming, fueled by whoever's there — *the light is the toll; pay it forward.*
## Gaia EDR3 3903379292080122368 *(catalogue station)*

- **Settled:** 2426; never promoted.
- An ember star, 81.26 ly — a ladder of locked heavy rocks and paired mooned giants. A three-gate relay of the Union's outer runs. The crews of twenty-one maintain the runs' newest tradition: a wall of unopened mail, by treaty choice, whose oldest letter has been waiting nine cycles for an addressee whose forwarding address is still being traced — the Register's margin: *the mail is honest; the routes are long; both are kept.*
## Stillharbor

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3734930606012228096 (GJ 3751), M, 71.20 ly

- **Links:** Trunk gates to Gaia EDR3 3901165013100805248 and Gaia EDR3 3903379292080122368.
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the ocean world's harbors never know a swell — no storms, no tides worth the word, the calmest water in the newest runs — and the crews' approach-calls all said the same. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board notes the harbor gauge has not moved since installation, and finds this restful.*

Stillharbor is the calm leaf. The cool ocean world's shore towns — the tideless harbors, the mirror fisheries, and the famous Gauge, the settlement's oldest instrument, its still needle a civic monument — hold the Union's newest water charter, and the population of fishers, keepers, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the Gauge's post: *the water is still; the settlement may be; neither is required to explain.*

Farhaven is the line's end. The cool ocean world's shore towns — the fishing, the end-of-line depot, and the famous Turning, the settlement's yearly gathering when the whole population faces Sol's direction for one bell before turning back to the sea — hold the Union's farthest water charter of the wave, and the population of fishers, depot families, and the newest arrivals lives by the charter's single line, cut in the harbor stone: *far is where you are; make it home; the fish agree.*

## Breadth

- **Settled:** 2426 (Union Concord-era charter, the newest runs).
- **Sovereignty:** USE
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-3900151160301345152 (17 Virginis), G (1.2 solar masses), 97.83 ly
- **Population:** 570,000
- **System:** a bright G sun over hot mooned rocks and airy stones, then the settled pair: a cold airy two-mooned world at 253 K, and a far airy wanderer at 267 K, seventeen years out in the long light — the newest runs' widest two-garden spread.
- **Links:** Trunk gate to Gaia EDR3 3907641136588506624 (the one road).
- **Registry name:** the crews' name, ratified 2426: the two worlds sit wider apart than any habitable pair in the newest runs — the settlement's whole span the crews called its breadth — and the survey filed the word as the petition. The Board's minute: *ratified; the Board confirms the distance, and needs to sit down.*

Breadth is the wide pair. The inner world's farm-towns and the far wanderer's hermit-holdings — one settlement stretched across the better part of a light-hour, its mail runs the longest local routes in the Union — hold the charter, and the population of farmers, hermits, and the ferry crews who stitch the two together lives by the settlement's single line, cut in both harbors: *we are far apart on purpose; the mail goes anyway; so does the news.*

## Gaia EDR3 3948760569364949632 *(catalogue station)*

## Greyling

Greyling is the grey sea leaf. The cool ocean world's boat-towns — the grey-sea fisheries, the two-moon tides, and the famous Grey Catch, the settlement's festival when the year's first haul is cooked grey and eaten grey by lamplight — hold the charter, and the population of fisher families, keepers, and the newest families lives by the charter's single line, cut in the first boat's gunwale: *the sea is grey; the lamp is warm; that is the whole arrangement.*

Edasich holds the middle watch. ## Dhanishta

- **Settled:** 2426 (chapter charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-1763000482062205056 (Dhanishta, β Delphini), G giant (1.8 solar masses, 9 solar luminosities), 116.93 ly
- **Population:** 280,000
- **System:** the old sky's drum-star — a bright G giant over a furnace gas giant, hot airy rocks, and a giant-rich tail with two colossi: the far northern web's busiest crossing under one of the ancestors' oldest names.
- **Links:** Regional gates to γ02 Del and Buena Vista's star; Mesh gates to HIP 101966, GJ 821.1, HIP 100970, and Gaia EDR3 1843169860576349312.
- **Registry name:** kept under the Standing Register rule: *Dhanishta* — the ancestors' Vedic name for the Dolphin's tail-star, the drum of the old nakshatra. The Chapter Register: *the drum is old; the dance is new; keep time.*

Dhanishta keeps the far north's time. The bright giant's way-stations — the chapter's busiest far-northern hostels, the assay annex that grades half the newest quarter's freight, and the famous Drum-Watch, the settlement's nightly count of the anchor rings' comings and goings, struck out on the old rhythm — hold six gates' worth of traffic, and the population of hostellers, assayists, and the newest crews lives by the Chapter Register's single line, cut in the hostel door: *the drum keeps time; the table keeps bread; the door keeps neither; enter.*
