## Algedi

- **Settled:** 2426 (Auction charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-6880093650314169984 (Algedi, α Capricorni), K giant (2.5 solar masses, 39 solar luminosities), 109.08 ly
- **Population:** 260,000
- **System:** the old sky's goat-kid — a vast orange K giant over distant bare rocks, a warm airy ocean world (364 K, 21% sea, one moon) out in the long light, and a giant-rich cold tail: the Freeports' brightest southern name.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Gaia EDR3 4188740080328695424, Gaia EDR3 6899801709040696960, and Gaia EDR3 6879883334353135488; Mesh gates to Hotdock's star and Gaia EDR3 6899859776999307264.
- **Registry name:** kept under the heritage carve-out: *Algedi* — the ancestors' Arabic word for the kid-goat of the old Capricorn, carried in the IAU's christening of α Capricorni. The auctioneer opened the sale by withdrawing the lot to applause, the Freeports' oldest ritual.

Algedi is the southern trade's bright mark. The orange giant's way-stations — the Freeports' far southern assay annex, the warm world's drying-yards and salt-pans, and the famous Kid-Light Festival, the port's annual night under a star the ancestors named for something small and stubborn — hold the newest wave's brightest southern crossing, and the population of assayists, salt-farmers, and the newest bidding families lives by the Freeports' single posted law, painted at the tariff hall: *the light is old; the price is current; both are honest.*

## Okab

- **Settled:** 2426 (chapter charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** St Raphael Commonwealth (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4314399312979641728 (Okab, ζ Aquilae), F (2.4 solar masses, 32 solar luminosities), 85.38 ly
- **Population:** 420,000
- **System:** the Eagle's wing-beat — a fierce white F sun over distant mooned rocks, then two habitable airy worlds (279 K and 250 K, each two-mooned) in the wide bright orbits: the far southern web's brightest double garden.
- **Links:** Regional gates to HIP 93966, HIP 92984, Gaia EDR3 4281877511292289152, and Laudes' star.
- **Registry name:** kept under the Standing Register rule: *Okab* — the ancestors' Arabic word for ζ Aquilae, the Eagle's wing. The Chapter Register: *the wing is old; the gardens are new; both are kept.*

Okab is the south's bright pair. Two temperate airy worlds — one the farm and orchard, one the school and workshop — share one fierce sun and one charter, and the chapter's far southern seminary sits here, training the newest generation under the brightest light of the web. The population of farm families, seminarians, and work-crews lives by the Chapter Register's single line, cut in both worlds' harbors: *the wing holds two; the charter holds all; fly well.*

## Bake-eo

- **Settled:** 2426 (Auction charter, the newest wave).
- **Sovereignty:** Vesper Freeports (League of Free Systems)
- **Catalogue:** gaia-edr3-4472097420946809600 (Bake-eo), A (2.1 solar masses, 21 solar luminosities), 96.93 ly
- **Population:** 380,000
- **System:** a fierce white A sun over hot mooned rocks, a warm ocean world (71% sea at 347 K, one moon) in the wide light, and a six-mooned giant at the rim: the far southern web's brightest new port, under the ancestors' name.
- **Links:** Regional gates to Cebalrai, HIP 86765, Gaia EDR3 4377070371869033600, and HIP 86765's approach; Mesh gates to Gaia EDR3 4393807554480456448, Gaia EDR3 4363458933311794816, and Gaia EDR3 4377070371869033600's approach.
- **Registry name:** kept under the heritage carve-out: *Bake-eo* — the ancestors' Korean star-name, carried in the IAU's christening. The auctioneer withdrew the lot to applause and sold only the port rights around the light.

Bake-eo is the far southern web's bright door. Six routes meet at the fierce white sun, and the settlement — the newest resort quarter's northern gate — sells its own brightness by the hour: solar-sail charging, aquaculture in the warm world's light-warmed shallows, and the famous Glare Schedule, the port's posted timetable of when the star's light hits each dock, when the tide of resort traffic flows, and when a hull is better waited than launched. The population of port crews, chargers, and aquaculturists lives by the posted law, painted at the tariff hall: *the light is free; the parking is posted; read the board.*
